Near-half-variable PC degree

Establish superpolynomial proof-size lower bounds for every fixed depth of \(\mathrm{AC}^0[2]\)-Frege via a Boolean formula family with a near-half-variable polynomial-calculus degree barrier. The family may depend on the fixed depth, but not on the proposed polynomial proof-size exponent. Finding the family and verifying the complete transfer are both required.

Origin: starting research record.

Starting point

Created at the user's request on 8 October 2026 as a separate alternative to the main notebook's nonlinear-error-rank route. Setup only: the research record is intentionally empty, and no parallel agent has been started. The starting result is the reviewed moving-cutoff source theorem. Pang's Section 8 supplies the original near-midpoint motivation. Claims and evidence remain in the shared registry; inherit the linked proofs, not a second copy of their record.

Where we stand

The goal is open; no suitable family is known. For fixed approximation depth \(\ell\ge2\), the source theorem needs an old Boolean PC barrier at \(D=\lfloor v/2-s\rfloor\), \(s=o(v/(\log v)^{\ell-2})\), with its original degree and product-only grammar hypotheses. The complete Frege transfer remains separate.

Main object: an evolving formula specification. The accepted envelope uses block-minimal unsatisfiable bounded-arity relations, polynomial canonical CNF encoding, essential scopes and at least two block occurrences per variable. It also requires \(\liminf h/v\ge1/(2w)\), where \(h\) counts variables in at least three blocks, and no global polarity making all rows intersection-closed, and joint consistency of local binary affine consequences. The width-four witness has positive linear degree and proves unbounded non-vacuity, not near-half hardness. Concrete families are existence and falsification controls; their special features do not silently constrain an abstract proof.

Route decisions. Before each next step, compare direct lower-bound constructions, proof-method repairs and structural restrictions by their effect on the necessary missing implication. Upper bounds are one possible test, not the default objective. If one is found, extract its structural scope and compare the gain with lost properties. Accept a specification revision only with an unbounded witness and full degree/encoding costs; otherwise keep it provisional. After proving a theorem, derive and check inexpensive corollaries that strengthen the formula specification; do not stop at a method observation when a useful structural consequence is cheaply available. Adopt the refinement only after verifying non-vacuity and the full encoding/degree budget, without hiding the hardness conclusion in admissibility. Preserve this decision rule across compactions, with dated arguments and withdrawn properties in the record.

Current route. The uniform-target modular source is excluded at depths at least three; central targets preserve an unbounded alternative but no near-half proof. All-context selectors now preserve compatible marginals on that source. Their vector kernels still fail in controls. Before repairing them, test whether their polynomial-dimensional state space can support the required degree barrier at all.

Evidence ledger. Actual upper bounds exclude only their proved regimes; certificate ceilings and failed invariants constrain methods. The earlier portfolio remains calibration material. Auxiliary imports provide conditional transfer tools and domain-loss warnings, while the OpenAI Math screen provides scoped leads. The latest pinned auxiliary import adds full-cube clause pruning for family 112, old-design slice representations and conditional rank splitting; their hard-survival and unit gaps remain. None supplies the missing family. The main notebook continues independently.

Spin is active; local checkpoints only.

Open statements

  1. Near-half Boolean PC family — open. For each fixed relevant approximation depth \(\ell\ge2\), construct a polynomial-size, preferably constant-width unsatisfiable Boolean CNF family on \(v\to\infty\) variables, independent of the proof-size exponent \(A\), with \(1\notin C_D\) over \(\mathbb F_2\) for \(D=\lfloor v/2-s\rfloor\), \(s=o(v/(\log v)^{\ell-2})\). A stronger near-square-root-deficit barrier also suffices. Growing width needs a new complete cost ledger, not automatic application of the fixed-input-degree theorem.
  2. Complete Frege transfer — open. For that same family and every fixed Frege depth and polynomial exponent, verify a source/proof interpretation covered by Pang\'s argument or the moving-cutoff theorem, including encoding length, original axiom and approximation degrees, coefficient domains and coherent source grammar. Derive the superpolynomial bound in formula length.

The remaining route

  1. Develop a non-vacuous specification and prove its near-half barrier. This is the highest-risk obligation. The general envelope has a positive-linear-degree witness. The coupled construction witnesses the joint rank/radius/projection requirements, but its small puncture separator gives short refutations. The stronger separator condition still has no unbounded witness. Private-coordinate projection proves the essential-scope normalization. Improve the specification together with its proof method; the near-half implication remains missing. Compare structural refinements and method changes by explicit gains, lost properties and degree/encoding costs. The fixed-cutoff local-state interface is one candidate method: control defect images in the common quotient and preserve one, rather than forbid every earlier fall. It is not mandatory machinery: an independently constructed larger proper closed space also suffices. The code route now excludes ternary graphical leader charts with sublinear deficit, but arbitrary wider columns remain open. The growing-modulus test is settled by absorption, and a stronger interpolation bound excludes its original uniform-target instances for depths at least three. Central targets retain an unbounded source with the same budget but no near-half proof. The local-section head has compatible idempotent selectors but no surviving graded kernel; its next test is whether the polynomial-dimensional state space has enough dimension for that endpoint. The random-relation, local-section and Kneser leads remain queued. Growing-width alternatives retain a separate source ledger. A universal consequence quotient or a condition asserting the desired hardness is not a construction.
  2. Verify the encoding and proof boundary. Count all Boolean and auxiliary variables, clauses, widths and degree changes in both directions of any encoding transfer. Keep a polynomial encoding and the correct field; adding or deleting variables is not free.
  3. Complete the parameter argument. Apply the exact source theorem for every fixed polynomial proof-size exponent on one family at each fixed depth. The eventual threshold may depend on that exponent; the family itself may not.

Proposed next step

On near-half-family, test the dimension budget of the new compatible state space before repairing its operators. Prove or refute a general lower bound on the quotient dimension of any proper degree-D Boolean kernel containing the active axioms of an unsatisfiable formula and closed under every legal variable multiplication. Use leading monomials and divisor closure, with exact cutoff and input-width hypotheses; check whether a small stabilized quotient would force a global solution. Apply the result to the polynomial-dimensional central-source construction. If its dimension is inadequate, enlarge the representation or return to an explicit larger kernel rather than continuing low-degree repairs inside it.

Working mathematical context

Global kernel test. In \(B=\mathbb F_2[x]/(x_i^2-x_i)\), use multilinear degree and the cylinder ideals of individual rows. Put \(K_d=\sum_a(J_a\cap B_{\le d})\), \(H_D=(K_D\cap B_{\le D-1})/K_{D-1}\), \(Q_D=B_{\le D}/K_D\). The maps \([q]\mapsto[x_jq]\) must vanish, with active axioms in \(K_D\) and \(1\notin K_D\), to imply ordinary PC hardness. A larger explicitly constructed proper closed kernel is allowed; the unknown PC quotient restates the goal.

Latest global tests. The canonical padded fall is absorbed by a majority-overlap row, uniformly over the adaptive pair choice; this proves neither full closure nor unit survival. The new marginal construction gives compatible idempotent selectors on the central source in polynomial dimension. Sparse quadratic moment maps handle its double overlaps. Scalar axiom annihilation does not give vector or cofactor annihilation; Hardy is an explicit failure. Full commuting selectors would require actual global solutions. Check the dimension needed by a proper truncated kernel before repairing this state space.

Method controls. The one-bit module allows nonzero falls with zero next action; all proper joint members can still miss multiplication. The Horn static design and Schur quotient preserve a unit but fail dynamic closure. A normalized original-input annihilator alone is NS, not PC. Binomially spanned proper pseudoideals containing Booleanity select a point through degree \(D-1\); characteristic-two affine two-state charts retain this obstruction, with the full evaluation kernel when both states are nonzero.

Normalization. Original width \(w_v\le D_v\) suffices for every whole-row core followed by private/irrelevant projection to retain \(n_v\ge D_v+1\) and deficit \(s'_v=\max\{0,s_v-(v-n_v)/2\}\). Terminal degree \(D\) replays through \(\max\{w_v,D\}\). Thus prove the barrier on the original source first. This preserves polynomial encoding and the near-half deficit, but neither makes logarithmic width constant nor completes Frege transfer.

Accepted specification. The fixed-width envelope has block-minimal UNSAT relations on essential scopes of size at most \(w\), at most \(Cv^b\) blocks, every variable in at least two blocks, and \(\liminf h/v\ge1/(2w)\) for the higher-occurrence count. It excludes common-polarity Horn systems and requires joint consistency of local binary affine consequences. Either excluded case has an original-width ceiling. The width-four witness has linear size, positive linear degree and \(h/v\ge2/5\); it passes both later screens. Canonical length is \(O(C2^wwv^b\log(v+1))\), counting all auxiliaries. These block-sensitive conditions do not cancel an upper bound in another paid representation.

Central modular source. The replacement keeps \(v=l2^l,k=8l,m=5\cdot2^{l-3},q\asymp v^2\), five random partitions and independent uniform coefficients, but sets \(b_a=\frac12\sum_i a_{ai}\). It is complement-invariant and field-consistent at all coordinates \(1/2\). Its zero fibre has exactly the uniform affine-row law on \(k-1\) bits. With high probability it is Boolean-empty, affine-full and pin-extendible through \(\lfloor k/64\rfloor\). Every normalized core retains \(n\ge e^{-512}v/256\), full-affine essential rows, two to five occurrences, no common Horn polarity and length \(O(n^9)\). Independent targets are lost; no variables are added. Higher-degree survival, branching density and growing-width transfer remain open.

Modular interpolation ceiling. A nonzero-coefficient row of width \(k_a\) interpolates through \(t_a=(k_a-1)/2\) at odd width, \(k_a/2-1\) at even noncentral width, and \(k_a/2\) at even central width, in proof characteristic different from the odd prime modulus. A fractional exact cover with weights \(\lambda_a\) gives \(d_F\le\max\{w,2,\lfloor v/2-\Delta\rfloor+1\}+1\), \(\Delta=\sum_a\lambda_a(k_a/2-t_a)\). Uniform targets yield original degree at most \(v/2-v/k+2\), excluding depth-at-least-three barriers at \(k=\Theta(\log v)\). Central even rows leave only \(v/2+2\). These bounds do not automatically reverse to selected cores.

Fixed-modulus code screens. For fixed odd prime \(q\), bounded original row width and proof characteristic different from \(q\), the rank ceiling is \(\max\{v-r,v/2-r/(2q)\}+o(v)\); the radius ceiling is \(\max\{(1-1/q)v/2+\rho/q,\rho\}+o(v)\). Near-half hardness needs \(\rho\ge v/2-o(v)\), \(r\le v/2+o(v)\). At rank half, deficit \(t=(v-r)-\rho=o(v)\) gives a systematic row basis of support at most \(q(t+1)\), not a polynomial canonical encoding automatically. Field-inconsistent systems already have a fixed gap below half.

Projection and separators. Let \(\beta(L)\) be the smallest Boolean-empty coordinate projection and \(\kappa(C)\) the minimum puncture size plus largest remaining direct-sum block. Their paid ceilings have form \(\max\{(1-1/q)v/2+z/(2q),z\}+o(v)\); each must be near half, eventually at least \(D-1\). The coupled width-ten witness meets rank/radius/projection and the structural conditions within \(O(\sqrt v)\), but has \(\kappa=O(\sqrt v)\) and short proofs. The full stronger conjunction lacks a witness. In ternary graphical leader charts, \(4t\) punctures leave codimension-two factors, giving degree \(v/3+o(v)\) when \(t=o(v)\). At zero deficit graphicality is automatic; wider columns at positive deficit remain open.

Other actual ceilings. Compact three-colouring is bounded by \(v/3+4/3\); four-colouring by \(v/2+1\). The matching-centre boundary system replays through \(\max\{D,6\}\), without reverse or truncated-closure equality. The Kneser source has growing odd girth but no PC bound. Grouped exact-one rows and paired two-weight rows are excluded at their exact recorded scopes; the latter has a quarter-variable ceiling. Subfield offsets have degree at most twice their input degree. Other predicates or fields require separate ledgers.

Occurrence and certificate limits. The all-field occurrence ceiling is \(h+(1/2-1/(4w-2))(v-h)+O_w(\sqrt{v\log(v+1)})\) at fixed \(w\); use the exact record for growing width. Respectful and immunity certificates reach at most one third and one quarter in their stated ranges, not actual-degree upper bounds. Generic-set certificates stay below one third.

Random-half alternative. At \(k=\lceil2\log_2v\rceil,m=v+k\), the half-table source retains linear-size normalized cores and binary immunity through \(\lfloor k/2-\sqrt k\rfloor\). Whole-row absorption fails as a near-half support method. The actual original-instance ceiling is \(v/2+3v/\sqrt k+O(1)\), above target, without reverse core transfer. Global hardness, terminal branching and growing-width transfer remain open.

Exact sufficient transfer source. With Booleanity and \(1\notin C_D(\Gamma_v)\), at most \(v^A\) complete binary ENS blocks may have strictly lower-level product-only dependencies: old polynomials of fixed degree \(r\), plus constant combinations of earlier whole products, never raw coefficients. Uniform \(h=\lfloor v^\epsilon\rfloor\), \(0<\epsilon<1/(3\ell)\), excludes original degree \(\lfloor v^{1/3}\rfloor\). Use \(K=D-\lceil v^{1/3}\rceil\), last approximation degree \(v^{o(1)}\), and one final multiplier. Product cost is \(h(\delta+1)\), companion cost adds \(\delta\); all companions and fresh Boolean equations count. The binary PC compiler needs coherent profiles and literal CNF; ordinary NS retains a proof-height factor.

Imported tools. Weighted map descent requires the scalar-extended binary pseudoideal, complete weighted fresh Boolean equations, \(T\ge1,B\ge2,b+TB\le D\); it keeps half the multiplication rank at \(b+k\le D\) without an extra \(3k\) buffer, but supplies no maps or old hardness. Later scoped imports retain clause-pruning, slice/conditioning and rank-splitting limits; the external source screen supplies no qualifying near-half theorem.

Gaps identified by formalization

No side-thread formalization has been assigned or performed; its working proofs remain informal. Inherited results retain their recorded verification status.

Research record

8 October 2026 — Subfield offsets give low-degree PC refutations

Working PC upper bound; subfield-offset candidate audit. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. Can the strong inverse-polynomial degree bounds for knapsack in positive characteristic supply the near-half Boolean PC family? The required implication is dynamic PC hardness over \(\mathbb F_2\), at a cutoff approaching half the actual Boolean variable count, for one polynomial-size family independent of the proof-size exponent. A high-degree static inverse does not by itself establish that implication.

New mathematics. The subfield-offset ceiling below is a direct application of the recorded Frobenius companion lemma, not a new mechanism. It gives a degree-\(2r\) binary-characteristic PC refutation for every multilinear degree-\(r\) polynomial with coefficients in a finite subfield and target outside it. This excludes the tested positive-characteristic knapsack construction as a source of the required PC barrier.

Goal progress. Unchanged. Neither a near-half family nor the complete Frege transfer is proved. The source audit rejects a particular proposed import; its IPS lower bound is not contradicted.

General statement. cor:subfield-offset-degree-ceiling holds for every finite binary field, strict finite subfield, outside target, variable count and degree in the statement. It is an application of the Frobenius companion lemma. The earlier compression gap already exhibits high inverse degree alongside cheap dynamic PC consequences.

Read the source with the correct proof system

Forbes–Shpilka–Tzameret–Wigderson, 15 June 2016 author manuscript, Proposition 5.3 and Appendix A, discuss knapsack inverse degree and classical PC bounds. The quoted PC theorem in Appendix A has characteristic-zero scope; Proposition 5.3 assumes characteristic greater than the variable count. These statements do not import a binary-characteristic PC bound.

Behera–Limaye, ICALP 2025, Theorem 6 and Lemmas 17–19, treat positive characteristic. Lemma 17 gives maximal inverse degree with probability at least \(1-(2^n-1)/|S|\) for linear-form coefficients sampled uniformly from \(S^n\), where \(S\) lies in a subfield and the target lies outside it. Thus high probability requires a sufficiently large sampling set. Theorem 6 uses a multilinear degree-five polynomial \(\sum_{i<j<k<l}\alpha_{ijkl}z_{ijkl}x_ix_jx_kx_l-\beta\), with coefficients in \(\mathbb F_{p^k}\) and \(\beta\in\mathbb F_{p^{2k}}\setminus\mathbb F_{p^k}\), and proves a size lower bound for multilinear constant-depth \(\mathrm{IPS}_{\mathrm{LIN}'}\). Its literal displayed Boolean variable count is \(v=n+\binom n4\), rather than just \(n\). The following PC upper proof applies when \(p=2\).

The subfield-offset ceiling

Corollary (subfield offset ceiling). Let \(E\) be a finite field of characteristic two and \(K=\mathbb F_{2^k}\subsetneq E\), with \(k\ge1\). Let \(\beta\in E\setminus K\) and let \(g\in K[x_1,\ldots,x_v]\) be multilinear of degree at most \(r\), where \(r\ge1\). The system consisting of \(g-\beta=0\) and all Boolean equations \(x_i^2-x_i=0\) has an ordinary PC refutation over \(E\) through degree \(2r\). No bound on \(k\), the number of monomials, or \([E:K]\) is required.

Proof. Write \(g=\sum_S a_Sx_S\), including a possible constant term, and define coefficientwise Frobenius conjugates \[ f_j=\sum_S a_S^{2^j}x_S-\beta^{2^j},\qquad 0\le j\le k. \] Each has degree at most \(r\), and \(f_0\) is an input. From a derived final line \(f_j\), multiply separately by its monomials and take their scalar combination to derive \(f_j^2\). These ordinary PC steps have degree at most \(2r\); the earlier derivation of \(f_j\) is reused without multiplying its old lines. In characteristic two, \[ f_j^2-f_{j+1}=\sum_S a_S^{2^{j+1}}(x_S^2-x_S). \] For \(S=\{i_1,\ldots,i_t\}\), the literal identity \[ x_S^2-x_S=\sum_{h=1}^t(x_{i_h}^2-x_{i_h})\prod_{a<h}x_{i_a}^2\prod_{a>h}x_{i_a} \] has every summand of degree at most \(2t\le2r\). Thus the Boolean axioms derive the displayed difference within the same ceiling, and subtraction derives \(f_{j+1}\). Repeating does not add degree costs. Since all \(a_S\in K\), subtracting \(f_0\) from \(f_k\) gives the constant \(\beta-\beta^{2^k}\). It is nonzero: the roots of \(T^{2^k}-T\) in \(E\) are exactly the \(2^k\) elements of \(K\), by the polynomial root bound. Scalar division yields one. Constant \(g\) already gives a nonzero constant input; this also satisfies the bound. \(\square\)

This is the existing bounded Frobenius argument with multiplier one. Alternatively expand the coefficients in an \(\mathbb F_2\)-basis of their span together with \(\beta\); the companion lemma recovers the constant coordinate. Its hypotheses allow an extension-valued input system, so the argument does not require the input itself to be defined over \(\mathbb F_2\). The proof above supplies the complete specialization and its ordinary-degree ledger. Finiteness of \(E\) is retained to match that lemma and the tested source, although the direct argument needs only the finite subfield; strictness follows from the outside-target assumption.

Application and encoding boundary

The source's affine instance has PC degree at most two; its degree-five instance has PC degree at most ten over its stated extension field. Both bounds hold for every coefficient choice in the source's subfield regime, even when the inverse polynomial has maximal degree. Increasing the coefficient-field size cannot change these upper bounds. Hence neither instance provides a PC ceiling growing near \(v/2\).

These are extension-coefficient systems, not yet CNFs over \(\mathbb F_2\). There is no automatic scalar descent for arbitrary extension-valued input axioms. The direct coordinate encoding is even easier: choose an \(\mathbb F_2\)-basis of \(E\) extending a basis of \(K\) and including \(\beta\). The \(\beta\)-coordinate equation of \(g-\beta=0\) is the nonzero constant equation \(1=0\). Thus this specific coordinate translation has degree-zero refutation. This observation does not assert that every circuit or CNF encoding inherits a degree-zero proof; any different encoding needs its own degree-transfer analysis.

The static inverse lower bound remains compatible with the dynamic upper bound. Iterated squaring and Boolean reduction keep the final consequences at degree \(r\), while flattening them into a certificate in the original axiom can create large cofactors. The previously recorded compression gap is an explicit control against identifying those two measures. The source itself also gives a non-multilinear IPS upper bound in Theorem 8; the present application concerns the separate ordinary-PC degree ledger.

Remaining task

On near-half-family, next inspect the binomial/code-based degree criterion for non-star-local constraints. Its exact field, distance parameter and Boolean encoding costs must be compared with the moving cutoff; a lower bound in code coordinates is insufficient without the variable conversion. The graph-star upper bounds and the respectful/immunity certificate ceilings remain applicable only in their recorded scopes. No new family is claimed here.

Sub-ideas

  • Read the binomial/code-based PC criterion and test whether its certified degree can approach half the actual Boolean variable count outside the graph-star setting, with polynomial encoding length and paid local degrees.

Verification gate. The sole isolated medium reviewer passed the proof and source application; its follow-up checked the supplied primary passages and requested the sampling-size qualification now included. The finite-field restriction is retained to match the source and existing lemma. Reread the full Frobenius companion record, its sharp compression-gap control and the historical reuse and degree-preserving domain-reduction proofs; inspected metadata and reviewed dependency links. Searches covered knapsack, subfields, Frobenius, coefficient conjugation, inverse degree and the historical index. This directly applies an existing rule, with no new encoding algorithm or numerical claim; no old finite suite was rerun. See the source audit and archived review evidence.

The first-entry append placed the article before the Research record heading; this new entry's placement is repaired, and a focused framework regression fix will follow this checkpoint. Restoration and setup reading preceded instrumentation.

8 October 2026 — Random intermediate sums bound modular PC degree beyond graphs

Working PC upper bound; non-star-local modular family test. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, develop the queued binomial/code criterion check. Can replacing graph incidence by a general sparse matrix avoid the graph-star upper bounds and supply the required near-half Boolean PC barrier? The test below distinguishes inconsistency over the entire modular field from inconsistency only after restricting variables to Boolean values.

New mathematics. A two-stage path through a uniformly random row combination keeps every intermediate support near \((1-1/q)v\). Combining it with the recorded balanced-character argument gives PC degree at most \((1/2-1/(2q))v+O(w\sqrt{v\log v}+w)\) for Boolean encodings of linear systems already inconsistent over \(\mathbb F_q\), with row width \(w\), prime \(q\), and proof-field characteristic different from \(q\). No graph locality or occurrence bound is needed.

Goal progress. Unchanged. This eliminates a larger candidate class at fixed odd modulus and bounded width, but supplies no near-half hard family or Frege transfer. Modular systems that have field solutions but no Boolean solution remain outside this theorem.

General statement. thm:random-modular-degree-ceiling is an all-parameter ordinary-PC upper bound. It adapts the Gaussian-width viewpoint of Ben-Sasson–Impagliazzo, 2010, Section 5 and the recorded balanced modular proof. It is not an assertion of independent novelty for Gaussian-width methods.

Source comparison and exact setting

Ben-Sasson–Impagliazzo's journal Theorem 5.1 bounds Gaussian width for bounded-width mod-2 systems; Corollary 5.4 gives a quarter-variable PC upper bound in odd proof characteristic. Its proof orders a minimally inconsistent family at random. The earlier Buss–Grigoriev–Impagliazzo–Pitassi Theorem 6 instead provides the general Laurent-relation lower-bound interface. Neither checked statement directly supplies the binary-characteristic odd-modulus window needed here. We use a random intermediate row combination to treat arbitrary prime modulus, and write out the Boolean PC transfer. The source proof’s displayed small-subsystem and sampling bookkeeping is not used: in general the subsystem has at most \(v+1\) rows, and sampling a family of \(m\) rows to have mean size \(j\) would require probability \(j/m\).

Let \(q\) be prime, \(F\) a field with \(\operatorname{char}F\ne q\), and \(a_i\in\mathbb F_q^v\), \(b_i\in\mathbb F_q\). The modular equations \(a_i\cdot x=b_i\) are assumed inconsistent over \(\mathbb F_q^v\) itself. Each row has support at most \(w\), where \(w\ge1\). The polynomial input over \(F\) consists of all Boolean equations and, for each row, the falsity indicator of each Boolean assignment to its support violating the modular equation. The modular coefficients are evaluated in \(\mathbb F_q\), not reduced into the proof field. This is a literal CNF encoding with at most \(\sum_i2^{|\operatorname{supp}a_i|}\) row clauses.

The degree bound

Theorem (random modular ceiling). In the setting above, select an inconsistent subsystem of \(m\le v+1\) rows. Put \[ C=\min\left\{v,\left\lceil (1-1/q)v+w\sqrt{2m\log(4m+2)}\right\rceil\right\}. \] There is an ordinary PC refutation of the Boolean encoding over \(F\) of degree at most \[ \left\lceil C/2\right\rceil+w+1. \] In particular, for fixed \(q,w\) the degree is at most \((1/2-1/(2q))v+O_w(\sqrt{v\log v})\). A contradictory zero row instead gives the constant axiom immediately.

Small subsystem. Choose a basis of the row space of the coefficient matrix. The corresponding equations are consistent since those rows are independent. Since the full system is not, one additional equation is incompatible with them. This yields at most \(v+1\) rows and scalars \(\lambda_i\in\mathbb F_q\) with \[ \sum_{i=1}^m\lambda_i a_i=0,\qquad \sum_{i=1}^m\lambda_i b_i\ne0. \] Refuting this subsystem refutes the original input. No assumption \(m\le v\) is needed.

Random path. Draw independent uniform \(\rho_1,\ldots,\rho_m\in\mathbb F_q\). Beginning at the zero combination, set row coefficients successively to \(\rho_1,\ldots,\rho_m\). Then set them successively to \(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_m\). This is a path of \(2m\) single-row changes from zero to an inconsistency witness. Its intermediate coefficient vectors are \[ (\rho_1,\ldots,\rho_j,0,\ldots,0),\qquad (\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_j,\rho_{j+1},\ldots,\rho_m). \] At a first-stage position, a coordinate of the resulting row sum is either zero or uniform in \(\mathbb F_q\). At a second-stage position it is uniform if some remaining random row has a nonzero coefficient there; otherwise it is zero, because the final row sum is zero. Primality of \(q\) makes every nonzero coefficient invertible. Thus at each of the \(2m+1\) states, the expected support is at most \((1-1/q)v\).

Changing one \(\rho_i\) alters the support size by at most \(w\). Exposing the independent coefficients gives a Doob martingale whose increments have absolute value at most \(w\). The bounded-difference estimate therefore gives \[ \Pr[W-\mathbb EW\ge t]\le \exp[-t^2/(2mw^2)]. \] For clarity, this estimate follows by applying the conditional bound \(\mathbb E e^{\theta Z}\le e^{\theta^2w^2/2}\) to each mean-zero increment \(Z\in[-w,w]\), multiplying these bounds, using Markov's inequality and minimizing over \(\theta=t/(mw^2)\). The conditional bound follows from convexity between the two endpoints and \(\cosh u\le e^{u^2/2}\). With \(t=w\sqrt{2m\log(4m+2)}\), a union bound over the states has failure probability at most one half. Hence some path has support at most \(C\) at every state. The cap by \(v\) is automatic. Reusing rows in this path is allowed in a proof.

Character realization. Adjoin a primitive \(q\)-th root \(\zeta\) in a finite algebraic extension \(E/F\). Define \[ u_j(t)=1+(\zeta^t-1)x_j,\qquad X(a)=\prod_j u_j(a_j). \] The literal identity \[ u_j(s)u_j(t)-u_j(s+t)=(\zeta^s-1)(\zeta^t-1)(x_j^2-x_j) \] gives multiplication and inverse reduction through degree two. For every scalar \(t\in\mathbb F_q\), \(X(ta_i)-\zeta^{tb_i}\) is a scalar linear combination of the row's forbidden Boolean indicators through degree at most \(w\): multilinear interpolation assigns zero coefficient to every allowed tuple. This also covers \(t=0\), whose polynomial is zero.

For a current row sum \(a\) and charge \(b\), retain a derived relation \[ A-\zeta^bB, \] where \(A,B\) are character products on disjoint supports, their exponent difference is \(a\), and each support has size at most \(\lceil|\operatorname{supp}a|/2\rceil\). The zero starting relation is available by subtraction. When the path changes row \(i\) by scalar \(t\), derive \[ (A-\zeta^bB)X(ta_i)+\zeta^bB(X(ta_i)-\zeta^{tb_i}) =AX(ta_i)-\zeta^{b+tb_i}B. \] Multiplication by an affine factor is simulated by variable multiplication and a scalar combination; multiplication by a product uses those steps in sequence. Both displayed summands fit degree \(\lceil C/2\rceil+w\). Repeated factors on a variable reduce by the literal identity above without increasing the current degree.

When both sides have a factor on the same variable, multiply the entire relation by the inverse of the second-side factor, and reduce. This removes that second-side factor and keeps on the first side only the exponent difference, possibly zero. Each operation increases temporary degree by at most one and neither reduced support increases. After removing common supports, their union is the support of the new row sum. If one side has at least two more factors, multiply by the inverse of a factor on that larger side and reduce; this transfers that factor, inverted, to the smaller side. The larger support does not increase. These operations balance the new relation while keeping all temporary degrees at most \(\lceil C/2\rceil+w+1\). Every Boolean correction is a multiple of the explicit degree-two identity by the other factors at the same ceiling. Thus these are PC inferences, not divisions or unbounded semantic consequences.

At the endpoint both character supports are empty and the charge is nonzero, giving \(1-\zeta^{\sum_i\lambda_i b_i}\ne0\). Scale to obtain one. Finally choose an \(F\)-basis of \(E\) containing one and take coefficient components of every proof line. Extension-scalar operations become \(F\)-linear combinations of components, and variable multiplication commutes with taking components. Since the original indicator and Boolean axioms lie in \(F[x]\), this yields the claimed refutation over \(F\) at the same degree. This descent does not assert a useful expanded proof-size bound. \(\square\)

What the theorem rules out

For the notebook's field \(F=\mathbb F_2\) and fixed approximation depth \(\ell\ge2\), take any fixed odd prime \(q\). At bounded row width the fixed gap \(v/(2q)\) eventually dominates both the error term and every permitted deficit \(s=o(v/(\log v)^{\ell-2})\). Thus these encodings cannot supply the near-half barrier, even when a variable occurs in three or more constraints and the graph-star model does not apply. For \(q=3\) the leading upper coefficient is one third. The previous graph bound gives \(v/4+o(v)\) on its fixed-degree graph families, stronger there; no stronger bound for that class is claimed.

The hypothesis of full-field inconsistency is essential to this proof. For example \(x=2\pmod3\) has no Boolean solution but has a field solution, so there is no linear inconsistency witness. This example is itself easy, and is only a scope control. More generally a field-consistent affine coset disjoint from \(\{0,1\}^v\) is not settled by the random path. Such systems are the remaining code-based formulation to examine. Nor does the theorem rule out growing moduli without comparing the shrinking gap with the target deficit and all other costs.

Exact controls and their limits

The fixed non-star-local control has three Boolean variables and rows \((1,1,0),(0,1,1),(1,0,1),(1,1,1)\) over \(\mathbb F_3\), with charges \((0,0,0,1)\). Every variable occurs in three rows; the all-one row multiplier is an inconsistency witness. The entire random-coefficient space has \(3^4=81\) elements and each path has nine states and eight steps. The concentration upper bound is numerically vacuous at this size: the checks target the exact expectation and PC identities, not evidence for the asymptotic upper coefficient. No larger sample is needed to test these identities.

The GAP script verifies all 81 three-coordinate interpolation identities, all 81 paths, 729 states, 648 balanced steps and 1,084 Boolean reductions over \(\mathbb F_4\). Complete output retains every path's supports and peak degree. The summed supports agree exactly with the predicted values at every position. Interpolation and the raw update are literal polynomial checks. The 1,084 reductions are Boolean-semantic checks on all eight points, not explicit PC certificates; recorded peaks cover the implemented multiplications, not every Boolean correction or field-descent line. Those degree guarantees follow from the written proof. A negative control checks the absence of a linear inconsistency witness for \(x=2\pmod3\). The first run stopped at GAP's comparison of a constant polynomial with a field element; the corrected zero-difference comparison passed. The final run additionally checks interpolation on each row’s actual support and removes that run’s harmless parse warning. No floating-point rank or lower-bound extrapolation is involved.

Follow-up. Continuing the queued code-criterion check. The full-field-inconsistent case is excluded at fixed modulus and width. Next test the field-consistent, Boolean-inconsistent coset setting: determine whether its Boolean restriction admits a proper truncated PC functional at the required cutoff, while retaining bounded-width equations and polynomial encoding. This is the same family-search obligation, not a claim that arbitrary modular systems have the bound above.

Verification gate. The sole isolated medium reviewer passed the theorem, constants, PC degree ledger and unchanged progress assessment. Its corrections distinguish Boolean-semantic tests from literal certificates, avoid the source’s small-subsystem/sampling bookkeeping, and make the approximation-depth range explicit. The balanced modular proof and its exact identities were reread in full, along with its metadata and dependency/correction links. The earlier Frobenius and scalar-descent scope warning remains respected. The source's Gaussian-width theorem, field restriction and proof were checked; the new random-coefficient construction has its own proof. Searches covered Gaussian width, random row sums, modular degree ceilings, code distance, and the historical index. Exact controls above check the new path and its use of the old algebraic rules.

Process assessment. Searching for upper bounds before importing a code lower bound exposed the older Gaussian-width architecture and a useful extension. The first-entry framework bug was fixed in a separate commit; cold-start scheduling reduced the measured full-suite run from 10.9 seconds to 7.6 seconds with 547 passing tests. User-requested context-setting diagnosis and change are recorded as overhead, not mathematics.

8 October 2026 — Five candidate Boolean families

Research proposals only; no near-half PC lower bound obtained. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Picked. The user requested: “Propose 5 boolean formula families.” This cycle supplies the portfolio and the first test of each, rather than asserting their hardness or carrying out five research attempts in one entry.

Question. Which concrete Boolean families remain plausible tests of near-half-family after the subfield-offset and modular-system exclusions? Keep actual variable counts visible and choose each family independently of the proposed proof-size exponent.

New mathematics. No hardness result. Five proposed families and their encoding parameters are set out below. Basic satisfiability and counting observations explain how to select unsatisfiable instances; they do not prove PC hardness, efficient constructibility, or the required Frege transfer. No new proof-system rule is introduced.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The required existence of a near-half Boolean PC family remains unproved. The user-selected portfolio gives candidate tests, not evidence that any passes the degree requirement.

General statement. The target remains an unsatisfiable polynomial-size Boolean family over \(\mathbb F_2\) with \(1\notin C_D\) for \(D=\lfloor v/2-s(v)\rfloor\) and \(s=o(v/(\log v)^{\ell-2})\), at each fixed \(\ell\ge2\). The registered all-parameter thm:random-modular-degree-ceiling screens the full-field-inconsistent modular class. None of the proposals below is a claim of satisfying the target.

1. Sparse ternary cosets without Boolean points

For \(n\) divisible by three, seek a full-row-rank matrix \(A\in\mathbb F_3^{(2n/3)\times n}\) with six nonzero entries per row, four per column, and an expanding incidence structure. Select \(b\notin A(\{0,1\}^n)\). Each row is encoded by the clauses forbidding its violating Boolean assignments. The proposed encoding has exactly \(n\) Boolean variables, width six and at most \(64(2n/3)\) clauses; there are no auxiliary variables.

Conditional on such a matrix, an excluded right-hand side exists since the Boolean image has size at most \(2^n<3^{2n/3}\). Full row rank makes every right-hand side attainable over \(\mathbb F_3\), so the selected system is field-consistent but Boolean-inconsistent. This avoids the hypothesis of the new modular upper theorem. No expanding full-rank matrix family or hard choice of \(b\) has been proved here; rank, expansion and exclusion of \(b\) are selection requirements, not findings. A block-diagonal sparse matrix would make the counting observation true but give short local contradictions, so it is not the intended candidate.

First test. Inspect a general truncated PC functional or reduction operator for the Boolean part of a coset. Test for a low-degree elimination or a small set of variables whose assignment makes the rest easy before trying to prove the target window. This is the first recommended development.

2. Compact three-colourability contradictions

For a non-3-colourable sparse graph on \(N\) vertices, use two bits \((u_v,v_v)\) per vertex with allowed states \(00,01,10\). Include \(\neg u_v\lor\neg v_v\), and, for each edge and each of the three colours, the width-four clause forbidding both endpoints from having that colour. This gives exactly \(2N\) Boolean variables and \(N+3|E|\) clauses, of width at most four. The target old degree is therefore near \(N\), not near \(N/2\).

Random fixed-degree graphs with degree at least six are a proposed source. Conneryd–de Rezende–Nordström–Pang–Risse, Theorem 1.1 and the following encoding paragraph, state linear PC lower bounds over every field for the standard colour-indicator encoding. That statement does not give our near-half coefficient or automatically verify this two-bit encoding. The transfer in both directions and its original degrees must be checked before importing any lower bound. This is the second recommended development.

First test. Audit the compact encoding and determine whether it has a strict degree ceiling below \(N\); then examine whether the source's reduction operator can reach that scale.

3. Non-two-colourable triple hypergraphs

Sample exactly \(3n\) triples independently and uniformly from the three-element subsets of an ambient \(n\)-set, and select non-two-colourable instances; repeated triples are allowed as constraint occurrences. For every triple \(\{i,j,k\}\), include \[ (x_i\lor x_j\lor x_k)\land(\neg x_i\lor\neg x_j\lor\neg x_k). \] There are \(6n\) clause occurrences of width three and no auxiliaries. Remove unused ambient variables, writing \(v\) for the actual count and retaining instances with \(v>n/2\). Then the formula has at most \(12v\) clause occurrences; its target is measured in \(v\), not \(n\). Each local predicate is not-all-equal; it is not a fixed modular equation, so the random modular ceiling does not apply on that description.

Select unsatisfiable members rather than assume every hypergraph works. The elementary first-moment motivation is that a fixed colouring makes at most \(3/4+O(1/n)\) of the distinct triples nonmonochromatic. For \(3n\) independently sampled triples, a union bound over \(2^n\) colourings tends to zero since \(2(3/4)^3<1\). This only locates unsatisfiable instances, not hard ones.

First test. Audit the characteristic-two quadratic representation of not-all-equal and test whether global overlap enables a decisive low-degree refutation. The representation itself needs its ordinary-PC degree accounting before use.

4. Majority local-generator contradictions

For \(n\ge19\), choose \(n+1\) independent uniform neighbourhoods \(I_j\subseteq[n]\) of size 19 and form the map \[ G(x)_j=\operatorname{MAJ}_{19}(x|_{I_j}). \] Select \(b\notin G(\{0,1\}^n)\) and require every local output to equal \(b_j\). Excluded outputs exist because the target has \(2^{n+1}\) points and the domain has \(2^n\). The direct forbidden-assignment encoding has width 19 and \((n+1)2^{18}\) clause occurrences. Remove unused variables and retain instances with actual count \(v>n/2\); then the size is at most \((2v+1)2^{18}\), a large constant times \(v\). The family is selected once per \(n\), not according to a proof-size exponent.

Applebaum–Lovett, Theorem 1.4 and its preceding rational-degree discussion, give a relevant PC/algebraic-attack perspective on local functions and majority. Their statement concerns its specified stretch and attack regime; no near-half PC coefficient for this minimal-stretch proposal is imported. In particular, local algebraic immunity is not a global lower bound, and the inherited immunity-certificate ceiling remains a warning.

First test. Test whether overlapping majority constraints create low-degree consequences or a small refutation despite the locally strong predicate. The known immunity criterion alone cannot establish our target.

5. Random three-CNFs

Sample \(6n\) clauses independently, each using three distinct uniformly chosen ambient variables and three independent uniform signs, and select unsatisfiable members. Remove unused variables and retain actual count \(v>n/2\). There are \(6n<12v\) clause occurrences, width three and no auxiliary variables. A fixed assignment satisfies a random clause with probability \(7/8\), so the probability that the formula has any satisfying assignment is at most \([2(7/8)^6]^n\), which tends to zero. This is an existence screen, not a degree argument.

First test. Seek a stronger structural PC lower-bound mechanism or an actual upper bound below half the variable count. Repeating the recorded respectful or immunity criterion would miss the target regardless of its constant improvements; those certificate ceilings are not upper bounds on actual degree.

Coverage screen for the random proposals. The occurrence condition above is compatible with the unsatisfiability screens. If at most \(n/2\) ambient variables occur, all neighbourhoods lie in some set of size \(\lfloor n/2\rfloor\). For neighbourhood size \(d\), one draw lies in such a fixed set with probability at most \(2^{-d}\), since the ratio of the relevant binomial coefficients is at most that value. A union bound over at most \(2^n\) sets gives failure probability at most \(2^n2^{-dm}\) for \(m\) independent neighbourhoods. This tends to zero for all three proposed parameter choices. For hypergraphs and three-CNFs, combine it with the unsatisfiability first-moment bound by a union bound. For the generator, choose any outside output after choosing a covering neighbourhood family. Thus the random proposals can retain \(v>n/2\) without charging absent variables to the goal; no claim of complete coverage or conditioned-distribution hardness is made.

Attempt before the portfolio request

Before the user requested five proposals, the coset investigation considered fixing nonpivot coordinates, then using a non-Boolean forced pivot value to obtain a full-field-inconsistent branch. The ordinary-PC lifting cost and resulting rank tradeoff were not completed or reviewed in this cycle. No upper bound from this sketch is asserted; this is retained as the first candidate’s concrete next test.

Choice and remaining uncertainty

Develop the sparse ternary coset proposal first, retaining compact three-colourability as the leading alternative. This is a qualitative priority based on their fit to the open encoding and degree gap, not a numerical probability of success. All five need hardness analysis and the separate Frege transfer. The matrix and excluded-output selection rules are not efficient uniform constructions yet; no such claim is made.

Sub-ideas

  • Develop the sparse ternary coset proposal: ensure a nondegenerate full-rank sparse family and test its actual Boolean PC cutoff, including small conditioning sets.
  • Audit the two-bit three-colour encoding in both proof directions and test whether degree near the number of vertices survives its local domain.
  • Audit the quadratic representation of triple not-all-equal constraints and test the resulting sparse hypergraph family for low-degree PC refutations.
  • Test majority local-generator contradictions at n+1 outputs for overlap-driven low-degree refutations; do not substitute local immunity for global hardness.
  • Test random three-CNFs beyond the recorded certificate criteria, seeking the required coefficient or a decisive actual upper bound.

Follow-up. Continuing the code-criterion item, with the five user-requested candidate tests retained as nested sub-ideas. No candidate is declared hard or closed by its first formulation.

Verification gate. The sole isolated medium reviewer passed the proposal semantics, counts, hypotheses and unchanged goal assessment. 8 October 2026 — Character transport excludes the proposed ternary cosets

Working PC upper bound; sparse-coset degree test. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. Test the first proposed family, a sparse field-consistent ternary system of rank \(2v/3\) with no Boolean point. Does its small number of free coordinates permit a PC refutation below the moving near-half cutoff once every original degree is paid?

New mathematics. Balanced character relations can be transported to prescribed cofactor positions at a maximum degree cost, without adding the cofactor degree to the earlier proof. Combining this with a random row-combination path and finite Fourier interpolation gives the coset ceiling below. At fixed modulus three, rank \(2v/3\) and bounded width, it is \(7v/18+o(v)\), excluding the proposed family.

Goal progress. Unchanged. One proposed family is ruled out, but the required existence of a near-half Boolean PC family and the Frege transfer remain unproved. This upper proof does not settle lower-rank cosets or compact three-colourability.

General statement. lem:character-binomial-transport gives the degree-preserving placement mechanism, and thm:coset-character-degree-ceiling composes it for every system in the stated regime. The proof refines the random modular path and its balanced-character antecedent; it is not a claim of a new lower-bound method.

Character binomials can change position cheaply

Let \(q\ge3\) be prime and let a field \(E\), of characteristic different from \(q\), contain a primitive \(q\)-th root \(\zeta\). Work in ordinary Boolean variables \(x_1,\ldots,x_v\) and write \[ u_i(a)=1+(\zeta^a-1)x_i,\qquad X(a)=\prod_{i:a_i\ne0}u_i(a_i),\quad a\in\mathbb F_q^v. \] Every character product is multilinear, of degree its support size. Retain all Boolean equations. The literal identity \[ u_i(a)u_i(t)-u_i(a+t)=(\zeta^a-1)(\zeta^t-1)(x_i^2-x_i) \] will pay for every character reduction.

Lemma (character binomial transport). Suppose \(X(a)-\gamma X(b)\), with \(\gamma\in E^\times\), has a PC derivation through degree \(B\), and \(a,b\) have disjoint supports differing in size by at most one. Let \(p-h=a-b\), and put \[ M=\max\{|\operatorname{supp}p|,|\operatorname{supp}h|, \lceil|\operatorname{supp}(a-b)|/2\rceil\}. \] Then \(X(p)-\gamma X(h)\) has a PC derivation through \(\max\{B,M+2\}\).

Proof. On coordinates common to \(p,h\), subtract \(h_i\) from both exponents, obtaining \(p',h'\) with disjoint supports and the same difference. Their respective support sizes are no larger than those of \(p,h\). For any coordinate of nonzero difference, a disjoint representation puts that difference on the first side or its negative on the second. Multiplying the relation by the inverse of its present factor and reducing transfers the coordinate between sides, preserving \(\gamma\) and the exponent difference.

Pair the transfers needed in opposite directions, performing the two moves of each pair consecutively. After each pair the side sizes return to their starting values; between its two moves a side can exceed them by at most one. Perform any remaining moves, all in the same direction, afterward; their side sizes lie between the starting and target sizes. Thus reduced side sizes never exceed \(M+1\), and every temporary multiplication and its explicit Boolean correction has degree at most \(M+2\). This derives \(X(p')-\gamma X(h')\). Reattach the previously removed common factors one at a time. Reduced supports grow only to the target supports; each temporary multiplication costs at most \(M+1\). The displayed identity, multiplied by the other factors, is a Boolean certificate at exactly those ceilings. Reuse the original derivation's final line; no earlier proof line is multiplied by an accumulating cofactor. This proves the lemma. \(\square\)

The nonzero phase and prime-modulus restrictions are retained as shared application conventions; the transport argument does not require their full strength. The theorem’s rank positivity follows from its consistency and Boolean-inconsistency assumptions.

The coset degree ceiling

Let \(q\ge3\) be prime, \(F\) any field with \(\operatorname{char}F\ne q\), and \(Ax=b\) a system over \(\mathbb F_q\) on \(v\) coordinates, of rank \(r\ge1\) and row support at most \(w\ge1\). Assume it has a field solution but no Boolean solution. Over \(F\), encode each row by all its forbidden Boolean-assignment indicators, and include Booleanity. Put \(k=v-r\) and \[ C=\min\left\{v,\left\lceil (1-1/q)v+(k+1)/q +w\sqrt{2r\log(4r+2)}\right\rceil\right\}. \]

Theorem (coset character ceiling). This Boolean encoding has an ordinary PC refutation over \(F\) through degree \[ B=\max\{\lceil C/2\rceil+w+1,\ k+3\}. \] For fixed \(q,w\), this is at most \[ \max\{v-r,\ v/2-r/(2q)\}+O_w(\sqrt{r\log(r+1)}+1). \] No polynomial proof-size bound is asserted.

Parametrize the actual field coset. Select \(r\) independent original rows, still of width at most \(w\). Full-field consistency makes every other equation their affine consequence, so this subsystem has the same solutions. Choose \(r\) pivot columns and let \(T\) be the \(k\) free coordinates, denoted \(y\). The field solutions are precisely \[ x_j=c_j+\ell_j\cdot y \quad(j\notin T),\qquad x_j=y_j \quad(j\in T), \] where \(c_j\in\mathbb F_q\), \(\ell_j\in\mathbb F_q^T\); use \(c_j=0,\ell_j=e_j\) for free coordinates. In particular, for each pivot and each \(s\in\{1,2\}\), the row-space vector \[ d=s(e_j-\ell_j) \] has support at most \(k+1\), and is a combination \(\lambda\) of the selected rows whose corresponding charge is \(sc_j\). Here \(\ell_j\) is embedded in the full coordinate space on \(T\).

A random path to a small nonzero target. Choose independent uniform \(\rho_i\in\mathbb F_q\), first set the row coefficients from zero to \(\rho\) one by one, then from \(\rho\) to \(\lambda\) one by one. At a first-stage state, each coordinate of the row sum is zero or uniform. At a second-stage state, it is uniform when an unprocessed random row touches it; otherwise it equals its final value \(d_i\). Consequently each state's expected support is at most \[ (1-1/q)v+|\operatorname{supp}d|/q\le (1-1/q)v+(k+1)/q. \] Changing one random coefficient affects at most \(w\) support coordinates. The same bounded-difference proof as in the random modular theorem gives tail \(\exp[-t^2/(2rw^2)]\). At \(t=w\sqrt{2r\log(4r+2)}\), a union bound over \(2r+1\) states has failure probability at most one half. Thus a path exists with all supports at most \(C\). A separate path may be used for each pivot and each \(s\); no common random choice or short proof inventory is needed.

Realize the path in ordinary PC. Adjoin \(\zeta\) in a finite algebraic extension \(E/F\). For every row \((a_i,b_i)\) and scalar \(t\), the polynomial \(X(ta_i)-\zeta^{tb_i}\) is a scalar sum of its forbidden assignment indicators through degree \(w\), by multilinear interpolation. An allowed tuple contributes zero. Maintain a balanced relation \(P-\zeta^bQ\) at each state, with disjoint character supports of sizes at most half its support, rounded up. A row change gives \[ (P-\zeta^bQ)X(ta_i)+\zeta^bQ(X(ta_i)-\zeta^{tb_i}) =PX(ta_i)-\zeta^{b+tb_i}Q. \] Both summands fit degree \(\lceil C/2\rceil+w\). Reduce repeated factors, remove common factors using inverse-character multiplication, and balance the sides by transferring factors. Each move adds at most one temporary degree; the explicit local Boolean identity pays for its reduction. This constructs the endpoint balanced relation through \(\lceil C/2\rceil+w+1\), with difference \(s(e_j-\ell_j)\) and phase \(\zeta^{sc_j}\). This argument uses no global inconsistency witness.

For every free exponent vector \(\beta\in\mathbb F_q^T\), apply character binomial transport to place this relation at \[ X(\beta)u_j(s)-\zeta^{sc_j}X(\beta+s\ell_j). \] The first term has support at most \(k+1\) and the second at most \(k\); the endpoint difference has support at most \(k+1\). Hence all such cofactored relations are derived through \(B\). Crucially, their proofs do not cost the previous ceiling plus \(k\).

Use the Boolean exclusion on the full parameter space

On the full \(q\)-ary parameter grid \(\mathbb F_q^T\), define functions \[ g_j(y)=\zeta^{2(c_j+\ell_j\cdot y)} -(1+\zeta)\zeta^{c_j+\ell_j\cdot y}+\zeta. \] They vanish exactly when \(x_j=c_j+\ell_j\cdot y\) is zero or one: the polynomial in its character value is \((z-1)(z-\zeta)\), with two distinct roots. Thus the \(g_j\) have no common zero on the full parameter grid. This uses the actual field consistency to define a nonempty global coset; no inconsistent-system pseudo-ideal is being treated as a global evaluation algebra.

For a pivot \(j\), combine the two derived cofactored relations with the literal polynomial identity \[ u_j(2)-(1+\zeta)u_j(1)+\zeta=0. \] It gives, through \(B\), the reduced polynomial \[ P_{j,\beta}=\zeta^{2c_j}X(\beta+2\ell_j) -(1+\zeta)\zeta^{c_j}X(\beta+\ell_j)+\zeta X(\beta). \] All exponents in free coordinates are taken modulo \(q\). For a free coordinate \(j\), multiply the same literal identity by \(X(\beta)\) and apply the local character-product correction at that one variable; this derives \(P_{j,\beta}\) through \(k+1\le B\). Its ordinary polynomial can even be zero after the Boolean substitution; that causes no problem.

At every point \(y\), choose one index \(j\) with \(g_j(y)\ne0\). Define \(H_j(y)=1/g_j(y)\) on points assigned to that index, and zero otherwise. Then \(\sum_jH_jg_j=1\) as functions on the full parameter grid. Its characters \(\zeta^{\beta\cdot y}\), \(\beta\in\mathbb F_q^T\), are a basis: explicitly \[ H_j(y)=\sum_\beta h_{j,\beta}\zeta^{\beta\cdot y},\qquad h_{j,\beta}=q^{-k}\sum_z H_j(z)\zeta^{-\beta\cdot z}. \] The inverse formula follows by multiplying the one-coordinate geometric sums; \(q\) is invertible in \(E\). It also covers \(k=0\).

Substituting these expansions into \(\sum_jH_jg_j=1\) and collecting exponents modulo \(q\) yields a coefficient identity in the character basis. Replace each free character by its ordinary multilinear polynomial \(X(\beta)\). This is a linear replacement of a coefficient identity, so \[ \sum_{j,\beta}h_{j,\beta}P_{j,\beta}=1 \] is a literal ordinary-polynomial identity. Every summand \(P_{j,\beta}\) is already a derived line through \(B\). No product \(H_jg_j\) of potentially doubled degree is inserted into the PC proof, and no unbounded certificate is used as a bounded one. Taking this final linear combination yields a refutation over \(E\). Coefficient components in an \(F\)-basis of \(E\) containing one give the refutation over \(F\) at the same degree, since all original forbidden indicators and Boolean axioms are defined over \(F\). This proves the theorem. \(\square\)

Outcome for the first proposal

At fixed \(q=3\), \(r=2v/3\) and \(w=6\), we have \(k=v/3\) and the bound is \(7v/18+o(v)\). Its fixed gap below half rules out the proposed ternary family at every fixed approximation depth \(\ell\ge2\), independently of the chosen excluded syndrome, expansion quality or construction algorithm. This is an actual PC upper bound, not a ceiling on a lower-bound certificate.

More generally, for fixed \(q,w\), every rank \(r\ge(1/2+\epsilon)v\) with fixed \(\epsilon>0\) is excluded from the moving near-half window. Lower ranks are not ruled out by this bound. In particular, modulus five and rank \(v/2\) have enough syndromes for the same missing-image counting argument, while this theorem gives only \(v/2+O(1)\) eventually at bounded width. This is an untested alternative, not a new hard-family claim; its detailed construction and lower-bound test are queued after the existing alternatives.

Follow-up. Closed the first candidate sub-idea in its proposed ternary rank-\(2v/3\) regime: the theorem is decisive for every matrix and excluded syndrome in that regime. The parent family-search item remains open. Next develop the first remaining queued candidate, compact three-colourability.

Sub-ideas

  • Test a modulus-five, rank-half sparse coset variant, for example a full-rank matrix with row degree six and column degree three when available. The missing-image count permits unsatisfiable Boolean syndromes; the present upper bound is only half the variable count plus a constant. Establish a nondegenerate construction and actual lower bound or obstruction before treating this as a hard family.

Exact controls and the superseded attempt

The initial coordinate-branching approach passed a focused review and its 1,458 local checks, but gave a leading \(5v/9\) bound at the chosen rank, too weak for the test. Before checkpointing it, the transport argument above removed the additive branch cost. The earlier evidence is retained; the final claim and next step concern the stronger proof.

The transport checker exhausts exponent pairs and all \(q\)-th-root phases for modulus three on three variables over \(\mathbb F_4\), and modulus five on two variables over \(\mathbb F_{16}\), starting from canonical balanced representatives. Arbitrary nonzero phases and other proof characteristics rely on the symbolic proof. Other balanced representatives are covered by coordinate relabelling and swapping the two sides. Its complete output records 5,312 cases and 11,114 unit moves, with maximum actual degree four. Each move verifies its literal Boolean-generator cofactor, not just pointwise equality, and checks the stated degree ceiling.

A separate control uses \(x_1+x_3=0\), \(x_2+x_3=2\) over \(\mathbb F_3\). Its three field points have no Boolean point. All nine Fourier coefficients and the three-point partition identity are checked, including three omitted-partition negative controls, and the pulled-back polynomial sum is literally one. The nonzero-target path checker separately checks all nine random intermediates for four scaled pivot targets, covering 180 states; its saved sums obey the predicted expectation bound at every position. These are small identity/expectation controls, not numerical evidence for an asymptotic cutoff; the latter follows from the proof. No larger run is needed.

Verification gate. The same isolated medium reviewer passed the strengthened transport and coset theorems, including \(k=0\), all degree peaks, Fourier recombination, scalar descent and unchanged parent-goal assessment. Its corrections qualify the finite phase/field coverage and explain the retained application conventions. The complete random modular and balanced-character proofs were reread, with their metadata and correction links; the historical finite-cover NS argument was checked to avoid importing its different branch cost. Searches covered character/binomial transport, coset and syndrome degree bounds, Gaussian width and the historical index. The exact controls above passed; their GAP parse warnings concern globals assigned before use. The earlier branch-only draft is superseded within this uncommitted cycle.

Process assessment. The weaker branch ledger did not settle the candidate; inspecting how completed character relations can be reused changed the decisive implication for that candidate. The full near-half existence goal remains open, and the remaining portfolio tests are retained.

8 October 2026 — Affine colour indicators exclude compact three-colouring

Working PC upper bound; compact-colouring degree test. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. Can the proposed two-bit three-colourability family require ordinary PC degree near \(N\) on \(v=2N\) Boolean variables? The missing implication for near-half-family is still existence of a family with the specified moving-cutoff barrier. This test ends if a uniform upper bound lies a fixed fraction below \(N\), or if the compact encoding survives and a lower-bound mechanism can be pursued.

New mathematics. A degree audit transfers the standard three-indicator polynomial encoding and the compact CNF in both directions with only a degree-four floor. An independent-set branching argument then refutes every compact non-three-colourability instance through \(2N/3+O(1)=v/3+O(1)\), regardless of graph expansion or maximum degree.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The second proposed family is excluded, but no near-half Boolean PC family or complete Frege transfer is established. The exclusion identifies a concrete weakness to avoid: local states represented by affine indicators and a large set of vertices that can be extended independently.

General statement. lem:compact-colouring-transfer applies to every simple graph and every field; thm:compact-colouring-degree-ceiling applies to every non-three-colourable simple graph. These are original-degree statements, not degree estimates in an uncharged Boolean quotient or finite-case extrapolations.

Encoding and both degree directions

At a vertex \(a\), the compact encoding uses Boolean variables \(u_a,v_a\), the domain axiom \(u_av_a=0\), and three point indicators \[\chi_{a,0}=(1-u_a)(1-v_a),\quad\chi_{a,1}=u_a(1-v_a),\quad\chi_{a,2}=(1-u_a)v_a.\] For every edge \(ab\) and colour \(c\in\{0,1,2\}\), include \(\chi_{a,c}\chi_{b,c}=0\). These are precisely the polynomial translations of the proposed clauses, up to harmless nonzero signs. The CNF has \(2N\) occurring variables (each occurs in its domain clause), \(N+3|E|\) clauses and width at most four.

Write \[L_{a,0}=1-u_a-v_a,\qquad L_{a,1}=u_a,\qquad L_{a,2}=v_a.\] Their sum is literally one, and \(\chi_{a,c}-L_{a,c}=\epsilon_cu_av_a\), where \((\epsilon_0,\epsilon_1,\epsilon_2)=(1,-1,-1)\). Thus \[\chi_{a,c}\chi_{b,c}-L_{a,c}L_{b,c} =\epsilon_cu_av_a\chi_{b,c}+\epsilon_cL_{a,c}u_bv_b.\tag{1}\] Each right-hand term is derivable from a domain axiom through degree at most four. Consequently all quadratic collision lines \(L_{a,c}L_{b,c}=0\) have ordinary PC derivations through degree four. Multiplication by any affine polynomial is implemented by separate variable multiplications and linear combination; it adds at most one degree to the current line. Earlier derivations are reused, not multiplied wholesale.

The standard polynomial system \(\operatorname{Col}(G,3)\) uses variables \(y_{a,c}\), row sums \(\sum_cy_{a,c}-1\), pair exclusions \(y_{a,c}y_{a,d}\) for \(c\ne d\), edge collisions \(y_{a,c}y_{b,c}\), and Booleanity. This is the system in Conneryd–de Rezende–Nordström–Pang–Risse, equations (2.2). Their Theorem 1.1 supplies linear lower bounds in the stated random-graph regimes over every field. Their Boolean reductions are free by convention; ours are charged. We use their encoding definition, not their lower-bound proof as a premise.

Lemma (compact colouring transfer). Over any field, an ordinary degree-\(D\) PC refutation of either of these two systems gives one of the other through degree \(\max\{D,4\}\).

Proof. In the standard-to-compact direction substitute \(y_{a,c}\mapsto L_{a,c}\). Row sums become zero. Put \(B_u=u^2-u\), \(B_v=v^2-v\), and \(Q=uv\) at one vertex. The remaining local images are \[L_0^2-L_0=B_u+B_v+2Q,\quad L_1^2-L_1=B_u,\quad L_2^2-L_2=B_v, \quad L_0L_1=-B_u-Q,\quad L_0L_2=-B_v-Q,\quad L_1L_2=Q.\] They follow through degree two in every characteristic. Edge images follow from (1) through four.

In the compact-to-standard direction substitute \(u_a\mapsto y_{a,1}\), \(v_a\mapsto y_{a,2}\). Booleanity and the compact domain axiom are standard axioms. Put \(R_a=\sum_cy_{a,c}-1\), \(Q_a=y_{a,1}y_{a,2}\), and write \(\widehat\chi\) for the substituted point indicators. Then \[\widehat\chi_{a,0}-y_{a,0}=-R_a+Q_a,\qquad \widehat\chi_{a,1}-y_{a,1}=\widehat\chi_{a,2}-y_{a,2}=-Q_a.\] For each edge, the identity \[\widehat\chi_{a,c}\widehat\chi_{b,c}-y_{a,c}y_{b,c} =(\widehat\chi_{a,c}-y_{a,c})\widehat\chi_{b,c} +y_{a,c}(\widehat\chi_{b,c}-y_{b,c})\] uses only row and pair axioms with products of degree at most four. The substituted edge clause therefore follows through four. In both directions replay every source line. An affine substitution never increases polynomial degree; a nonzero source multiplication has predecessor degree at most \(D-1\), so its affine-variable replay fits \(D\). Zero lines can be discarded. Insert the local axiom derivations once and reuse their final lines. This proves the maximum, without an additive cost per vertex. \(\square\)

This is the same affine replay mechanism already audited in the ternary Fourier transfer and the historical polynomial substitution lemma. The current local domain and clauses are different, and their certificates are given above. No new general substitution principle is claimed. The source's linear lower bound is compatible with the upper bound below; a positive unspecified linear coefficient is not our near-half target.

An independent set makes the remaining branching cheap

Theorem (compact colouring ceiling). Let \(G\) be a non-three-colourable simple graph on \(N\) vertices. Let \(H\) be any non-three-colourable subgraph with an independent set \(I\), and put \(S=V(H)\setminus I\). Its compact CNF has an ordinary PC refutation over every field through \[\max\{4,|S|+1\}.\tag{2}\] In particular, choose an inclusion-minimal such \(H\) on \(h\ge4\) vertices. Then the original formula has a refutation through \[h-\left\lceil\frac{h-1}{3}\right\rceil+1 \ \le\ \frac{2N}{3}+\frac43 =\frac v3+\frac43.\tag{3}\] The displayed integer bound is at least four for \(h\ge4\).

Proof. For every map \(\alpha:S\to\{0,1,2\}\), define the branch polynomial \[P_\alpha=\prod_{a\in S}L_{a,\alpha(a)}.\] It has degree at most \(|S|\), and their sum is literally \(\prod_{a\in S}\sum_c L_{a,c}=1\). We derive every \(P_\alpha=0\).

If \(\alpha\) gives the same colour \(c\) to two adjacent vertices \(a,b\in S\), multiply the already derived collision line \(L_{a,c}L_{b,c}\) by the branch factors at all other vertices of \(S\). This derives \(P_\alpha\) through degree \(|S|\), after the degree-four setup.

Otherwise \(\alpha\) is a proper colouring of \(H[S]\). Some vertex \(t\in I\) must have neighbours of all three colours in \(S\): if every vertex of \(I\) had an available colour, choosing one independently at each would extend \(\alpha\) to \(H\), since \(I\) is independent. This would contradict the hypothesis. For each \(c\), choose a neighbour \(a_c\in S\) with \(\alpha(a_c)=c\). The three neighbours are distinct. Multiply \(L_{t,c}L_{a_c,c}\) by all branch factors except the one at \(a_c\), obtaining \(L_{t,c}P_\alpha\) through degree at most \(|S|+1\). Sum the three lines. Their sum is exactly \(P_\alpha\), without Boolean reduction. Sum all branch lines to obtain one. This proves (2); it makes no polynomial proof-size assertion.

For the uniform bound, minimality makes \(H-z\) three-colourable for any \(z\in V(H)\). A largest colour class in such a colouring is independent in \(H\) and has at least \(\lceil(h-1)/3\rceil\) vertices. Use it as \(I\). Formula axioms of \(H\) are a subset of those of \(G\), so the same derivation refutes the original formula without assigning or charging any outside variables. Since \(h\le N\), (3) follows. \(\square\)

The proof is ordinary PC throughout. It does not use Booleanity after the local collision setup (which itself uses only the domain clauses), and the subgraph in (2) need not be induced; Booleanity is retained as part of the declared CNF encoding and is used in the two-way transfer. Its low ceiling uses the derived affine collision lines and their final-line reuse; multiplying their full degree-four derivations by all branch factors would give an unnecessary additive overhead. The affine partition idea has a prior analogue in whole-column conditioning; the independent-set extension and critical-subgraph argument here supply the graph-specific bound.

What the exclusion teaches

The user requested: “When you find an upper bound that rules out a candidate family, use this for learning: how it might apply to other cabdidates, whether they can be strengthened, or perhaps another stronger candidate could be proposed to replace the ruled out one.” Increasing expansion, density, or graph degree cannot rescue this three-colour encoding: (3) assumes none of their upper bounds. The structural issue is that three allowed states on two bits have affine indicators, while a critical graph supplies a large independent set. Both features are needed for this proof.

For the other queued candidates, Boolean states also have affine indicators, but the independent-extension argument does not transfer automatically. In a triple not-all-equal hypergraph, a colour class may contain two vertices of a triple while containing no full triple; assigning the third leaves a joint constraint on those two, so “independent” in that weak hypergraph sense does not permit separate extensions. A set meeting every constraint in at most one unassigned variable would permit a related conditioning test, but no sufficiently large such set has been proved for the proposed random family. The same missing structural guarantee applies to the majority generator and random three-CNF candidates. Their queued tests should include this possibility; no exclusion of those families follows here.

A proposed replacement is compact four-colourability: use all four states of two bits, omit the domain clause, and forbid equal endpoint states for each edge. On a graph without isolated vertices this has \(2N\) actual variables and \(4|E|\) width-four clauses. Seek sparse vertex-critical non-four-colourable graphs, using the critical core itself as the instance so that \(h=N\); this is a candidate selection requirement, not a hardness theorem. The four point indicators cannot all be affine: already the indicator of \(11\) is zero at \(00,10,01\) and one at \(11\), whereas every affine function satisfies \(f(11)=f(10)+f(01)-f(00)\). Thus the degree-one state-branching shortcut is unavailable over any field. The direct degree-two indicator version of (2) costs at most \(2(|S|+1)\), and the critical-subgraph argument only guarantees an independent set of size \(\lceil(h-1)/4\rceil\). The resulting bound is \(2(h-\lceil(h-1)/4\rceil+1)\), which is above the target \(N\) when \(h=N\), so this particular exclusion does not decide the replacement in that normalization. If the ambient graph has a much smaller critical core, this bound can instead fall below \(N\); merely requiring a growing core would not prevent that failure. Other low-degree representations or upper bounds may still rule it out; that is the queued test.

Checks and remaining obligation

The smallest nonvacuous simple-graph control is \(K_4\), with eight Boolean variables, a three-vertex cover and 27 colour branches. The tested prediction is a literal degree-four certificate for each branch and the complete partition, not a near-half asymptotic lower bound. A single run over \(\mathbb Q,\mathbb F_2,\mathbb F_3\) checks 264 local identities in both encoding directions and all 81 branches: 63 internal-collision cases and 18 exhausted-colour cases. Twenty-one negative controls omit a necessary colour or a partition branch and correctly fail the claimed identity. All checks pass, with maximum degree four; GAP reports only parse-time unbound-global warnings. The script and full results preserve the exact arithmetic and case counts. The all-parameter proof, not these cases, establishes (3); larger graph runs would not answer the missing family-existence implication.

Follow-up. Closed: the compact three-colouring test is answered by thm:compact-colouring-degree-ceiling, which rules out this candidate at every fixed approximation depth. The parent portfolio remains active; develop triple not-all-equal next. The four-colour replacement goes behind the already queued alternatives.

Sub-ideas

  • Test compact four-colourability on two bits per vertex, retaining all four states and selecting sparse vertex-critical non-four-colourable graphs with the core itself as the instance. Audit alternative low-degree local representations and actual PC upper bounds before attempting a near-half lower bound; the affine three-colour exclusion alone does not decide this replacement.

Verification gate. The sole isolated medium reviewer passed the two main arguments and unchanged goal assessment; its correction qualifies the core-to-ambient comparison, and the revision chooses the critical core itself as the four-colour instance, and its unused-hypothesis audit removes inducedness from (2) and identifies the ceiling proof’s unused Boolean axioms. The same reviewer passed the corrected comparison and non-induced scope; its final wording correction distinguishes the reviewer’s qualification from our choice of replacement. Exact local encoding and exhaustive smallest-instance branch certificates passed. The source's Theorem 1.1, degree convention and equations (2.2) were read. The historical substitution and column-conditioning proofs and the current ternary Fourier transfer record were reread; their settings differ and the present proof is explicit. Searches covered colour indicators, independent sets, vertex covers, branching and encoding substitution, including the historical index and relevant reviewed relationship metadata. No novelty is asserted for the general affine replay or partition mechanism.

Process assessment. A structural upper bound answers the candidate test without a large search, and the user's learning rule now turns that exclusion into a precisely motivated replacement rather than an unsupported list of harder-looking graphs. The initial critical-subgraph planning overlapped the opening reading phase before its mathematics marker; no time was backfilled.
