8 October 2026 — Smaller cores exclude typical random triple samples

Working PC upper bounds; triple not-all-equal degree test. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. Does the third proposed family, \(3n\) random not-all-equal triples, retain a near-half PC barrier after actual-variable normalization? On near-half-family, test the quadratic local encoding and the independent-set lesson from compact colouring. An actual upper bound below the moving cutoff would reject the tested sampling regime; a bound at half alone would leave critical-core variants open.

New mathematics. The local characteristic-two quadratic and the two-clause encoding transfer through a degree-three floor. For every non-two-colourable triple hypergraph, conditioning outside an independent set leaves unsatisfiable 2-CNFs, giving degree at most \(h-\alpha(H)+3\). A vertex-minimal core gives \(\lceil h/2\rceil+3\). The proposed random density typically supplies a smaller contradictory core, and hence degree at most \(95v/196+4\), where \(v\) is the actual number of occurring variables.

Goal progress. Unchanged. Typical samples at the proposed density fail the target, but this is not a universal exclusion of every exceptionally selected non-two-colourable triple hypergraph. The critical-core half-degree bound itself does not rule out the near-half goal, and no matching lower bound or Frege transfer is obtained.

General statement. lem:triple-quadratic-transfer covers every characteristic-two field. thm:triple-colouring-degree-ceiling covers all non-two-colourable triple hypergraphs over every proof field. cor:random-triple-degree-ceiling specifies the original random law and a probability tending to one, rather than silently upgrading a typical-case bound to an all-instance claim.

The quadratic encoding pays only a local constant

On a triple of distinct Boolean variables \(x,y,z\), the two CNF clauses have falsifying polynomials \[c_0=(1-x)(1-y)(1-z),\qquad c_1=xyz.\] In characteristic two put \[Q=1+x+y+z+xy+xz+yz.\] Lemma (triple quadratic transfer). Replacing every pair \(c_0,c_1\) by its \(Q\), retaining all Boolean equations, transfers ordinary PC refutations in both directions through \(\max\{D,3\}\), for arbitrary collections of triples over every characteristic-two field.

Proof. Literally \(Q=c_0+c_1\), so the quadratic follows through degree three. In the other direction \[xQ-c_1=(x^2-x)(1+y+z)\] in characteristic two, which derives \(c_1\) through three; then \(c_0=Q-c_1\). Insert these local derivations and reuse their final lines in the unchanged proof. Costs do not accumulate over triples. The factorization \[Q=(1+x+y)(1+x+z)+(x^2-x)\] also shows why this is a product of two affine factors modulo Booleanity, not a single fixed modular equation. It does not meet the full-field-inconsistent linear-system hypothesis of the modular upper theorem. \(\square\)

Independent sets leave a width-two problem

Use the standard CNF encoding with two clauses per triple, no auxiliary variables, and ordinary Boolean equations. A hypergraph independent set here means a vertex set containing no complete edge; it may contain two vertices of an edge. Repeated edges may be discarded.

Theorem (triple colouring ceiling). Let \(H\) be a non-two-colourable 3-uniform hypergraph on \(h\) vertices, and let \(I\) contain no edge. Set \(S=V(H)\setminus I\). Over every field the CNF has an ordinary PC refutation through \[|S|+3.\tag{1}\] Thus its degree is at most \(h-\alpha(H)+3\), where \(\alpha(H)\) is the largest independent-set size. If \(H\) is a vertex-minimal induced non-two-colourable core of any larger hypergraph, its formula, and hence the larger formula, has a refutation through \[\left\lceil\frac h2\right\rceil+3.\tag{2}\]

Proof of the width-two ingredient. Every unsatisfiable 2-CNF has a resolution refutation using clauses of width at most two. For completeness, eliminate a variable \(x\), keeping clauses not containing it and adding every non-tautological resolvent of a clause \(x\lor A\) with a clause \(\neg x\lor B\). Here \(A,B\) have at most one literal. Any assignment satisfying the resulting clauses extends to the eliminated variable: if the parents demanded both choices of \(x\), their corresponding resolvent would be false. Thus unsatisfiability is preserved, and eliminating all variables eventually produces the empty clause. Duplicate literals are collapsed and tautological clauses discarded.

This has ordinary PC degree at most three over every field. Write \(a,b\) for the falsifying polynomials of \(A,B\), taking one for an empty disjunction. From \((1-x)a\) and \(xb\), derive \[b(1-x)a+a(xb)=ab\] through degree at most three. For equal residual literals replace their square by the literal using \(y^2-y\); for opposite literals the resolvent is tautological and is discarded. All corrections have degree at most two. Linear factors such as \(1-y\) are implemented by variable multiplication and subtraction. This supplies the claimed ordinary-PC simulation, without a free multilinearization convention.

Proof of (1). Fix any Boolean assignment \(\sigma:S\to\{0,1\}\) and put \[P_\sigma=\prod_{i\in S:\sigma_i=1}x_i\prod_{i\in S:\sigma_i=0}(1-x_i).\] Every triple meets \(S\), so after this restriction every remaining clause has width at most two. The restricted formula is unsatisfiable, since an assignment satisfying it would extend \(\sigma\) to a proper two-colouring of \(H\). Use the preceding width-two refutation and multiply its lines by \(P_\sigma\).

The weighted axioms have literal low-degree derivations from the original formula. If a clause survives the restriction, all its assigned literals are false, so their falsifying factors already equal the corresponding factors of \(P_\sigma\). Multiply the original clause polynomial only by the branch factors on assigned variables absent from that clause. The result is exactly \(P_\sigma\) times the restricted clause, without repeated factors. Its degree is at most \(|S|+2\); a satisfied clause contributes no axiom. Weighted Boolean axioms for unassigned variables follow through \(|S|+2\). Replay the width-two derivation: each variable multiplication and each duplicate-literal Boolean correction, multiplied by the same weight, fits \(|S|+3\). The last line is \(P_\sigma\). Finally, \[\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}^S}P_\sigma=\prod_{i\in S}(x_i+(1-x_i))=1\] literally, giving (1). This is a degree bound and permits exponentially many branches.

For (2), delete a vertex \(z\) from a vertex-minimal induced core. The remaining hypergraph has a proper two-colouring. Its larger colour class has at least \(\lceil(h-1)/2\rceil\) vertices and is independent also in \(H\): none of its triples contains \(z\). Taking that class as \(I\) gives \(|S|\le h-\lceil(h-1)/2\rceil=\lceil h/2\rceil\). The core's axioms are a subset of the original formula's axioms, so no outside variables are charged. \(\square\)

The structural difference from the graph-colouring proof matters: unassigned vertices here need not extend independently. They may interact in binary clauses, but that residual class is still refutable in constant degree. This replaces an unnecessarily strong independence-of-extension demand by the sufficient requirement of a uniformly easy residual proof system. The older literal clause-lifting controls already verified the same missing-factor identity; the complete general argument is supplied above and the new Fano run checks its composition.

The proposed random density usually leaves a smaller core

Corollary (random triple ceiling). Sample \(3n\) triples independently and uniformly from the three-element subsets of an ambient \(n\)-set, as in the original proposal, and let \(v\) be the actual number of occurring variables. With probability tending to one, the sample is non-two-colourable and its CNF has, over every field, an ordinary PC refutation of degree at most \[\frac{95}{196}v+4.\tag{3}\] This also holds with probability tending to one after conditioning on unsatisfiability and on the proposal's occurrence screen \(v>n/2\).

Proof. Let \(U\) be a fixed set of \(u=\lfloor19n/20\rfloor\) ambient vertices. The number \(M\) of sampled triples contained in \(U\) is binomial with mean \[3n\frac{\binom u3}{\binom n3},\qquad \frac{\mathbb E M}{u}\longrightarrow3(19/20)^2=1083/400>5/2.\] Its variance is \(O(n)\), so Chebyshev's inequality gives \(M\ge\lceil5u/2\rceil\) with probability tending to one. Conditional on the retained count (or positions), the retained triples are independent uniform triples of \(U\).

A fixed two-colouring of \(U\), with colour classes of sizes \(a,u-a\), makes a random triple nonmonochromatic with probability \[\frac{3a(u-a)}{u(u-1)}\le\frac{3u}{4(u-1)}.\] For all sufficiently large \(u\) the latter is below one. With at least \(\lceil5u/2\rceil\) independent triples, the expected number of proper two-colourings is at most \[2^u\left(\frac{3u}{4(u-1)}\right)^{5u/2} =O(1)\left(2(3/4)^{5/2}\right)^u=o(1),\] because the square of the base is \(4(3/4)^5=243/256<1\). Markov's inequality therefore gives a non-two-colourable induced subhypergraph on \(U\) with probability tending to one. It contains a vertex-minimal core of size \(h\le u\), to which (2) applies.

To normalize actual variables, set \(j=\lceil n/50\rceil\). If at least \(j\) variables are absent, some specified \(j\)-set is missed by every sampled triple. Hence \[\Pr[n-v\ge j]\le\binom nj \left(\frac{\binom{n-j}3}{\binom n3}\right)^{3n} \le(en/j)^j e^{-9j}\le(50e^{-8})^j=o(1).\] The base is below one, for example since \(e>2\) and \(2^8>50\). Outside this event, \(v\ge n-j+1>49n/50\). Combining the two high-probability events gives \[\operatorname{PCdeg}\le\lceil h/2\rceil+3 \le\frac{19n}{40}+\frac72 <\frac{95}{196}v+4.\] Both unsatisfiability and \(v>n/2\) already hold on an event of probability tending to one, so conditioning on their conjunction preserves the conclusion. \(\square\)

Learning from this upper bound

The local quadratic representation alone does not make all triple not-all-equal systems easy. Its global half-core ceiling is compatible with a near-half lower bound on a carefully chosen core. What defeats the proposed random density is the combination of that ceiling with a contradictory induced subformula on a strict fraction of the actual variables. A typical-sample exclusion must not be described as ruling out every instance obtainable by an exceptional selection rule.

A stronger variant is to use a growing vertex-minimal non-two-colourable triple hypergraph itself as the formula, deduplicating edges. It has at most \(2\binom v3\) clauses, so polynomial size is automatic, and no outside variables inflate the target. This is a proposal, not an existence proof with the required hardness. More precisely, a barrier at \(D=\lfloor v/2-s\rfloor\) can survive (1)–(2) only if every contradictory core has at least \(v-2s-O(1)\) vertices and \(\alpha(H)\le v/2+s+O(1)\). Criticality addresses the first condition; it does not prove the second, and neither condition proves hardness. This defines the replacement's next meaningful test.

The same core normalization warning applies to compact four-colouring, already queued with its core as the instance. For the majority generator, forcing \(n+1\) prescribed outputs need not prevent a smaller contradictory subsystem; for random three-CNFs, an overly dense sample can likewise contain smaller unsatisfiable subformulas. Those candidates have no half-core theorem established here, so core existence alone does not exclude them. Their tests must combine any such structural reduction with a paid degree bound, rather than importing (2) across predicates.

Verification and next review

The Fano triple system is a nonvacuous composition control: seven vertices, fourteen CNF clauses, an independent four-set and predicted degree six, strictly below its variable count. The script first checks all 128 Boolean assignments for unsatisfiability, then all eight assignments on the complementary line. A single exact run over \(\mathbb Q,\mathbb F_2,\mathbb F_3,\mathbb F_4\) checks 100 local width-two resolution patterns, all 32 weighted Fano branch refutations, 200 lifted axioms and 216 weighted resolution steps, each within degree six. Eight negative controls omit a needed parent or branch and reject the resulting identity. The six characteristic-two transfer identities pass over \(\mathbb F_2,\mathbb F_4\); rational checks verify the displayed density margins. The script and complete output retain every branch and inference. No random experiment or finite PC degree lower bound is claimed; the probability result is proved analytically.

Follow-up. Closed: the proposed density-three sampling test and its quadratic encoding audit are answered by the registered transfer, general upper bound and high-probability corollary. This closes the specified sampling test, not the entire not-all-equal language or exceptional selection rules. The strengthened critical-core variant remains as a new sub-idea, and the parent portfolio continues with the majority candidate after the due route review.

Sub-ideas

  • Test growing vertex-minimal non-two-colourable triple hypergraphs, using the core itself as the instance. Seek the necessary independence bound alpha(H) at most v/2 plus the allowed sublinear slack, or prove a stronger actual PC ceiling; criticality and the two necessary conditions alone are not hardness evidence.

Verification gate. The sole isolated medium reviewer passed all three arguments, their degree and actual-variable ledgers, the necessary-condition qualification and unchanged goal assessment. Its unused-hypothesis audit notes that the occurrence screen is not needed for the unconditional probability bound; it is retained only to match the original proposal’s conditional sampling description. Exact encoding, resolution, lifting and Fano controls passed. Searches covered not-all-equal quadratics, hypergraph independent sets and criticality, width-two PC/resolution and restriction lifting; the historical substitution and current conditioning mechanisms were compared without importing an ENS theorem into CNF refutation. The primary Mikša–Nordström manuscript notes width-two resolution completeness, but the required completeness and ordinary-PC simulation are proved here. No novelty is asserted for that standard ingredient. No general upper-bound theorem from the unrelated search results is used.

Process assessment. The last exclusion's structural lesson led to an easy residual class, then to a core-normalization test with a general proof instead of a large sample search. The scheduled architecture review must address six unchanged family-existence cycles; opening planning briefly overlapped reading before the mathematics marker.

8 October 2026 — From candidate screening to a multiplication-compatible invariant

Reviewed route assessment; finite translation controls. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. After six research entries with unchanged family-existence progress, what mechanism could prove a near-half ordinary-PC lower bound rather than merely identify another family not yet excluded? This is the scheduled ordinary route review; no previous review is ten research entries back.

New mathematics. No new all-parameter lower bound. Exact state-space controls distinguish multiplication-compatible vector evaluations from a normalized scalar moment; two further controls falsify direct quantum-trace and positive-chemical-equilibrium translations. These are the scoped finite tests ex:near-half-state-controls, with full identities below.

Goal progress. Unchanged. Neither open statement is discharged. The exclusions are useful negative information and motivate stronger candidates, but their accumulation is not evidence that a near-half family exists. This review changes the next test to an explicit ordinary-PC invariant with a stated relation-preservation obligation.

General statement. The sufficient endpoint remains the old Boolean barrier in thm:moving-cutoff-source-exclusion, with its separate product-only source grammar and original-degree ledger. The all-instance and typical-instance upper bounds, especially thm:coset-character-degree-ceiling, thm:compact-colouring-degree-ceiling and thm:triple-colouring-degree-ceiling, delimit the next construction. None of the finite review controls proves the required endpoint.

What the six entries actually established

The subfield audit separates static inverse hardness from dynamic PC; iterated Frobenius refutes the tested offset systems through twice the input degree. The random modular path excludes bounded-width fixed-modulus systems inconsistent over the full modular field. The user-requested portfolio supplies proposals, not a lower bound. The coset theorem then excludes the ternary rank-two-thirds proposal and motivates the still-open modulus-five rank-half variant.

The colouring theorem excludes all proposed two-bit three-colour instances; affine indicators and critical-core structure, not low graph degree, cause the loss. Four-colouring with the core itself as the instance avoids that specific affine shortcut. The triple theorem gives a half-core ceiling and rejects typical samples from the proposed density-three law after actual-variable normalization. It leaves exceptional selections and core-normalized variants open. Majority local generators and random three-CNFs have not yet been developed. No review follow-up is presented as changed merely because it was mentioned again.

Obstacle

The line is stuck on constructing a nonzero invariant that survives all degree-bounded PC inferences after cancellations near half the actual variable count. Ordinary local satisfiability, static inverse degree, a surviving scalar moment, and an uncharged constant-factor reduction do not establish that implication. The current candidate screens have not produced the invariant for any family. The line remains mathematically possible, but the evidence supports rejecting several regimes, not describing the unexcluded regimes as likely hard.

Architecture review

Necessary target. For each fixed relevant approximation depth \(\ell\ge2\), one polynomial-size unsatisfiable Boolean CNF family over \(\mathbb F_2\), with \(v\to\infty\) actual variables and independent of the proof-size exponent, must satisfy \(1\notin C_D\) for \(D=\lfloor v/2-s_\ell(v)\rfloor\), \(s_\ell=o(v/(\log v)^{\ell-2})\). Constant width pays the presently available fixed-input-degree source theorem. Growing width is allowed only after redoing that theorem's complete budget. For the Frege goal, the same family must also admit the recorded coherent grammar/encoding interpretation. The threshold may depend on the proof-size exponent; the family may not.

Extra requirements. Efficiently selecting the hard instance, one family working for every depth, a square-root deficit, and a large space of surviving states are stronger than this existence endpoint and are not imposed. Nor do we require a globally consistent function algebra, a globally flat moment matrix, or multiplication to act on the kernel of a single scalar functional. It suffices to exhibit a vector-valued linear map \(E\) on Boolean multilinear polynomials through \(D\), with \(E(1)\ne0\), all active clause axioms mapped to zero, and \[E(\operatorname{ml}(x_i p))=T_iE(p)\qquad(\deg p\le D-1).\tag{1}\] Here \(T_i\) are linear maps defined on the needed image subspaces and may be extended linearly elsewhere; only the displayed filtered identities are required. Induction on ordinary PC lines proves the lower bound: multilinearization kills Boolean axioms, linear combinations preserve zero, and every nonzero multiplication predecessor has degree at most \(D-1\). This is the familiar pseudo-reduction/closed-kernel criterion, not a new lower-bound theorem. Defining \(E\) as the unknown consequence quotient would merely restate the goal.

Independent alternative. Before seeing the preferred portfolio and next candidate, the sole medium reviewer received only the goal and bounded available-result statements. It proposed vector-valued spaces of local solutions with condition (1), and explicitly rejected this as an established existence route without a gluing theorem. Its suggested critical triple family overlaps our newly queued replacement; the genuinely useful comparison is a proof-mechanism requirement rather than a new family name. The preferred route revealed afterward was to finish the portfolio screens, with majority next. We retain that next object but require an explicit map and its cancellation test, instead of another screen alone. The independent brief response does not claim a materially different proved architecture was found.

Rank normalization. Any dimension comparison must take place in the same filtered image \(E(V_{\le d})\) and the same coefficient field. The goal only needs \(E(1)\ne0\). A scalar augmentation of a larger state space can destroy relation preservation even while remaining normalized; the control below demonstrates this. Ambient monomial counts, local solution counts and actual PC quotient ranks are not interchangeable. Relative contraction may help the later source transfer, but it cannot replace the missing old-PC invariant.

Decision. Replace the screen-only tactic by an explicit filtered local-state construction and an early test of (1). Keep the known upper bounds as compulsory falsification controls and the variable/degree ledger as a gate. The majority candidate is the next existing queue item; the stronger coset, four-colour and critical-triple alternatives remain queued. A failure of a proposed state construction calls for changing its relations or representation, not merely increasing the sample size or citing another unspecified linear lower bound.

Decisive test. In the next majority development, write a concrete local-state map for the actual predicate and its first overlapping constraints, then check every relation in its relevant low-degree kernel after multiplication by every variable. Derive the general relation-transport condition in that construction. A failing relation rejects that map and identifies information it lost; a pass licenses the next all-parameter closure proof, not a near-half claim. At the same time, test whether the chosen output constraints reduce to a substantially smaller contradictory core or easy residual class. Report the outcome under Architecture test, linking this review. Do not substitute the universal PC quotient for the missing construction.

Outside leads

  • Local sections and cohomology. Abramsky–Mansfield–Barbosa, Proposition 4.2 characterizes vanishing of its obstruction by compatible linear combinations of local sections anchored at one actual section; Proposition 4.3 gives its necessary implication for extendability. This suggests retaining local state modules when trying to glue a map for near-half-pc-family. The source's anchored abelian-group statement is not silently imported as an \(\mathbb F_2\) PC theorem. Our test instead uses two overlapping not-all-equal triples, on variables \(123\) and \(345\), and their actual compatible states. There are 18 such states. Evaluation of all 16 squarefree monomials of degree at most two has binary rank 14; both kernel-basis relations remain zero after multiplication by each of the five variables, giving ten nonvacuous checks through degree three. Collapsing to a scalar local moment fails, as shown below. This passes the local vector representation and overlap calibration only; all 18 states are genuine satisfying assignments, so it says nothing about survival on a globally unsatisfiable family. Answers. A filtered gluing theorem preserving these vector relations and the empty state up to the required cutoff would supply the missing ordinary-PC invariant. Test. Passed: exact vector overlap and relation-transport calibration; the scalar simplification was falsified. Develop the global filtered gluing condition, not larger satisfiable examples.
  • Border bases and flat extension. Laurent–Mourrain, Theorem 1.6 identifies when a connected-to-one border prebasis over a field gives a quotient basis: its multiplication matrices commute. Theorem 1.5's printed flat-moment statement is over the reals; no finite-field positivity theorem is presumed. This offers a precise comparison with (1), but imposing a full commuting Boolean representation of all clauses is too strong. Indeed, for commuting idempotents \(T_i\), the joint projectors \(P_a=\prod_{a_i=1}T_i\prod_{a_i=0}(I-T_i)\) sum to \(I\). If every clause polynomial vanishes at the operators and the Boolean formula is unsatisfiable, then for every assignment \(a\) some falsifying clause has \(q(a)=1\), whence \(q(T)P_a=P_a=0\). Thus \(I=0\), contradicting a nonzero state. This is the usual Boolean-completeness argument, not a new obstruction theorem. Partial, degree-bounded operators remain possible. Answers. A filtered version that transports only the relations permitted by (1), without extending to a global solution algebra, could supply the needed invariant; a global flat extension cannot. Test. Falsified: the proposed full-representation convenience collapses for every unsatisfiable Boolean input. The weaker filtered condition is retained in the selected local-state development, not declared impossible.
  • Algebraic CSP interpretations. Atserias–Ochremiak, Theorem 6 preserves PC degree up to a linear factor over the same field under its stated pp-interpretations, homomorphic equivalence and core constructions. It is a possible way to import hardness, but constants cannot be discarded here. If a source has lower coefficient \(c\), the target uses \(b\) times as many variables, and a target degree-\(D\) proof replays in source degree at most \(tD+O(1)\), the transferred coefficient is only \(c/(bt)\). In the concrete control, the source is the standard polynomial three-colour encoding with three Boolean indicators \(y_{a,0},y_{a,1},y_{a,2}\) at each vertex, hence \(3N\) variables; the target is the compact CNF with \(u_a,v_a\), domain \(u_av_a=0\), and three forbidden equal-colour clauses per edge, hence \(2N\) variables. The compact colouring transfer proves both directions through \(\max\{D,4\}\): \(y_0\mapsto1-u-v,y_1\mapsto u,y_2\mapsto v\) and \(u\mapsto y_1,v\mapsto y_2\), with explicit degree-two domain and degree-four edge certificates in that record. Thus \(b=2/3,t=1\). Transferring the compact upper bound \(2N/3+O(1)\) back to the \(3N\)-variable source gives source coefficient at most \(2/9\); the exact compression therefore leaves at most \(1/3\) in target variables. Answers. A specific interpretation with a proved source bound and an exact ledger reaching \(1/2-o(1)\) would answer the encoding part of near-half-pc-family; the general linear-factor theorem alone does not. Test. Falsified: uncharged constant-factor preservation as a near-half argument. The exact ledger passes its rational control, but no qualifying interpretation is supplied or queued as a result.

Absurd bridges

  • Quantum error correction. The Knill–Laflamme criterion, Theorem 3.2, characterizes correctable errors by scalar diagonal and zero off-diagonal matrix elements of products of errors on a complex code space. The proposed translation would use a protected state space to preserve low-degree polynomial relations while leaving a nonzero vector. Its coefficient field and commutativity requirements must be rebuilt, not borrowed from complex Hilbert space. The smallest useful stress test keeps a nontrivial phase in characteristic two: over \(\mathbb F_4\), let \(\omega^2+\omega+1=0\), \(U=\operatorname{diag}(1,\omega,\omega^2)\), and let \(V\) cyclically shift the three basis vectors. Then \(UV=\omega VU\). Direct matrix substitution already fails commutativity; taking traces does not repair it, since \(\operatorname{Tr}(UVU^2V^2)=\omega\) while \(\operatorname{Tr}(U^3V^3)=1\). Each matrix is an affine linear combination of its three spectral idempotents, so this discrepancy cannot be hidden by merely naming those idempotents Boolean variables. Answers. A code-space construction obeying every required commuting-polynomial identity through the cutoff could yield (1); error-correction alone does not give it. Test. Falsified: the direct matrix and trace translations, by an exact degree-six reordered-word control. This does not rule out every filtered quantum-inspired construction, and no such replacement is asserted.
  • Chemical reaction networks. Feinberg's deficiency-zero theorem, Theorem 5.1, supplies a positive equilibrium in each positive stoichiometric compatibility class for weakly reversible deficiency-zero mass-action networks with positive real rates. One might try to use compatible local equilibria as nonvanishing state functionals for near-half-pc-family. The one-species reversible network \(A\rightleftarrows2A\) has deficiency zero and rates \(1,\alpha\), where \(\alpha>0\) satisfies \(\alpha^2+\alpha-1=0\); its positive equilibrium is \(1/\alpha\). Reduce the coefficient ring modulo two by \(\alpha\mapsto\omega\in\mathbb F_4\). Boolean activity means \(x=1\), and the proposed equations are \(x-\omega x^2=0\), \(x^2-x=0\), \(x-1=0\). They have the degree-two certificate \[(x-\omega x^2)+\omega(x^2-x)-(1-\omega)(x-1)=1-\omega\ne0.\] Both rates remain nonzero after this reduction; it is the positive-real equilibrium interpretation that fails to provide a Boolean state. Answers. A finite-field, Boolean-compatible replacement for the equilibrium invariant could address the nonvanishing requirement, but the positive-real theorem does not cross that boundary. Test. Falsified: direct equilibrium-to-Boolean-state transfer, by the exact one-species certificate. Any future chemical construction would need a different invariant and its full variable/degree accounting.

Exact scalar control and evidence

Over \(\mathbb F_2\), let \(\Lambda(f)=f(1,0,0)+f(0,1,0)+f(0,0,1)\). All three points satisfy one not-all-equal constraint. We have \(\Lambda(1)=1\), \(\Lambda(1+x)=0\), but \(\Lambda(y(1+x))=1\). Thus its scalar kernel is not closed under multiplication even at this small local piece. This refutes the proposed scalar-kernel shortcut; it does not refute the existence or usefulness of scalar PC-annihilating functionals, nor claim that this satisfiable example has a refutation. Keeping the vector of evaluations retains the information that scalar summation discards.

The single fixed GAP script and complete output verify the 18-state overlap, its rank and all ten shifted relations, scalar-kernel failure, the Weyl matrices, reordered traces, all 18 within-context spectral-projector products and both affine reconstructions, the chemical certificate and the exact encoding ratio. Linear algebra uses GAP's exact finite-field routines. These are decisive tests of particular translations, not probability estimates or asymptotic evidence. The global Boolean-representation test is the all-parameter algebraic argument above; no numerical rank surrogate is used for it.

Follow-up. Continuing near-half-family under the unchanged two open statements. The existing portfolio queue remains; the sole passed Outside lead is appended for its own development. Both failed bridges remain documented in their precise formulations, without claiming their entire subject areas irrelevant. Their one-step failures are not a license to declare the family-existence statement without a mechanism or to abandon it.

Sub-ideas

  • For the local-section lead, formulate a filtered vector gluing construction with explicit kernel relations and verify multiplication compatibility after cancellation; avoid a globally flat solution algebra and distinguish it from the unknown universal PC quotient. The next majority cycle's architecture test is the already queued candidate task, not evidence that this general gluing problem is solved.

The sole medium reviewer completed the independent architecture stage, then passed the consolidated comparison, filtered-PC criterion, scoped controls and unchanged goal assessment. 8 October 2026 — Separate majority ideals miss a reusable cross-constraint consequence

Working method obstruction; majority local-state architecture test. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. For the majority local-generator candidate, can the sum of complete separately truncated local vanishing ideals be the kernel of the review’s proposed vector-valued invariant? This is a concrete construction, not the unknown global consequence quotient. Test its multiplication compatibility after cancellation on the actual majority predicate before attempting an asymptotic lower bound.

New mathematics. The local sum fails for two opposite odd-majority constraints sharing just one variable, over every field. A degree drop creates a consequence that PC can reuse without raising its old ceiling, while the separately truncated local ideals miss a spectator multiple. The construction applies to majority-19 through original degree 19, and can be placed at arbitrarily large near-half cutoffs. Joining the two constraints into a single local member repairs this particular gap.

Goal progress. Unchanged. This rejects the additive invariant, not the proposed unsatisfiable majority family. The calibration system is satisfiable, and additional constraints in a full instance can change its local-sum kernel. No near-half lower bound, universal upper bound for majority instances, or Frege transfer is obtained.

General statement. prop:majority-local-span-gap covers all odd arities, coefficient fields and cutoffs in its statement. Its local minimum-degree input is the already recorded all-field Hamming-ball certifier argument; the recent majority-fiber rank calculation uses the same triangular interpolation. This is a scoped application of that mechanism and final-line reuse, not a claim to a new general theory of gluing.

The explicit candidate kernel

Fix a field \(F\) and \(k\ge1\). Let \(A,B\) be disjoint sets of \(2k\) Boolean variables, with one additional shared variable \(z\). Require \[\operatorname{MAJ}_{2k+1}(z,A)=1,\qquad \operatorname{MAJ}_{2k+1}(z,B)=0.\tag{1}\] Use the original full forbidden-assignment CNF for each constraint, of width \(2k+1\), and include Booleanity. Additional spectator variables are free. Write \(\mathcal B\) for the Boolean function algebra, identified with multilinear representatives, with degree filtration \(\mathcal B_{\le d}\). Let \(I_+,I_-\) be the vanishing ideals of the two local allowed sets, extended to all variables, and define \[K_d=(I_+\cap\mathcal B_{\le d})+(I_-\cap\mathcal B_{\le d}).\tag{2}\] The proposed map is the explicit quotient \(E_d:\mathcal B_{\le d}\to\mathcal B_{\le d}/K_d\). It kills both local axiom sets when they fit the cutoff. It also preserves the unit: \(z=0\), all variables in \(A\) equal to one, and all variables in \(B\) equal to zero is a joint satisfying assignment, for any spectator values.

Local normal form and costs. In complemented coordinates on \(z,A\), the allowed majority-one set is the Hamming ball of weights at most \(k\). Its vanishing ideal in the Boolean algebra is spanned by squarefree monomials using at least \(k+1\) of those local coordinates, multiplied by arbitrary outside monomials. Indeed, these monomials vanish, whereas the remaining monomials have an invertible inclusion-evaluation matrix on the ball: order subsets by inclusion to obtain a triangular matrix with diagonal one. The same statement in the original coordinates holds for the majority-zero set on \(z,B\). In particular, neither local ideal contains a nonzero local polynomial of degree at most \(k\), over any field. Affine coordinate complementation preserves each total-degree filtration.

These are complete local ideals, not just spans of input clauses. Their degree-\(d\) parts have ordinary local-PC derivations through \(\max\{d,2k+1\}\). To derive a degree-\(k+1\) forbidden monotone clause, sum all full forbidden-assignment indicators extending its fixed falsifying pattern. Their sum is the clause times the product of the remaining literal partitions, hence exactly the clause, through degree \(2k+1\). Multiply its final line by the additional distinct literal and outside factors of any normal-form monomial; this reaches that monomial through its actual degree, without multiplying earlier proof lines. Linear combination gives every element of the local truncated ideal. This pays the original truth-table width even though the smallest local consequences have degree \(k+1\).

A uniform failure after one multiplication

Proposition (majority local span gap). Let \(D\ge2k+1\) and suppose the ambient Boolean system above has at least \(D-2k\) spectator variables, equivalently at least \(D+2k+1\) variables in total. Then \(K_D\) contains both local axiom sets and consists of ordinary degree-\(D\) PC consequences, and \(1\notin K_D\). Nevertheless there are \(q\in K_D\), \(\deg q=D-1\), and a spectator variable \(w\) with \[wq\notin K_D,\qquad wq\in C_D.\tag{3}\] Thus the quotient in (2) does not support the required multiplication maps. The statement includes \(k=9\), the proposed majority-19 predicate. For arbitrarily large even ambient count \(v\), it applies at \(D=v/2\) once \(D\ge19\); it is a failure of a uniform invariant construction, not a hard-family claim based on padding.

Proof. Choose \(A_0\subseteq A\), \(B_0\subseteq B\), each of size \(k\), and put \[a=\prod_{i\in A_0}(1-x_i),\qquad b=\prod_{j\in B_0}x_j, \qquad p=ab.\] The two local forbidden monotone clauses \[g=(1-z)a\in I_+,\qquad h=zb\in I_-\] have degree \(k+1\). Their literal relation is \[bg+ah=ab=p.\tag{4}\] Both products have degree \(2k+1\), while \(p\) has degree \(2k\). By the local derivations above and final-line reuse, (4) is an ordinary PC derivation through the original width \(2k+1\).

We first show \(p\notin K_{2k}\). Complement all private coordinates in \(A\), writing \(y_i=1-x_i\), while retaining \(z\), \(B\) and spectators in their original coordinates. Then \(p=y_{A_0}x_{B_0}\). On a polynomial in \(I_+\cap\mathcal B_{\le2k}\), take the coefficient of the exact outside monomial \(x_{B_0}\), with every other outside exponent zero. Because outside variables are unrestricted, this coefficient is a polynomial in the local ideal on \(z,A\). Its degree is at most \(k\), so it is zero by the local minimum-degree fact. In particular the coefficient of \(y_{A_0}x_{B_0}\) is zero. On a polynomial in \(I_-\cap\mathcal B_{\le2k}\), instead extract the exact outside monomial \(y_{A_0}\); its local coefficient on \(z,B\) likewise has degree at most \(k\) and is zero. The mixed coefficient functional therefore annihilates all of \(K_{2k}\), but takes value one on \(p\). Coefficient extraction preserves membership in a local vanishing ideal because evaluations on all free outside Boolean points give a unique multilinear expansion.

Let \(W\) be the product of \(D-2k-1\) distinct spectators, using one when this number is zero, and let \(w\) be another spectator. Put \(q=Wp\). Multiplying (4) by \(W\) expresses \(q\) as the sum of a member of \(I_+\cap\mathcal B_{\le D}\) and a member of \(I_-\cap\mathcal B_{\le D}\), so \(q\in K_D\), with degree \(D-1\). It also has an ordinary PC derivation through \(D\): derive \(p\) first, then multiply its final line by the spectators. One more variable multiplication derives \(wq\) in degree \(D\).

If \(wq\in K_D\), extracting the coefficient of the exact spectator monomial \(wW\), of degree \(D-2k\), from its two local summands would put \(p\) in \(K_{2k}\), a contradiction. The local normal-form argument already proves \(K_D\subseteq C_D\) for \(D\ge2k+1\); all polynomials here are their multilinear representatives. The joint model proves \(1\notin K_D\). This establishes every assertion. \(\square\)

For majority-19 with \(D=19\), there are 37 variables in the two local neighbourhoods and one spectator. The extracted clauses have degree ten, \(p\) degree 18, and \(wp\) degree 19. The missing member is a genuine degree-19 PC consequence even though the proposed quotient sends it to a nonzero vector. The construction uses the actual predicate and original width; no smaller-arity surrogate is used to infer this case.

What repairs this gap, and what remains unproved

Using a better basis for each single-constraint ideal cannot fix (3): (2) already contains their complete degree-bounded parts. Replace the two separate members by the full joint ideal \(J=I_++I_-\) in the Boolean algebra. This is the vanishing ideal of their common allowed set: Boolean point idempotents show that the sum of two vanishing ideals vanishes exactly on the intersection. Consequently \(J\cap\mathcal B_{\le D}\) is closed under every multiplication that remains within the cutoff, contains \(p\) and its spectator multiples, and does not contain one because the joint set is nonempty. The repair is valid for this satisfiable pair; it is not a construction for an unsatisfiable global family, whose full joint ideal would be the unit ideal.

The claim search found an important antecedent not supplied by the preceding review’s independent brief. The older cover-relative gluing and sequential cover-generation records already isolate the transport of top-degree relations across overlapping members. Their later review warns that pairwise checks need not give global generation. The closed-member correction also requires checking that the actual base equations vanish on each local allowed set. Here the base is Booleanity alone, so our actual local states meet it; no weak-PHP row condition is imported. Those records concern a different field and base and do not prove the present global majority construction. This entry identifies the prior mechanism rather than presenting local gluing as a new architecture.

The needed repair for the full candidate is therefore a bounded, explicitly chosen cover of joint constraint sets, together with a proof that its filtered relations are transported under multiplication while one survives. Adding every global consequence would restate the target; joining all constraints would kill one on an unsatisfiable instance. A pair repair does not decide how large the necessary members become or how their interactions fit a cutoff near half the actual variable count.

Architecture test and the candidate boundary

Architecture test. The scheduled test falsifies the explicit additive kernel (2) and verifies the pair repair, with a general proof covering majority-19 and arbitrary large cutoffs. The missing information is a cross-constraint consequence created by cancellation and reused by PC. This changes the next construction from separate local ideals to controlled joint members; it does not close the majority family.

The previous half-core argument for triple not-all-equal does not automatically extend. Majority-19 restrictions need not become 2-CNF: fixing nine inputs to one and seven to zero leaves the positive three-literal OR on the remaining three inputs when the required majority output is one. Fixing seven to one and nine to zero with output zero leaves the negative three-literal clause. Thus the easy-residual step requires a new structural hypothesis; it cannot be inferred from the predicate’s local satisfiability. This exact residual example uses the actual arity and is an obstruction to that proposed reduction, not a proof of hardness.

The simple density calculation that excluded the original triple sampling law also does not directly supply a core here. A fixed ambient fraction \(\alpha\in(0,1)\) of the variables retains an expected \((n+1)\binom{\lfloor\alpha n\rfloor}{19}/\binom n{19}\sim\alpha^{19}n\) neighbourhoods, or density \(\alpha^{18}<1\) relative to that ambient subset. With independent uniform output labels, conditional on a retained count \(M\), the expected number of satisfying assignments to its \(u\) ambient variables is exactly \(2^{u-M}\), not a vanishing first-moment bound at this density. This does not prove local satisfiability, does not normalize absent variables for an actual degree claim, and does not describe the law of labels selected outside the full generator image. No smaller-core degree exclusion is established by this calculation. The full family remains the original proposal, with unused variables removed and all target costs charged to the actual count.

Exact checks and limits

The smallest complete matrix control has \(k=1,D=3\), six Boolean variables and 42 degree-at-most-three monomials. Over each of \(\mathbb F_2,\mathbb F_3\), the six local degree-two basis rows have rank six; adjoining \(p\) raises it. The 26 local degree-three rows have rank 26; they contain \(p\), but adjoining \(wp\) raises the rank. The pair has 12 joint states, whose degree-three evaluation rank is 12, so its full joint kernel has dimension 30; it absorbs \(wp\) and preserves the unit. This measures the proposed kernel itself, not a Gröbner-degree surrogate.

The majority-19 control checks (4), its original peak 19, the extracted degree-ten clauses and the degree-18/19 witness over \(\mathbb F_2\). It enumerates all 512 extensions on each extracted clause’s nonzero support, checking that all 1,024 violate the corresponding majority output. The all-parameter coefficient proof, not a giant matrix or extrapolation from \(k=1\), establishes nonmembership at \(k=9\). The script and complete final output retain these results. GAP’s only diagnostics are parse-time warnings for globals assigned before use.

Follow-up. Continuing the local-section lead: the naïve additive invariant is refuted and a pair repair is verified, using majority as the concrete calibration. The actual family’s near-half degree remains open. The portfolio remains queued after its completed spell; the user-picked abstract-specification task comes next, with these exact obstructions and the older gluing record available.

The present gap does not itself refute majority formulas, but can motivate either a repaired method or a structural restriction that makes the method work. 8 October 2026 — A non-vacuous specification with degree-preserving core normalization

Working proofs with a published import; abstract specification and non-vacuity. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Picked. The user asks for “a specification of formula which will improve over time” that is “non-vacuous”, and clarifies: “either try another proof method, or perhaps that failure demands a structural change of the spec”. This task takes precedence over the queued local-section development, without closing it.

Question. On near-half-family, establish a structural search class that contains unbounded families with a proved degree barrier, but does not assume the desired near-half barrier in its definition. Determine which normalization is harmless for a hypothetical solution and which stronger requirements still need independent existence proofs.

New mathematics. An elementary application of axiom deletion gives the exact actual-variable and moving-cutoff ledger for minimal unsatisfiable cores. A standard boundary-expansion import, together with the full probability argument below, supplies unbounded clause-minimal three-CNFs with linear ordinary PC degree over every field. These are a non-vacuity witness, not a new near-half lower bound; novelty is not claimed.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The near-half Boolean PC family obligation remains open. The result validates a search envelope and a normalization; it neither proves near-half hardness for its members nor supplies a structural refinement with that conclusion. The complete Frege transfer is also untouched.

General statement. prop:minimal-core-degree-transfer applies to every finite unsatisfiable grouped Boolean constraint system with the canonical CNF encoding. cor:minimal-core-nonvacuity gives an unbounded family and a fixed positive degree fraction, using the imported imp:cnf-boundary-degree. The claims are all-parameter statements, not extrapolations from finite instances.

Accepted structural envelope

Fix constants \(w\ge3\), \(b\ge1\), and \(C>0\), allowed to depend on the fixed approximation depth but not the proof-size exponent. A member is a finite list of constraints \((X_i,R_i)\), where \(X_i\) is a set of at most \(w\) Boolean variables and \(R_i\subseteq\{0,1\}^{X_i}\) is the allowed relation. Encode it by one clause for each forbidden assignment on \(X_i\), and include Booleanity. Let \(V\) be the variables actually occurring in these clauses, \(v=|V|\ge1\); no declared unused coordinate contributes to \(v\). Require at most \(Cv^b\) constraints, unsatisfiability, and block minimality: deleting any whole constraint makes the system satisfiable. Call the resulting class \(\mathcal S(w,b,C)\). The search envelope is the union over these fixed constants, rather than a demand that every parameter choice has members.

Each constraint contributes at most \(2^w\) clauses of width at most \(w\), so its ordinary input degrees are bounded by \(\max\{w,2\}\), its clause count by \(C2^wv^b\), and its explicit encoding length by \(O(C2^wwv^b\log(v+1))\). All auxiliaries introduced by a future encoding must be included in \(V\). A block that is a single clause is allowed, while majority, colour and code relations can remain whole blocks. Block minimality does not assert clause minimality or that every variable is semantically indispensable. Relational padding still counts if it occurs in the canonical clauses.

This is an admissibility specification, with a theorem-backed infinite witness below. It is deliberately not a sufficient hardness condition: easy minimal contradictions can belong too. The next target is an explicitly described non-vacuous refinement, or a construction inside this envelope, with a uniform proof of the required near-half barrier. No conjecture that every member of the present envelope is near-half hard is made. Randomness and boundary expansion will certify one witness; neither becomes a condition imposed on all members or on later abstract arguments.

Minimal cores preserve the relevant degree budget

Proposition (minimal core transfer). Let \(\Gamma\) be an unsatisfiable canonical Boolean CNF on at most \(n\) variables, grouped into whole constraints. Let \(\Gamma'\) consist of an inclusion-minimal unsatisfiable subcollection of these constraints, with exactly \(v\) occurring variables, and discard the other variable names. For any field \(F\), writing \(d_F\) for minimum ordinary PC refutation degree,

\[ d_F(\Gamma)\le d_F(\Gamma')\le v\le n. \tag{1}\]

For positive \(n,v\), this implies \(d_F(\Gamma')/v\ge d_F(\Gamma)/n\). If \(d_F(\Gamma)\ge\eta n\) for fixed \(\eta>0\), then \(v\ge\eta n\). Thus an original \(O(n^b)\) clause or constraint bound becomes \(O(v^b)\), and width does not increase. In particular an \(O(n)\)-size family stays linear in its actual variable count.

Proof. Every proof using the retained axioms is a proof from the full list, giving the first inequality; this is the historical axiom-deletion lemma. Removing unused variables changes no minimum degree. To see the nontrivial direction, substitute zero for those variables throughout a proof. Linear combinations survive; multiplication by a removed variable becomes zero, and its Boolean axiom vanishes. Other multiplication steps and all remaining axioms retain their validity without a degree increase.

For the upper bound in (1), take any assignment \(a\in\{0,1\}^{V}\), choose a clause it falsifies, and write its falsity polynomial \(q_a\) as the product of the corresponding literal indicators. Multiply by the indicator literals for the coordinates outside this clause to obtain

\[ \delta_a(x)=\prod_{a_j=1}x_j\prod_{a_j=0}(1-x_j). \]

Multiplication by \(1-x_j\) is implemented by a variable multiplication followed by subtraction. All factors use distinct coordinates, so the original degree never exceeds \(v\). Sum these derived lines over all assignments. The literal partition identity \(\sum_a\delta_a=\prod_j(x_j+(1-x_j))=1\) holds in the ordinary polynomial ring over every field. This is a degree-\(v\) refutation without using Boolean reduction. For an empty-variable contradiction the empty clause gives degree zero. The ratio and size conclusions follow from (1). \(\square\)

Minimality is needed to put the retained system in the specification, not for the transfer inequalities: the same proof applies to any unsatisfiable whole-block subcollection. This separates a normalization choice from the hypotheses actually used by the degree argument.

Moving cutoff. Suppose a family has no ordinary refutation through \(D_n=\lfloor n/2-s_n\rfloor\), where \(s_n\ge0\) and \(s_n=o(n/(\log n)^{\ell-2})\), for fixed \(\ell\ge2\). Its cores satisfy \(v_n\ge D_n+1\), hence \(v_n\to\infty\) and \(n=O(v_n)\). Put

\[s'_n=\max\{0,s_n-(n-v_n)/2\}.\]

If the second argument of the maximum is nonnegative, then \(v_n/2-s'_n=n/2-s_n\). Otherwise \(v_n/2<n/2-s_n\). In either case

\[ \lfloor v_n/2-s'_n\rfloor\le D_n,\qquad s'_n\le s_n=o(v_n/(\log v_n)^{\ell-2}).\tag{2}\]

A refutation of the core through the left cutoff would refute the original through \(D_n\), a contradiction. Fixing a deterministic ordering and choosing the first minimal core makes the selection independent of the proof-size exponent whenever the original family is. No efficient core-selection algorithm is claimed. Width and polynomial encoding costs are preserved. This proves that block minimality is an admissible normalization for the constant-width part of the target, rather than an extra unproved structural guess. It does not prove the complete source/Frege interface for the resulting family, which still requires its own audit. Core selection can discard a preferred construction law or regularity; lower-bound transfer does not require retaining them.

An unbounded existence witness

Imported boundary theorem. Mikša–Nordström's June 2024 author journal manuscript, Theorem 5.1, gives \(d_F(\Gamma)>\delta s/2\) when every set of at most \(s\) clause vertices has at least \(\delta\) times its size unique variable neighbours. This holds over every field. The source uses multilinear PC in Section 2; multilinearizing an ordinary proof cannot increase degree, so the same lower bound applies here. Its literal convention is related to ours by degree-preserving complementation. This is an imported theorem, not a new proof of the expansion method.

Corollary (minimal core nonvacuity). There exist absolute constants \(\eta>0\) and \(C>0\) and a sequence in \(\mathcal S(3,1,C)\), consisting of single-clause blocks, with actual variable counts \(v\to\infty\) and ordinary PC degree at least \(\eta v\) over every field. One choice is \(\gamma=4000^{-4}\), \(\eta=\gamma/8\), \(C=6/\eta\). These intentionally crude constants certify existence only.

Proof. On an ambient set of \(n\) variables, sample \(m=6n\) clauses independently, each on a uniformly chosen set of three distinct variables and with independent uniform signs. Repeated samples are permitted initially. For a fixed assignment the chance of satisfying all clauses is \((7/8)^{6n}\), so

\[\Pr(\Gamma\text{ is satisfiable})\le2^n(7/8)^{6n}=(117649/131072)^n\longrightarrow0.\tag{3}\]

We also bound the probability that some \(t\) clause positions have at most \(7t/4\) distinct variable neighbours. For \(t=1\) this is impossible. For \(t\ge2\) put \(r=\lfloor7t/4\rfloor\) and \(\theta=r/t\in[3/2,7/4]\). In the range \(t\le\gamma n\), \(r<n\). A union bound over clause positions and an \(r\)-set containing their neighbours gives

\[\begin{aligned} P_t&\le {6n\choose t}{n\choose r}\left({{r\choose3}\over{n\choose3}}\right)^t\\ &\le \left[6e^{1+\theta}\theta^{3-\theta}(t/n)^{2-\theta}\right]^t \le\left[1000(t/n)^{1/4}\right]^t.\tag{4} \end{aligned}\]

Here \({N\choose j}\le(eN/j)^j\) and \({r\choose3}/{n\choose3}\le(r/n)^3\). For the last inequality, \(t/n\le1\), \(2-\theta\ge1/4\), and \(6e^{1+\theta}\theta^{3-\theta}\le6\cdot27(7/4)^3<1000\). No independence among the events in the union bound is assumed.

For \(2\le t\le\sqrt n\), the last base in (4) is at most \(1000n^{-1/8}\), so the sum of these terms tends to zero by a geometric bound starting at exponent two. For \(\sqrt n<t\le\gamma n\), it is at most \(1000\gamma^{1/4}=1/4\), whose geometric tail also tends to zero. Thus, with probability tending to one, every set \(S\) of at most \(s=\lfloor\gamma n\rfloor\) clauses has \(|N(S)|>7|S|/4\).

If \(u\) of these neighbours occur in exactly one clause in \(S\), counting the \(3|S|\) incidences yields \(3|S|\ge u+2(|N(S)|-u)\), hence \(u\ge2|N(S)|-3|S|>|S|/2\). The incidence graph is therefore an \((s,1/2)\)-boundary expander. For sufficiently large \(n\), the intersection of this event with unsatisfiability has positive probability by (3)–(4). The expansion event also excludes duplicate scopes once \(s\ge2\), so treating the successful sample as a CNF set causes no multiplicity issue.

Choose one such formula for every sufficiently large \(n\). The imported theorem gives \(d_F(\Gamma)>s/4\ge\gamma n/8=\eta n\) once \(n\ge2/\gamma\), simultaneously for every field. Take an inclusion-minimal unsatisfiable set of clauses and relabel its \(v\) occurring variables. By (1), \(\eta n\le v\le n\), its degree remains at least \(\eta n\ge\eta v\), and it has at most \(6n\le(6/\eta)v\) clauses of width three. Its encoding length is \(O(v\log(v+1))\). Since \(v\ge\eta n\to\infty\), this proves unbounded nonvacuity, without claiming a member at every prescribed integer \(v\) or efficient construction. \(\square\)

What the specification permits us to change

The current envelope retains the goal's field, literal encoding and actual-variable budget, but does not force a particular geometry, random law, symmetry, or predicate. Its non-vacuity witness already has a genuine linear degree barrier, so it is more informative than a padded constant contradiction. The tiny constant is not an attempt to approach one half by better expansion: the previously proved certificate ceiling prevents that method from supplying the target even with better constants.

The upper-bound ledger remains scoped. Minimality alone does not remove the character-transport or compact-colouring obstructions, and it does not make independent-set branching expensive. Conversely, the typical random-triple upper bound does not exclude all minimal cores as a new sampling law. A proposed structural filter excluding cheap branching or large cancellations must have its own unbounded witness; the witness above is not automatically a witness for that filter.

Decision. Accept block minimality now because (1)–(2) justify it without losing a hypothetical near-half solution. Keep the abstract class broad. Do not yet impose a global commuting solution algebra, which would contradict unsatisfiability, or declare the separate-local-ideal kernel valid, which the majority gap disproves. The next comparison is between enlarging the joint members used by the proof and structural conditions limiting the relations that must be transported between those members. Judge the smallest sufficient version of each by non-vacuity and paid degree costs; neither route has priority merely because it preserves the current method.

Decisive next test. On the queued local-section route, use the existing cover-gluing and chain-generation results to formulate the weakest sufficient degree-filtered relation-transport condition in the present Boolean CNF setting. Determine whether it is a separately checkable structural condition with a non-vacuous witness and a useful cutoff, or only a reformulation of the missing PC closure. If it needs global satisfiability or a member containing an unsatisfiable core, reject that formulation and test a weaker transport requirement. The later chain-generation result already shows, in its different characteristic-three free regime, that sequential quotient freeness can fail while relation generation holds. Its subsequent review leaves generation beyond that regime open; the closed-member correction requires the actual base to vanish on member states. These records were read before choosing this test; no characteristic-three theorem is silently imported over the Boolean target. This test concerns the general implication; another majority matrix size does not answer it.

Verification gate. The sole fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed all claims, the inventory and the unchanged goal assessment. Its one hypothesis clarification is incorporated: minimality is unnecessary for the transfer inequalities and is used only for membership in the specification. Exact dependencies reread: historical axiom deletion; the primary journal theorem and PC convention; the current expansion-certificate ceiling and its reviewed graph; and the full majority obstruction. Claim searches covered minimal cores, axiom deletion, normalized degree, boundary expansion and random CNFs, including the historical index. The probability estimates and ordinary-PC partition proof are analytic; no numerical experiment or new proof-system rule is asserted.

Process assessment. A normalization with an explicit implication to the original target avoids accumulating arbitrary admissibility conditions. The non-vacuity proof uses one primary theorem and elementary estimates instead of another family sweep. Earlier restoration and source-reading windows remain in the measured interval; no unmeasured work is backfilled.

8 October 2026 — A fixed cutoff needs transport of falls, not their absence

Working all-field proofs; filtered local-state method. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, develop the head local-section lead after the abstract-envelope checkpoint. The needed implication is global multiplication closure and survival of one near half the actual variables. Does adapting the older gluing theorem unnecessarily require every lower-degree cancellation to disappear?

New mathematics. An explicit quotient records precisely the extra multiplication tests needed for a chosen cover at one fixed cutoff. A small analytic calibration separates that condition from the stronger absence of all degree falls. A general coefficient-extraction argument shows why free spectator variables can turn any earlier fall into a failure at a later cutoff. This generalizes the mechanism of the majority obstruction; no novelty is claimed for the underlying filtered linear algebra.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The criterion is a proof target for an explicit cover, not a non-vacuous structural hardness specification. No unbounded unsatisfiable witness meeting it near half is proved. The accepted block-minimal envelope remains unchanged; adding the criterion as an admissibility axiom would hide the missing work.

General statement. prop:fixed-cutoff-cover-transport applies to every finite collection of ideals in the Boolean function algebra over any field. prop:free-variable-transport-obstruction applies to every degree fall in such a collection and every permitted larger cutoff, generalizing the earlier spectator lift.

Compare the actual previous results

The relative-gluing proof and its cover version establish injectivity of the homogenizing variable in every degree below the cutoff, from liftable top relations. This is sufficient for PC hardness. The functional-base refinement has a different base and variable ledger and is not imported here. The chain result proves generation without the stronger sequential freeness condition, but only in its stated free regime. The later coupled-cover obstruction also shows that insisting on generation solely by Koszul and individual-member relations can fail while an alternative argument preserves hardness. These are reasons to keep only the endpoint needed.

The closed-member correction remains essential: actual base equations must vanish on the allowed sets. Our base here is Booleanity alone. The first-fall criterion already identifies lifting with removal of degree falls in its setting; its closed-member discussion is read with that correction. The fall-invariance result rules out assuming that splitting a relation automatically repairs its lift. No repair by renaming or uncharged auxiliary variables is used below.

The exact fixed-cutoff test

Let \(B=F[x_1,\ldots,x_v]/(x_j^2-x_j)\), identified with multilinear polynomials and filtered by total degree. Write \(B_{\le d}\) for its degree filtration, with \(B_{\le-1}=0\). Let \(J_1,\ldots,J_m\) be ideals of \(B\); in the intended application each is the full vanishing ideal of the Boolean assignments satisfying a chosen joint member of constraints. Put

\[K_d=\sum_a(J_a\cap B_{\le d}),\qquad H_D=\frac{K_D\cap B_{\le D-1}}{K_{D-1}},\qquad Q_D=B_{\le D}/K_D.\tag{1}\]

All sums and intersections use the same ambient Boolean algebra. \(H_D\) records the lower-degree results of cancellations at the cutoff that cannot be represented by lower-degree pieces of the same members. It is not assumed zero.

Proposition (fixed cutoff transport). For \(D\ge1\), the maps

\[\Delta_j:H_D\longrightarrow Q_D,\qquad [q]\longmapsto[x_jq]\tag{2}\]

are well-defined, using multilinear multiplication. They all vanish if and only if \(K_D\) is closed under multiplication of each of its elements of degree at most \(D-1\) by each variable. If every active input polynomial belongs to \(K_D\), \(1\notin K_D\), and all maps (2) vanish, then the input system has no ordinary degree-\(D\) PC refutation.

Proof. Every element of \(K_{D-1}\) is a sum of \(p_a\in J_a\cap B_{\le D-1}\). Since \(J_a\) is an ideal, \(x_jp_a\in J_a\cap B_{\le D}\). Hence \(x_jK_{D-1}\subseteq K_D\), proving that (2) is independent of the representative. Vanishing is precisely the required closure condition on \(K_D\cap B_{\le D-1}\). An ordinary PC proof can be multilinearized line by line: input Boolean axioms become zero, addition stays addition, and a multiplication step with nonzero predecessor of ordinary degree at most \(D-1\) maps to its Boolean product. The zero predecessor is harmless. Induction puts every line of a degree-\(D\) refutation into \(K_D\), contradicting \(1\notin K_D\). This only uses the direction from ordinary to multilinear proofs; it makes no claim that Boolean reduction has zero original cost in the converse direction. \(\square\)

Lemma (top relation surjection). Let \(G_{a,D}\subseteq B_{\le D}/B_{\le D-1}\) be the degree-\(D\) leading parts of \(J_a\cap B_{\le D}\), and let \(R_D\) be the kernel of the summation map \(\bigoplus_aG_{a,D}\to B_{\le D}/B_{\le D-1}\). Choose linear lifts \(s_a:G_{a,D}\to J_a\cap B_{\le D}\). Then

\[r\longmapsto\left[\sum_a s_a(r_a)\right]\in H_D\tag{3}\]

is well-defined independently of the lifts and is surjective. Indeed, the sum has zero leading part, and changing a lift changes it by an element of \(K_{D-1}\). For any \(q=\sum_a p_a\in K_D\cap B_{\le D-1}\), the leading parts of the \(p_a\) form a relation, and the difference from its chosen lifts lies in \(K_{D-1}\). Thus the test can be performed on these explicit leading relations by composing (3) with (2). It does not require a module-freeness assertion or a prescribed generating list of Koszul relations.

Requiring \(H_d=0\) for every \(1\le d\le D\) is stronger. It implies \(K_D\cap B_{\le d}=K_d\) by repeated descent and hence all the maps (2) vanish. If every \(J_a\) is proper, \(K_0=0\); repeated descent also gives \(1\notin K_D\). This is the familiar no-fall route. At a single cutoff, by contrast, unit survival must be checked separately: transport alone holds even for \(K_D=B_{\le D}\).

A complete analytic separation

Proposition (cutoff transport separation). Over any field on three Boolean variables \(x,y,z\), let \(g=(1-z)(1-x)\), \(h=zy\), \(J_1=(g)\), \(J_2=(h)\). These are the vanishing ideals of the two allowed clause sets. The first ideal has basis \(g,yg\), and the second has basis \(h,xh\): multiplication by its fixed local coordinates is either zero or unchanged, while the one free coordinate has basis \(1,y\) or \(1,x\). Their nonzero supports are disjoint since they require different values of \(z\), so the four vectors are independent.

Consequently \(K_2=\operatorname{span}\{g,h\}\), while \(K_3=J_1+J_2\) is their whole sum ideal. The identity

\[ yg+(1-x)h=(1-x)y=:p \tag{4}\]

puts the degree-two \(p\) in \(K_3\), but not in \(K_2\): its \(xy\) coefficient is \(-1\), whereas both \(g,h\) have zero \(xy\) coefficient. The degree-three leading-part map on the four-dimensional \(K_3\) has rank one (the common \(xyz\) term), so \(H_3\) has dimension one. Nevertheless every \(\Delta_j\) vanishes because \(K_3\) is an ideal and \(B=B_{\le3}\). Also \(1\notin K_3\), for example by evaluation at \(x=1,z=0,y=0\). This proves that a nonzero fall space need not obstruct the fixed-cutoff invariant. It is a satisfiable method calibration, not a non-vacuity witness for an unsatisfiable hard specification.

Adding a free variable \(w\) changes the test. The new truncated ideal pieces have bases \(g,yg,wg\) and \(h,xh,wh\) through degree three. They still contain \(p\), but not \(wp\): its \(xyw\) coefficient is \(-1\), while that coefficient is zero on all six listed basis vectors. Thus multiplication by \(w\) has a nonzero defect. This is an exact symbolic calculation, with no numerical rank assumption.

Every earlier fall has a free-variable obstruction

Proposition (free variable obstruction). In any Boolean algebra \(B_0\) with ideals \(J_a\), suppose \(q\in K_{d+1}\cap(B_0)_{\le d}\) but \(q\notin K_d\), for some \(d\ge0\). Extend all ideals to a larger Boolean algebra by adjoining at least \(D-d\) free variables, where \(D\ge d+1\). Denote its corresponding sums by \(\widehat K_e\). Then \(\widehat K_D\) fails multiplication closure at degree \(D\).

Proof. Take \(D-d-1\) of the free variables and let \(W\) be their product (one if none), with one more free variable \(w\). Multiply a representation of \(q\) as a sum of pieces of degrees at most \(d+1\) by \(W\). This proves \(Wq\in\widehat K_D\), while \(\deg(Wq)\le D-1\). If \(wWq\in\widehat K_D\), extract the coefficient of the exact free-variable monomial \(wW\) from each ideal piece of such a representation. This coefficient belongs to the original \(J_a\) and has degree at most \(D-(D-d)=d\). Membership follows either from the tensor decomposition of the extended ideal or by evaluating the free coordinates and using uniqueness of multilinear interpolation. Summing the coefficients would give \(q\in K_d\), a contradiction. Thus multiplying \(Wq\) by \(w\) leaves \(\widehat K_D\). \(\square\)

Corollary (uniform padding strictness). In particular, demanding fixed-cutoff closure uniformly after adjoining \(D\) free variables forces \(H_{d+1}=0\) for every \(0\le d<D\). The converse follows from repeated descent, together with coefficientwise extension: if all those original equalities hold, the degree filtration of the extended sum is strict through \(D\) as well. Indeed the coefficient of a free monomial of degree \(t\) lies in the original \(K_{e-t}\), so a fall from \(e\) to \(e-1\) would give an original fall from \(e-t\) to \(e-t-1\). This identifies exactly where the weaker endpoint ceases to be weaker under arbitrary free padding. The earlier majority obstruction is a concrete application with a proved original fall. Its special local immunity is needed there to exhibit the fall, not for the present propagation argument.

Method decision and remaining implication

Decision. Retain the fixed-cutoff maps (2) as the weaker proof target, and compare images in the actual quotient \(Q_D\) rather than insisting that all of \(H_D\) vanish. This changes the method, without adding an unproved condition to the accepted formula specification. The gain is that harmless falls are allowed; the cost is a separate proof that one survives and a global analysis of the defect images. The free-variable theorem prevents claiming this flexibility from merely tensoring a local calibration with unused coordinates. On an actual fully constrained family, other members might absorb the images, but that needs proof in their common quotient.

Outcome of the scheduled test. The endpoint is strictly weaker than all-degree no-fall gluing, and its obstruction can be computed from the chosen local ideals without first computing the unknown PC closure. However, as a condition on a cover it is still an exact reformulation of that cover's multiplication closure. No independent structural theorem currently forces its vanishing near half. Thus the test does not license a new hardness specification. The earlier promise to avoid hiding the target in admissibility is enforced here.

Follow-up. Continuing the local-section lead. Next attempt: determine whether cross-member cancellation defects can be absorbed by already available member ideals in the common quotient, without merging their entire constraint sets. Begin with the general two-member overlap decomposition, with separator assignments explicit, and seek a sufficient condition on its fibres. A condition demanding full global consistency or just restating (2) does not discharge the implication.

Sub-ideas

  • For the fixed-cutoff maps, derive a two-member overlap formula with explicit separator fibres and test whether defect images can vanish through the target degree without requiring a joint global solution or all-degree freeness. Use the actual quotient by all members; an absolute bound on the fall-space dimension is not enough.

Verification gate. The sole fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed the proofs and unchanged goal assessment, with no unused hypotheses or mathematical corrections. Its inventory request is addressed by separately registering the top-relation surjection, analytic separation and uniform-padding equivalence. The relative and cover gluing proofs, functional refinement, chain and sequential generation, later review, base correction, fall invariance, first-fall criterion and coupled-cover obstruction were read in full. Reviewed dependency and correction links were inspected. The majority proof was reread; the new free-variable claim is identified as a generalization of its coefficient argument. Searches covered fixed cutoffs, filtered strictness, top relations, defect quotients and spectator padding. All checks in this entry are exact algebraic proofs, with no new inference rule or encoding.

Process assessment. Reading the later correction and obstruction chain avoided reinstating quotient freeness or a fixed syzygy generating list as mandatory requirements. The new quotient isolates the weaker endpoint but does not count an equivalent reformulation as goal progress; that concern is carried to the next scheduled architecture review.
