8 October 2026 — Remove private coordinates before imposing overlap conditions

Working all-field proofs; specification refinement by projection. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, develop the local-section lead's separator-fibre test. Can conditioning repair multiplication transport cheaply enough, or does it suggest a better structural normalization of the formula specification?

New mathematics. The general separator decomposition was already in the record. Its Boolean cost motivates existential projection of coordinates confined to one constraint block. Every new forbidden-assignment clause is a scalar sum of old ones, so repeated projection costs a maximum of the original width and the final proof degree, not an additive charge per elimination. The resulting essential scopes have no variable occurring in just one block. The earlier infinite witness already has this property.

Goal progress. Unchanged. This validates another normalization and a more economical admissibility specification; it does not prove a near-half barrier. It eliminates private coordinates as a source of an apparent normalization gain without establishing the missing global transport theorem or complete Frege transfer.

General statement. prop:private-projection-transfer covers every finite Boolean relation system in canonical CNF over every proof field, with arbitrary repetitions of the allowed elimination operations. cor:shared-core-normalization records the moving-cutoff and unbounded-witness consequences for the evolving specification.

Use the existing separator result

The fibre-decomposition bound already proves that two systems with disjoint private variables and shared coordinates \(S\) decompose any joint vanishing polynomial of degree \(d\) into separate ideal pieces through \(d+(p-1)|S|\). Its many-member refinement proves the same mechanism for any number of private factors, stated over \(\mathbb F_3\). The proof restricts the shared coordinates, uses degree-compatible tensor splitting of the disjoint factors, and restores the coordinates with Lagrange indicators. In the Boolean setting the indicator cost is \(|S|\). This is a specialization of that recorded argument, not a new decomposition theorem.

The one-coordinate star theorem and adapted Helly extension further localize top relations under their precise adapted-basis assumptions. They do not remove all lifting costs or validate a near-half Boolean witness. The filtered-freeness transfer proof was also read: unfiltered semisimplicity alone does not give degree-preserving fibre splittings. Therefore simply repeating the separator calculation would leave the previous entry's transport maps unproved at the same cutoff.

The literal projection identity

Let the original formula be the canonical forbidden-assignment CNF of blocks \((X_i,R_i)\), with \(|X_i|\le w\), and Booleanity. A coordinate is private to a block if it occurs in no other block's scope. For \(P\subseteq X_i\) consisting of private coordinates, replace \(R_i\) by its existential projection onto \(S_i=X_i\setminus P\). A projected assignment \(a\) is forbidden exactly when all its extensions to \(P\) are forbidden in \(R_i\). With \(\delta_a\) denoting its Boolean point indicator,

\[\delta_a(x_{S_i})=\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}^{P}}\delta_{a,b}(x_{X_i}).\tag{1}\]

This is an identity in the ordinary polynomial ring over every field: the right side is \(\delta_a\prod_{j\in P}(x_j+(1-x_j))\). Every summand is an existing input clause polynomial. Thus the new clause follows by linear combinations alone through degree at most \(w\), without Boolean reduction.

The same operation may remove a semantically irrelevant coordinate from a block even if that coordinate occurs elsewhere: if membership in \(R_i\) is independent of it, projection leaves that block's constraint logically unchanged. Such removals can create private coordinates in other blocks. Repeat both operations until every remaining scope is essential and every occurring variable belongs to at least two scopes, or until an empty-scope contradiction remains. Delete universal blocks and unused names as necessary. Each nontrivial operation decreases the total number of incidences, so the procedure terminates. Constants are handled literally; a false empty-scope relation is the clause polynomial one.

Proposition (private projection transfer). Let \(\Pi\) be the terminal formula on \(q\) actually occurring variables. The original and terminal systems are equisatisfiable; more precisely, satisfiability is preserved for every fixed subcollection of block labels. If the original is block-minimal unsatisfiable, the retained nontrivial blocks are again block-minimal unsatisfiable. Every clause of \(\Pi\) is a linear combination of original clause polynomials through degree at most \(w\). If \(\Gamma\) is unsatisfiable, then

\[d_F(\Gamma)\le\max\{w,d_F(\Pi)\}\le\max\{w,q\}\tag{2}\]

for every proof field \(F\). Width and clause count do not increase. These assertions allow arbitrarily many elimination stages.

Proof. A solution of the old system restricts to a solution of the projected one. Conversely, a projected solution extends on the private coordinates using the definition of the projected relation, independently of all other blocks. For a subcollection omitting that block, choose these coordinates arbitrarily; for a subcollection retaining it, use the same extension argument. Removing an irrelevant coordinate from one scope changes its predicate on no assignment, so is harmless for every subcollection too. Composing these arguments proves the logical assertions and block-minimality preservation. In a minimal unsatisfiable system, a universal block cannot remain, since deleting it would not change satisfiability.

Identity (1) proves the clause assertion at each stage. Since a subsequent new clause is again a scalar combination of current clauses, substitute their already established scalar expressions, or concatenate their derivations. The maximum degree stays \(w\) throughout; there is no multiplication of an earlier derivation. Retained Booleanity axioms already occur in the original system. Append the terminal refutation after these derived clauses to get the first inequality in (2). The point-indicator partition proof from minimal core transfer gives \(d_F(\Pi)\le q\), including \(q=0\). Every projected forbidden pattern consumes all its old extensions, disjointly for different patterns, so each block's forbidden-pattern count cannot increase. Scope sizes only decrease. \(\square\)

The maximum with \(w\) is necessary. A single empty relation on \(w\) variables has all degree-\(w\) point indicators as its input clauses, while eliminating them gives the degree-zero clause one. For \(w\ge1\), the original needs degree at least \(w\), since without using any non-Boolean input axiom its system is satisfiable, and every such input has that degree; summing the indicators refutes in degree \(w\). Private-variable elimination can therefore preserve a large degree barrier but does not preserve exact small-degree minima. Eliminating a shared variable existentially from two blocks is also unsound: \(x=0\) and \(x=1\) would separately project to true. Privacy or semantic irrelevance is an essential hypothesis.

Refine the specification without losing its witness

Corollary (shared core normalization). For a sequence of unsatisfiable formulas, at fixed depth \(\ell\ge2\), with original width at most fixed \(w\), actual variable count \(v\to\infty\), and no PC refutation through \(D_v=\lfloor v/2-s_v\rfloor\), where \(s_v=o(v/(\log v)^{\ell-2})\), the terminal projected counts satisfy \(q_v\ge D_v+1\) for all sufficiently large \(v\). The projected systems retain nonrefutation through

\[\left\lfloor q_v/2-s'_v\right\rfloor,\qquad s'_v=\max\{0,s_v-(v-q_v)/2\}=o(q_v/(\log q_v)^{\ell-2}).\tag{3}\]

Polynomial encoding and independence from the proposed proof-size exponent are preserved. Thus the current block-minimal envelope may additionally require essential scopes and at least two block occurrences of every variable, after adjusting the fixed size constant. Write \(\mathcal S_{\mathrm{ess}}(w,b,C)\) for this essential-scope subclass of the original envelope. It is still non-vacuous with the same unbounded linear-degree witness as before.

Proof. Eventually \(D_v\ge w\). A terminal proof through \(D_v\) would give an original proof through that degree by (2), so none exists. The upper bound \(d_F(\Pi)\le q_v\) gives \(q_v\ge D_v+1\). Consequently \(v=O(q_v)\), so polynomial clause counts and encoding lengths stay polynomial in the new actual count. Formula (3) and its little-oh estimate are the exact same floor calculation as in minimal core transfer, with \(v,q_v\) in place of \(n,v_n\). Fix an order for eligible operations, independent of the proof-size exponent; this preserves that independence.

For non-vacuity use the previously proved minimal unsatisfiable three-CNF cores with degree at least \(\eta v\), \(\eta=4000^{-4}/8\). Every coordinate of a non-tautological clause is essential to its relation. No variable of a minimal unsatisfiable CNF can occur in only one clause: a satisfying assignment of the other clauses exists by minimality, and changing that private variable to satisfy the remaining clause changes none of them, a contradiction. Hence this witness already belongs to the refined class, with no variables removed and the same size and degree bounds. \(\square\)

Decision and next obstruction

Decision. Make the structural refinement rather than impose a new separator-freeness condition on the method. The gain is a justified removal of private or semantically irrelevant coordinates before degree normalization; the lost feature is the original private-variable construction law. Equisatisfiability, block minimality, the large-degree barrier and polynomial encoding survive by proof. The old infinite witness survives unchanged. This is an accepted normalization, not a provisional near-half hardness condition. The fixed-cutoff transport criterion remains a candidate method and its global vanishing remains unproved.

Follow-up. Continuing the local-section lead through the user's method/specification comparison. The next general test is whether formulas in which almost every variable occurs in exactly two blocks are already excluded by the graph-frontier upper bound. The existing matched-frontier proof was read in full. Its statement is for simple graphs, whereas two-occurrence variables produce a possibly parallel-edge incidence graph. Audit that distinction and the cost of conditioning the exceptional higher-occurrence variables before adopting any branching requirement. A bound would apply to an abstract regime, not merely one concrete predicate family.

Sub-ideas

  • Audit the matched-frontier upper bound for bounded-arity block systems whose variables have two occurrences, including parallel edges, and then quantify the effect of conditioning the variables occurring in three or more blocks. If it excludes a regime, identify a non-vacuous structural strengthening rather than silently adding an unproved filter.

Verification gate. The sole fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed subject to clarifications now incorporated: the degree bounds and moving-cutoff corollary explicitly assume unsatisfiability, the depth is fixed with \(\ell\ge2\), and the width-overhead example is analytic rather than an executed degree check. Minimality is used only for the corresponding preservation and envelope assertions. The fibre-decomposition proof and its many-member refinement, star locality, adapted Helly locality, filtered-freeness transfer and the simple-graph frontier proof were read in full, with newer-version metadata. The core-normalization and non-vacuity proofs are the immediately preceding checkpoint's dependencies. Exact GAP checks exhaust 278 relations through arity three, 2,122 private-subset cases and 1,944 integer indicator identities, examining 16,658 extension assignments. All 256 two-block compositions pass satisfiability preservation, including 18 minimal-unsatisfiable pairs. The shared-variable negative control passed. The original-width sharpness example is proved analytically above; the checker only prints its description and does not compute a PC minimum. These tests verify the encoding rule, not an asymptotic degree claim. The initial launcher invocation did not recognize the script as a command; the first GAP run then stopped on a reserved identifier. After correction the checker passed; remaining diagnostics are parse-time global-variable warnings. Full output and script are retained under the cycle's results directory.

Process assessment. Claim search found the proposed separator bound already proved, including its many-member version. Reusing it led to a costed specification refinement instead of duplicating the theorem or refining another finite matrix. The necessary near-half implication remains unchanged and is carried to the scheduled architecture review. The reviewer first received an ambiguous excerpt path; the same handle was given its exact path and completed the review.

8 October 2026 — Near-half hardness requires a positive density of branching variables

Working all-field proofs; structural occurrence screen. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, continue the local-section lead's structural alternative after private projection. Can a bounded-arity system with very few variables occurring in more than two blocks meet the near-half target? Audit the graph argument's parallel-edge scope, and require an unbounded witness before accepting the resulting specification condition.

New mathematics. The existing matched-frontier argument extends to loopless multigraphs. Conditioning on the higher-occurrence variables yields a degree ceiling depending only on their number and the block arity. In particular any near-half family must have limiting lower branching density at least \(1/(2w)\). A separate width-four construction gives a block-minimal, essential-scope, linear-degree witness with branching density at least \(2/5\), paying every copied and splitting variable.

Goal progress. Unchanged. This excludes a structural regime and validates a necessary screen. It does not construct a near-half-hard family or prove the fixed-cutoff transport maps vanish. The witness has only a fixed positive linear degree fraction, and the complete Frege transfer remains open.

General statement. thm:multigraph-frontier-degree-ceiling extends the recorded graph upper bound; thm:occurrence-degree-ceiling applies to every unsatisfiable normalized bounded-arity Boolean block system over every proof field; prop:branching-nonvacuity supplies an unbounded family for the resulting structural screen. These are not estimates fitted to examples.

Parallel edges do not invalidate matched frontiers

Theorem (multigraph frontier ceiling). Let \(G\) be a finite loopless multigraph, with one Boolean variable per edge, maximum degree \(w\) counting multiplicities, and multilinear input constraints confined to vertex stars. Include Booleanity and assume unsatisfiability. For \(n\ge3\) nonisolated vertices and a matching of size \(a\), put \(b=n-a\ge2\). There is an ordinary PC refutation through

\[\left\lceil\frac{(m-a)b}{2(b-1)}+w\sqrt{8b\log(2b)}\right\rceil+2w.\tag{1}\]

For fixed \(w\ge3\), with \(m\) edges, all cases including fewer vertices satisfy

\[d_F\le c_wm+E_w(m),\qquad c_w=\frac12-\frac1{4w-2},\quad E_w(t)=4w\sqrt{(t+1)\log(4t+4)}+3w+2.\tag{2}\]

Proof. We reuse the mechanism, with its full degree accounting, of the simple-graph theorem. For a star assignment \(\alpha\) violating an input \(f\), its point indicator \(\chi_\alpha\) satisfies \(f\chi_\alpha-f(\alpha)\chi_\alpha\in I_{2w}(\mathrm{Bool})\). Telescope each monomial of \(f\) about \(\alpha\); the identity \((x_i-\alpha_i)\chi_\alpha=(2\alpha_i-1)(x_i^2-x_i)\prod_{j\ne i}\ell_{\alpha_j}(x_j)\) bounds each resulting Boolean multiple by degree \(2w\). Multiplication and division by the nonzero scalar \(f(\alpha)\) derive every forbidden star indicator through \(2w\). For a canonical relation these indicators are already inputs.

For a vertex order with cutwidth \(C\), maintain the relation on crossing edge variables consisting of assignments extendible to all processed vertex constraints. When adding a vertex, the bag is the old frontier together with its star, of size at most \(C+w\). Every bad bag assignment violates either an old forbidden frontier assignment or the new star relation. Extend the corresponding indicator by the missing distinct literals, costing at most the bag size. Sum all completion indicators of a forbidden new-frontier assignment; eliminated coordinates contribute \(x+(1-x)=1\). At the full prefix derive one. Thus the ceiling is \(\max\{2w,C+w\}\). Distinct parallel edges are distinct Boolean coordinates, so none of this uses simplicity.

Group the endpoints of each matched edge into a block, leaving unmatched vertices single. There are \(b\) blocks and at most \(m-a\) interblock edges; additional parallel edges internal to a matched pair only improve this inequality. Order the blocks by independent uniform keys. At the first \(j\) blocks the expected crossing count is at most \(2j(b-j)(m-a)/(b(b-1))\). Changing one key swaps at most two blocks, containing at most four vertices; the cut changes by at most \(4w\), with multiplicities counted. Bounded differences therefore bounds the upper tail at \(u\) by \(\exp(-u^2/(8bw^2))\). Taking \(u=w\sqrt{8b\log(2b)}\) and summing over \(1\le j<b\) gives a probability below one. Expand matched pairs consecutively, adding at most \(w\) to cutwidth, and apply the preceding elimination to get (1).

A maximal matching satisfies \(a\ge m/(2w-1)\): every edge touches a selected edge, and each selected edge's endpoints touch at most \(2w-1\) edges, even counting parallel edges. After deleting isolated vertices, \(n\le2m\), \(n\ge2m/w\), and \(b\ge n/2\). For \(b\ge2\), \(m/(2(b-1))\le w\), while \(w\sqrt{8b\log(2b)}\le4w\sqrt{m\log(4m)}\). Substitution into (1), including rounding and \(2w\), is bounded by (2). If there are just two nonisolated vertices, all edges lie in one star, \(m\le w\), and the point indicators give degree at most \(2w\), also below (2). The restriction \(w\ge3\) for the displayed uniform form is retained to match the specification; the multigraph argument also works for positive smaller degree bounds. A false isolated-vertex constraint gives degree zero; otherwise isolated constraints are discarded. With no edges an unsatisfiable system is constant and immediate. \(\square\)

A ceiling in terms of higher occurrences

Theorem (occurrence degree ceiling). Let \(\Gamma\) be an unsatisfiable canonical Boolean relation system on \(v\) actual variables, with scopes of size at most fixed \(w\ge3\), and every variable occurring in at least two distinct blocks. Let \(H\) be the variables occurring in at least three blocks and \(h=|H|\). Then, over every field,

\[d_F(\Gamma)\le h+c_w(v-h)+E_w(v-h).\tag{3}\]

Consequently any family meeting the near-half target \(d_F>\lfloor v/2-s_v\rfloor\), with \(s_v=o(v)\), must satisfy

\[h>\frac{v}{2w}-\frac{4w-2}{2w}\bigl(s_v+E_w(v-h)\bigr),\qquad\liminf_{v\to\infty}\frac hv\ge\frac1{2w}.\tag{4}\]

Proof. Fix an assignment \(\rho\) to \(H\). The residual system is unsatisfiable. Every remaining variable belonged to exactly two original blocks, so it is an edge joining those two block vertices. Preserve these declared incidences even when restriction makes a variable irrelevant to a residual predicate. This is a loopless multigraph with \(v-h\) edges and maximum degree at most \(w\). The residual constraints remain local to their stars, so (2) applies uniformly in \(\rho\). Constant residual contradictions are allowed.

Let \(\delta_\rho\) be the point indicator on \(H\). A residual forbidden clause on a scope \(X_i\setminus H\) comes from the old forbidden full assignment on \(X_i\) agreeing with \(\rho\). Multiply that old indicator by the literals on \(H\setminus X_i\). The result is exactly \(\delta_\rho\) times the residual clause, with no repeated variables and degree at most \(h+w\). Weighted residual Boolean axioms follow by multiplying the original Boolean axioms, through \(h+2\). Multiply every subsequent residual proof line by \(\delta_\rho\); linear combinations and multiplication by residual variables remain valid, and the degree is at most \(h\) plus the residual ceiling. Since the bound in (2) exceeds \(w\), it pays all weighted initial lines. This derives \(\delta_\rho\). Sum over \(\rho\) to obtain one via the literal partition identity, proving (3). No polynomial proof-size upper bound is asserted.

The integer lower bound on \(d_F\) implies \(d_F>v/2-s_v\). Compare with (3), use \(1-c_w=2w/(4w-2)\), and rearrange to get (4). For fixed \(w\), \(E_w(v-h)=O_w(\sqrt{v\log(v+1)})=o(v)\), giving its limit statement. The stronger depth-dependent deficit of this notebook in particular has \(s_v=o(v)\). \(\square\)

This applies to every predicate in the structural regime, not just modular equations or flow. A sublinear number of variables with three or more occurrences cannot rescue an otherwise graph-like system. The remaining high-overlap coset, majority and colouring variants are not excluded merely by this count. All occurrences here refer to the chosen whole blocks, not to the number of clauses in their truth-table expansion.

The branching screen has an unbounded witness

Proposition (branching nonvacuity). There is an unbounded family of minimal unsatisfiable CNFs with width at most four, linear clause count, essential scopes, at least two occurrences of every variable and at least \(2/5\) of variables occurring in at least three clauses. Its ordinary PC degree is at least a fixed positive fraction of its actual variables over every field. Thus the asymptotic condition in (4) can be added to the search specification without making it vacuous. The construction is an existence witness, not a way to preserve a near-half fraction.

Proof: bounded-occurrence copying. Start with the proved minimal three-CNF witness, whose original clauses each have exactly three distinct literals (the construction takes a subcollection of clauses without shortening), on \(n\) variables with \(m\le C_0n\) clauses and degree at least \(\eta n\), where \(C_0=6/\eta\) and \(\eta=4000^{-4}/8\). Replace each literal occurrence of \(x_i\) by a fresh copy \(y_{i,j}\). For its \(r_i\ge2\) occurrences, add the directed cycle of implications \(y_{i,j}\to y_{i,j+1}\), cyclically indexed. Boolean solutions of this cycle have all copies equal: any one propagates around the cycle, and otherwise all are zero. Thus the copied system is unsatisfiable. It has \(N_0=3m\) variables, clauses of widths two and three, and exactly three clause occurrences per variable.

Substitute \(y_{i,j}=x_i\) in any ordinary PC refutation of the copied system. Original-clause copies become original clauses, implication polynomials become \(x_i(1-x_i)=-(x_i^2-x_i)\), and Booleanity remains Booleanity. This linear substitution never increases degree and gives an original refutation. Hence the copied degree is at least \(\eta n\ge\kappa N_0\), with \(\kappa=\eta/(3C_0)\). Take a minimal unsatisfiable subcollection and delete unused names. Axiom deletion preserves this lower bound. If it has \(r\) actual variables and \(t\) clauses, then its degree is at least \(\kappa r\), \(r\to\infty\), every variable occurs two or three times, and

\[2t\le\text{number of incidences}\le3r,\qquad t\le3r/2.\tag{5}\]

The first inequality uses that its retained clauses still have width two or three; no shortening or unit propagation has been performed.

Proof: splitting each clause. For each retained clause \(C_j\), introduce one fresh \(z_j\) and replace it by \(C_j\vee z_j\) and \(C_j\vee\neg z_j\). Their conjunction is equivalent to \(C_j\). The resulting formula is minimal unsatisfiable: if either half is deleted, a satisfying assignment of all the old clauses except \(C_j\) exists and must falsify \(C_j\); choose \(z_j\) to satisfy the surviving half. All other split clauses are satisfied because their old clauses are true. Every variable in each proper clause is essential.

There are \(N=r+t\le5r/2\) actual variables and \(2t\le3r\) clauses. Every old variable now occurs four or six times; every new variable occurs twice. Thus precisely \(r\) variables branch, and \(r/N\ge2/5\). Restricting every \(z_j\) to zero turns the formula back into the old core plus zero polynomial axioms, so its PC degree is at least the core's, hence at least \((2\kappa/5)N\). Conversely each old clause polynomial \(q_j\) is the exact sum \(q_jz_j+q_j(1-z_j)\), derived through width at most four. Therefore the degree increase itself is at most the maximum with four. The gain in branching density does not mean a gain in degree divided by variables: all \(t\) fresh variables are paid. This proves every asserted witness property. \(\square\)

Specification decision and representation warning

Decision. Add to the essential-scope family envelope the necessary asymptotic branching condition \(\liminf h/v\ge1/(2w)\), at its fixed arity bound \(w\). The width-four witness above proves non-vacuity and retains a positive linear-degree barrier. Compared with the earlier witness, its width rises from three to four and the degree fraction decreases by explicit constants. Those costs are acceptable for an existence witness, but do not produce the target near-half family.

Branching is representation-sensitive. Merging each split pair and removing its now irrelevant \(z_j\) recovers the previous core; nothing in the witness proves that this encoding is intrinsically harder. Apply every known upper bound in any representation for which a paid proof transfer is available. The new condition is a necessary screen, not a replacement for such transfers or a sufficient hardness theorem. In particular the earlier graph, coset, colouring and subfield exclusions remain active in their proved scopes.

Direct inconsistent binary linear equations have linear-elimination refutations. Family 200 is homogeneous characteristic-zero Hilbert-function theory, not the required binary filtered result. The signed restriction mechanism in family 112 still needs finite-field denominators, paid degrees and preservation of the hard instance; the backward scale mechanism in family 274 still needs an appropriate dual map, terminal regime and degree ledger. 8 October 2026 — Short coset representatives replace the free-coordinate ceiling

Working all-parameter proofs; code covering-radius screen and scoped imports. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, develop the rotated code/binomial item at the level of general systems. In the consistent-coset upper proof, is the full free-coordinate cost intrinsic, or can a different character basis improve it? Compare this test with building a proper Boolean character invariant, rather than assuming that further upper bounds are automatically the best route.

Route comparison. The user's latest instruction is to judge among direct lower-bound constructions, method repairs and specification restrictions before each next step. The auxiliary charged quotient provides a real bounded multiplication-compatible construction, but its local ternary domain does not impose the binary subdomain in the present consistent coset. A direct Boolean invariant therefore still needs its properness and cancellation proof. The covering-radius alternative below has a concrete all-parameter argument addressing the existing interpolation cost, so it is the selected test for this cycle. Weighted-map descent belongs to the later transfer obligation while the old family is missing. This is a local choice of task, not a policy to prefer upper bounds.

New mathematics. A basis of shortest row-code coset representatives replaces the old coordinate-section basis in the Fourier certificate. It gives an actual degree ceiling controlled by the row code's covering radius. Near-half hardness in this code regime therefore needs covering radius near half the original variable count. At rank near half, this is near the redundancy bound, and an elementary deep-hole argument imposes a sparse systematic basis condition.

Goal progress. Unchanged. This is a new necessary screen for the code route, not a near-half lower bound. No unbounded witness satisfying the narrowed code conditions together with all accepted structural requirements has been proved. Accordingly the code-specific refinement remains provisional; the accepted general branching specification and its existing witness are retained. Complete Frege transfer remains open.

General statement. thm:covering-radius-degree-ceiling applies to every field-consistent Boolean-empty bounded-row-width system over a prime modulus different from the proof characteristic. cor:near-half-covering-radius gives its necessary asymptotic condition. prop:deep-hole-basis-support applies to every finite-field linear code and uses no proof-complexity assumption.

Choose representatives in the row-code quotient

Let \(q\ge3\) be prime, \(F\) a proof field with \(\operatorname{char}F\ne q\), and \(Ax=b\) an \(\mathbb F_q\)-system on \(v\) Boolean variables, with rank \(r\ge1\) and original row support at most \(w\ge1\). Assume its field solution set \(L\) is nonempty, but \(L\cap\{0,1\}^v=\varnothing\). Use all forbidden Boolean-assignment clauses for each row and Booleanity. Let \(C\subseteq\mathbb F_q^v\) be the row space of \(A\), not its kernel, and put

\[\rho=\max_{a\in\mathbb F_q^v}\min_{c\in C}|\operatorname{supp}(a-c)|.\tag{1}\]

For each class \(g\in\mathbb F_q^v/C\), choose a representative \(a_g\) of support at most \(\rho\), with \(a_0=0\). Over a finite algebraic extension \(E/F\) containing a primitive \(q\)-th root \(\zeta\), use

\[u_i(t)=1+(\zeta^t-1)x_i,\qquad X(a)=\prod_{i:a_i\ne0}u_i(a_i).\]

As functions on the field coset \(L\), the characters \(\chi_g(x)=\zeta^{a_g\cdot x}\) form a basis. Indeed \(L=x_0+\ker A\), and two exponent vectors restrict to proportional characters exactly when their difference is in \((\ker A)^\perp=C\). There are \(q^{v-r}\) such classes, and character orthogonality on \(\ker A\) proves independence. Field consistency makes the phase functional \(\theta(\lambda A)=\lambda b\) well-defined on \(C\).

The covering-radius ceiling

Theorem (covering radius ceiling). Put

\[ t=\min\{v,2\rho+1\},\qquad M=\min\left\{v,\left\lceil(1-1/q)v+t/q+w\sqrt{2r\log(4r+2)}\right\rceil\right\}.\]

The canonical Boolean system has an ordinary PC refutation over \(F\) through

\[B_\rho=\max\{\lceil M/2\rceil+w+1,\rho+3\}.\tag{2}\]

In particular, for fixed \(q,w\),

\[d_F\le\max\left\{\frac{1-1/q}{2}v+\frac\rho q,\rho\right\}+O_w(\sqrt{r\log(r+1)}+1).\tag{3}\]

This complements the old free-coordinate bound; take the smaller of the two applicable ceilings. It is not uniformly a strengthening when \(\rho\) is as large as the old free-coordinate count.

Proof: derive all needed translated relations. Select \(r\) independent original rows. For a class \(g\), coordinate \(j\), and \(s\in\{1,2\}\), let \(g'=g+se_j+C\) and

\[d=a_g+se_j-a_{g'}\in C,\qquad\gamma=\zeta^{\theta(d)}.\]

The vector \(d\) has support at most \(t\). Write it as \(\lambda A\) in the selected rows. Use the previously proved random intermediate path, whose argument is recalled here: choose independent uniform row coefficients, move from zero to this random vector one coefficient at a time, then from it to \(\lambda\). At each state a coordinate is either uniform, zero, or its fixed final value; the expected support is at most \((1-1/q)v+|\operatorname{supp}d|/q\). Changing one random coefficient changes at most \(w\) support coordinates. Bounded differences at \(w\sqrt{2r\log(4r+2)}\), followed by a union bound over \(2r+1\) states, gives a path with all supports at most \(M\).

For each row and scalar multiple, its character equation is a scalar sum of its original forbidden indicators through degree \(w\). Maintain the balanced character binomial for the accumulating row combination. One row update and subsequent balancing derive the next binomial through \(\lceil M/2\rceil+w+1\), exactly as in the complete transport proof. Every repeated factor is paid by the literal Boolean identity

\[u_i(a)u_i(t)-u_i(a+t)=(\zeta^a-1)(\zeta^t-1)(x_i^2-x_i).\]

The endpoint binomial has exponent difference \(d\) and phase \(\gamma\). Apply character-binomial transport to place it at \(X(a_g+se_j)-\gamma X(a_{g'})\). Both endpoint supports are at most \(\rho+1\), and \(\lceil|\operatorname{supp}d|/2\rceil\le\rho+1\), so the additional ceiling is \(\rho+3\). Applying the same local Boolean identity converts the first term to \(X(a_g)u_j(s)\) through degree at most \(\rho+1\). Thus

\[X(a_g)u_j(s)-\gamma_{g,j,s}X(a_{g+se_j+C})\in C_{B_\rho}(\Gamma).\tag{4}\]

All earlier derivations are reused as final lines; no cofactor multiplies their old proof lines. Zero differences and the zero class cause no difficulty.

Proof: recombine on the actual field coset. The literal affine identity \(u_j(2)-(1+\zeta)u_j(1)+\zeta=0\), multiplied by \(X(a_g)\), combines the two lines (4) into the derived polynomial

\[R_{j,g}=\gamma_{g,j,2}X(a_{g+2e_j+C})-(1+\zeta)\gamma_{g,j,1}X(a_{g+e_j+C})+\zeta X(a_g).\tag{5}\]

Its degree is at most \(\rho\), although its derivation is charged through \(B_\rho\). On \(L\), the corresponding character expression is \(\chi_g f_j\), where

\[f_j(x)=\zeta^{2x_j}-(1+\zeta)\zeta^{x_j}+\zeta.\]

The roots of \(z^2-(1+\zeta)z+\zeta\) are exactly \(1,\zeta\). Hence the \(f_j\)'s have no common zero on \(L\), by Boolean emptiness. At each point choose one nonzero \(f_j\), assign the value \(1/f_j\) there to \(H_j\), and zero to the others. Then \(\sum_jH_jf_j=1\) as functions on \(L\). Expand each \(H_j\) in the character basis \(\chi_g\). Multiplication of characters reduces by the same phases as (4), so this is a coefficient identity in that basis, with its right side \(\chi_0=1\).

Apply the linear replacement \(\chi_g\mapsto X(a_g)\) to that coefficient identity. It gives a literal polynomial identity \(\sum_{j,g}h_{j,g}R_{j,g}=1\). The replacement need not be injective or multiplicative; only a linear image of an already collected coefficient identity is used. Every line (5) has its bounded PC derivation, so their final linear combination refutes through (2). Finally expand the proof's coefficients in an \(F\)-basis of \(E\) containing one. Original axioms are defined over \(F\); taking components commutes with every inference and recovers the constant-one component with no degree increase. This proves the theorem. \(\square\)

A necessary metric restriction, and what it implies

Corollary (near-half radius necessity). In the theorem's fixed-\(q,w\) regime, a family with \(v\to\infty\) and no refutation through \(\lfloor v/2-s_v\rfloor\), \(s_v=o(v)\), must have \(\rho\ge v/2-o(v)\). Since \(\rho\le v-r\), it also has \(r\le v/2+o(v)\). If \(r=v/2+o(v)\), its covering-radius slack \((v-r)-\rho\) is \(o(v)\).

Proof. If \(\rho\le(1/2-\epsilon)v\) on an infinite subsequence with fixed \(\epsilon>0\), (3) gives degree at most \((1/2-\epsilon/q)v+o(v)\), eventually below the required cutoff. This is impossible. To prove the redundancy bound, choose pivot coordinates for a row basis of \(C\); subtract a codeword matching any vector on those \(r\) coordinates. At most \(v-r\) nonzero coordinates remain. The other conclusions follow. The argument does not assert the same asymptotic screen for growing \(q\) or \(w\). \(\square\)

Proposition (deep-hole basis bound). For any linear code \(C\subseteq\mathbb F_q^v\), with codimension \(k\), covering radius \(\rho\), and slack \(t=k-\rho\), there is a systematic row basis of \(C\) in which each row has support at most \(q(t+1)\). More precisely, choose a maximum-distance coset leader and extend its support to a column basis of a parity-check matrix. In that systematic matrix, a nonbasis column having \(z\) nonzero entries outside the leader support has at most \((q-1)(z+1)\) nonzero entries on the leader support.

Proof. Let \(H\) be a rank-\(k\) parity-check matrix with kernel \(C\), and let \(a\) be a coset leader of weight \(\rho\), with support \(S\). The columns of \(H\) on \(S\) are independent. Otherwise a nonzero codeword supported inside \(S\), scaled to agree with \(a\) at one nonzero coordinate, could be subtracted without creating a new nonzero coordinate, reducing the leader weight. Extend \(S\) to a column basis \(B\) of size \(k\) and row-reduce \(H_B\) to the identity. The syndrome is then the vector \(a_B\), nonzero at the \(\rho\) positions of \(S\), zero at the other \(t\) basis positions.

For a nonbasis column \(h\), let \(z\) count its nonzero entries in those \(t\) zero-syndrome positions. Represent the same syndrome using coefficient \(\lambda\ne0\) on this column and residual coefficients \(a_B-\lambda h\) on the basis. If \(M_\lambda\) coordinates of \(S\) satisfy \(a_i=\lambda h_i\), the new representative has weight \(1+\rho-M_\lambda+z\). Minimality gives \(M_\lambda\le z+1\). Each nonzero entry of \(h\) on \(S\) contributes to exactly one of the \(q-1\) such match counts. Thus its support on \(S\) is at most \((q-1)(z+1)\), and its total support is at most \(qz+q-1\le qt+q-1\). The systematic generator \(e_j-\sum_{i\in B}h_i e_i\) adds one more coordinate. These generators are independent on the nonbasis coordinates and span \(C\), proving the bound. \(\square\)

At zero slack the bound is attained by the one-dimensional code with parity check \([I_{q-1}\mid h]\), where \(h\) lists all nonzero field elements. The vector with ones on the basis and zero on the last coordinate has distance \(q-1\): its ratios against a generator list every field element, so every scalar multiple matches exactly one coordinate. This is a sharpness control, not an unbounded hard family. At the rank-half endpoint, the corollary and proposition force a systematic basis of support \(o(v)\); they do not assert constant width or a polynomial-size canonical re-encoding of that basis.

What is and is not an accepted refinement

The new test replaces an arbitrary coordinate choice by an intrinsic code metric. A candidate code specification can now demand the necessary high covering radius and, at rank half, confront the nearly extremal redundancy regime and its systematic-basis restrictions. But an unbounded family meeting these conditions and the current block-minimal, essential, branching and encoding requirements has not been supplied. The code-specific refinement therefore remains provisional. Direct sums of short repetition codes can have maximal covering radius while their Boolean contradictions are local or their normalized cores remain bounded; that is not the required witness.

The main positive alternative remains an explicit Boolean truncated character space. The full function algebra of \(L\) is not such a space: imposing all Boolean-coordinate equations there gives the unit ideal because \(L\) has no Boolean point. The auxiliary charged construction avoids that collapse by its local ternary domain and a bounded phase relation, under a different inconsistency hypothesis. Importing its multiplication compatibility without the domain and phase proof would not establish ours. The covering-radius argument uses the full field coset only to construct a refutation, where this distinction is legitimate.

Auxiliary imports retained at their exact scope

At the user's explicit request, the pinned auxiliary import record preserves the applicable results from local aux commit 20c6252b5, with its later comparison at 7658a1360. The three new source records were read in full from the pinned notebook blob; the inherited Fourier, charged, binary-descent and cut-code rank records were also reread. No merge or publication was performed.

Weighted map descent. For a scalar-extended binary degree-\(D\) pseudoideal containing old Booleanity, complete binary source polynomials of original degree at most \(B\), old polynomial coefficient maps of degree at most \(T\), and a surviving weight of degree at most \(b\), the source theorem descends maps and weight with the same degrees when \(T\ge1\), \(B\ge2\), \(b+TB\le D\) and every weighted fresh Boolean equation is included. At one specified \(b+k\le D\), coefficient-component specialization retains at least half the multiplication rank, without the separate new-multiplier \(3k\) reserve. This is useful for the transfer obligation after map existence is proved; it supplies no old family, map existence or smaller ordinary NS cofactor.

Charged tuples and phase growth. The graph construction and binary one-hot descent retain \(\max(4,2\Delta)\le D<\varepsilon n/8\). Its tuple floor needs \(d_0+R\le D\), \(R=\ell s\), and gives \(\lambda\ge\max\{1,[h(R)/(h(d_0)\dim Y)]^{1/\ell}\}\). Its dichotomy concerns enlarged-image certificates, not optimized errors. The input-phase result has a canonical largest binary-descending minimizer, with reserve \(d_0+2\ell\delta\), but its actual affine-domain control can retain only one dimension out of \(v+6\) under growth below \(3/2\). These are precise comparison tools and a warning against assuming useful fraction retention. They neither apply automatically to this code coset nor supply the missing near-half threshold.

Exact checks and route status

The new checker uses a fixed six-coordinate quintic code: a parity-check matrix with columns \((1,t,t^2)\), \(t\in\mathbb F_5\), and \((0,0,1)\), and its three-dimensional nullspace as the original row code. It enumerates all 15,625 exponent vectors, finding 125 classes with leader-weight counts \(1,24,100\), hence covering radius two while the free-coordinate count is three. The Boolean image has size 52, so a field-consistent Boolean-empty syndrome exists. Its 125 character functions on the field coset have rank 125, while their chosen ordinary Boolean representative polynomials have rank 17. This explicitly tests the use of a noninjective linear replacement.

One shared exact finite-field matrix inverse computes every coefficient in the pointwise partition certificate. All 1,500 transport relations pass 187,500 phase evaluations; multiplying each phase by the nontrivial root is rejected, and omitting a nonzero polynomial term destroys the final identity. The full short-representative certificate is literally one over \(\mathbb F_{16}\). The complete certificate data and script are retained. These are nonvacuous identity and parameter controls, not evidence for an asymptotic PC minimum; at this small size the theorem's additive costs need not improve brute force.

Follow-up. Continuing the code/binomial item. The free-coordinate endpoint was partly a representation cost: short coset leaders give a different ceiling, and the surviving rank-half regime now requires nearly maximal covering radius. The next cycle is the scheduled architecture review. It must compare the extremal-code route with direct Boolean invariants and the retained source leads, and test non-vacuity before accepting a narrower code specification.

The systematic basis bound is available, but sublinear support does not imply polynomial canonical encoding.

8 October 2026 — Separate admissible structure from a surviving PC invariant

Reviewed route assessment; alternative random-relation encoding. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. Six research entries have changed the tools and admissible structure without proving the near-half family. Which necessary implication should the next cycle attack, and which conditions have become stronger than the goal needs? This is the scheduled ordinary review; no review is ten research entries back.

New mathematics. The elementary random-relation encoding calculation below verifies an unbounded alternative source of unsatisfiable, essential, polynomial-size Boolean systems at logarithmic width. It proves no degree lower bound. The remaining work is a comparison of proved normalizations, actual upper bounds and explicit source/translation controls.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The six entries leave both open statements open. A nonempty class with a positive linear degree witness, several necessary restrictions, and a sufficient invariant criterion do not imply a near-half witness. The new growing-width ensemble is provisional and does not replace the accepted fixed-width specification.

General statement. prop:random-relation-encoding gives only the alternative ensemble's encoding and unsatisfiability guarantees. The required lower-bound implication remains the near-half barrier; prop:fixed-cutoff-cover-transport is one sufficient method, and thm:covering-radius-degree-ceiling supplies a necessary code screen, not that implication.

What the six entries changed

The majority overlap exposed a genuine multiplication defect in separate local ideals. Core selection and private projection then supplied degree-preserving normalizations with actual-variable accounting and an unbounded positive-linear witness. The fixed-cutoff maps weakened all-degree strictness to transport of the relevant falls. The occurrence ceiling forced a density of higher-occurrence variables, with a width-four witness showing that the general refinement is nonempty. Finally, short character representatives gave a covering-radius ceiling and a deep-hole basis restriction. This is substantial mathematics, but each entry correctly reported the necessary near-half lower bound unchanged.

The code-specific restriction is not yet accepted: its combined witness is missing. The general branching witness is presentation-sensitive, so it cannot justify hardness after an easier paid representation is found. The imported weighted-map theorem belongs to the eventual transfer step; it does not remove the family bottleneck. No queue item's status changes merely because it is compared again here.

Obstacle

There is still no explicit, proper degree-truncated Boolean invariant for an unbounded unsatisfiable family at the required cutoff. Local satisfiability and local immunity do not control cancellations between constraints. Conversely, repeated necessary restrictions can leave an empty proposed class. The present code route has an additional concrete existence question: can nearly maximal row-code covering radius coexist with an unbounded Boolean-empty minimal essential instance, after every paid normalization?

Architecture review

Necessary target. For each fixed relevant approximation depth \(\ell\ge2\), exhibit an unsatisfiable polynomial-size Boolean CNF family on actual \(v\to\infty\) variables, independent of the proof-size exponent, with \(1\notin C_D\) over \(\mathbb F_2\), where \(D=\lfloor v/2-s\rfloor\) and \(s=o(v/(\log v)^{\ell-2})\). Existence is sufficient. The same family must eventually meet the separate Frege/source grammar and original-degree requirements. Here \(C_D\) is the ordinary degree-\(D\) PC consequence space. Growing width is not forbidden, but the current fixed-input-degree transfer theorem does not automatically cover it.

Extra requirements. We do not need efficient construction, a large surviving space, a single family uniform in depth, absence of all earlier degree falls, or closure of the first chosen sum of local ideals. For a proposed \(K_D\), vanishing of every defect map is a sufficient convenience: one may instead construct a larger, independently described proper space that contains the axioms and is closed under all legal multiplications. Calling the unknown PC consequence space that larger space is only a restatement. The accepted structural specification itself makes no hardness assertion.

Independent alternative. Before seeing the preferred route, the sole fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer proposed random local truth tables on \(k=C\log_2v\) coordinates with \(m=v+t\) constraints. It preferred analyzing their complete local consequence spaces and overlap-induced PC closure over imposing closure of the initial local sum. After receiving the current code and fixed-cutoff routes, it retained that preference, while explicitly identifying growing-width transfer and global closure as unresolved. Its staged brief and response summaries are retained with this entry. The concrete elementary part of the proposal is proved below; no generic-rank or near-half claim is adopted.

Rank normalization. A local restriction matrix has columns indexed by actual allowed assignments and rows by low-degree monomials. Its left kernel (equivalently, the kernel of the transposed evaluation map) is a local consequence space, not the global PC closure. Any subsequent dimension loss must be measured in the same quotient, after all relations already charged. A nonzero dual kernel is also insufficient unless it contains a functional nonzero on one. The mechanical control below isolates this distinction. Relative contraction may replace absolute loss bounds only after these domains and the distinguished unit agree.

Decision. Retain the non-vacuous general specification and relax the proof-method preference: an explicit enlargement of the initial local sum is allowed. Queue the random-relation/Reed–Muller mechanism as a serious alternative. For the next cycle, retain the current code head but change the question from improving its ceiling to proving or refuting a concrete non-vacuity construction in the surviving metric regime. The reason for this order is the available exact structural lemma and fixed-width source ledger; the random proposal adds a uniform growing-width ledger and has no global closure mechanism yet. This is a bounded comparison, not a claim that codes are more likely to succeed. Reassess from the construction's outcome, rather than adding restrictions by default.

Decisive test. On the existing code/binomial head, attempt to couple short maximum-radius components into an unbounded Boolean-empty minimal system while retaining \(r=v/2+o(v)\), \(\rho\ge v/2-o(v)\), essential scopes and the required encoding budget. Write the coupling and count its rank, radius loss and variables before any parameter sweep. The rank-half condition selects this candidate regime; the necessary screen alone only gives \(r\le v/2+o(v)\). Direct sums are the negative control: a contradiction in one component leaves a bounded minimal core. A construction passing these gates permits a direct invariant attempt; a proved obstruction to the coupling rejects that construction, not all near-extremal codes. Report the actual outcome under Architecture test in the next cycle. The random local-code alternative remains queued rather than being treated as established hardness.

An alternative with honest non-vacuity

Proposition (random relation encoding). Fix \(m\) scopes of size \(k\ge1\) in \(v\) Boolean variables. Independently for every scope occurrence and each of its \(2^k\) tuples, allow the tuple with probability \(1/2\). The canonical forbidden-tuple CNF has at most \(m2^k\) clauses of width \(k\), no auxiliary variables, and \[\Pr[\text{satisfiable}]\le2^{v-m},\qquad \Pr[\text{some scope coordinate is inessential}]\le mk\,2^{-2^{k-1}}.\tag{1}\] If the scopes cover every variable at least twice, then outside the second exceptional event all \(v\) variables are actual, essential and occur in at least two relation blocks.

Proof. A fixed global assignment is allowed by all \(m\) independently sampled tables with probability \(2^{-m}\), even when scopes repeat. Summing over the \(2^v\) assignments bounds the expected number of solutions, and Markov's inequality gives the first assertion. For a fixed coordinate in one table, inessentiality requires equality of the allowed/forbidden bits in each of \(2^{k-1}\) disjoint pairs. These independent equalities each have probability \(1/2\). Union bound gives the second assertion. Each forbidden tuple is encoded by its usual width-\(k\) clause; the clause forbids precisely that tuple. Essentiality then gives the actual-variable and block-occurrence claims. \(\square\)

For example, take \(k=\lceil2\log_2v\rceil\), \(m=v+\lceil2\log_2v\rceil\), and, for large \(v\), the \(v\) cyclic length-\(k\) scopes followed by any additional scopes. Every variable occurs at least twice. Both exceptional probabilities tend to zero, and the encoding length is \(O(m2^kk\log(v+1))\), polynomial in \(v\). Thus such unsatisfiable essential systems exist at unbounded actual variable counts. The cyclic choice is only an existence control and may be easy; it is not recommended as a hard ensemble. Block minimality, survival after normalization and a PC lower bound are unproved for this alternative. In particular this does not establish non-vacuity of a near-half-hard subclass or of the fixed-width specification.

Outside leads

  • Reed–Muller erasure capacity. Kudekar–Kumar–Mondelli–Pfister–Şaşoğlu–Urbanke, Theorem 27, gives block-MAP capacity for the stated constant-rate binary Reed–Muller sequence. For a random allowed set \(R\subseteq\{0,1\}^k\), the kernel of evaluation on \(R\) is exactly \(I(R)\cap B_{\le d}\). A nonzero kernel is an erased codeword when the complement of \(R\) is erased. At fixed rate below \(1/2\), the theorem therefore controls a single random-half local set. It supplies neither a simultaneous estimate over all growing scopes nor global PC closure. Answers. A filtered overlap theorem for these actual binary evaluation spaces could produce the missing invariant for near-half-pc-family without the modular-coset restriction. Test. Passed: the local kernel/erasure identification uses the same binary field and exact evaluation code. The four-point affine plane in three variables has a nonzero affine vanishing polynomial, whereas \(0,e_1,e_2,e_3\) has none; this verifies why actual ranks, rather than an arbitrary generic-matrix assumption, matter. Develop the global compatibility and width ledger; the source theorem itself is only local input.
  • Random vanishing spaces. Bhandari–Harsha–Saptharishi–Srinivasan, Theorem 1.1, proves full column rank for a uniformly chosen set of \((1-\epsilon)\binom{k}{\le d}\) distinct points when \(d<\gamma_0k\), for a small absolute constant. Its exact rank formulation could help a local-state construction, but it is not a theorem for half the cube at \(d\) near \(k/2\). Answers. A matching-range extension with uniform overlap control would answer the local-rank part of near-half-pc-family; the printed theorem does not answer the global step. Test. Falsified: direct use at the proposed local midpoint violates both the printed small-degree regime and its sample-size prescription. This is a scope rejection, not a refutation of random predicates. The distinct-point, low-degree formulation remains available if a later construction needs it.
  • Resampling witness trees. Moser–Tardos, Theorem 1.2, turns the local-lemma inequalities into a satisfying assignment and bounds resamplings for independent underlying variables. A degree-truncated analogue of its witness-tree bookkeeping might organize propagation without assuming independence of derived consequences. It cannot be applied globally to certify an unsatisfiable family. Answers. A bounded-region replacement controlling every PC multiplication, rather than only event resampling, could address the cancellation obstacle in near-half-pc-family. Test. Falsified: for two adjacent exactly half-forbidden blocks, the required two inequalities imply \(1/4\le x_1x_2(1-x_1)(1-x_2)\le1/16\). This rejects the direct half-density resampling transfer. It does not reject other densities or a genuinely different filtered witness construction.

Absurd bridges

  • Mechanical self-stress and boundary modes. Kane–Lubensky's Maxwell index relation, equation (1), separates zero modes from states of self-stress. Translate relation columns into a constraint matrix and a PC-annihilating functional into a left zero mode; the distinguished unit must have nonzero pairing with that mode. The rank-nullity part works over \(\mathbb F_2\), while the real energy interpretation need not. Answers. A protected, unit-pairing zero mode stable under all permitted multiplier columns would supply a dual invariant for near-half-pc-family. Test. Falsified: a dimension-only or energy-kernel translation. For \(Q=(1,1)^T\) over \(\mathbb F_2\), \(Q^TQ=0\) although \(\ker Q=0\). Separately, for relation column \(M=(1,0)^T\) and distinguished unit \(e_1\), the left kernel is nonzero but annihilates \(e_1\). Thus neither a Gram zero mode nor a positive nullity alone preserves one. No conclusion is drawn about a construction tracking the marked mode itself.
  • Quantum tensor-network rank. Hastings, Theorem 1, approaches the min-cut rank for a fixed network and ordering as complex bond dimension tends to infinity. The proposed translation would encode local Boolean state maps as tensors and protect a boundary image after gluing. It needs an exact binary rank theorem and a PC-compatible marked state, not complex singular-value moments. Answers. Such a theorem for the actual finite-field tensors could keep a nonzero boundary state while accounting for overlap losses in near-half-pc-family. Test. Falsified: direct moment or counting contraction transfer. Over \(\mathbb F_2\), \(I_2\) has rank two but \(\operatorname{tr}(I_2^TI_2)=\operatorname{tr}((I_2^TI_2)^2)=0\). Contracting two all-ones length-two vectors gives zero although both coordinates contribute one. These controls reject positive-moment and scalar-count shortcuts, not every tensor representation. The source's fixed-graph complex asymptotic is not a binary growing-graph theorem.

The passed local Reed–Muller translation is appended as a lead. The four failed direct translations are retained above at their precise scope and are not used to declare the open statement without a mechanism. The existing code head continues; the local-section item and its source alternatives keep their positions. No finite parameter series was run: the tests above are exact identities, theorem-hypothesis comparisons and the elementary probability calculation.

  • Attempt an explicit coupling of short maximum-radius code components and count its rank, radius loss, actual variables and essential minimal Boolean-empty core. This develops the existing near-extremal code non-vacuity question; direct sums are the negative control.
  • For the passed Reed–Muller lead, prove a uniform growing-width source ledger and a global multiplication-compatible construction for overlapping random relation ideals. Single-block erasure capacity and the first-moment unsatisfiability bound do not give either. Keep all original width and actual-variable costs.

8 October 2026 — A coupled maximum-radius witness still has short PC refutations

Working all-parameter proofs; code non-vacuity and exact-one obstruction. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. Resume the highest-risk near-half-pc-family obligation by performing the scheduled construction test. Can short maximum-radius components be coupled into an unbounded Boolean-empty minimal system while retaining rank and radius at half the actual variables? Compare this with repairing a direct invariant or switching to the queued random-relation source. The concrete construction test is selected because it settles the recorded non-vacuity gap without assuming a new growing-width transfer theorem.

New mathematics. Adding coupling directions to a paired repetition code lowers its radius according to the support of those directions in the quotient. Mixing the original rows instead preserves the code and yields an explicit unbounded, essential, block-minimal Boolean-empty family with rank and covering radius exactly half the variables. Its ordinary PC degree is nevertheless exactly six. A general parity-nullity argument explains the failure and excludes every bounded-arity paired exact-one system, not merely the cyclic example.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The narrower code regime now has an unbounded structural witness, but the witness has constant degree, and no near-half lower bound has been obtained. This resolves a specification non-vacuity question, not the goal's necessary hardness implication. The complete Frege transfer remains open.

General statement. prop:pair-supercode-radius applies to every linear extension of the paired repetition code. prop:paired-cycle-nonvacuity gives the all-size coupled witness. thm:grouped-exact-one-ceiling gives an ordinary binary PC upper bound from the nullity of a grouped exact-one incidence matrix.

Couple the presentation without changing the code

Proposition (pair supercode radius). Over any finite field \(\mathbb F_q\), let \[C_0=\{(z_1,z_1,\ldots,z_n,z_n):z\in\mathbb F_q^n\},\qquad C=C_0+U.\] Put \(\pi(a_1,b_1,\ldots,a_n,b_n)=(a_1-b_1,\ldots,a_n-b_n)\), \(H=\pi(U)\), and let \(a\) be the number of coordinates on which \(H\) is nonzero. Then \[\dim C=n+\dim H,\qquad \rho(C)=\rho(H)\le n-a/q.\tag{1}\] Here the radius on the right is ordinary Hamming covering radius in \(\mathbb F_q^n\).

Proof. The kernel of \(\pi\) is \(C_0\), and \(C=\pi^{-1}(H)\). To change one pair's difference to a specified value requires no coordinate change if it already agrees and exactly one change otherwise. Thus distance to \(C\) equals distance of the difference vector to \(H\); surjectivity of \(\pi\) proves equality of radii. For a uniformly chosen \(h\in H\), each active coordinate is uniform in \(\mathbb F_q\). The expected distance from any fixed vector is at most \((n-a)+(1-1/q)a\). Its minimum distance is at most that expectation. Rank-nullity gives the dimension formula. \(\square\)

Thus adding directions touching a positive fraction of pairs cannot keep radius \(n-o(n)\) at fixed \(q\). This is not an obstruction to mixing a generating basis while keeping its span fixed. Nor does code decomposition imply that the original constraint presentation has disconnected components. The construction below uses precisely that distinction.

A full structural witness

Proposition (paired cycle witness). For every \(n\ge4\) with \(3\nmid n\), take \(v=2n\) Boolean variables \(x_i,y_i\), cyclic indices modulo \(n\), and the \(n\) modular equations over \(\mathbb F_7\) \[x_i+y_i+x_{i+1}+y_{i+1}+x_{i+2}+y_{i+2}=1.\tag{2}\] Use the full forbidden-tuple CNF of each row and Booleanity. This is a field-consistent Boolean-empty system of rank \(n\), whose row code has covering radius \(n\). Its relation blocks are minimally unsatisfiable, have essential scopes of size six, and every variable occurs in exactly three blocks. There are \(58n\) canonical clauses and no auxiliary variables. Over every proof field its ordinary PC refutation degree is exactly six.

Field system and code. Write \(t_i=x_i+y_i\) over \(\mathbb F_7\), and let \(T\) be the cyclic matrix whose rows select three consecutive coordinates. For a homogeneous solution, subtract consecutive equations to get \(t_i=t_{i+3}\). Since \(\gcd(n,3)=1\), all coordinates agree, and \(3t_i=0\) implies they vanish. Thus \(T\) is invertible. For right side one, its unique solution is \(t_i=1/3=5\). It has field lifts, for example \(x_i=5,y_i=0\), but no Boolean lift because a Boolean pair sums to \(0,1,2\). The original matrix has duplicated columns and equals the invertible row transform \(T\) of the pair-sum matrix. Its row code is \(C_0\) above. Any vector can be matched on one coordinate of every pair, giving radius at most \(n\); the vector with every pair \((0,1)\) needs at least one change per pair, giving equality.

Minimality and exact encoding. On six Boolean variables the integer sum ranges from zero to six, so equation (2) means exactly one variable is one. It has six allowed tuples and \(64-6=58\) forbidden tuples. Each coordinate is essential, and each pair belongs to three distinct cyclic rows. To delete row \(n\), whose groups are \(n,1,2\), proceed as follows. If \(n=3a+1\), set \(t_i=1\) exactly when \(i\equiv0\pmod3\). If \(n=3a+2\), set \(t_i=1\) exactly when \(i\equiv2\pmod3\). Each remaining triple sums to one, including row \(n-1\), which wraps around to coordinate one. The deleted triple sums to zero in the first case and two in the second. Lift by \(x_i=t_i,y_i=0\). Cyclic rotation supplies a satisfying assignment after deletion of any row, proving block minimality. This does not assert clause minimality of the expanded CNF. Encoding length is \(O(n\log n)\), and the branching count is \(h=v\).

Ordinary PC degree, with original costs. For a multilinear polynomial \(f\) on a row's six variables that vanishes on its six allowed tuples, the literal interpolation identity \[f=\sum_{a\text{ forbidden}}f(a)\prod_{j:a_j=1}z_j\prod_{j:a_j=0}(1-z_j)\tag{3}\] derives \(f\) by scalar combinations through degree six. It holds over every field. In particular derive \(P_i=s_i+s_{i+1}+s_{i+2}-1\), where now \(s_i=x_i+y_i\) is in the proof field, and all products of two distinct variables in a row. Their sums give \(s_i s_{i+1}=0\) through the same ceiling. Consecutive \(P_i\)'s give \(s_i-s_{i+3}=0\), hence all \(s_i=s_1\), by linear combinations only. In characteristic three, \(P_1\) then yields the nonzero constant \(-1\). Otherwise put \(c=1/3\); derive \(L_i=s_i-c\). With \(R=s_1s_2\), \[R-s_2L_1-cL_2=c^2.\tag{4}\] Multiplication by \(s_2\) is the sum of two permitted variable multiplications, with degree two. Reuse the already derived degree-one and degree-two lines; do not multiply their degree-six derivations. This proves the upper bound six. Below six none of the non-Boolean input polynomials is available, since every canonical forbidden indicator has degree six. Booleanity alone is satisfiable, so no smaller-degree refutation exists. \(\square\)

The general exact-one mechanism

Theorem (grouped exact-one ceiling). Partition the actual Boolean variables into nonempty disjoint groups \(G_1,\ldots,G_n\). Each constraint requires exactly one variable in a union of whole groups to be one, and has at most \(w\ge2\) variables. Let \(H\) be the binary constraint-by-group incidence matrix, \(r=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb F_2}H\), and \(b=n-r\). If the system is unsatisfiable, its canonical CNF plus Booleanity has ordinary PC degree over \(\mathbb F_2\) at most \[\max\{w,b+2\}.\tag{5}\] If every group is a pair and every constraint contains at most fixed \(k\ge1\) groups, with every group occurring, then \[d_{\mathbb F_2}\le\max\{2k,n-\lceil n/k\rceil+2\}.\tag{6}\] For fixed \(k\), this is at most \((1/2-1/(2k))v+O_k(1)\), where \(v=2n\), and rules out a near-half family in this entire paired exact-one class.

Proof. Put \(s_i=\sum_{x\in G_i}x\). Interpolation as in (3), on each constraint's own scope, derives its linear equation \(\sum_{i\in S}s_i-1\) and every within-scope product \(xy\) of distinct variables through degree at most \(w\). For any two different groups in a common constraint, derive \(s_i s_j=0\) by summing these quadratic products. Also \[s_i^2-s_i=\sum_{x\in G_i}(x^2-x)\tag{7}\] in characteristic two. If \(Ha=\mathbf1\) is inconsistent, a scalar combination of the derived linear equations already gives one. Otherwise choose a free-coordinate set \(F\) of size \(b\) for its affine solution space. Every \(\alpha\in\mathbb F_2^F\) extends uniquely to a solution \(a\).

For this \(\alpha\), set \[W_\alpha=\prod_{j\in F}(s_j+1-\alpha_j),\qquad \sum_\alpha W_\alpha=1.\tag{8}\] The partition identity is literal. From (7), multiply by the other \(b-1\) linear factors to derive \(W_\alpha(s_j-\alpha_j)=0\) through degree \(b+1\) for each free coordinate. When \(b=0\) this step is empty and \(W=1\). Multiply each previously derived linear constraint by the \(b\) factors of \(W_\alpha\); final-line reuse costs only \(\max\{w,b+1\}\). Gaussian elimination with the fixed free values then gives \[W_\alpha(s_i-a_i)=0\quad\text{for every }i\tag{9}\] through this ceiling. These are scalar combinations of weighted equations, not an uncharged substitution.

The binary vector \(a\) cannot have exactly one one in every row of \(H\): if it did, choosing one representative variable in every group with \(a_i=1\), and zero elsewhere, would satisfy the original system. Since each row has odd sum modulo two, some row therefore contains at least three groups with \(a_i=1\). Choose two of them, \(i,j\). Multiplying the derived local quadratic \(s_is_j=0\) by \(W_\alpha\) costs \(\max\{w,b+2\}\). Multiplying (9) for \(i\) by the linear form \(s_j\), and combining with (9) for \(j\), derives \(W_\alpha=0\) through the same ceiling. Summing (8) gives one. Multiplication by every linear form used here is expanded as variable multiplications and scalar combinations, so the proof is ordinary PC. Finally choose \(r\) original rows forming a row basis of \(H\). Their supports cover every nonzero column; otherwise that column vanishes on every row. Each selected row contains at most \(k\) groups, so \(n\le kr\). Substitute \(b\le n-\lceil n/k\rceil\) and \(w\le2k\) into (5). \(\square\)

This is an application of the familiar common-feature indicator partition, not a claim that conditioning itself is new. The historical common-feature lemma lifts ENS designs and is not the present PC theorem. Here the specific incidence nullity, local exact-one quadratics and reuse of low-degree final lines yield the maximum ceiling (5), rather than multiplying every old proof by a weight.

Architecture test and learning

Architecture test. The scheduled coupling test passes structural non-vacuity: the row-mixed paired cycles have unbounded minimal cores, essential scopes, all variables branching, fixed modulus and width, polynomial encoding, rank \(v/2\) and radius \(v/2\). It fails as a hardness construction, by the exact degree-six proof. The broader theorem shows that replacing the cyclic incidence pattern by another bounded-arity paired exact-one hypergraph cannot repair this failure, even if binary nullity is large. A code metric can remain extremal while low-degree consequences in the proof field are decisive.

Specification decision. The combined rank-half code conditions are now known nonempty; their earlier provisional status due solely to missing non-vacuity is resolved. They remain a candidate subregime, not a hardness assertion and not a replacement for the broader specification's positive-linear-degree witness. The new witness has no positive-linear degree. Do not add an arbitrary new filter just to exclude it: a predicate change must evade the actual parity-and-collision argument while supplying a new unbounded witness. General non-exact-one modular rows and the queued random relations are not covered by (5).

Checks and next implication

The single bounded GAP checker and complete output verify all 64 local Boolean tuples, the 58 forbidden tuples and sixteen integral interpolation identities; all 49 pair-code quotient inputs; and 9,801 ternary words across \(n=4,5,6,7,8\). The two smallest sizes test both residue classes; seven and eight exceed the constant input-degree threshold; six is the necessary divisible-size negative control, with three satisfying group assignments. Every nondivisible case has full modular rank and a witness after each deleted row. All 128 three-group incidence families are screened: 109 cover all groups, 65 are unsatisfiable, 61 have inconsistent parity, and four have collision witnesses. A separate nullity-two control verifies four branch weights and eight weighted Boolean identities; omitting a weight fails the partition. These check the construction and proof identities, not an empirical asymptotic lower bound.

The first checker launch had a reserved-name syntax error; a later polynomial comparison used an integer equality rather than GAP's polynomial identity predicate. Both were corrected. A subsequent control expansion tested nonzero nullity because the three-group unsatisfiable consistent cases had nullity zero. No failed script output is treated as mathematical evidence. No larger run is needed: the all-parameter proof answers this construction test.

Follow-up. Continuing the code/binomial head. The useful next question is whether non-exact-one local rows in a paired-column presentation can have a large enough parity-compatible consequence space without the collision conclusion used in (5). Compare such a direct invariant attempt with the queued random-relation mechanism; preserve the actual-variable and field ledgers. The completed non-vacuity questions leave the queue below.

  • Follow-up. Closed: superseded by the proved all-parameter construction; prop:paired-cycle-nonvacuity gives an unbounded witness for the stated essential, block-minimal, branching, Boolean-empty near-redundancy-radius conditions; hardness remains separate.
  • Follow-up. Closed: superseded by the proved all-parameter construction; prop:paired-cycle-nonvacuity completes the explicit coupling and parameter test; thm:grouped-exact-one-ceiling identifies the resulting hardness failure.

  • Test a non-exact-one bounded-width modular predicate on paired columns, with an explicit unbounded Boolean-empty minimal witness. Compute its full binary affine and quadratic local consequence spaces symbolically before seeking a multiplication-compatible invariant; the exact-one collision argument must genuinely fail, and a high covering radius alone does not count as hardness.
