8 October 2026 — Two local weights remove affine equations but overlaps recover carry bits

Working all-parameter proofs; two-weight modular predicates. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, test a predicate change suggested by the paired exact-one obstruction. A six-variable row modulo five with target zero or one admits two weights of opposite parity, so the previous rowwise parity equation disappears. Does this give a useful local state space for a direct invariant, and can an unbounded minimal witness retain the structural requirements?

Route comparison. A direct local-space test is more informative here than imposing another code-radius filter: the new rows have exactly computable affine and quadratic consequence spaces. The queued random-relation alternative has no established global closure theorem and still needs a growing-width ledger. We therefore test the present fixed-width local mechanism and one explicit witness, stopping the finite checks once their predicted identities are decided.

New mathematics. The two-weight relation has no nonzero binary affine consequence and is affinely equivalent to an at-most-one relation. A graph of overlapping rows nevertheless gives affine carry equalities. Conditioning on its component carries and free group parities yields an ordinary PC bound \(\max\{10,n-r_2+c+2\}\). An explicit unbounded minimal modular chain realizes the new predicate, but loses rank-half and positive branching density and has degree at most ten.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The local affine obstruction is genuinely avoided, but the coupled witness still has a short refutation and fails the accepted branching requirement. No near-half invariant or lower bound is proved. The broader structural specification and its existing witnesses remain; this attempted replacement is not adopted.

General statement. lem:two-weight-local-ideal applies to every odd integer modulus in the stated one-row range. thm:paired-carry-degree-ceiling applies to every unsatisfiable system of the six-variable paired rows below. prop:modular-chain-core supplies all sizes of the tested minimal chain.

The complete local consequence space

Lemma (two weight normal form). Let \(q\ge3\) be odd, \(N=q+1\), \(b\in\{0,1\}\), and let \(R_b\subseteq\{0,1\}^N\) consist of assignments whose integer weight is congruent to \(b\pmod q\). In the binary Boolean algebra put \[S=\sum_{i=1}^N x_i,\qquad u_i=S+x_i+b.\tag{1}\] This is an affine involution, sending \(R_b\) onto \(\{0,e_1,\ldots,e_N\}\). The vanishing ideal of \(R_b\) is generated by the \(u_i u_j\), \(i<j\), in that Boolean algebra. It contains no nonzero affine polynomial, and its degree-at-most-two part has dimension \(\binom N2\), with the multilinear reductions of these products as a basis. Each such ordinary quadratic consequence is derivable from the canonical row clauses and Booleanity through degree \(N\).

Proof. Since \(N\) is even, \(\sum_i u_i=S\), so applying (1) twice gives \(x_i\). For \(b=0\), the allowed weights are zero and \(q\); zero maps to zero, and the all-ones vector missing coordinate \(i\) maps to \(e_i\). For \(b=1\), the allowed weights are one and \(N\); the all-ones vector maps to zero and \(e_i\) maps to itself. On the target set, \(1,u_1,\ldots,u_N\) are independent functions. In its Boolean algebra, reducing every product \(u_i u_j\), \(i\ne j\), to zero leaves precisely their span. Thus these products generate the ideal; the degree-two kernel has the displayed basis and dimension. Affine substitution and Boolean reduction preserve the degree filtration in both directions. For an ordinary quadratic representative, multilinear reduction costs at most two, and its multilinear form is a scalar sum of the original forbidden indicators through degree \(N\). \(\square\)

This is a concrete affine instance of the historical column-exclusive normal form, whose statement and proof were reread. Its local properness is not a global PC invariant: different rows use different affine coordinates.

Carry agreement is a joint consequence

Now fix \(q=5\). Partition the \(v=2n\) actual variables into pairs. Every row is the union of three distinct pairs, has all coefficients one, and has right side \(b_a\in\{0,1\}\). Use its full forbidden-tuple CNF and Booleanity. The binary pair-incidence matrix is \(H\), with rank \(r_2\). This rank is not the modular rank over \(\mathbb F_5\).

On an allowed row, its integer weight is \(b_a+5\eta_a\) for a bit \(\eta_a\). In the proof field this bit is represented by the affine form \[h_a=\sum_{x\in V_a}x+b_a.\tag{2}\] Join two rows in a graph if they have the same right side and share at least one pair, or different right sides and share at least two pairs. Let \(c\) be its number of components, including isolated rows.

Carry equality and its degree. Set \(d=6-|V_a\cap V_{a'}|\). For a joint allowed assignment, the difference of the two integer weights has absolute value at most \(d\). Unequal carries would make that difference have absolute value at least \(5-|b_a-b_{a'}|\), which is greater than \(d\) on every declared edge. Hence \(h_a+h_{a'}=0\) on the joint allowed set. It has an ordinary PC derivation through the union size, at most ten. To see the cost directly, interpolate the affine polynomial on all assignments to the union. Each assignment with a nonzero coefficient violates an original row; assign it to one such row. Its union indicator is that row's forbidden indicator times the indicators of the remaining coordinates. Derive those products through the union size and add. Along a spanning forest, scalar combinations derive equality with one representative form \(h_C\) per component through degree ten. When a forest uses only two-pair overlaps, the same step costs eight instead. The different-right-side one-pair overlap is deliberately excluded: the checker gives joint satisfying assignments with unequal carries there.

A bound for all carry components

Theorem (paired carry ceiling). Every unsatisfiable system in this setting has ordinary binary PC degree at most \[\max\{10,n-r_2+c+2\}.\tag{3}\] In particular, if it has no refutation through \(D=\lfloor n-s\rfloor\ge10\), then \[r_2-c\le\lceil s\rceil+1.\tag{4}\] Thus near-half hardness requires \(r_2\le c+o(n)\), not that these two quantities be asymptotically equal. When every group occurs and every row meets three groups, \(r_2\ge\lceil n/3\rceil\); a positive linear excess of this rank over the number of carry components excludes the target.

Proof: condition with original Boolean polynomials. Put \(s_i=x_i+y_i\), choose \(n-r_2\) free coordinates for the kernel of \(H\), and put \(d=c+n-r_2\). For every choice \(\epsilon\) of the \(c\) component bits and choice \(\alpha\) of those free group parities, form the product \(W\) of their \(d\) affine bit indicators. All these products sum literally to one. The forms need not be independent. Every binary affine form \(l\) satisfies \(l^2-l=\sum_{x\text{ occurring in }l}(x^2-x)\). Multiplying this degree-two identity by the other \(d-1\) factors derives \(W(l-\text{chosen value})=0\) through degree \(d+1\). Reusing the degree-one carry equalities already derived through ten gives \[W\bigl(Hs-(b+\epsilon)\bigr)=0\tag{5}\] through \(\max\{10,d+1\}\), where each row uses its component's bit. If the corresponding affine binary system is inconsistent, scalar elimination gives \(W=0\). Otherwise the chosen free coordinates determine a unique binary solution \(a\), and the same elimination gives \(W(s_i-a_i)=0\) for all groups.

Conditional local collisions. In row \(a\), fix \(\epsilon_C\) and put \(z_x=x+\epsilon_C\). On the allowed assignments with this carry, the \(z\)'s have integer weight either zero or one: it is \(b_a\) for carry zero and \(1-b_a\) for carry one. Thus, for distinct row variables \(x,y\), \[(h_a+1-\epsilon_C)z_xz_y=0\tag{6}\] has a derivation through degree six: reduce its degree-three polynomial modulo Booleanity, then interpolate on that row. Replace \(h_a\) by \(h_C\) using their derived affine difference multiplied by \(z_xz_y\), at ceiling ten. Multiply by the remaining \(d-1\) factors of \(W\). This derives \(Wz_xz_y=0\) through \(\max\{10,d+2\}\). Crucially the old degree-ten derivation is not multiplied wholesale. Since a pair has even size, its sum of \(z\)'s equals \(s_i\). Two groups with \(a_i=a_j=1\) in a row therefore give \(W=0\), exactly by combining the weighted parity equations with the weighted collision product.

Cross-component consistency. Suppose no row has two such groups. If \(a_i=0\), choose a row containing its pair. Its within-pair collision is \(Wz_xz_y=0\), and \(W(z_x+z_y)=0\). Multiply the latter by \(z_x\) and use the former and the weighted Boolean identity for \(z_x\) to derive \(Wz_x=0\); likewise for \(z_y\). All costs are at most \(\max\{10,d+2\}\). Thus the pair is weighted-forced to \((\epsilon_C,\epsilon_C)\). If two incident components have different bits, subtract these forced values to get \(W=0\).

If neither a row collision nor such a component conflict occurs, construct an actual satisfying assignment: every group with \(a_i=1\) gets \((1,0)\); every group with \(a_i=0\) gets the common pair \((\epsilon_C,\epsilon_C)\) of its incident components. In a row, after complementing by its carry, a parity-one group contributes exactly one one and a parity-zero group contributes none. There is at most one contributing group, and (5) makes that number \(b_a+\epsilon_C\) modulo two, hence exactly the required zero or one. Undoing the complement gives weight \(b_a+5\epsilon_C\), a contradiction to unsatisfiability. Every branch therefore derives \(W=0\); their sum is one, proving (3). Every multiplication by an affine form is expanded into permitted variable multiplications and scalar combinations. The integer inequality (4) follows from (3) and \(D=n-\lceil s\rceil\). For the rank bound, the supports of an original row basis cover every nonzero column of \(H\), so \(n\le3r_2\). \(\square\)

The argument extends the weighted free-parity mechanism of thm:grouped-exact-one-ceiling, but does not apply it without modification: rowwise affine equations are absent, carry bits can differ between components, and parity-zero pairs shared across components need the explicit consistency step. This is why the common quotient and all coupling costs matter.

An unbounded minimal witness, with its lost properties

Proposition (modular chain witness). For each \(a\ge1\), take pair-groups \(A_0,\ldots,A_a\), \(B_0,\ldots,B_a\), \(C_0,\ldots,C_{a-1}\). There are \(n=3a+2\) groups and \(v=6a+4\) Boolean variables. In the following equations each capital letter denotes the sum of its two variables over \(\mathbb F_5\): \[P_j:A_j+B_j+C_j=0,\quad Q_j:B_j+C_j+A_{j+1}=0\quad(0\le j<a),\] \[P_a:A_a+B_a+A_0=0,\qquad Q_a:B_a+A_0+B_0=1.\tag{7}\] These \(m=2a+2\) rows are field-consistent, Boolean-empty and block-minimal, with essential scopes of width six and \(57m\) canonical clauses. Every variable occurs twice except the four variables in \(A_0,B_0\), which occur three times. Both modular and binary group-incidence ranks are \(m\). Their row-code radius is between \(n\) and \(2n-m\). Ordinary binary PC degree lies between six and ten.

Unsatisfiability. All zero-target rows form a chain in the carry graph: \(P_j,Q_j\) share two groups and \(Q_j,P_{j+1}\) share one. The last one-target row shares two groups with \(P_a\) and \(P_0\), so there is one carry. If that carry is zero, all zero-target rows have weight zero; \(P_a\) and \(P_0\) force all variables in \(Q_a\) to zero, contradicting its required weight one. If the carry is one, complement every Boolean variable and write \(u_G\) for the sum in group \(G\). Row \(Q_a\) has weight zero and all other rows weight one. Thus \(u_{A_0}=u_{B_0}=u_{B_a}=0\). Subtracting the integer equations \(P_j,Q_j\) gives \(u_{A_j}=u_{A_{j+1}}\), so \(u_{A_a}=0\), contrary to \(P_a\).

Deletion witnesses. Removing \(Q_a\) permits the all-zero original assignment. For every other deletion use carry one, specify \(u_G\in\{0,1\}\), and realize it by the original pair \((1-u_G,1)\). All \(u_{B_i}=0\). If \(P_a\) is removed, set all \(u_{A_i}=0\) and all \(u_{C_i}=1\). If \(P_j\), \(j<a\), is removed, set \(u_{A_i}=0\) for \(i\le j\), one for \(i>j\), and \(u_{C_i}=1\) for \(i<j\), zero otherwise. If \(Q_j\), \(j<a\), is removed, use the same \(A\)'s and set \(u_{C_i}=1\) for \(i\le j\), zero otherwise. Direct substitution satisfies every remaining zero/one-weight row. The deleted \(P_j\) has transformed weight zero, the deleted \(Q_j\) weight two, and the deleted \(P_a\) weight zero. This proves minimality.

Rank, radius and encoding. In the order \(A_0,B_0,C_0,A_1,B_1,C_1,\ldots,A_a,B_a\), these are the cyclic consecutive-triple rows whose one-based starting index is not divisible by three. The full \(n\)-row cyclic matrix is invertible over \(\mathbb F_2\) and \(\mathbb F_5\): subtracting adjacent homogeneous equations makes coordinates three-periodic, hence constant because \(n\equiv2\pmod3\), and then three times the constant is zero. Its selected rows are independent, proving both ranks and field consistency. Duplicating its columns puts the row code inside the paired repetition code, so a vector having pair \((0,1)\) everywhere has distance at least \(n\). The redundancy bound gives radius at most \(2n-m\). Each row has seven allowed assignments and 57 forbidden ones; every coordinate is essential. Counting incidences gives the stated two exceptional groups. Encoding length is \(O(a\log(a+1))\).

Constant PC bound. Derive carry agreement through ten as above, and condition on its one affine bit with weights \(W_0=1+h_C\), \(W_1=h_C\). In a row whose conditional transformed weight is zero, each transformed variable is zero: derive its conditional linear consequence by degree-six interpolation, replace the carry form through ten, and reuse the final line. For \(W_0\), rows \(P_0,P_a\) thus force all variables in \(Q_a\) to zero; its weighted parity equation gives \(W_0=0\). For \(W_1\), row \(Q_a\) forces the three transformed group sums to zero. Weighted differences of \(P_j,Q_j\) propagate \(W_1u_{A_j}=0\) down the chain, and \(P_a\) gives \(W_1=0\). These later lines have degree at most two; all costs remain at most ten. Sum the weights. The lower bound six follows because all non-Boolean input polynomials have degree six. \(\square\)

This witness is not a replacement for the accepted code subregime. Its rank ratio is \(m/v\to1/3\), not \(1/2\), and its branching density is \(h/v=4/(6a+4)\to0\), violating the existing positive-density requirement. It does prove unbounded minimality and absence of a local affine equation for the changed predicate, so those properties alone are not a repair. No lost property is hidden by recounting or deleting auxiliary variables.

Controls, decision and remaining implication

The GAP checker and full output test 672 one-row Boolean inputs for \(q=3,5,7\), with exact affine ranks \(5,7,9\) and quadratic-basis ranks \(6,15,28\). All 5,376 two-row inputs at overlaps two, four and six test the carry criterion; opposite right sides at overlap two admit unequal carries, rejecting an overbroad graph definition. Of 81 four-group configurations, 72 cover all groups and six are unsatisfiable; all 352 compatible parity branches satisfy the collision-or-lift classification. A separate two-component, parity-zero control has no compatible lift. Finally, 6,804 ternary pair-sum inputs at chain sizes \(a=1,2\) verify unsatisfiability, every deletion witness, both ranks and the occurrence ledger. No PC minimum is inferred from these finite controls.

Decision. The local-space calculation passes its intended test: the exact-one affine equation is absent. The direct invariant attempt nevertheless encounters a new joint relation, carry agreement, and the explicit unbounded witness loses required branching. Keep the broader specification unchanged. Before another structural filter, test a possible representation change suggested by the local normal form: retain only the pair parities and express the compatibility needed to reconstruct the original pair values. Prove the encoding equivalence and all ordinary-PC costs before using it. If this works, compare the independent-set quotient and conditioning on its complement; otherwise retain the rank-versus-component question. This tests whether the present pair representation itself is unnecessary, rather than increasing the chain size or modulus. The queued local-section and random-relation alternatives remain available.

Follow-up. Continuing the code/binomial head. This is its third entry in the current spell. The new all-parameter bound identifies a surviving rank-versus-component question, but does not earn a spell extension by itself because the goal implication is unchanged.

Sub-ideas

  • Test a pair-parity-only encoding of these two-weight rows, including compatibility for a parity-zero pair shared between different rows. Establish equisatisfiability and a paid proof map before comparing an independent-set quotient with conditioning on its complement.
  • For six-variable paired modulo-five rows with right sides zero or one, test whether a non-vacuous essential minimal family can simultaneously retain positive branching and binary rank at most the number of carry components plus the allowed deficit. A rank count must use the same row space and account for dependencies across components; do not assume the components' dimensions add.

Verification gate. The sole fresh-context native GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed with two scope clarifications, now incorporated: full group coverage is explicit for the rank corollary, and the cyclic row indexing is one-based. The main degree bound does not require full coverage. It checked dependent weights, conditional costs, component conflicts, satisfying lifts, all deletion witnesses and unchanged goal progress. The complete paired exact-one proof and the historical column-exclusive normal form were reread; current dependency links and relevant claim searches were checked. Searches covered affine at-most-one forms, two weights, modular carries, component conditioning and chain cores. The finite checks reached their success marker on the first run. The local normal form and indicator partition are identified as uses of established techniques; no independent novelty is claimed.

Process assessment. The test analyzed the actual local ideal before proposing an invariant, then exposed the joint carry relations and the replacement's lost rank and branching rather than counting a new nonempty family as near-half progress.

8 October 2026 — Pair parities expose a quarter-variable ceiling

Working all-parameter proofs; parity compression and graph bound. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, carry out the recorded representation test before refining the carry-component restriction. Is one bit in each pair only an orientation choice that can be reconstructed after solving explicit compatibility equations? A valid proof map would let a different representation decide whether the whole two-weight class can support the desired barrier.

Route comparison. The local normal form and the previous satisfying-lift argument suggest this precise map. Testing it is preferable to another rank-versus-component estimate, which may reflect the chosen proof rather than intrinsic hardness. The queued local-section method remains the next mechanism after this code item's spell; neither a new source search nor larger chain samples are needed for the present implication.

New mathematics. Pair parities give an equivalent quadratic system with half as many variables and paid PC transfers in both directions. A graph independent-set quotient and conditioning on its complement give complementary upper bounds for that quadratic system. Every unsatisfiable paired two-weight formula of maximum width \(W\) therefore has binary PC degree at most \(\max\{W,\lfloor v/4\rfloor+2\}\). The result covers all incidences and all carry-component counts in this predicate class.

Goal progress. Unchanged. This excludes the whole tested paired two-weight class, not the general near-half-family statement. No suitable lower-bound family or Frege transfer is obtained. The code regime's structural witness remains valid but easy; the accepted general specification retains its separate positive-linear witness.

General statement. prop:paired-parity-compression gives the semantic and proof maps. thm:guarded-graph-degree-ceiling is an all-graph binary PC theorem. cor:paired-two-weight-exclusion is their all-instance composition with the original variable ledger.

An equivalent system on pair parities

Partition the actual Boolean variables into \(n\) pairs \((x_i,y_i)\). A row \(a\) selects a set \(S_a\) of \(k_a\ge2\) distinct pairs, with \(N_a=2k_a\) original variables and a label \(b_a\in\{0,1\}\). Its allowed integer weights are \(\{0,N_a-1\}\) when \(b_a=0\), and \(\{1,N_a\}\) when \(b_a=1\). Use its complete forbidden-tuple CNF and Booleanity. Put \(W=\max_a N_a\), \(K=W/2\); every group occurs. These are class conventions: \(k_a\ge2\) selects odd moduli at least three, while full coverage enforces the actual-variable ledger; the proof can also handle one-pair endpoint rows or unused pairs with arbitrary reconstructed orientation. The previous modulo-five class has \(k_a=3,W=6\). More generally an odd modulus \(q\) with row width \(q+1\) and targets zero or one has exactly this relation. The argument also allows different even row widths.

Introduce \(n\) Boolean variables \(z_i\), and define \[h_a(z)=b_a+\sum_{i\in S_a}z_i.\] The quadratic system \(\Psi\) has Booleanity, the products \(z_i z_j=0\) whenever two different groups occur in a common row, and \[(1+z_i)(h_a(z)+h_{a'}(z))=0 \quad\text{whenever }i\in S_a\cap S_{a'}.\tag{1}\] The coefficient of \(z_i\) in \(h_a+h_{a'}\) cancels. There are at most \(O(mK^2+m^2K)\) such equations for \(m\) rows, each with polynomially many written terms. Thus the new polynomial encoding has polynomial size. No auxiliary variables are added to the original formula.

Proposition (paired parity compression). The original formula \(\Gamma\) and \(\Psi\) are equisatisfiable. Over \(\mathbb F_2\), a degree-\(D\) refutation of \(\Psi\) gives a refutation of \(\Gamma\) through \(\max\{W,D\}\). Conversely, a degree-\(D\) refutation of \(\Gamma\) gives one of \(\Psi\) through \[\max\{2D,C_W\},\qquad C_W=\max\{4,2W,K^2+2\}.\tag{2}\] These are degree transfers, not polynomial proof-size simulations. The new input is a quadratic polynomial system; no free conversion of its equations into CNF is presumed.

Projection and reconstruction. Set \(z_i=x_i+y_i\) in the binary field. In any allowed row there is at most one odd pair. If a pair is even, its two bits both equal the row carry \(h_a(z)\). These statements hold in all four cases: weights zero, one, \(N_a-1\), and \(N_a\). They imply the edge products and (1). Conversely, fix a Boolean solution \(z\) of \(\Psi\), and choose one incident row \(a(i)\) for every group. Set \[x_i=(1+z_i)h_{a(i)}(z),\qquad y_i=x_i+z_i.\tag{3}\] If \(z_i=0\), equation (1) makes all incident carries agree, so both bits equal that carry. If \(z_i=1\), the pair is \((0,1)\). Every row has either no odd pair or one odd pair. With no odd pair, its carry is \(b_a\) and its weight is \(N_ab_a\), hence zero or \(N_a\). With one odd pair, its carry is \(1+b_a\), and its weight is \(1+(N_a-2)(1-b_a)\), hence \(N_a-1\) or one. These are precisely its allowed weights.

Refutations of the parity system lift without a degree factor. Substitute \(z_i\mapsto x_i+y_i=:s_i\). New Booleanity is the sum of the two old Boolean equations. Each edge product \(s_i s_j\) vanishes on its original row, hence follows by multilinear interpolation from that row's forbidden indicators through degree \(W\). For a row containing pair \(i\), the ordinary quadratic \[(1+s_i)(x_i+h_a(s))\tag{4}\] also vanishes on its allowed Boolean assignments: for an even pair its two terms agree, and for an odd pair its first factor is zero. Multilinear reduction costs two; interpolation then derives (4) through \(W\). Adding (4) for rows \(a,a'\) derives the substituted equation (1), literally. Replay a new-system proof using these final lines. A multiplication by \(z_i\) becomes the sum of multiplications by \(x_i\) and \(y_i\), so it increases degree by at most one. The resulting ceiling is \(\max\{W,D\}\).

The reverse map pays its quadratic cost. Use the polynomial substitution (3), of degree at most two. Its Boolean images follow through four from old Booleanity in \(z\): for Boolean affine forms \(A,B\), \[(AB)^2+AB=B^2(A^2+A)+A(B^2+B),\] and the Boolean relation for a sum is the sum of the relations in characteristic two. For an original row, the image of a canonical forbidden indicator has degree at most \(2W\) and depends on \[T_a=\bigcup_{i\in S_a}S_{a(i)},\qquad |T_a|\le k_aK\le K^2.\] This set includes \(S_a\). The target edge equations within \(S_a\), together with (1) comparing \(a\) to \(a(i)\), involve only \(T_a\) and imply that the reconstructed row is allowed. Hence its indicator image vanishes on the Boolean solutions of those local quadratic equations.

For completeness, a polynomial \(f\) of degree \(B\) on \(t\) Boolean variables that vanishes on the common zeros of binary quadratics \(g_j\) has a derivation from those quadratics and Booleanity through \(\max\{B,t+2\}\). First multilinearize \(f\) through \(B\). For each point \(\xi\) where its value is one, choose a violated \(g_j\); then \(\delta_\xi g_j\) reduces to \(\delta_\xi\) modulo Booleanity through \(t+2\). The point indicators sum with their values to the multilinear polynomial, giving the claimed derivation. Apply this with \(B\le2W,t\le K^2\). All substituted axioms follow through \(C_W\). Finally, the ordinary quadratic-substitution simulation maps a degree-\(D\) proof to degree \(2D\): a predecessor of degree at most \(D-1\), multiplied by a quadratic image, has degree at most \(2D\). Reuse the derived axiom images to obtain (2). This is the costed direction of the historical substitution lemma; it is not a degree-preserving equivalence. \(\square\)

Complementary graph bounds

Theorem (guarded graph ceiling). Let \(G\) be a graph on \(n\ge1\) Boolean variables. Over \(\mathbb F_2\), consider an unsatisfiable system consisting of Booleanity, every edge product \(z_i z_j\), arbitrary affine equations, and any equations \[(1+z_i)L(z_{N_G(i)})=0,\tag{5}\] where \(L\) is affine and supported on neighbors of \(i\). With \(\alpha=\alpha(G)\), its ordinary PC degree is at most \[\min\{\alpha+2,n-\alpha+2\}\le\lfloor n/2\rfloor+2.\tag{6}\] This is a graph independent set, containing at most one endpoint of each edge, not the different hypergraph notion used in the triple-colouring record.

Small independent sets: the whole quotient is low-degree. Modulo Booleanity and edge products, every monomial reduces without increasing degree to either zero or an independent-set monomial. These monomials form a basis of functions on the independent sets: evaluation at their indicator vectors is triangular under inclusion, with diagonal entries one. Thus every function on this finite domain has a representative of degree at most \(\alpha\). The remaining affine and quadratic equations have no common zero there. At each point choose one violated equation, and partition the point among those choices. Their point indicators, weighted by the reciprocals of the chosen nonzero values, give coefficients \(H_f\) of degree at most \(\alpha\) such that \(\sum_f H_f f=1\) in the quotient. The difference from one has ordinary degree at most \(\alpha+2\); Boolean and edge reductions give a certificate in the base ideal through that degree. Derive each product \(H_f f\) through the same ceiling and subtract this certificate. This proves the first bound. It works over any field for arbitrary remaining equations of degree at most two; only the complementary affine argument below needs the binary setting.

Large independent sets: condition on the complement. Choose a maximum independent set \(I\) and put \(C=V(G)\setminus I\), of size \(n-\alpha\). Under any Boolean assignment \(\sigma\) to \(C\), every edge product becomes affine. In (5), if \(i\in C\) the guard is constant and the rest affine; if \(i\in I\), every neighbor is assigned, so \(L\) is constant and the guard affine. All surviving non-Boolean equations are therefore affine over \(\mathbb F_2\). Their system is inconsistent: a binary solution would also satisfy Booleanity and extend to a solution of the original system. Gaussian elimination expresses one as a scalar combination of these restricted equations.

Let \(P_\sigma\) be the usual product of the \(|C|\) assignment indicators. For each original equation \(f\) of degree at most two, derive \(P_\sigma f\), then replace assigned variables by their values through degree \(|C|+2\). The replacements have explicit Boolean certificates: \(P_\sigma(z_j-\sigma_j)\) is, up to sign, \(z_j^2-z_j\) times the other \(|C|-1\) factors. Telescope each monomial of \(f\); its remaining factor has degree at most one. Thus \(P_\sigma f|_\sigma\) is derived with the stated ordinary cost. The affine scalar combination gives \(P_\sigma\), and the literal sum of all assignment weights is one. This proves the second bound and (6). The earlier independent-set restriction proof uses the same weighted principle, but a width-two residual class instead of this binary affine class. \(\square\)

The original variable ledger

Corollary (paired quarter ceiling). Every unsatisfiable paired two-weight formula above, with \(v=2n\) actual Boolean variables and maximum row width \(W\), has ordinary binary PC degree at most \[\boxed{\max\{W,\lfloor v/4\rfloor+2\}}.\tag{7}\] In particular the entire paired six-variable modulo-five class with targets zero or one is excluded from a near-half family, regardless of rank, minimality, branching density or carry-component count.

Proof. Make \(G\) the graph on groups, joining every pair of groups that occurs in an original row. Its edge products are exactly those included in \(\Psi\). In (1), the guard variable \(z_i\) cancels from \(h_a+h_{a'}\); every other variable there is a neighbor of \(i\). Hence \(\Psi\) meets (5), is unsatisfiable by reconstruction, and has degree at most \(\lfloor n/2\rfloor+2\). The forward proof map gives (7). Every original bit, including each reconstructible orientation bit, remains counted in \(v\); the factor of two is justified by the explicit degree-one projection and paid local consequences. \(\square\)

The bound still excludes this predicate class when \(W=o(v)\); in particular growing an odd modulus to logarithmic size with row width \(q+1\) does not repair it. It does not apply to arbitrary modular coefficients, arbitrary right sides, rows not built from whole equal-coefficient pairs, or general code cosets. The broader rank-half code specification is not refuted. Nor is there a polynomial-size bound on the refutations constructed by the two graph arguments.

Checks and what the representation taught

The bounded exact checker and complete output compare both solution sets and reconstruct every compressed solution in 85 systems: all 81 four-group configurations and four two-row systems sharing only one pair. These cover 24,832 original assignments and 1,424 parity assignments, including the case where carries can differ. The reverse-map local support test checks 8,192 assignments and labels on nine groups; 896 satisfy its local compatibility equations, and omitting a guard gives an explicit failed reconstruction. These are encoding controls, not an empirical degree theorem.

All 64 graphs on four vertices have full-rank independent-set evaluation bases, totaling 545 basis rows. There are 891 literal ordinary restriction identities, of which 648 have nonzero Boolean correction terms; omission of a needed Boolean term fails. Twelve forward local polynomial certificates and six Boolean corrections verify the original-width axiom ledger, and the quadratic Boolean substitution identity checks the reverse direction. The smaller encoding cases have an original-width floor that can dominate (7), so they are not offered as numerical evidence for its asymptotic coefficient. No PC-minimum search or larger sample series was needed.

Decision. Close the paired two-weight near-half candidate: the obstruction is not lack of local affine immunity, but a paid projection to a guarded graph system with half as many variables. The rank-versus-carry-component condition is therefore no longer a useful hardness gate for this class. Any replacement must avoid the complete reconstruction and proof map, not merely alter its carry graph. The broader code alternatives remain queued; neither unequal coefficients nor a different alphabet is asserted hard.

Follow-up. Continuing the broader code/binomial item, but this is the fourth entry in its current spell. There is no newly advanced near-half goal implication, so do not extend the spell. Rotate it to the tail with its unresolved alternatives. The next head is the existing local-section lead; use its explicit filtered relation-transport problem rather than starting another paired-modular screen.

  • Follow-up. Closed: superseded by prop:paired-parity-compression, which proves the complete encoding and both paid proof maps.
  • Follow-up. Closed: superseded by cor:paired-two-weight-exclusion; the whole paired two-weight class has a quarter-variable ceiling, so its rank-versus-component screen cannot recover near-half hardness.

Sub-ideas

  • Use the existing fibre-decomposition proof to test cancellation of separator interpolation costs in the fixed-cutoff common quotient, against the majority and free-padding obstructions. This refines the queued separator question; an additive-cost decomposition alone does not settle multiplication closure.

8 October 2026 — A one-bit separator has an explicit defect module

Working all-parameter proofs; filtered local-section mechanism. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, develop the now-head local-section lead. The required implication is multiplication closure of an explicit proper invariant near half the actual variables. The existing fibre decomposition permits an extra separator degree; it does not prove closure at the original cutoff. Test exactly when that extra degree disappears, and how the remaining falls behave under multiplication.

Route comparison. A new formula family is not forced by the last scoped code exclusion. The one-bit overlap already contains the majority obstruction, so a general formula there tests the local-section method directly. We use the existing decomposition and degreewise-gluing results rather than claim another interpolation bound. A successful local calculation is still not a global unsatisfiable-family construction.

New mathematics. For two ideals with one shared Boolean coordinate, four fibre leading ideals give a canonical graded quotient describing every degree fall and its private-variable multiplication maps. The separator itself has zero defect action. For the opposite-majority pair this quotient is a free spectator module with an explicit Hilbert function, yielding the exact cutoff at which the separate local sum becomes closed. A source-based control also shows why compatibility of local section coefficients alone need not survive a proposed conditioning operation.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The formula computes the obstruction of a specified two-member construction; it does not remove that obstruction on an unbounded unsatisfiable family. The majority calibration is satisfiable, and its free spectators are not a hardness witness. No new structural restriction is adopted without a witness.

General statement. thm:one-bit-fibre-defect applies over every field to arbitrary two Boolean local ideals with disjoint private coordinates. cor:majority-fall-module computes the resulting module for all majority arities and spectator counts. ex:hardy-selector-failure is a scoped algebraic counterexample to the specified componentwise selector, not a no-go theorem for cohomology.

Antecedents and the source boundary

The degreewise-gluing lemma already identifies the quotient of joint low-degree vanishing polynomials by the separate local sum as the obstruction to filtered gluing. The fibre-decomposition proof supplies the one-degree Boolean separator bound used below. The conditional coupling-distributivity record also distinguishes vector-space counts from the relevant operator action under its different base and pullback hypotheses. The new calculation gives an explicit fibre-leading presentation and actions for our Boolean overlap; it is not a new general theory of gluing.

The full Abramsky–Mansfield–Barbosa source was read. Its printed Propositions 4.2–4.3 concern anchored compatible linear combinations and contextuality, not polynomial degree or PC multiplication. The HTML conversion renumbers them 4.3 and 4.5. The control below reuses its Hardy family; our defect module is not identified with the paper's cohomology group.

The earlier design-presheaf translation, read during the next-task audit, already identifies gluing of designs with locality of PC closure in its stated weak-base setting. It explicitly leaves transfer of its raw-ideal counterexample to PC closures unchecked. The present fibre formula computes a particular local obstruction and its actions; it does not supply the missing global locality theorem.

Four fibre ideals in one graded algebra

Let \(F\) be any field. Let \(A,B,C\) be disjoint finite sets of Boolean coordinates, where \(C\) consists of free spectators, and let \(z\) be another coordinate. Put \[R=F[A,B,C]/(x^2-x),\qquad \mathcal B=R[z]/(z^2-z),\] with their ordinary multilinear degree filtrations. Take an ideal on \(\{z\}\cup A\) and one on \(\{z\}\cup B\), and extend them to ideals \(I,J\subseteq\mathcal B\). Their fibres at \(z=\epsilon\) extend to ideals \(U_\epsilon,V_\epsilon\subseteq R\), respectively. In particular \(U_\epsilon\) is generated on \(A\), and \(V_\epsilon\) on \(B\). Empty or full allowed fibres are permitted.

Let \(T=\operatorname{gr}R\cong F[t_x:x\in A\cup B\cup C]/(t_x^2)\). Write \(\mathfrak a_\epsilon=\operatorname{gr}U_\epsilon\), \(\mathfrak b_\epsilon=\operatorname{gr}V_\epsilon\), as homogeneous ideals in this same algebra. Define \[\mathcal M= \frac{(\mathfrak a_0+\mathfrak b_0)\cap(\mathfrak a_1+\mathfrak b_1)} {(\mathfrak a_0\cap\mathfrak a_1)+(\mathfrak b_0\cap\mathfrak b_1)}.\tag{1}\] The denominator is contained in the numerator and both are ideals, so this is a graded \(T\)-module. Also put \[L=I+J,\qquad K_d=(I\cap\mathcal B_{\le d})+(J\cap\mathcal B_{\le d}),\qquad H_{d+1}=\frac{K_{d+1}\cap\mathcal B_{\le d}}{K_d}.\tag{2}\] Closure at cutoff \(D\) means \(x(K_D\cap\mathcal B_{\le D-1})\subseteq K_D\) for every variable. All intersections and sums in (1) are taken inside \(T\); no ranks from different quotients are subtracted.

Theorem (separator defect module). For every \(d\ge0\), taking the degree-\(d\) leading part of the fibre at \(z=0\) induces canonical isomorphisms \[\frac{L\cap\mathcal B_{\le d}}{K_d}\ \cong\ H_{d+1}\ \cong\ \mathcal M_d.\tag{3}\] At cutoff \(d+1\), the fixed-cutoff defect map for a private or spectator coordinate \(x\) corresponds to \[t_x:\mathcal M_d\longrightarrow\mathcal M_{d+1}.\tag{4}\] Its target is the subspace \((L\cap\mathcal B_{\le d+1})/K_{d+1}\) of the full quotient \(\mathcal B_{\le d+1}/K_{d+1}\), not that entire quotient. The defect map for \(z\) is zero. Consequently \(K_{d+1}\) is closed under variable multiplication of predecessors of degree at most \(d\), at cutoff \(d+1\) exactly when every map (4) vanishes. Unit survival must still be checked separately.

Strict splitting within a fixed fibre. Because \(U_\epsilon\) and \(V_\epsilon\) are generated on disjoint private sets, every element of \((U_\epsilon+V_\epsilon)\cap R_{\le e}\) splits into pieces of degree at most \(e\) in those ideals. One direct proof chooses filtered bases of the two private Boolean algebras adapted to their respective ideals, completing the ideal basis at each filtration level. Tensor them, with the spectator monomial basis. The resulting tensor basis is adapted to total degree, and the sum ideal is spanned by the basis tensors having at least one ideal factor. Separating those tensors proves the assertion, including zero or unit ideals. In particular \[\operatorname{gr}(U_\epsilon+V_\epsilon)=\mathfrak a_\epsilon+\mathfrak b_\epsilon.\tag{5}\] This is the disjoint-fibre step of the existing decomposition proof.

The obstruction to spending no separator degree. Write \(f\in\mathcal B\) uniquely as \(f_0+z(f_1-f_0)\). It has degree at most \(d\) exactly when both fibres have degree at most \(d\) and their degree-\(d\) leading parts agree. An element of \(L\) has \(f_\epsilon\in U_\epsilon+V_\epsilon\). By (5), its common leading part belongs to the numerator of (1). Every element of that numerator occurs: choose two fibre polynomials with that common leading part and interpolate them with \(1-z,z\).

The leading part of the zero fibre of an element of \(I\cap\mathcal B_{\le d}\) lies in \((\mathfrak a_0)_d\cap(\mathfrak a_1)_d\), and every such element is obtained by choosing its two lifts separately and interpolating; their equal leading parts keep the degree at \(d\). The same holds for \(J\). Hence \(K_d\) maps onto the denominator of (1).

It remains to check the kernel, not just count dimensions. Suppose the common leading part of \(f\) is \(a+b\), with \(a\) in the first intersection of the denominator and \(b\) in the second. Choose \(g_\epsilon\in U_\epsilon\), \(h_\epsilon\in V_\epsilon\) of degree at most \(d\) lifting \(a,b\). Then \(f_\epsilon-g_\epsilon-h_\epsilon\) has degree at most \(d-1\) and belongs to \(U_\epsilon+V_\epsilon\). Split it through that degree using the preceding paragraph. Adding those lower-degree pieces to \(g_\epsilon,h_\epsilon\) gives separate fibre pairs with common leading parts \(a,b\). Their interpolants belong to \(I\cap\mathcal B_{\le d}\) and \(J\cap\mathcal B_{\le d}\) and sum to \(f\). For \(d=0\), the lower-degree remainders are zero. This proves the first quotient identification.

The one-step fall and its action. Independently split each fibre of an element of \(L\cap\mathcal B_{\le d}\) through degree \(d\). Multiplying its zero-fibre pieces by \(1-z\) and its one-fibre pieces by \(z\) puts it in \(K_{d+1}\). Thus \(K_{d+1}\cap\mathcal B_{\le d}=L\cap\mathcal B_{\le d}\), proving (3). For \(x\ne z\), the top private part of \((xf)|_{z=0}\) in degree \(d+1\) is exactly \(t_x\) times the degree-\(d\) top of \(f_0\); repeated variables vanish at the associated-graded level. This yields (4) in the target subspace specified above. For \(zf\), the zero fibre is zero, so its class in that target vanishes. Finally, \(xK_d\subseteq K_{d+1}\) for every variable. Therefore only the classes in \(H_{d+1}\) can obstruct multiplication at the cutoff, as in prop:fixed-cutoff-cover-transport, giving the closure criterion. \(\square\)

The complete majority module

Corollary (majority fall module). Consider the opposite-majority pair from the earlier obstruction, with \(|A|=|B|=2k\), \(k\ge1\), shared bit \(z\), and \(c\ge0\) spectators. Over every field, its module (1) has one basis vector for every pair of \(k\)-subsets of \(A,B\), tensored with the square-zero spectator algebra, shifted to degree \(2k\). The \(A,B\) coordinates annihilate this module. In particular \[\operatorname{Hilb}(\mathcal M;t)=\binom{2k}{k}^{\!2}t^{2k}(1+t)^c.\tag{6}\] At cutoff \(D\ge2k+1\), put \(j=D-1-2k\). Then \[\dim H_D=\binom{2k}{k}^{\!2}\binom cj.\tag{7}\] For \(c\ge1\), each spectator defect map has rank \(\binom{2k}{k}^{2}\binom{c-1}{j}\), taking binomial coefficients outside their usual range as zero. All private and separator defect maps are zero. The complete separate local sum is multiplication-closed at such a cutoff exactly when \[D\ge2k+c+1.\tag{8}\] It preserves one at every cutoff because the pair is satisfiable.

Proof. Complement the \(A\) variables and leave \(z,B,C\) unchanged. The first allowed fibre is the Hamming ball of radius \(k-1\) at \(z=0\), and radius \(k\) at \(z=1\). Its leading ideals are generated by the \(A\)-monomials of degrees \(k\) and \(k+1\), respectively. For the second member, the \(B\)-fibre ideals have thresholds \(k+1\) and \(k\). The Hamming-ball monomial description follows from the inclusion-evaluation basis, as proved in the earlier majority record; no numerical interpolation hypothesis is added.

All four leading ideals are monomial ideals. In the numerator of (1), a monomial must either have at least \(k+1\) variables in either private block, or have at least \(k\) variables from each private block. The denominator removes exactly the monomials with more than \(k\) variables in either block. The remaining basis monomials use exactly \(k\) coordinates from each block, and any spectator subset. This proves the module description and (6)–(7). A private multiplication either repeats a variable or raises its block count to \(k+1\), so it acts as zero. A spectator multiplies precisely the subsets not already containing it, giving the rank formula. For \(0\le j<c\) some such action is nonzero; for \(j\ge c\) all actions vanish. This proves (8), including \(c=0\). The original pair has the joint model \(z=0,A\equiv1,B\equiv0\), with arbitrary spectators, proving unit survival. \(\square\)

The ordinary input-width cost is unchanged: local monotone consequences follow by summing original forbidden indicators through \(2k+1\), and final-line multiplication derives every local ideal element through the maximum of that width and its own degree. Thus the module refines prop:majority-local-span-gap rather than treating Boolean reduction as free PC. With no spectators, the fall in degree \(2k\) is nonzero but all its next actions vanish; with spectators, every nonzero fall below the top spectator layer can be moved outside the local sum by some spectator. This proves the full pattern, not only the earlier single witness.

This is a local, satisfiable calibration. At fixed width and increasing free-variable count, (8) is far above the desired near-half cutoff. Taking \(k\) proportional to the variable count would instead make the canonical truth-table width and encoding grow beyond the fixed-width source setting. Neither choice is an unsatisfiable-family non-vacuity argument.

A compatibility control from the source

Control (Hardy selector failure). On contexts \((a,b),(a,b'),(a',b),(a',b')\), take the source's coefficient vectors, in tuple order \(00,01,10,11\), \[r_1=(1,0,0,0),\quad r_2=(0,1,1,-1),\quad r_3=r_4=(0,0,1,0).\tag{9}\] They have matching overlap marginals over every field. Apply the selector \(1-a\) to the components that contain \(a\), leaving the other two components unchanged. Then \(r_2\) becomes \((0,1,0,0)\): its \(b'\)-marginal is \((0,1)\), whereas that of \(r_4\) is \((1,0)\). Hence this specified selector does not preserve compatibility, even though all component masses remain one. This does not exclude operators that update other components, or a different filtered construction. The source's compatible family is not itself a PC invariant.

Checks and remaining global obligation

The exact checker and complete final output test 1,080 systems, 3,870 cutoff identifications and 14,270 multiplier ranks over \(\mathbb F_2,\mathbb F_3\), including 416 nonzero fall quotients. The census exhausts all 256 pairs of two-variable relations with zero or one spectator. Additional two-private-bit relations test nonmonomial leading ideals; the majority controls with \(k=1,c=0,1,2\) verify the full profiles \(4\), \(4,4\), and \(4,8,4\), including the zero-action top layer. These predictions were derived before the run. Each ideal is echelonized once in descending-degree columns, reusing that basis at every cutoff; no degree-truncated Gröbner surrogate is used. The largest control algebra has dimension 128.

The Hardy marginal and selector identities pass over \(\mathbb Q,\mathbb F_2,\mathbb F_3\); their displayed algebraic argument covers every field. The initial census already passed, but lacked the opposite-majority and full spectator-module checks, so those were added in a second bounded run. Both full outputs are retained. No larger computation is needed to prove the all-parameter monomial description.

Decision. Use the module action, not the fall-space dimension alone, when testing this local construction. The one-bit fibre cost disappears at degree \(d\) exactly when \(\mathcal M_d=0\), whereas fixed-cutoff multiplication only asks that the next actions vanish. These are different requirements. For a full unsatisfiable family, additional members may absorb the pair's images, while genuinely multi-member relations may create new ones. The two-member formula proves neither global generation nor preservation of one. The old degreewise-gluing counterexamples already forbid assuming pairwise checks settle the whole cover.

Follow-up. Continuing the local-section lead. The one-bit separator calculation and majority test are complete. Next test whether a specified collection of joint members can absorb these explicit degree-one images in the full common quotient while keeping one out; compare that construction with the known multi-member gluing obstructions before claiming a general rule. A larger local matrix or another additive separator bound would leave the necessary implication untouched.

Sub-ideas

  • For a concrete bounded joint-member cover of an unsatisfiable candidate, test absorption of the one-bit separator module's degree-one images in the full quotient, including relations involving more than two members and separate unit survival. Do not infer global closure from pairwise module actions.

Verification gate. The sole fresh-context native GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed with two wording corrections, now incorporated: the cutoff closure statement names the predecessor degree, and the majority threshold says at least \(k+1\). It found no unused mathematical hypothesis and checked the exact kernel, target subquotient, all multiplier actions, ordinary-width scope and unchanged goal assessment. The fixed-cutoff and majority proofs, fibre decomposition, degreewise gluing and conditional coupling-distributivity records were reread with their scopes and relationship metadata. The cohomology paper was read in full, and its printed theorem numbering checked against the PDF. Searches included fibre leading terms, distributivity defects, Mayer–Vietoris, spectator modules and the dual compatibility formulation. No primary-source PC-hardness theorem, Lean verification or independent novelty is claimed.

Process assessment. The existing interpolation and gluing results were reused. The calculation now determines both the obstruction and its operator action, but is recorded as a method result with unchanged goal progress rather than another claimed route to near-half hardness.

8 October 2026 — Even all proper joint members can miss a cutoff multiplication

Working all-parameter proof; satisfiable method calibration. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, the missing implication is a proper multiplication-closed invariant for an unsatisfiable family at the target cutoff. The local-section lead proposed absorbing the separator defects by adding jointly constrained members. Test the general claim that sufficiently many proper joint members automatically absorb the missing actions, using actual PC closures as well as full vanishing ideals.

Route comparison. A direct new family would still need a surviving invariant. The last calculation gives a two-member module, but does not justify replacing its target by a sum of joint members. A guarded chain tests precisely that repair with unbounded member count and constant width. It can falsify automatic closure; it cannot justify a restriction of the accepted specification, since its private coordinates and satisfiability place it outside that specification.

New mathematics. Over every field, a width-three guarded chain gives a sum over every proper joint member which preserves one but fails multiplication closure at a cutoff equal to half the declared ambient coordinate count. The failure holds even for the sum of genuine degree-cutoff PC closures: an explicit polynomial belongs to that sum, its next variable multiple does not even belong to the larger sum of truncated vanishing ideals, but the global PC closure contains the multiple.

Goal progress. Unchanged. This refutes a family-independent repair of the local invariant, not a near-half family and not every possible cover or invariant. The chain is satisfiable. It supplies no contradiction at one and does not transfer to the odd-prime weak PHP base.

General statement. thm:guarded-chain-cover-gap covers every chain length, positive private block size and stated spectator budget over every field. The case of private block size one and three spectators has fixed width and a half-ambient-dimension cutoff.

Setting and statement

Let \(F\) be any field, and let \(t,k,c,D\) be integers with \(t\ge2,k\ge1,c\ge0\). Take disjoint private sets \(A_1,\ldots,A_t\), each of size \(k\), separators \(z_1,\ldots,z_{t-1}\), and \(c\) spectators. Write \(a_i=\prod_{x\in A_i}x\), \(P=\prod_{i=1}^t a_i\), and work in the Boolean function algebra \(\mathcal B\), filtered by multilinear degree. The original clause polynomials are

\[g_1=a_1(1-z_1),\qquad g_i=a_i z_{i-1}(1-z_i)\ (2\le iThese are canonical forbidden-assignment indicators on their scopes, hence a literal CNF with \(t\) clauses, widths at most \(k+2\), and \(kt+t-1+c\) declared Boolean coordinates, including the free spectators. For \(t=2\), both widths are \(k+1\). For \(S\subseteq[t]\), let \(J_S\) be the full Boolean vanishing ideal of the clauses indexed by \(S\), extended to all variables. Let \(V_D(S)\) be the images in \(\mathcal B\) of polynomials derivable in ordinary PC through original degree \(D\) from those clauses and all Boolean axioms. Thus \(V_D(S)\subseteq J_S\cap\mathcal B_{\le D}\). Put

\[K_D=\sum_{S\subsetneq[t]}(J_S\cap\mathcal B_{\le D}),\qquad E_D=\sum_{S\subsetneq[t]}V_D(S).\tag{2}\]

Theorem (guarded chain cover). If \(D\ge kt+1\) and \(c\ge D-kt\), select \(D-kt\) distinct spectators, write \(W\) for the product of the first \(D-kt-1\), and \(w\) for the last. Then

\[ WP\in E_D\cap\mathcal B_{\le D-1}\subseteq K_D,\qquad wWP\notin K_D,\qquad wWP\in V_D([t]).\tag{3}\]

Moreover \(1\notin K_D\). Consequently neither \(E_D\) nor \(K_D\) is closed under legal multiplication at cutoff \(D\), although every proper subfamily is included. Setting \(k=1,c=3,D=t+1\) gives \(v=2t+2\), \(D=v/2\), constant width at most three and \(t\) clauses. This is a satisfiable control, not a PC lower-bound instance.

Proof with original degrees

A simultaneous disabling assignment. For each proper \(S\), choose \(j\notin S\). Set \(z_i=1\) for \(i

A separating coefficient. Consider the coefficient of the monomial \(wWP\), which has degree exactly \(D\) and contains no separator. In any polynomial \(f\) of multilinear degree at most \(D\), no monomial containing a separator can contribute to that coefficient after specializing the separators: it would have to contain all \(D\) coordinates of \(wWP\) and at least one more coordinate. Hence this coefficient is unchanged by any separator specialization. The preceding disabling assignment shows that it is zero for every \(f\in J_S\cap\mathcal B_{\le D}\). It therefore vanishes on \(K_D\), but takes value one on \(wWP\). This proves the middle assertion in (3), over every field. The same argument gives \(P\notin K_{kt}\).

Prefix derivations. Put \(P_l=a_1\cdots a_l\) and \(F_l=P_l(1-z_l)\) for \(1\le l \[F_l=a_l(1-z_l)F_{l-1}+P_{l-1}g_l.\tag{4}\]

The two summands cancel their terms containing \(z_{l-1}\). Each right-hand product is derived by successive variable multiplications and linear combinations, interpreting multiplication by \(1-z_l\) as subtraction from the predecessor. The maximum ordinary degree is \(kl+2\); the previously derived prefix uses no more. Consequently the entire prefix \(F_{t-1}\) is derived from \(g_1,\ldots,g_{t-1}\) within degree \(kt+1\): for \(t=2\) its input degree is \(k+1\), and for \(t\ge3\), \(k(t-1)+2\le kt+1\). Finally,

\[P=a_tF_{t-1}+P_{t-1}g_t.\tag{5}\]

Both summands have degree \(kt+1\), but their sum has degree \(kt\). The first belongs to \(V_{kt+1}([t-1])\), by multiplying the prefix final line by the \(k\) variables of \(A_t\). The second is a monomial multiple of the last clause and belongs to \(V_{kt+1}(\{t\})\). Both subfamilies are proper. Multiplying the two derivations by the \(D-kt-1\) spectator variables of \(W\) yields \(WP\in E_D\), with every original degree at most \(D\). No multiplication of a degree-\(D\) predecessor is used.

Global reuse after the fall. Equation (5) is a global degree-\(kt+1\) derivation of the literal polynomial \(P\). Reuse this final line and multiply by the \(D-kt\) variables in \(wW\). The maximum of the preceding proof degree \(kt+1\) and the resulting degrees is \(D\). Thus \(wWP\in V_D([t])\), proving (3). All these displayed polynomial identities hold before Boolean reduction; the coefficient separation takes place in the unique multilinear image.

Unit survival. Set all private variables to zero. Since \(k\ge1\), this satisfies every clause, with arbitrary separators and spectators. Evaluation at this common model annihilates each \(J_S\) and therefore \(K_D\), and sends one to one. This proves the last assertion. \(\square\)

Scope and prior obstructions

The covering obstruction already refutes a raw-ideal locality claim on its ternary form space. The design-presheaf result identifies the remaining locality requirement for actual PC closures, explicitly leaving an obstruction for those closures and at one unchecked. Here there is a literal PC-closure gap on the free Boolean domain, but it is at \(wWP\), not at one; both global and local normalized evaluations exist. This is no correction or extension to the weak-base theorem's instances.

The proper-row PHP obstruction already separates compatible prescribed local moments from global realizability. It varies moment domains and their prescribed values; the present statement instead fixes one ambient polynomial space and sums the closures of proper axiom subfamilies. Neither statement is inferred from the other. The sub-fill conjecture has randomized-prefix and fill-point hypotheses not supplied here; the chain does not refute it.

Learning from the failed repair. Adding every proper joint member can still miss a relation requiring all original blocks. Even replacing each member by its genuine PC closure does not make their sum closed. A successful cover needs a family-specific generation theorem for these higher-order actions, or a different invariant that can absorb them while keeping one out. There is no new upper bound on an admissible candidate and no adopted restriction. In particular, the half-ambient-dimension number here is achieved with private coordinates and free spectators that the accepted minimal essential specification excludes; the spectators are not variables occurring in the non-Boolean clauses and disappear under actual-variable normalization.

Follow-up. Continuing the local-section lead. This completes the guarded-chain absorption test named by the previous entry. The next necessary step is constructive: test a common-domain signed functional for the compact four-colour control, using first-bit fibres and their residual two-colour equations. This tests whether the local-section approach can handle fibres that are individually inconsistent while retaining a proper truncated global space. The control does not replace the abstract specification or assert its non-vacuity.

Sub-ideas

  • For the local-section invariant, formulate the compact four-colour control over the binary field as first-bit fibres with residual two-colour equations. Seek an explicit signed functional in one common ambient space and audit all ordinary-degree costs; do not replace a truncated fibre by its full vanishing ideal, which is already the unit ideal when that fibre is inconsistent.

Checks. The exact GAP checker and full output verify eight parameter/field controls, 40 proper-subfamily truncated ideals and 12 displayed derivation identities. The cases are chain lengths two and three, private block sizes one and two, and one or two spectators, over the binary and ternary fields; the largest algebra has dimension 128. Every tested cutoff has the predicted separation, so each test can falsify the claim. The previous descending-degree ideal basis is reused. The first launcher invocation omitted the GAP interpreter and failed before computation; the explicit interpreter run passed. No larger case is needed for the coefficient proof.

Verification gate. The sole fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed with presentation corrections: restore the inline MathJax delimiters and explicitly declare the integer domains. Both are corrected. It checked the all-parameter coefficient separator, every original-degree step, unit survival and unchanged goal assessment; no unused mathematical hypothesis was found. Boolean axioms belong to the declared system but the displayed derivations do not use them. The fixed-cutoff interface, separator module, covering obstruction and design-presheaf proofs were read in full; the PHP proper-row obstruction and sub-fill statement were checked for scope. Searches covered guarded chains, proper subfamilies, coefficient separators and local closure; no novelty claim is made.

Process assessment. The fourth unchanged cycle warning is carried to the scheduled architecture review. This cycle answers the recorded joint-member test rather than declaring the small local calculation a hardness result; another generic cover obstruction would not supply the missing invariant.

8 October 2026 — Four-colour fibres require cancellation between inconsistent linear systems

Working proofs; binary colouring interface and method obstruction. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, attempt the recorded constructive local-section test on compact four-colouring. The required implication is an explicit proper multiplication-closed space at a near-half cutoff. Full first-bit fibres have simple residual equations; test whether normalized signed functionals on those fibres can be combined into the required global functional, keeping original PC degrees.

Route comparison. The guarded chain invalidates automatic joint-member absorption, so another general local-cover rule would leave the main implication untouched. A direct common-domain construction on the unexcluded four-state relation can expose the actual compatibility cost. The graph is a control for the abstract method, not a replacement of the evolving specification.

New mathematics. In characteristic two, the compact four-colour clauses are degree-four equivalent to one quadratic per edge. Fixing the first bit yields linear two-colour systems. On every non-four-colourable graph these fibres have zero-mass annihilators, so independently annihilating each complete fibre cannot yield a normalized global functional. Explicit interpolation gives degree at most \(\max\{4,N+1\}\) on \(2N\) original bits.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The ceiling is \(v/2+1\), above the required lower-bound cutoff, so it does not exclude compact four-colouring. The independent-fibre method fails, but no construction retaining cancellation between fibres is proved. No near-half family, source transfer or new structural specification is claimed.

General statement. lem:four-colour-quadratics covers every finite simple graph in characteristic two; thm:four-colour-fibre-ceiling covers every non-four-colourable one. prop:four-colour-fibre-mass excludes precisely the independent complete-fibre annihilator ansatz.

Four states and quadratic equations

Let \(F\) have characteristic two, and let \(G\) be a finite simple graph with \(N\) vertices. The original variables are \(u_i,v_i\), with all Boolean axioms. Put \(l_0(x)=1+x\), \(l_1(x)=x\). For every edge \(ij\) and every colour \((a,b)\in\{0,1\}^2\), the canonical width-four clause polynomial is

\[C_{ij}^{ab}=l_a(u_i)l_b(v_i)l_a(u_j)l_b(v_j).\]

On a graph without isolated vertices all \(2N\) variables occur, with \(4|E|\) clauses. The graph instances relevant to an unsatisfiable core have no isolated vertices. Define

\[E^u_{ij}=1+u_i+u_j,\qquad E^v_{ij}=1+v_i+v_j,\qquad q_{ij}=E^u_{ij}E^v_{ij}.\]

Lemma (four-colour quadratics). Ordinary degree-\(D\) PC refutations of the canonical clauses plus Booleanity and of the quadratics \(q_{ij}\) plus Booleanity transfer in both directions through degree \(\max\{D,4\}\), over every field of characteristic two.

Proof. Since \((1+x)(1+y)+xy=1+x+y\) literally, \(\sum_{a,b}C_{ij}^{ab}=q_{ij}\) before Boolean reduction. Thus every quadratic is derived through degree four from the canonical clauses. In the other direction, write \(B_x=x^2+x\), \(A_u=l_a(u_i)l_a(u_j)\), \(A_v=l_b(v_i)l_b(v_j)\), and \(\chi_i=l_a(u_i)l_b(v_i)\). The literal identities

\[l_a(u_i)E^u_{ij}=A_u+B_{u_i},\qquad l_b(v_i)E^v_{ij}=A_v+B_{v_i}\]

give

\[\chi_i q_{ij}=C_{ij}^{ab}+B_{u_i}A_v+B_{v_i}A_u+B_{u_i}B_{v_i}.\tag{1}\]

Every term on the right other than the clause is a Boolean consequence through original degree four. The left is obtained from the quadratic through four. Derive each target axiom once and replay the source proof on its unchanged variables, reusing the derived final lines. This gives a maximum, not an additive loss. \(\square\)

Complete fibres and interpolation

Fix a root vertex \(r\). Introduce \(b_i\) for \(i\ne r\), put \(b_r=0\), and retain the \(v_i\). Replace the quadratics by

\[\widehat q_{ij}=(1+b_i+b_j)(1+v_i+v_j),\tag{2}\]

with Boolean axioms for the new variables. Under the affine substitution \(b_i\mapsto u_i+u_r\), each (2) is exactly \(q_{ij}\), and each new Boolean axiom becomes \(B_{u_i}+B_{u_r}\). Hence any ordinary degree-\(D\) refutation of (2) transfers through \(\max\{D,2\}\) to the old quadratic system. The root normalization uses no assumption on a colouring or division by the number of colours.

Theorem (four-colour fibre ceiling). If \(G\) is not four-colourable, its compact CNF has an ordinary PC refutation in characteristic two through degree \(\max\{4,N+1\}\). The statement is a degree upper bound, with no polynomial proof-size assertion.

Proof. Set \(m=N-1\). For \(\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{V\setminus\{r\}}\), extend \(\alpha_r=0\). The fibre of (2) contains the linear equations \(1+v_i+v_j=0\) exactly on edges with \(\alpha_i=\alpha_j\); other edge equations vanish. If both induced graphs on the two parts were bipartite, their bipartition bits together with \(\alpha\) would give a four-colouring. Thus one part contains an odd cycle \(C\). Summing its residual equations gives one, because each vertex bit occurs twice and the cycle has odd length. No Boolean reduction is needed for this fibre refutation.

To charge the interpolation, put \(\delta_\alpha=\prod_{i\ne r}l_{\alpha_i}(b_i)\), of degree \(m\). For a same-part edge,

\[\delta_\alpha(1+b_i+b_j)+\delta_\alpha =\delta_\alpha(b_i+\alpha_i)+\delta_\alpha(b_j+\alpha_j).\tag{3}\]

The root term, if present, is zero. For any other endpoint \(i\), \(\delta_\alpha(b_i+\alpha_i)=B_{b_i}\prod_{h\ne r,i}l_{\alpha_h}(b_h)\), a Boolean consequence through degree \(m+1\). Multiply (3) by \(1+v_i+v_j\); its corrections follow through \(m+2\). Multiplying the original quadratic (2) by \(\delta_\alpha\) also costs at most \(m+2\). Their sum derives \(\delta_\alpha(1+v_i+v_j)\) within that same ceiling. Sum these derived lines over the odd cycle to obtain \(\delta_\alpha\). Finally \(\sum_\alpha\delta_\alpha=1\) literally. The normalized quadratic proof has degree at most \(m+2=N+1\). The affine root substitution and the preceding lemma give the claimed original-CNF ceiling. \(\square\)

Proposition (fibre mass obstruction). Assume that the graph is not four-colourable. For every complete first-bit assignment \(\alpha\) above, any \(F\)-linear functional \(\lambda_\alpha\) on the second-bit Boolean algebra which annihilates all its active residual linear equations satisfies \(\lambda_\alpha(1)=0\). Consequently no functional of the form

\[\Lambda(p)=\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha\bigl(p(b=\alpha,v)\bigr)\tag{4}\]

with that independent annihilation requirement can have \(\Lambda(1)=1\). Arbitrary scalar weights can be absorbed into the \(\lambda_\alpha\); allowing signed coefficients does not repair the ansatz.

Proof. Apply \(\lambda_\alpha\) to the odd-cycle linear combination equal to one. Every summand has value zero. Summing these zero masses in (4) proves the assertion. \(\square\)

This excludes neither a global truncated functional nor a decomposition whose components fail individual residual annihilation and cancel across fibres. Below the interpolation cost, global annihilation does not imply the independent condition imposed in (4). Nor does the failure exclude partial-fibre constructions: those require a new explicit compatibility proof.

What survives

The three-state affine-indicator exclusion still does not cover four states. Quadratic edge compression does not compress the number of actual bits, unlike the paired modular compression. The new ceiling is one above half the original bit count; it leaves a barrier below half numerically possible. The one-bit ideal-fibre formula cannot be iterated here using full complete-fibre ideals: these are already unit ideals, and discard the truncation on which hardness depends.

The lower-bound search therefore returns to coupled truncated reductions or functionals. The colouring paper's pseudo-reduction interface explicitly requires compatibility with multiplication. Its statement and degree convention were checked: Boolean reductions are free there and its encoding uses colour indicators. No numerical lower bound or transfer from that paper is imported in this entry. The proposed full-fibre normalized construction fails before that compatibility gate.

Follow-up. Continuing the local-section lead, with its failed complete-fibre ansatz recorded. A route review is now due. Compare the weakest necessary coupled invariant against partial-fibre reductions and the queued random-relation alternative; do not insist on a normalized annihilator in each inconsistent complete fibre. The specification and its existing unbounded witness remain unchanged.

Sub-ideas

  • For a coupled partial-fibre construction on the compact four-colour control, specify an explicit proper invariant with the multiplication maps in one quotient, and prove survival without assuming normalized functionals on the inconsistent complete fibres. Assess against the actual near-half cutoff and the source's ordinary-degree budget.

Checks. The literal GAP certificates and complete output cover 10 local identities, all 160 root-normalized branches for the complete five-vertex graph and the join of a five-cycle with an edge over the binary and four-element fields, and 832 weighted Boolean corrections. Maximum degrees are six and eight, respectively. Each of the 160 omitted-cycle-edge controls fails the asserted branch identity, and a four-colourable complete four-vertex control has a consistent fibre. Thus the 161 negative/positive controls test both cancellation and the necessary non-colourability hypothesis. The smallest nontrivial graph has five vertices and 16 first-bit branches; the series was capped at 64 branches per graph/field. These are exact tests of the displayed certificates, not closure lower bounds or evidence for the asymptotic goal.

Verification gate. The sole fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed with one scope correction: the fibre-mass proposition now explicitly assumes non-four-colourability. It confirmed both encoding directions, the root affine replay, all Boolean and interpolation degrees, final-line reuse and unchanged goal assessment. No unused mathematical hypothesis was found. All bounded controls passed. The compact three-colour proof, separator and guarded-chain controls were read in full. Searches covered colour conditioning, quadratic indicators and fibre functionals; the prior colour-transfer dependency graph was inspected. No external hardness theorem is a premise.

Process assessment. This sixth unchanged cycle must be assessed by the scheduled architecture review. The attempted construction supplied a precise failed requirement and a matching-scale upper bound, not the required surviving invariant. Further reformulations alone would not count as progress.
