8 October 2026 — Replace whole contexts by explicit boundary relations

Working proofs and goal-level review; global near-half survival remains open. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. This scheduled review follows six research entries without progress on the necessary near-half degree implication. Two open statements remain, as twelve research entries ago, so this is a goal-level review. Which method requirement can be weakened without hiding the missing global argument?

New mathematics. A growing-modulus ensemble has field-consistent, Boolean-empty essential relations and polynomial CNF size, but even connected instances can contain logarithmic-size contradictory cores. For compact four-colouring, exact star elimination is generated by degree-four boundary determinants, each derivable through ordinary PC degree four. Unconditional whole-vertex closure has a fixed-fraction method ceiling. A zero-odd-moment ansatz already fails on a satisfiable triangle.

Goal progress. Unchanged. None of these statements gives an unbounded family with a surviving near-half invariant. They supply a non-vacuity control, a more selective algebraic operation and precise rejected requirements. The fixed-width specification and its existing positive-linear witness are retained; the growing-width alternative remains provisional. Complete Frege transfer is still separate.

General statement. prop:growing-modulus-source gives all-parameter source probabilities and an unbounded instantiation, including a connected easy-core control. lem:four-colour-star-projection gives an exact all-arity elimination ideal and constant original-degree generators. cor:whole-vertex-support-ceiling bounds only the stated support method; obs:odd-moment-triangle-obstruction refutes only the specified parity of moments.

What changed in the six entries

The previous construction test passed rank-half, maximal radius, minimality, essentiality and branching, but the witness has degree six. Two further entries exposed carries and then a quarter-variable ceiling for the entire paired two-weight class. This is an actual family exclusion, not evidence against all modular coefficients. The next three entries determined the one-bit defect module, refuted automatic absorption even by all proper joint members, and found inconsistent complete first-bit fibres and a midpoint-plus-one ceiling for compact four-colouring. None constructed the required proper global invariant.

Obstacle

The line lacks a multiplication-compatible reduction or a proper closed space containing the original axioms at the required cutoff. Full local ideals, compatible local coefficient families and independent complete-fibre annihilators impose different, sometimes impossible, extra requirements. Merely renaming the actual unknown PC closure supplies no construction. On the alternative growing-modulus route, small Boolean-empty cores are an additional uncontrolled obstruction.

Across the goal

  • Near-half Boolean PC family. Still open. This is the first goal-level review: all 18 research entries since notebook creation have named this obligation, including the six assessed here. They supplied exclusions, controls and interfaces, but no near-half witness.
  • Complete Frege transfer. Still open. No research entry has taken this as its primary obligation; imported conditional tools and encoding warnings do not complete it. The missing family remains the highest-risk prerequisite.

Architecture review

Necessary target. For every fixed relevant approximation depth \(\ell\ge2\), exhibit a polynomial-size unsatisfiable Boolean CNF family on actual \(v\to\infty\) variables, independent of the proposed proof-size exponent, with \(1\notin C_D\) over \(\mathbb F_2\), \(D=\lfloor v/2-s\rfloor\), \(s=o(v/(\log v)^{\ell-2})\). Here \(C_D\) is ordinary degree-\(D\) PC closure. One proper multiplication-closed space containing all active axioms suffices; no large survivor space is necessary. A growing-width family also needs its full source/Frege degree ledger. The initial-degree floor alone is not a useful substitute for that transfer.

Extra requirements. We need not preserve every local monomial, retain all full-fibre states, make every chosen context satisfiable, or forbid all earlier degree falls. In particular, a boundary function can be put into the kernel explicitly rather than force its entire vertex context into a support. We also do not need a uniform result for arbitrary context choices. The support theorem discussed below is sufficient, and its stable-irreducibility condition is stronger than the bare existence of the required operator.

Independent alternative. Before seeing the current route, the sole fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer proposed field-consistent, Boolean-empty affine systems with growing odd modulus and logarithmic scopes. It suggested testing a hypothetical uniform lower bound with loss \(O(v/q+k)\), while explicitly not claiming that bound. It singled out minimal-core entropy and uniformity in \(q\) as rejection tests. After receiving the six-cycle outcomes and the connected small-core control below, the same reviewer preferred exact star projection and controlled composition as the next local test, because it has an explicit degree-four operation. The staged review evidence preserves both comparisons.

Rank normalization. All projection ideals below are in one Boolean function algebra on the boundary; their equality is not equality of degree-bounded PC closures. Properness must be checked after all derived boundary relations have been charged. A nonzero nullspace before multiplication closure does not ensure a marked unit survives. Once a space is both proper and closed under every legal multiplier, it cannot contain one, since multiplication would then generate every monomial through the cutoff. No ranks from different quotients are subtracted.

Decision. Retain the abstract structural specification, but replace unconditional whole-vertex absorption by exact boundary polynomials in the immediate local-section test. This keeps the fixed-width control and supplies a concrete operation that can compose; the random-half and growing-modulus alternatives still lack both a global PC invariant and the growing-width transfer ledger. This is a bounded choice for the current queue head, not a claim that star composition proves hardness. The new modular lead is queued with its core problem explicit.

Decisive test. On the local-section head, compose the degree-four star projections across overlapping centres in a common boundary algebra. Begin with an independent set of centres, where semantic projections factor, then identify exactly what overlap adds. Seek a proper closed truncated space or an explicit composed relation that destroys it within the target budget. Count every original-degree and actual-variable cost. A semantic equality of elimination ideals alone, another star table, or another whole-vertex closure ceiling will not satisfy this test. The next research entry must report its actual outcome.

A non-vacuous growing-modulus source

Proposition (growing modulus source). Let \(q\) be an odd prime. Fix a bipartite incidence graph with \(m\) row vertices of degree \(k\ge3\) and \(v\) variable vertices of degree three, with no repeated edge, so \(mk=3v\). Sample every supported matrix entry and every component of \(b\) independently and uniformly in \(\mathbb F_q\); unsupported entries of \(A\) are zero. With probability at least \(1-\varepsilon\), where

\[\varepsilon=\frac{m+3v}{q}+\frac{mq}{2^k}+\frac{2^v}{q^m},\tag{1}\]

the system \(Ax=b\) has full row rank and a field solution, has no Boolean solution, every supported coefficient is nonzero, and every row has at least one allowed Boolean tuple. Each row relation is essential on all its \(k\) variables, every variable occurs in exactly three blocks, and the canonical CNF has at most \(m2^k\) clauses of width \(k\), with no auxiliary variables. With an additional failure probability at most \(q/(m2^k)\), the sum of the row allowed-set sizes is at most \(2m2^k/q\).

Proof. For any row set \(T\), incidence counting gives \(k|T|\le3|N(T)|\), hence \(|N(T)|\ge|T|\). Hall's theorem supplies a matching saturating the rows. The corresponding \(m\)-column minor has a nonzero determinant polynomial: its matching monomial is a product of distinct edge-entry indeterminates and cannot cancel with a different permutation's monomial. Its total degree is \(m\). The elementary finite-field polynomial zero bound therefore gives rank-failure probability at most \(m/q\). This bound is the multiplicity-one consequence of the recorded all-field Schwartz–Zippel theorem. Equivalently, induct on variables, split off a highest power \(d\), and combine the leading-coefficient failure bound with the univariate \(d/q\) root bound. A union bound gives probability at most \(3v/q\) of any zero supported entry. Full row rank makes every \(b\) field-consistent.

For a fixed Boolean vector \(x\), independently sampled targets make \(\Pr[Ax=b]=q^{-m}\). Thus the expected number of Boolean solutions is \(2^v/q^m\), giving the third term in (1). For one row, let \(X\) be the number of allowed tuples. Its \(2^k\) tuple indicators have mean \(1/q\) and are pairwise independent: the two linear forms with coefficient vectors \((x,-1)\) and \((y,-1)\) in the random row entries and target are independent whenever \(x\ne y\). Hence \(\mathbb EX=2^k/q\) and \(\operatorname{Var}X\le2^k/q\). Chebyshev gives \(\Pr[X=0]\le q/2^k\); union bound gives the second term. The row counts are independent across rows, even when scopes overlap, so the same variance calculation for their sum gives the final asserted failure probability. No independence between the four bad events is assumed.

If a row has an allowed tuple and all coefficients are nonzero, flipping any coordinate changes its modular value by a nonzero amount, so that coordinate is essential. This also proves the variables are actual in the canonical encoding. Each forbidden tuple contributes its usual width-\(k\) clause. \(\square\)

Unbounded parameters. For integer \(l\to\infty\), take \(v=l2^l\), \(k=4l\), \(m=3\cdot2^{l-2}\), and choose a prime \(v^2<q<2v^2\). Three partitions into blocks of size \(k\) provide an incidence graph with the required degrees. The bounds in (1), including the additional row-count bound, tend to zero: the local-emptiness term is at most \(O(l^2 2^{-l})\), the rank and coefficient terms are \(O(1/v)\), and \(2^v/q^m\le2^{-v/2}\). The encoding length is \(O(m2^k k\log(v+1))=O(v^5)\). Thus this is an honest unbounded source class, not an assertion of minimality, PC hardness, or applicability of the fixed-width transfer.

Connected core trap. Connectivity does not fix the missing minimal-core control. Partition the variables into blocks \(B_j\) of size \(k\), choose \(x_j\in B_j\), and take two copies of each block as the first two row layers. In the third layer replace \(B_j\) by \((B_j\setminus\{x_j\})\cup\{x_{j+1}\}\), cyclically. Every column still has three occurrences, every row has size \(k\), and for at least two blocks the incidence graph is connected. The three rows based at any fixed block involve only \(k+1\) variables. Their probability of having a Boolean solution is at most \(2^{k+1}/q^3\), by the same first moment. This tends to zero for the displayed parameters. Intersect this event with the good event from (1): with probability tending to one, the whole system has all the stated properties and already has a contradiction on at most \(k+1\) variables. A complete assignment-branching refutation of that canonical subformula costs at most \(k+2\): select a falsified clause for each assignment, multiply it to that assignment's full indicator and sum the indicators. This is an actual easy family, so essentiality, three occurrences and connectedness alone cannot be adopted as hardness conditions. A smaller inclusion-minimal core remains within those variables.

Exact star projection

Work over a field of characteristic two with the quadratic encoding. A centre \(c\) has distinct neighbours \(i\in T\), with star equations

\[q_i=(1+u_i+u_c)(1+v_i+v_c).\]

Let \(I_T\) be their ideal in the full Boolean algebra on the star. For a four-element neighbour subset \(U\), define

\[\Delta_U=\det\bigl[(1,u_i,v_i,u_iv_i)\bigr]_{i\in U}.\tag{2}\]

Lemma (four-colour star projection). The elimination ideal \(I_T\cap B_T\) in the boundary Boolean algebra is generated by the \(\Delta_U\) for \(U\subseteq T\), \(|U|=4\). Each generator has degree at most four and has an ordinary PC derivation through degree four from the star quadratics, and through degree four from their original canonical CNF. If \(|T|<4\), the elimination ideal is zero. The statement concerns full ideals and these specific degree-four derivations, not equality of every truncated consequence space.

Proof of elimination. A boundary assignment extends to a centre colour exactly when it uses at most three of the four states. The evaluation matrix of the functions \(1,u,v,uv\) on all four Boolean states has determinant one in characteristic two. Therefore \(\Delta_U\) takes value one exactly when those four neighbours use all four distinct colours, and zero otherwise. The common zero set of all these determinants is precisely the extendible boundary set. Every ideal in a Boolean function algebra is the ideal of functions vanishing on its common zeros: point indicators decompose the algebra into a product of copies of the field. Projection of the full star zero set gives the same extendible set, proving the ideal equality.

Proof of the degree ledger. For four neighbours, let \(M\) have rows

\[\bigl((1+u_i)(1+v_i),\ 1+v_i,\ 1+u_i,\ 1\bigr).\]

Then \(M(1,u_c,v_c,u_cv_c)^T=(q_i)_{i\in U}\) literally. Its determinant equals \(\Delta_U\), since its columns are an invertible triangular change and reversal of the basis in (2), with determinant one in characteristic two. The first row of the adjugate identity gives

\[\Delta_U=\sum_{i\in U} \operatorname{adj}(M)_{1i}q_i.\tag{3}\]

Each adjugate coefficient here omits the first column and uses the remaining column degrees \(1,1,0\), so has degree at most two. Every product in (3) has ordinary degree at most four and is obtained by variable multiplications and linear combinations. The quadratic edge axioms themselves follow from the canonical clauses through degree four; reusing their final lines gives the same ceiling. \(\square\)

Two support requirements that are too strong

Corollary (whole vertex support ceiling). Consider the support choice that associates a monomial to all Boolean and edge axioms on its touched vertices. For any graph with a vertex having four neighbours, this choice fails the stable-irreducibility condition of the cited monomial-support lemma at cutoff five, for any degree-compatible monomial order under which it is a support. Moreover, on an \(N\)-vertex vertex-critical non-four-colourable graph, any whole-vertex support rule which contains the touched vertices and is closed under adding a vertex with four neighbours already present violates the local-satisfiability condition at a monomial degree at most \(N-\lceil(N-1)/4\rceil\). These are method ceilings, not PC upper bounds.

Proof. Take four neighbours \(U\) of a centre. Their injective colouring satisfies every edge among them, so \(\Delta_U\) is not in the induced boundary ideal \(J_U\). But it belongs to the induced ideal on \(U\cup\{c\}\). Reduce \(\Delta_U\) modulo \(J_U\) in the chosen degree-compatible order, obtaining a nonzero remainder \(r\) of degree at most four, still on the four neighbours. Its leading monomial \(m'\) is irreducible modulo \(J_U\), and hence modulo the smaller induced ideal on its own touched vertices, but is reducible modulo the induced ideal on all five vertices. The support of \(m'\) is contained in that of a degree-five monomial touching all five vertices. This contradicts the source's required equivalence of reducibility for nested supports.

For the second assertion, a vertex-critical non-four-colourable graph has minimum degree at least four: a vertex of smaller degree could be greedily coloured after colouring its deletion. Deleting any vertex gives a four-colouring, whose largest colour class is an independent set \(I\) of size at least \(\lceil(N-1)/4\rceil\). Put \(S=V\setminus I\). Every vertex of \(I\) has all its at least four neighbours in \(S\). Thus the stated closure sends \(S\) to the whole graph. A monomial with one bit from each vertex of \(S\) has degree \(|S|\), but its closed support is the unsatisfiable whole instance. \(\square\)

The determinant operation avoids that unconditional growth: it removes particular boundary functions instead. Its monomials need not preserve every partial state, and their composition must be measured in one quotient. It is not a proof that such a proper composed quotient survives.

Obstruction (odd moment triangle). In characteristic two, on the three-vertex complete graph there is no normalized linear functional through degree three that annihilates every edge quadratic and its legal degree-three variable multiples and is zero on all odd-degree squarefree monomials. In particular, directly assigning even principal Pfaffians to monomial moments and zero to odd moments fails, although the triangle is four-colourable.

Proof. With \(\{i,j,k\}=\{1,2,3\}\), direct expansion gives the literal identity

\[\sum_{\{i,j\}}(1+u_k)q_{ij} =1+u_1+u_2+u_3+\sum_{i<j}u_i u_j(v_i+v_j).\tag{4}\]

The left is annihilated by the proposed functional; the right maps to one because every nonconstant monomial has odd degree. This is a contradiction. An affine change of centre can alter the odd-moment condition; the statement does not exclude every shifted or coupled Pfaffian ansatz. \(\square\)

Outside leads

  • Schwartz–Zippel and sparse affine sources. The recorded all-field multiplicity theorem of Dvir–Kopparty–Saraf–Sudan, Lemma 2.7, bounds the zero set of the matched determinant. Together with the elementary first/second moments it proves the growing-modulus source above. Answers. An overlap or core theorem for this ensemble, followed by a uniform characteristic-mismatch invariant, could supply the missing near-half-pc-family source beyond the fixed-modulus exclusions. Test. Passed: field consistency, Boolean emptiness, essentiality, occurrence and polynomial encoding coexist at unbounded sizes. The connected core trap shows exactly what this test does not establish. Develop minimal-core control and a uniform degree theorem; do not adopt hardness as an admissibility condition.
  • Monomial-dependent reduction supports. Conneryd–de Rezende–Nordström–Pang–Risse, Definition 4.1 and Lemma 4.6, obtain a pseudo-reduction from a valid support satisfying local satisfiability and stable irreducibility. The statement and proof were checked, including its universal nested-monomial condition. Answers. A bit-sensitive support or weaker directly verified reduction identity could control the missing multiplier action for near-half-pc-family. Test. Falsified for the naive induced-vertex and four-neighbour-closure translations by the corollary above. The complete three- and four-vertex controls alone had passed; the general star obstruction identifies the first structural defect. The source theorem is not refuted, and the exact determinant repair remains on the existing local-section head.
  • Combinatorial Nullstellensatz. Alon's Theorems 1.1–1.2 provide exact grid reduction and a nonvanishing coefficient criterion. For modular rows, the direct indicator product is \(\prod_a[1-(L_a-b_a)^{q-1}]\) over \(\mathbb F_q\). Answers. A low-degree, proof-field-compatible coefficient certificate could supply a marked surviving functional for near-half-pc-family. Test. Falsified for this direct product in the proposed entropy regime: \(m\log_2q>v\) implies \(m(q-1)>v\), while the Boolean-grid nonvanishing criterion requires top exponents at most one and total degree at most \(v\). The nonzero linear row factors give this product exactly degree \(m(q-1)\). Boolean reduction of the product is zero on a Boolean-empty system. This does not exclude other polynomials or a different coefficient construction.

Absurd bridges

  • Fermionic Gaussian amplitudes. Rajabpour–Seifi Mirjafarlou's Theorem 1 and computational-basis formula express complex amplitudes by principal Pfaffians. The proposed algebraic translation assigns those Pfaffians to squarefree monomial moments, omitting the complex normalization; a unit moment is specified separately. This is a proposed translation, not the paper's theorem over a binary field. Answers. A compatible moment hierarchy with a marked unit could give the coupled functional missing in near-half-pc-family. Test. Falsified for the direct zero-odd-moment translation by (4), already on a satisfiable triangle. Reducing Boolean polynomials before assigning moments avoids the separate repeated-variable problem but does not remove (4). Shifting coordinates can pass an ordinary satisfying-assignment control, but no coupled unsatisfiable-family construction follows.
  • Quantum LDPC protected logical states. Panteleev–Kalachev's Theorem 2 provides fixed-field quantum LDPC families with positive rate and linear distance; the chain-complex interpretation was checked. Try to turn a protected logical sector into a unit class immune to low-degree Boolean constraints. Answers. A translation preserving the noncommutative logical obstruction and the full Boolean-variable budget could give a surviving object for near-half-pc-family. Test. Falsified for the direct binary parity-check translation: if the added binary syndrome is inconsistent, Gaussian elimination gives a constant linear combination equal to one. The affine parity row is a sum of its canonical forbidden indicators through its original width, so bounded-width inputs have bounded-degree PC refutations. A consistent syndrome still has Boolean solutions. Quantum distance therefore does not transfer by treating the checks as classical binary equations; nonlinear encodings require a separate construction and ledger.

Sub-ideas

  • Compose the exact degree-four star projections for independent and then overlapping centres, tracking actual truncated PC derivations and a common unit class. Establish an explicit invariant or find a cutoff-respecting composition that destroys it; semantic projection alone does not settle the test.

Checks. The first complete control log checks the naive support on the complete three- and four-vertex graphs, the triangle identity over the binary and four-element fields, and the exact probability bounds at four parameter choices. The augmented probability estimate is not below one at the smallest tested parameter, but is below one at the three larger ones; the theorem is asymptotic. The star table exhausts all 256 boundary assignments and four centre choices over each of those fields, finding 24 injective boundaries and 324 allowed star assignments. The literal adjugate certificates verify all four degree-two cofactors and degree-four products. The first control attempt stopped on a GAP record-name length limit; the corrected cache key passed. All completed logs have explicit final success markers; no larger instance was run after the relevant identities and scope questions were decided.

Verification gate. The same fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer supplied the blind alternative, compared the revealed route, and passed the full draft without mathematical corrections. It checked all four claims, the connected core trap, original-degree ledgers, method-only ceilings, unchanged goal assessment and chosen composition test, finding no essential unused hypothesis. The four-colour transfer and all-field Schwartz–Zippel statement and proof were reread in full; exact dependency metadata and correction links were inspected. Primary source statements and relevant definitions were screened for all five outside items; the support lemma and Combinatorial Nullstellensatz proofs were read. No unreviewed quantum theorem is used as a PC-hardness premise, and no new Lean verification is claimed.

Process assessment. Six unchanged cycles trigger this goal-level comparison, not another early review. The next test concerns the actual kernel and composition of an explicit operation; an enlargement of the toolkit alone will again be recorded as unchanged. No publication or Lean work is authorized.

8 October 2026 — Adjacent centres add a degree-six boundary conflict

Working all-parameter projections; the truncated invariant remains open. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, perform the scheduled composition test. The missing implication is a proper multiplication-closed invariant at the target cutoff. Independent star centres can share boundary neighbours, but edges between centres may add constraints that separate projections miss. Determine the exact boundary relations and their original PC cost before treating projection as an invariant.

Route comparison. The review chose an explicit algebraic repair over further whole-context conditions or another non-vacuity construction. This cycle tests that choice on an unbounded class of centre sets, not a growing list of graphs. The pair conflict below is also a falsifying control for assuming that separate projected ideals already preserve the global unit.

New mathematics. Three distinct neighbour colours force a centre's missing colour through weighted linear equations of final degree three. Centres inducing a matching have an exact common boundary projection: single-centre determinants and additional degree-six conflicts for paired centres. All these generators derive through degree six from the original canonical CNF, regardless of how different components share boundary neighbours. For the unbounded join of an odd cycle with two adjacent anchors, omitting pair conflicts leaves a satisfiable boundary system, while the conflicts produce one through degree six. These formulas are minimal, essential and fixed-width.

Goal progress. Unchanged. This gives explicit composition and a unit-loss control, but no proper global truncated invariant. Equality of full projection ideals is not equality of cutoff closures; only the stated one-way proof replay is proved. Only the explicit two-anchor subclass is excluded asymptotically; general compact four-colouring remains open.

General statement. lem:three-neighbour-forcing holds over every characteristic-two field. thm:matching-centre-projection covers arbitrary finite simple graphs and centre sets inducing maximum degree at most one. thm:two-anchor-colouring-ceiling gives an unbounded minimally unsatisfiable fixed-width control with degree-six refutations.

Weighted missing colours

Use the quadratic four-colour encoding \(q_{ci}=(1+u_c+u_i)(1+v_c+v_i)\), together with Booleanity. Let \(A=\{1,2,3\}\) be three distinct neighbours of \(c\). Define

\[\tau_A=\det[(1,u_i,v_i)]_{i\in A}=\sum_{i<j}(u_i v_j+u_j v_i),\qquad a_A=\sum_{i\in A}u_i,\quad b_A=\sum_{i\in A}v_i.\tag{1}\]

On Boolean points, \(\tau_A=1\) exactly when the three colours are distinct. In that case \((a_A,b_A)\) is the missing fourth colour, since the coordinatewise sum of all four colours is zero.

Lemma (three neighbour forcing). The literal polynomials \(\tau_A(u_c+a_A)\) and \(\tau_A(v_c+b_A)\), each of degree at most three, derive through ordinary PC degree three from the star quadratics and Boolean axioms, and through degree four from the canonical CNF.

Proof. Write \(B_x=x^2+x\). With \(\{i,j,k\}=A\), direct expansion gives

\[\tau_A(u_c+a_A)=\sum_{i\in A}(u_j+u_k)q_{ci}+\sum_{i\in A}B_{u_i}(v_j+v_k),\tag{2}\]

and the same identity with \(u,v\) exchanged. One way to check (2) is to use the three rows \(((1+u_i)(1+v_i),1+v_i,1+u_i,1)\). Their last-three-column determinant is \(\tau_A\); the Cramer cofactors for the centre's first bit are \(u_j+u_k\). The constant-column numerator differs from \(\tau_A a_A\) by precisely the Boolean correction displayed in (2). Alternatively expand both sides term by term. Every product in (2) has degree at most three. The prior quadratic-to-CNF setup costs four and its final lines are reused. \(\square\)

One common boundary for many centres

Let \(G=(V,E)\) be a finite simple graph over a characteristic-two proof field, and let \(C\subseteq V\) be a centre set such that \(G[C]\) is a matching plus isolated vertices. Put \(B=V\setminus C\), and write \(T_c=N(c)\cap B\). Keep all original boundary edges \(G[B]\). For each centre and each four-subset \(U\subseteq T_c\), keep the star determinant \(\Delta_U=\det[(1,u_i,v_i,u_iv_i)]_{i\in U}\). For every internal edge \(cd\in E(G[C])\), every triple \(A\subseteq T_c\), and every triple \(A'\subseteq T_d\), add

\[R_{A,A'}=\tau_A\tau_{A'}(1+a_A+a_{A'})(1+b_A+b_{A'}).\tag{3}\]

The triples can overlap. These formulas are taken as Boolean functions for full-ideal statements; either their displayed degree-at-most-six representatives or their multilinearizations can be used as ordinary PC axioms.

Theorem (matching centre projection). The full Boolean ideal of the original colouring instance, intersected with the boundary algebra, is generated by the boundary edge quadratics, all these \(\Delta_U\), and all these \(R_{A,A'}\). Each generator has an ordinary PC derivation through degree at most six from the original canonical CNF. Consequently a degree-\(D\) refutation of this boundary polynomial system replays in the original CNF through \(\max\{D,6\}\). This is one-way replay, not a reverse simulation or a lower-bound theorem.

Proof of the projection. Fix a proper colouring of \(G[B]\). The available list \(L_c\) at a centre is the set of colours not used on \(T_c\). The determinants say precisely that \(L_c\ne\varnothing\). Isolated centres can then be coloured independently, even when their boundary neighbourhoods overlap. For a paired component \(cd\), two nonempty lists admit distinct representatives exactly unless both are the same singleton. If they are the same singleton, choose three neighbours of each centre witnessing the other three colours. Their triples have \(\tau_A=\tau_{A'}=1\) and the same missing colour, so (3) has value one. Conversely, when the lists are nonempty and (3) is one, both lists are forced to that same missing colour. Thus the displayed equations characterize exactly the extendible boundary assignments. Different components of \(G[C]\) have no edges between them and may be extended separately. The equality of full ideals follows from the product-of-fields description of Boolean function algebras. This also covers centres of boundary degree less than three or four, whose corresponding generator lists are empty.

Proof of the degree ledger. Boundary edge equations and star determinants already derive through four. Write \(T=\tau_A\), \(S=\tau_{A'}\), \(a=a_A,b=b_A,a'=a_{A'},b'=b_{A'}\), and put

\[U=1+u_c+u_d,\quad V=1+v_c+v_d,\quad U'=1+a+a',\quad V'=1+b+b'.\]

The preceding lemma gives final lines \(F_u=T(u_c+a)\), \(F_v=T(v_c+b)\), \(G_u=S(u_d+a')\), \(G_v=S(v_d+b')\), each of degree at most three, through four. The literal identity

\[TSUV+TSU'V'=SVF_u+TVG_u+SU'F_v+TU'G_v\tag{4}\]

derives \(R_{A,A'}=TSU'V'\) from \(TSq_{cd}\) and those four lines. Every product in (4) has degree at most six. Each polynomial multiplier is implemented monomial by monomial using variable multiplication and linear combination; earlier proofs of the final lines are not multiplied wholesale. Boolean multilinearization, if desired, costs no more than six. This proves the generator ledger. Derive the boundary axioms once and replay a boundary proof on its unchanged variables, giving the claimed maximum. \(\square\)

Inventory and variables. The boundary system has at most

\[|E(G[B])|+\sum_{c\in C}\binom{|T_c|}{4}+\sum_{cd\in E(G[C])}\binom{|T_c|}{3}\binom{|T_d|}{3}\]

non-Boolean generators, hence polynomial inventory, with support on at most twelve boundary bits per generator. Its declared boundary count is \(2|B|\); any unused variables must be removed when applying an actual-variable bound. The system here is polynomial, not a claimed free CNF conversion. No reverse degree bound for reinstating centre variables is asserted.

Actual unit loss across an internal edge

Control (adjacent star unit gap). In the complete five-vertex graph, choose two adjacent centres and let the other three vertices be the boundary. Each separate centre has only three boundary neighbours, so its single-star elimination ideal is zero. The retained boundary graph is a triangle and is four-colourable. Thus boundary edges plus all single-centre determinants preserve one. But the pair projection adds \(\tau_A^2\), where \(A\) is that common boundary triple, and this yields one through degree six from the original canonical CNF.

Proof. Here \(A=A'\), so both factors in (3) after \(\tau_A^2\) are literally one. Booleanity derives \(\tau_A^2+\tau_A\) through degree four: write \(\tau_A\) as its six squarefree quadratic monomials, apply Frobenius in characteristic two, and for each product \(xy\) use

\[(xy)^2+xy=(x^2+x)y^2+x(y^2+y).\]

Therefore the pair derivation gives \(\tau_A\) through six. The three boundary edge quadratics satisfy the literal identity \(\sum_{ij\in E(K_3)}q_{ij}=1+\tau_A\). Summing gives one. On the other hand, assigning three distinct colours to the boundary satisfies its edge equations, so the separate-projection system has a genuine evaluation functional nonzero on one. \(\square\)

This refutes independent composition across an internal centre edge. It does not refute the exact matching projection, which includes the conflict, or prove a near-half upper bound on a growing critical family. The control is deliberately small because it tests a general missing relation, not an asymptotic lower bound.

An unbounded target-budget control

Theorem (two anchor colouring ceiling). For every odd integer \(r\ge3\), let \(G_r\) be the join of an \(r\)-cycle with two adjacent anchor vertices. Its compact four-colour CNF has \(v=2r+4\) actual variables and \(12r+4\) clauses of width four. As a relation-block system it is minimally unsatisfiable, every scope coordinate is essential and every variable occurs in at least four blocks. Over every characteristic-two field it has an ordinary PC refutation through degree six. Keeping only the separate anchor-star projections leaves a satisfiable boundary system, but the exact pair projection destroys its unit through six.

Structural proof. The anchors require distinct colours, leaving only two colours for the odd cycle, which is impossible. Deleting their mutual edge permits both anchors to use one colour and the cycle to use the other three. Deleting a cycle edge leaves a path, which uses two colours distinct from the two anchor colours. Deleting an anchor-cycle edge permits its cycle endpoint to share that anchor's colour; the remaining path uses two other colours, and the second anchor uses the fourth. Thus deletion of any edge relation makes the instance satisfiable. The inequality relation on two two-bit states is essential in each of its four bits. Every rim vertex has degree four and each anchor has degree \(r+1\). There are \(3r+1\) edge relations, proving all counts and the branching assertion.

Degree-six proof. Number the cycle vertices cyclically, with edge \(12\). Put \(d_u=u_1+u_2\), \(d_v=v_1+v_2\). For every \(i\ge3\), the identical triple \(\{1,2,i\}\) at both anchors gives the pair consequence \(\tau_{12i}^2\) through six. The Boolean Frobenius correction above gives \(T_i=\tau_{12i}\) through six. Put \(T_1=T_2=0\). The literal expression

\[T_i=d_u(v_i+v_1)+d_v(u_i+u_1)\tag{5}\]

holds for every cycle vertex, including the two zero cases. For a rim edge \(ij\), write \(U=u_i+u_j\), \(V=v_i+v_j\). Then

\[d_u q_{ij}+(1+U)(T_i+T_j)+d_v(B_{u_i}+B_{u_j})=d_u(1+U).\tag{6}\]

Indeed \(T_i+T_j=d_uV+d_vU\), and \(U^2+U=B_{u_i}+B_{u_j}\). Every new product in (6) has degree at most three; reusing the degree-six derivations of \(T_i\) keeps the full ceiling six. The swapped identity derives \(d_v(1+V)\). Summing the first weighted equations over the odd cycle gives \(d_u\), and summing the second gives \(d_v\). Finally \(q_{12}=(1+d_u)(1+d_v)\); multiplying the derived \(d_u\) by \(d_v\) and combining these lines gives one, still through six. This proves the claimed ceiling without conditioning exponentially many branches.

For the separate-projection control, use any proper three-colouring of the rim. All four-neighbour determinants at either anchor vanish, since only three boundary colours occur, and all rim edges are satisfied. Each anchor separately can take the unused fourth colour. The internal anchor edge is precisely what requires the additional pair relations. The proof above shows that those relations force one through six in the boundary system as well. \(\square\)

What the upper bound teaches. Long odd cycles, relation minimality and full branching cannot rescue this family: two common adjacent anchors leave a binary residual colouring problem, and the matching projection recovers its linear contradiction. The structural reason applies to this anchor-based subclass, not arbitrary four-colour-critical graphs or the modular alternatives. No new admissible specification is adopted. For any permitted deficit \(s=o(v/(\log v)^{\ell-2})\) at fixed \(\ell\ge2\), the target \(D=\lfloor v/2-s\rfloor\) eventually exceeds six. Thus this unbounded fixed-width control, unlike the isolated five-vertex example, witnesses unit destruction within the actual target budget, without padding or unused variables.

Architecture test. The review's requested test passes for independent centres even with shared boundary neighbours, and reveals exactly the new degree-six relations when centres induce a matching. The unbounded two-anchor family shows actual loss of the unit through degree six once those relations are included, eventually within the specified near-half cutoff on its actual variables. This completes the requested negative composition test. It does not construct a proper invariant on a surviving family, so near-half-pc-family remains open.

Follow-up. Continuing the local-section idea, but rotating it at the end of its four-entry spell: this spell supplied no fresh advance of the near-half open statement, so no extension is earned. The explicit projection and the remaining overlapping-component problem are retained. The random-relation/Reed–Muller lead becomes the head, as already queued.

Sub-ideas

  • For centre components containing paths or cycles beyond a matching, derive the additional boundary conflicts and their original-degree cost, then determine whether a specified composition admits a proper cutoff-closed invariant. Do not infer this from full-ideal projection.
  • Before using the projected boundary system as a literal source family, audit a canonical CNF encoding and any reverse proof interpretation, counting all actual variables and constant-width costs. The current replay only goes from the polynomial boundary system to the original CNF.

Checks. The exact composition checker and full certificates verify eight literal pair identities and two complete five-vertex unit certificates over the binary and four-element fields. All 21,824 boundary assignments in the bounded overlap/empty-list controls match the projected system, with both extendible and forbidden assignments in each case. The anchor-cycle check and complete output verify 76 new literal identities at odd rim lengths five and seven, and all 38 edge-deletion witnesses. These test the exact all-parameter proof mechanisms, not an extrapolated degree lower bound. The initial GAP attempt used a reserved identifier and aborted before testing; it is retained as failed execution, not validation. The renamed run and the new anchor controls end with explicit success markers. No larger case is needed.

Verification gate. The sole fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed the forcing and matching projection, but correctly required an honest partial-outcome label for the isolated five-vertex budget test. The unbounded two-anchor theorem resolves that concrete gap; the same reviewer then passed the strengthened proof, actual-variable and minimality ledger, completed target-budget negative test, unchanged goal assessment and queue rotation without further corrections. No unused hypotheses were found. The star-projection and four-colour-transfer proofs were reread, with their dependency graphs checked; no stronger version or correction was found. Searches covered Cramer forcing, missing-colour constraints and matching-centre elimination. No independent novelty or global hardness is asserted.

Process assessment. The requested composition test produced an all-parameter operation and an explicit unit-loss control, rather than another case table. Since global survival remains untouched, the queue rotation is enforced instead of extending this line for additional local lemmas.

8 October 2026 — Strong local erasure quality does not control global degree

Working source-based probability theorem and all-field PC ceiling. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, develop the Reed–Muller lead now at the queue head. The missing implication is global multiplication closure and unit survival, not local matrix rank. Determine whether the source gives simultaneous local quality with polynomial encoding, then test whether that quality can distinguish a globally hard overlap pattern from an easy one.

Route comparison. A conditional good-table ensemble was proposed to avoid an unsupported union bound. Reading the stronger source proof removes that extra conditioning: its quantitative tail already covers polynomially many scopes. We test the resulting local condition against the cyclic-scope existence control previously recorded as potentially easy. This is a named general test of the local-to-global implication, not evidence against every random-scope ensemble.

New mathematics. At logarithmic scope size, independently sampled half-allowed tables simultaneously have no nonzero low-degree vanishing polynomial through local degree half the scope size minus its square root, while the global system is unsatisfiable and essential with high probability. Nevertheless, on cyclic interval scopes the same ensemble has ordinary PC refutations of logarithmic degree. The obstruction is a small variable interface between successive parts, which local erasure quality does not measure.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The new local condition has an unbounded polynomial-size witness but does not yield a near-half global barrier. It remains provisional: growing-width transfer and retention of actual variables under minimal-core normalization are unproved. No refinement asserting hardness is adopted.

General statement. thm:simultaneous-rm-local-quality is a simultaneous asymptotic source theorem for arbitrary prescribed scopes. lem:cyclic-window-degree-ceiling applies to every unsatisfiable cyclic-window CNF over every field. Their combination, cor:local-immunity-global-gap, refutes the unrestricted local-quality-to-near-half implication.

The exact local source and its useful tail

Put \(L=2^k\), \(d_k=\max\{0,\lfloor k/2-\sqrt{k}\rfloor\}\), and let \(R\) include each point of \(\{0,1\}^k\) independently with probability one half. Let \(\delta_k\) be the probability that evaluation on \(R\) has a nonzero kernel in the multilinear binary polynomials of degree at most \(d_k\).

The Kudekar–Kumar–Mondelli–Pfister–Şaşoğlu–Urbanke source, Theorems 25 and 27 and the latter's proof, gives an EXIT function \(h\) with rate limit \(\rho=Q(2)\in(0,1/2)\), \(h(p')\to0\) for fixed \(p'<1-\rho\), and on every fixed interior interval eventually

\[h'(p)\ge c\log L\log\log L\,h(p)(1-h(p)),\qquad P_B(p)\le Lp h(p).\tag{1}\]

Choose \(1/2<p'<1-\rho\). Eventually \(h(p')\le1/2\). Integrating the logarithmic odds from \(1/2\) to \(p'\) gives

\[\delta_k=P_B(1/2)\le L^{1-c(p'-1/2)\log\log L}=L^{-\omega(1)}.\tag{2}\]

The equality identifies an erased nonzero codeword with a nonzero polynomial vanishing on the unerased set \(R\). Thus for every fixed \(A>0\), eventually \(\delta_k\le L^{-A}\). This is a direct consequence of the source proof, not a new capacity theorem; no explicit finite threshold is claimed.

Simultaneous good tables with no Boolean model

Theorem (simultaneous local quality). For sufficiently large \(v\), set \(k=\lceil2\log_2v\rceil\) and \(m=v+\lceil2\log_2v\rceil\). Fix any \(m\) scopes of \(k\) distinct coordinates each, covering every variable at least twice. Sample the allowed tables independently, each tuple allowed with probability one half. With probability \(1-o(1)\), the canonical CNF is unsatisfiable, every row is essential on its full scope, and every row ideal in its \(\mathbb F_2\) Boolean function algebra, using multilinear representatives, has zero intersection with the degree-at-most-\(d_k\) space. It has no auxiliary variables, all \(v\) variables are actual and occur in at least two relation blocks (counting scope occurrences), and its encoding length is \(O(v^3(\log v)^2)\).

Proof. Each local rank-failure event has probability \(\delta_k\). Since \(L\ge v^2\), choosing \(A=2\) in (2) gives \(m\delta_k=o(1)\). This union bound requires no independence between overlapping scopes; the distribution of each table is exactly the binary erasure model. In particular all allowed sets are nonempty, since the constant polynomial would vanish on an empty one.

For a fixed global assignment, independence of the table occurrences gives acceptance probability \(2^{-m}\), even if scopes repeat. Consequently satisfiability has probability at most \(2^{v-m}\le v^{-2}\). For one coordinate of one table, inessentiality requires equal membership bits on each of \(2^{k-1}\) disjoint pairs, with probability \(2^{-2^{k-1}}\). A union bound over \(mk\) coordinates makes this exceptional probability tend to zero. These are the exact previous encoding estimates. Union with the local rank failures proves coexistence; no conditioning or generic-matrix assumption is used. Essentiality makes all covered coordinates occur in the canonical clauses. There are at most \(m2^k\) width-\(k\) clauses, with variable names of length \(O(\log v)\), giving the asserted length. \(\square\)

The theorem holds for arbitrary fixed scope geometry. It proves neither block minimality nor any global PC lower bound. The local degree refers to polynomials in one row's variables, not half of the global variable count.

A complete low-degree overlap control

Lemma (cyclic window ceiling). Let \(1\le k\le v\). Suppose every clause of an unsatisfiable CNF on a cyclically ordered set of \(v\) variables is supported in a cyclic interval of at most \(k\) consecutive variables. Over every field, the CNF has an ordinary PC refutation through degree at most \(2k-1\). Boolean axioms may be present but are not needed in the displayed compilation.

Proof. Assign the first \(k-1\) variables. Each surviving clause is supported in an ordinary interval of at most \(k\) consecutive remaining variables: wrapped intervals lose their prefix part to the assigned cut. For this fixed assignment, eliminate the remaining variables from left to right by resolution. At the step eliminating \(x_i\), all clauses mentioning it have support inside \(\{x_i,\ldots,x_{i+k-1}\}\), truncated at the end. Resolvents have support in the same interval with \(x_i\) removed, so the invariant persists. Variable elimination preserves satisfiability: a satisfying assignment of all resolvents can be extended to the pivot unless two parents force opposite values, which would falsify their resolvent. Hence eliminating every variable from an unsatisfiable restriction eventually yields the empty clause.

Every resolution step has an ordinary PC simulation within the size of its pivot interval. Write its two falsity polynomials as \(x_i C A\) and \((1-x_i)C B\), where \(C\) consists of their shared literals and \(A,B\) are the remaining disjoint literal products. Tautological resolvents can be discarded. For a non-tautological one, multiply the first parent by \(B\), the second by \(A\), and add:

\[x_iCAB+(1-x_i)CAB=CAB.\tag{3}\]

No literal is duplicated in these products. All intermediate degrees are at most the number of variables in the parent union, at most \(k\). Multiplication by a literal is implemented by variable multiplication and a linear combination. This proves the degree-\(k\) bound for each restriction, with no Boolean simplification charged for free.

For an assignment \(\alpha\) to the cut, let \(P_\alpha\) be its point indicator, of degree \(k-1\). Each surviving restricted input clause is obtained from an original clause by removing fixed falsity literals, whose product divides \(P_\alpha\). Multiply the original clause by the remaining cut-indicator literals to obtain \(P_\alpha\) times that restricted clause, through degree at most \(2k-1\). Replay the restricted derivation with every line multiplied by \(P_\alpha\). All multipliers are remaining variables, so the degree is at most \(k+(k-1)\). This derives \(P_\alpha\). Sum over all cut assignments; their literal partition is one. \(\square\)

This is the standard variable-elimination mechanism, with the original-degree ledger written out. It does not require randomness, a particular coefficient field, or a bound on the number of clauses.

Corollary (local immunity gap). There are unsatisfiable polynomial-size Boolean CNFs on unbounded actual \(v\), with \(k=\lceil2\log_2v\rceil\), such that every row is essential and has no nonzero binary vanishing polynomial through \(d_k\), every variable lies in at least \(k\) row blocks, yet the entire CNF has PC degree at most \(2k-1=O(\log v)\) over every field.

Proof. Take the \(v\) cyclic length-\(k\) scopes and repeat any of these scopes for the additional \(m-v\) independently sampled tables. Every variable belongs to exactly \(k\) of the first \(v\) scopes. The simultaneous-quality theorem gives the local conditions and unsatisfiability with probability tending to one. All clauses have cyclic-window support, so the ceiling applies. \(\square\)

What the upper bound teaches

The strong local erasure condition can hold throughout an easy unsatisfiable system. The weakness is its global interface, not its row rank: after exposing a logarithmic cut, all remaining elimination bags are logarithmic. The same ceiling screens any candidate whose original clauses have such a cyclic layout, regardless of local predicate complexity. It does not exclude random scopes with substantially different geometry.

A structural revision would therefore need an unbounded witness avoiding this global bottleneck, a retained actual-variable count after minimal-core normalization, and a proof mechanism controlling cancellations. The theorem's arbitrary-scope non-vacuity helps the first construction step but proves neither the core property nor closure. The previously recorded expansion and immunity certificate ceilings remain method warnings; merely adding an expansion adjective would not supply the missing near-half implication.

Follow-up. Continuing the Reed–Muller lead. Simultaneous local quality is now justified without conditioning, and the unrestricted local-quality implication is falsified. Next test a specified nonlocal scope geometry and its core retention together with a concrete multiplier-compatible construction; do not run larger local rank matrices or infer global closure from (2).

Sub-ideas

  • For the good-table ensemble on nonlocal scopes, prove retention of a linear number of actual variables in every relevant Boolean-empty core and identify a global multiplication-compatible space. Test the proposed overlap mechanism against the cyclic-window and fixed-cutoff fall obstructions before adopting it as a specification refinement.

Checks. The exhaustive local compilation check and complete output enumerate all 729 parent pairs on three non-pivot variables over each of the rationals, binary and ternary fields. All 1,029 compatible pairs satisfy the literal identity and pivot-bag degree bound; 1,158 tautological pairs are discarded. The largest pivot bag has four variables. This tests the actual inference and its ordinary degree, not the asymptotic erasure bound; no larger local rank calculation was run.

The sole fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed with two scope clarifications, now incorporated: the local algebra and its multilinear degree space are explicitly binary, and the conclusion retains the twice-covered occurrence property. It checked the quantitative source consequence, simultaneous probabilities, independence distinctions, encoding size, cyclic elimination invariant and full original-degree replay. The unchanged goal assessment is accepted. The exact compilation control passed. Source Theorems 25 and 27, the binary evaluation/erasure definitions, Theorem 22's proof, Lemma 34 and the affine-symmetry proofs were read. The earlier random-relation encoding proof and metadata were checked. No finite computation purports to verify the asymptotic capacity theorem.

9 October 2026 — Random scopes retain linear-size normalized cores

Working core-retention theorem; no near-half PC lower bound. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, continue the Reed–Muller lead beyond the cyclic-window obstruction. Can a specified nonlocal scope model retain polynomial encoding in the actual variables of every minimal unsatisfiable core, even after private and irrelevant coordinates are removed? The main missing implication is still global PC closure and unit survival near half.

Route comparison. Better local rank alone was falsified by cyclic scopes. Random scope expansion can instead address a necessary normalization gap. We reuse the existing immunity-peeling and private-projection proofs; the new point is a uniform lower bound on the variables retained after arbitrary allowed normalization, not another local matrix calculation. A proof of small-subfamily satisfiability is not called a PC invariant.

New mathematics. A deterministic expansion/immunity lemma bounds normalized core size. Uniform random logarithmic scopes satisfy its hypotheses together with the already established random-table quality. With high probability every minimal unsatisfiable block core, after any complete private/irrelevant-coordinate normalization, retains a fixed positive fraction of the original variables, remains block-minimal and essential, and has at least two occurrences per variable. Its encoding stays polynomial in its own actual variable count, with logarithmic width and preserved binary local immunity.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The core-retention sub-obligation for this source model is now proved, but no near-half PC barrier or growing-width Frege transfer is established. The terminal higher-occurrence density required by the accepted fixed-width envelope is not proved. This is a non-vacuous logarithmic-width source class, not a replacement of the accepted specification or a conjectural hardness conclusion hidden in admissibility.

General statement. lem:immunity-core-retention is a deterministic all-parameter statement. thm:random-scope-normalized-cores supplies an explicit unbounded random model to which it applies simultaneously for every core and every permitted normalization order.

Deterministic retention

Let Boolean relations \(R_a\) have scopes \(X_a\) of size exactly \(k\). For a row set \(S\), write \(N(S)=\bigcup_{a\in S}X_a\). Let \(I(R_a)\) be the vanishing ideal in the Boolean function algebra over a field \(F\), with multilinear total degree. Assume \(0\le d<k\) and \(I(R_a)\cap B_{\le d}=0\) for every row. Thus no nonzero Boolean function of degree at most \(d\) vanishes on all allowed tuples.

Lemma (immunity core retention). Suppose \(R\ge1\) is an integer, \(1/2\le\beta<1\), and

\[|N(S)|>\beta k|S|\quad(1\le|S|\le R),\qquad d+1\ge2(1-\beta)k.\tag{1}\]

Every subfamily of at most \(R\) rows is satisfiable. If the full system is unsatisfiable, each block-minimal unsatisfiable core has more than \(R\) rows. After any complete sequence of existential projections of currently private coordinates and deletion of semantically irrelevant coordinates, its actual variable count \(n\) satisfies

\[n>R\bigl(d+1-(1-\beta)k\bigr).\tag{2}\]

The terminal system remains block-minimal unsatisfiable, all scopes are essential, every variable occurs in at least two blocks, and all projected row ideals still have zero degree-at-most-\(d\) intersection.

Peeling proof. If a row cannot extend some assignment to at most \(d\) of its coordinates, the indicator of that assignment is a nonzero degree-at-most-\(d\) function vanishing on its allowed set, a contradiction. This is the extension argument from the recorded immunity proof. In a row set \(S\), if \(b\) coordinates occur exactly once, incidence counting gives \(k|S|\ge2|N(S)|-b\), hence \(b>(2\beta-1)k|S|\). Some row therefore has more than \((2\beta-1)k\) private coordinates and fewer than \(2(1-\beta)k\le d+1\) shared coordinates. Solve the other rows recursively and extend to this row on its private coordinates. This proves small-subfamily satisfiability, including the empty induction base.

Normalization proof. The private-projection theorem preserves satisfiability for every subcollection of block labels, so a minimal unsatisfiable core stays minimal. Every terminal relation is the existential projection of its original relation onto some retained scope \(Y_a\subseteq X_a\). A degree-at-most-\(d\) vanishing function on that projection would lift to such a function on the original row. Immunity is therefore preserved. The original allowed sets are nonempty, as otherwise the constant one violates immunity, and their projections remain nonempty. Minimality prevents a projected row from being universal. A forbidden-tuple indicator in a non-universal projected row has degree \(|Y_a|\), so \(|Y_a|\ge d+1\).

Select any \(R\) rows of the core, possible by peeling. Their retained union satisfies

\[\begin{aligned} \left|\bigcup_{a\in S}Y_a\right| &\ge |N(S)|-\sum_{a\in S}|X_a\setminus Y_a|\\ &>\beta kR-R(k-d-1)=R(d+1-(1-\beta)k). \end{aligned}\]

This proves (2). The argument in fact gives the same union estimate for every retained row subset of size at most \(R\). Termination and the final essentiality/two-occurrence properties follow because every operation removes an incidence, until no private or irrelevant coordinate remains. The full original expansion constant is not asserted for the projected incidence graph. \(\square\)

A uniform random-scope witness

Theorem (random normalized cores). For sufficiently large \(v\), put

\[k=\lceil2\log_2v\rceil,\quad m=v+k,\quad d=\lfloor k/2-\sqrt{k}\rfloor,\quad \alpha=e^{-64},\quad R=\left\lfloor\frac{\alpha v}{k}\right\rfloor.\tag{3}\]

Choose each of the \(m\) scopes independently and uniformly among the \(k\)-subsets of \([v]\). Repetitions of scopes are permitted; coordinates within a scope are distinct. Independently for every row occurrence and each of its tuples, allow that tuple with probability one half. With probability \(1-o(1)\), the system is unsatisfiable on all \(v\) actual variables and every minimal unsatisfiable block core, after any permitted complete normalization, has

\[n\ge \gamma v,\qquad \gamma=\alpha/16>0.\tag{4}\]

Every such terminal system has at most \(m\) essential non-universal rows, no singly occurring variables, width at most \(k=O(\log n)\), binary local immunity through \(d\), and canonical encoding length \(O(n^3(\log n)^2)\). No assertion of higher-occurrence density, a near-half PC barrier, or a full source transfer is made.

Uniform union expansion. Set \(\beta=31/32\). For a fixed size \(s\le R\), failure of (1) means that some \(s\) rows lie in a variable set of size \(u=\lfloor\beta ks\rfloor\). If \(u<k\) the event is impossible. Otherwise its probability is at most

\[\binom ms\binom vu\left(\frac{\binom uk}{\binom vk}\right)^s \le \left(\frac{em}{s}\right)^s\left(\frac{ev}{u}\right)^u\left(\frac uv\right)^{ks}.\tag{5}\]

The logarithm of the last two factors, \(u\log(ev/u)+ks\log(u/v)\), increases with \(u\) for \(0<u\le v\). Replacing \(u\) by \(\beta ks\), using \(m\le2v\), \(ks/v\le\alpha\), and, for large \(v\), \(\log(2ev)/k\le1/2\), bounds the logarithm of (5) by

\[ks\left[\frac12+\beta+(1-\beta)\log(\beta\alpha)\right] \le ks\left[\frac12+\frac{31}{32}-2\right]<-ks/2.\tag{6}\]

Summing over \(1\le s\le R\) gives failure at most \(e^{-k/2}/(1-e^{-k/2})=o(1)\). This is uniform over every row subset, not a statement for one independently chosen core.

Coverage and tables. The number of scopes containing one fixed coordinate has distribution \(\operatorname{Bin}(m,k/v)\). A union bound gives probability at most \(v(1+2k)e^{-k+k/v}=o(1)\) that any coordinate occurs fewer than twice. Conditional on any scope realization with this coverage, the simultaneous local-quality theorem gives unsatisfiability, essential rows and zero local ideal through \(d\), with a uniform \(1-o(1)\) bound. Combine the exceptional probabilities; no independence between these good events is required.

Apply the deterministic lemma. For \(k\ge16\), \(d\ge\lfloor k/4\rfloor\). Thus \(d+1>k/4>k/16=2(1-\beta)k\), so peeling applies. Moreover, \(d+1-k/32>k/8\). For sufficiently large \(v\), \(R\ge\alpha v/(2k)\), giving \(n>kR/8\ge\alpha v/16\). All core and normalization choices are covered by the deterministic conclusion, so they can be made after observing the whole instance.

Encoding and degree ledger. Since \(n\ge\gamma v\), \(m=O(n)\), \(2^k=O(n^2)\) and \(k=O(\log n)\). Projection cannot increase the forbidden-tuple count, so the length remains \(O(n^3(\log n)^2)\). Fixing any deterministic core/normalization order independent of the proof-size exponent gives an unbounded family. For every proof field \(K\), a degree-\(D\) refutation of a terminal canonical CNF lifts to the original instance through \(\max\{k,D\}\): its clauses are scalar sums of original forbidden indicators, and the core uses only original block labels. This is the existing projection ledger, not a new hardness assertion. \(\square\)

What has and has not been repaired

Unlike the cyclic control, this source has uniform small-row-set expansion and cannot hide an unsatisfiable core on sublinear actual variable count after the permitted normalization. Local immunity, essentiality, block minimality, at least two occurrences and polynomial encoding survive. The original strongest expansion constant need not survive coordinate removal, and the terminal branching density is not supplied. These differences prevent silently claiming the full fixed-width specification.

The remaining highest-risk implication is a proper global cutoff-closed space for these instances. The old immunity criterion is quantitatively limited to a quarter of the variable count, and respectful expansion to a third in its stated regime. Core retention does not repair either certificate ceiling. The result removes a source-construction obstruction; it does not turn local satisfiability into PC nonrefutability.

Follow-up. Continuing the Reed–Muller lead. With an unbounded normalized source now available, test a concrete global reduction or dual space on it, using the retained original-to-terminal projection maps and accounting for every multiplier. Do not spend another cycle sharpening the small constant in (4) or rerunning local rank matrices.

Sub-ideas

  • Construct a multiplication-compatible global space for the normalized good-table cores, or prove a scoped obstruction to the proposed space. The source now has a uniform actual-variable retention bound, but its terminal strong expansion and branching density are not automatic.

Scoped auxiliary import

At the user’s request, all five supplied full records were read at auxiliary commit 5dbddd2f67cd39675f0fba5f38e9a2fab1dea849. The pinned scope ledger records paths and hashes. This is context for the existing queues, not a second developed route or a whole-branch merge.

  • Family-112 pruning. The minimal-wide-clause count gives literal narrow divisors on the full cube, avoiding direct reduction of real reciprocals. Its selected-input lemma retains original PC/NS degrees only with the supplied degree-adapted multiple identities and complete fresh blocks. The affine version is fibre-specific. Retain this for the queued restriction question; hard survival and arbitrary nonlinear input coverage remain open.
  • Binary slices and flow moments. Retain the exact Lucas slice seed, its stated charged-flow lift and nondegenerate quotient pairing as representations of an existing old design. Graph-vertex counts are not Boolean-variable counts; moments through twice the cutoff do not assert a pseudoideal at twice that cutoff. The pin-degree loss, even conditional fibres and odd-mass easy-tree control remain attached.
  • Radical rank splitting. Retain the rectangular-map bound, including stacked cross-ranks and image radicals, as a possible overlap tool once one common nondegenerate space is supplied. It constructs neither the proper invariant nor the unit. Small real variance still gives no field-rank bound, even with odd rational denominators.

Verification gate. The sole fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed after two clarifications: independent Bernoulli-half tuple sampling is explicit, and the valid endpoint at beta equal to one half is included. It checked every core and normalization order, row-label preservation, expansion probabilities and floors, actual-variable and encoding bounds, the one-way degree ledger and unchanged goal assessment. No unresolved gap or unused hypothesis remains. The proof is analytic; no finite sweep was run to simulate an asymptotic verification. The complete random-table, private-projection and immunity-peeling records were read with dependency metadata. No numerical experiment is claimed to verify the asymptotic source theorem, and no encoding rule is changed.

Process assessment. The existing peeling mechanism was reused rather than renamed as new. The added counting argument follows every retained row through normalization and proves an all-core bound, while leaving the main lower-bound obligation explicitly open.

9 October 2026 — Whole-row reductions saturate random supports near half

Working method obstruction; no PC degree upper bound. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, test an explicit global construction on the Reed–Muller random-relation source. Associate a monomial to a variable context, enlarging it by a whole row whenever a local leading monomial can reduce a monomial supported in that context. Could the resulting satisfiable contexts supply a global reduction near half? This tests the missing multiplication-compatible invariant, not another local-rank estimate.

Route comparison. Direct random-table hardness remains possible, but no global cancellation argument is known. Whole-row support closure is a concrete method repair to test against the source now known to retain linear-size cores. A first coarse test would activate a row at the certified immunity threshold. We strengthen the test to actual local leading ideals: even waiting until every monomial at a larger degree is reducible causes saturation. Thus improving the local immunity estimate or choosing another degree-compatible monomial order does not repair this particular construction. Exact boundary relations or a different source remain possible.

New mathematics. Boolean evaluation duality converts the already established erasure estimate into simultaneous local interpolation through degree just above half the row width. A two-batch random-scope argument shows that the associated whole-row support rule sends a fixed near-half seed to all variables with probability tending to one. This holds for every choice of degree-compatible local orders on the same good instance.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The theorem rules out a specified satisfiable-context construction on the original random ensemble. It proves neither an upper bound on actual PC degree nor a saturation statement for every adaptively chosen normalized core. The source remains non-vacuous; the accepted structural specification is unchanged. A proper global space can contain local reductions without absorbing entire scopes.

General statement. lem:dual-erasure-local-reduction gives exact evaluation duality for every Boolean allowed set. thm:random-row-support-saturation gives the asymptotic all-order obstruction for the specified random ensemble and every fixed seed sequence in its stated range.

Local interpolation from the complementary erasure problem

Let \(\Omega=\{0,1\}^k\), and work over \(\mathbb F_2\). Write \(E_j\) for evaluations of multilinear polynomials of degree at most \(j\) on \(\Omega\), with \(E_{-1}=0\). For \(R\subseteq\Omega\), let \(I(R)\) be its vanishing ideal in the Boolean function algebra.

Lemma (dual erasure reduction). For \(0\le t\le k\), evaluation \(E_t\to\mathbb F_2^R\) is surjective if and only if no nonzero member of \(E_{k-t-1}\) vanishes on \(\Omega\setminus R\). When surjectivity holds, every squarefree monomial of degree greater than \(t\) is the leading monomial of an element of \(I(R)\), for every degree-compatible monomial order.

Proof. Under the nondegenerate coordinate dot product on \(\mathbb F_2^\Omega\),

\[E_t^\perp=E_{k-t-1}.\tag{1}\]

Indeed, the product of one monomial from each side has degree less than \(k\), hence misses a coordinate and has even evaluation sum. The dimensions add to \(2^k\), by binomial symmetry, proving equality. Failure of surjectivity onto \(R\) is equivalent to a nonzero vector on \(R\) annihilating every restricted element of \(E_t\). Extending that vector by zero gives exactly a nonzero vector in \(E_t^\perp\) supported on \(R\), proving the equivalence. If \(x_U\) has degree greater than \(t\), surjectivity supplies a polynomial \(p\) of degree at most \(t\) agreeing with it on \(R\). Then \(x_U-p\in I(R)\), with leading monomial \(x_U\) in every degree-compatible order. The assertion uses only this ideal membership, not a claimed low-degree PC derivation of that polynomial. \(\square\)

Use the proved erasure tail with \(d=\lfloor k/2-\sqrt{k}\rfloor\) and \(t=k-d-1\), for sufficiently large \(k\). The complement of an independently half-allowed table has the same distribution as the table. Therefore, with the same failure probability \(\delta_k=(2^k)^{-\omega(1)}\), evaluation through degree \(t\) is surjective. For \(m=v+k\) tables and \(k=\lceil2\log_2v\rceil\), a union bound proves this simultaneously for all rows. Put

\[r=t+1=k-d=k/2+\sqrt{k}+O(1).\tag{2}\]

Every monomial on at least \(r\) coordinates of a row is locally reducible, irrespective of the choice of degree-compatible order.

Two batches fill the support

Theorem (random support saturation). Choose \(m=v+k\) independent uniform \(k\)-subsets \(X_a\) of \([v]\), and independent Bernoulli-half allowed tables, with \(k,d,r\) as above. Fix a deterministic seed \(S_v\subseteq[v]\) of size \(\lfloor v/2-s_v\rfloor\), where \(s_v\ge0\) and \(s_v=o(v/\sqrt{k})\). Starting from this seed, repeatedly add \(X_a\) whenever \(|X_a\cap S|\ge r\). With probability \(1-o(1)\), two batches of additions suffice to obtain all of \([v]\): first use only the first \(\lfloor m/2\rfloor\) rows and their intersections with the initial seed; then use only the remaining rows and their intersections with the enlarged seed.

On the same event, the original instance is unsatisfiable and has the previously proved local quality and core retention. For every selection of degree-compatible local orders, any monotone context rule that contains the seed and absorbs a whole row whenever the context supports a locally reducible monomial contains all variables. If it then includes all rows whose scopes lie in that context, its context is unsatisfiable. This contradicts the requirement that every degree-at-most-\(|S_v|\) monomial have a satisfiable context. The quantifier is a fixed seed sequence, not simultaneous uniformity over all adaptive seeds or all normalized cores.

A positive activation probability. For a fixed \(j\notin S_v\), conditional on \(j\in X_a\), the other \(k-1\) coordinates are sampled uniformly without replacement from \(v-1\) positions. Their intersection count with \(S_v\) is within total variation \(O(k^2/v)\) of \(Z\sim\operatorname{Bin}(k-1,p)\), where \(p=|S_v|/(v-1)=1/2+o(k^{-1/2})\). Couple independent draws to draws without replacement until the first repeated index; a union bound on collisions gives this error.

The law of \(k-1\) independent Bernoulli-\(p\) bits is within \(o(1)\) total variation of fair bits. To see this without a central-limit assumption, let \(L\) be its likelihood ratio relative to fair bits. Then \(\mathbb E(L-1)^2=(1+4(p-1/2)^2)^{k-1}-1=o(1)\), so Cauchy–Schwarz bounds total variation by half its square root.

For fair \(Z_0\sim\operatorname{Bin}(k-1,1/2)\), \(\Pr[Z_0\ge r]\) is bounded below by an absolute positive constant for all large \(k\). Here is an elementary bound. Let \(N=k-1\), \(h=\lfloor N/2\rfloor\), and \(b_j=2^{-N}\binom Nj\). Chebyshev places at least \(3/4\) of the mass within distance \(\sqrt N\) of \(N/2\), on at most \(2\sqrt N+2\) integers. Since the central value is maximal, \(b_h\ge1/(4\sqrt N)\) for all sufficiently large \(N\). For \(1\le i\le2\sqrt N+3\),

\[\frac{b_{h+i}}{b_{h+i-1}}=\frac{N-h-i+1}{h+i}\ge1-\frac{10}{\sqrt N}\]

for large \(N\). Multiplying at most \(2\sqrt N+3\) such factors, and using \(\log(1-x)\ge-2x\) for \(0\le x\le1/2\), gives \(b_{h+i}\ge e^{-50}b_h\) throughout this range, eventually. At least \(\sqrt N/4\) integers lie between \(h+\lceil3\sqrt N/2\rceil\) and \(h+\lfloor2\sqrt N\rfloor\); all exceed \(r\) eventually by (2). Hence \(\Pr[Z_0\ge r]\ge e^{-50}/16\). Combining the two total-variation errors, fix \(c=e^{-50}/32>0\); for sufficiently large \(v\),

\[\Pr[|X_a\cap S_v|\ge r\mid j\in X_a]\ge c.\tag{3}\]

First batch. Let \(m_1=\lfloor m/2\rfloor\ge v/2\) eventually, and let \(S_1\) be the union of the initial seed and the scopes from this batch activated by that initial seed. Independence of scopes and (3) give, for every \(j\notin S_v\),

\[\Pr[j\notin S_1]\le(1-ck/v)^{m_1}\le e^{-ck/2}\le v^{-c/\log2}.\tag{4}\]

Set \(\eta=c/(2\log2)>0\). The expected number of uncovered coordinates is at most \(v^{1-2\eta}\), so Markov gives \(\Pr[|[v]\setminus S_1|>v^{1-\eta}]\le v^{-\eta}=o(1)\). The constants are deliberately not optimized.

Second batch. It is independent of \(S_1\). Conditional on any \(S_1\) with complement of size at most \(v^{1-\eta}\), a row containing an uncovered \(j\) has all its other coordinates in \(S_1\) with probability at least

\[1-\frac{(k-1)v^{1-\eta}}{v-1}=1-o(1),\tag{5}\]

by a union bound. Since \(r\le k-1\) eventually, that is sufficient for activation. With \(m_2=m-m_1\ge v/2\), another independence and union bound give

\[\Pr[S_2\ne[v]\mid S_1]\le v\exp\!\left(-\frac{m_2k}{v}(1-o(1))\right) \le v^{1-1/\log2+o(1)}=o(1).\tag{6}\]

This proves saturation. A fully iterative closure contains these two batch enlargements, so it also saturates.

Local ideals and source properties. Intersect this scope event with simultaneous interpolation above and with the good-source event. Each is an unconditional probability-\(1-o(1)\) event in the joint ensemble; their intersection follows by a union bound. Core retention is not asserted conditional on an arbitrary scope realization. Every row with at least \(r\) present coordinates then has a supported monomial in its local leading ideal, under every degree-compatible order. Any whole-row rule triggered by local reducibility therefore contains the two-batch closure. With all variables present, including every contained row gives the original unsatisfiable system. This is failure of the imposed satisfiable-context requirement. It is not an ordinary PC derivation or a proof that the unit lies in a smaller algebraic invariant. \(\square\)

Consequences for the route

At approximation depth \(\ell\ge3\), the required deficit \(s=o(v/(\log v)^{\ell-2})\) is in the theorem's range. It also covers the proposed stronger square-root-scale deficit. At depth two it does not cover every permitted \(s=o(v)\). The polynomial canonical encoding and all-core retention coexist with this failure on the original instance; it is not a padding or cyclic-width example. No conclusion is transferred to the incidence geometry of an adaptively selected normalized core.

The repair test matters: replacing a coarse immunity threshold by actual leading-ideal reducibility and allowing arbitrary degree-compatible orders still does not rescue whole-row absorption. A different structural source might avoid this growth, but avoiding it alone would not ensure global hardness: the cyclic-window control already does that poorly. We retain the source and trade away mandatory satisfiable whole contexts. The next attempt must work with exact polynomial kernel relations in one ambient space, or establish a direct random-table survival theorem; it must not relabel this support closure.

Follow-up. Continuing the Reed–Muller lead. The remaining test is whether the local interpolation relations can be combined in a proper global space without absorbing whole scopes. Neither interpolation nor a large local nullspace ensures that the marked unit survives multiplication closure.

Sub-ideas

  • For the random-table source, test a common-quotient construction that retains exact local interpolation relations rather than whole satisfiable scopes. Prove its multiplication compatibility and unit survival, or identify an actual obstruction in that quotient; the current saturation theorem does not address normalized-core geometry or all possible reductions.

Verification gate. The sole fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed with one wording correction, now incorporated: the final good-event intersection is unconditional, not a claim of core retention conditional on arbitrary scopes. It found no other gap or unused substantive hypothesis and accepted the fixed-seed/all-order quantifiers and unchanged goal assessment. Exact dependencies read: the simultaneous erasure proof and source ledger, core-retention theorem, fixed-cutoff transport and the earlier whole-vertex obstruction. The only imported asymptotic estimate is the already recorded complementary erasure tail. The new arguments are analytic, introduce no encoding or inference rule and require no numerical sweep.

Process assessment. The test targets the missing global construction and develops its natural local-order repair before rejecting it; it does not count another method obstruction as a near-half lower bound. The repeated unchanged implication is carried to the scheduled review.
