9 October 2026 — Local interpolation gives an actual midpoint upper bound

Working PC upper bounds; the near-half lower-bound goal remains open. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, replace whole-row support absorption by actual local polynomial relations in a common quotient. Does coverage by their leading monomials force a low-degree refutation, or could the uncovered monomials form the required invariant? The test concerns the global multiplication step, with original input degrees paid.

Route comparison. The previous support obstruction is not an actual PC bound. A direct algebraic test can distinguish those conclusions. We test local interpolation relations and the degree needed to make the induced variable operators commute. This develops the common-quotient repair rather than declaring every method impossible after the support failure. The resulting upper bound will be compared with the target before any specification revision.

New mathematics. An all-field interpolation/coverage criterion gives degree at most one above the coverage cutoff, with the original width floor explicit. Its proof constructs commuting variable operators on one quotient of the actual bounded PC space; no uncharged critical-pair step is used. For the logarithmic random-table source, uniform scope coverage and the complementary erasure theorem give binary PC degree at most \(v/2+3v/\sqrt{k}+O(1)\), where \(k=\lceil2\log_2v\rceil\).

Goal progress. Unchanged. The upper bound lies above half, so it does not exclude the requested barrier below half. Neither absence of leading coverage nor local erasure injectivity proves a surviving PC invariant. The bound counts the original \(v\) variables and is not automatically an upper bound in the actual variables of an adaptively normalized core. Both the near-half family and the full transfer remain open.

General statement. prop:local-interpolation-degree-ceiling is a deterministic all-field statement for arbitrary relation systems with its stated coverage property. thm:random-table-midpoint-ceiling applies it to the full random ensemble at unbounded sizes.

Source comparison and exact conventions

Salizzoni’s Proposition 3.10 bounds mutant solving degree by the maximum of degree of regularity plus one and input degrees, over any field and for a degree-compatible order. Its Definitions 2.4–2.5 use the linear space closed under monomial multiplication within the cutoff, which equals ordinary PC closure by successive variable multiplications. Theorem 3.8 and its proof justify the extra degree. The statement, definitions and proof through Proposition 3.10 were read. This identifies the algebraic principle as known, not new. Below is a direct Boolean quotient proof of the paid local specialization; no genericity, characteristic-zero lex-plus-powers result or heuristic solving-degree estimate is used.

Work in the ordinary ring \(F[x_1,\ldots,x_v]\), not with free multilinear reduction. Include every Boolean equation \(x_i^2-x_i\). Write \(C_D\) for the vector space of polynomials derivable by ordinary degree-at-most-\(D\) PC from the canonical relation clauses and Boolean equations.

A paid interpolation criterion

Proposition (local interpolation ceiling). Let an unsatisfiable Boolean relation system have scopes \(X_a\) of size at most \(w\). For each row choose an integer \(0\le t_a<|X_a|\) such that evaluation of its multilinear polynomials of degree at most \(t_a\) onto the allowed tuples is surjective. Put \(r_a=t_a+1\). Suppose an integer \(E\) satisfies \( \max\{w,2\}\le E\le v\), and every \(E\)-subset \(S\subseteq[v]\) has a row with \(|S\cap X_a|\ge r_a\). Then the original canonical CNF has an ordinary PC refutation through degree \(E+1\) over \(F\).

Local relations and their cost. For each \(U\subseteq X_a\) with \(|U|=r_a\), choose \(p_U\) of degree at most \(t_a\) agreeing with \(x_U\) on the allowed tuples. The multilinear polynomial \(g_U=x_U-p_U\) vanishes there. By the Boolean point-indicator expansion, it is exactly a scalar linear combination of the row’s forbidden-tuple indicators, as ordinary multilinear polynomials. Thus it has a derivation through degree at most \(|X_a|\le w\). This does not claim that its smaller final degree pays for its original derivation.

Every ordinary monomial \(M\) of degree \(E\) has a relation

\[M-\rho(M)\in C_E,\qquad \deg\rho(M)\le E-1.\tag{1}\]

If \(M\) is squarefree, coverage gives \(U\) as above contained in its support. Multiply \(g_U\) by \(M/x_U\): the leading degree is \(E\), every other term has degree at most \(E-1\), and the derivation costs at most \(E\). If \(M\) is not squarefree, multiply some \(x_i^2-x_i\) by \(M/x_i^2\) to obtain the same conclusion. Fix these choices and extend \(\rho\) linearly to polynomials of degree at most \(E\), making it the identity through degree \(E-1\). Then \(f-\rho(f)\in C_E\) throughout this domain.

One common quotient. Set

\[V=F[x]_{\le E-1}/(C_{E+1}\cap F[x]_{\le E-1}),\qquad T_i[p]=[\rho(x_ip)].\tag{2}\]

This is well-defined: if \(p\in C_{E+1}\) has degree at most \(E-1\), then \(x_ip\in C_{E+1}\), and (1) puts \(\rho(x_ip)\) in that space too. The operators commute. Indeed, for a representative \(p\) of degree at most \(E-1\), the difference \(x_jp-\rho(x_jp)\) is in \(C_E\) and has degree at most \(E\). Multiplying it by \(x_i\) costs at most \(E+1\). Applying (1) once more shows that the low-degree representative of \(T_iT_j[p]\) differs from \(x_ix_jp\) by an element of \(C_{E+1}\). The same holds with \(i,j\) swapped; subtraction gives equality in \(V\). Likewise \(T_i^2=T_i\), since \(x_i^2p-x_ip\) is a Boolean-axiom multiple of degree at most \(E+1\). These calculations explicitly account for the extra degree; a full-ideal equality alone would not suffice.

Unit and original inputs. Let \(e=[1]\). For each monomial \(M\) of degree at most \(E\), successive application of the operators gives \(M(T)e=[\rho(M)]\). Induct on the degree: at each step the previous representative differs from the previous monomial by a member of \(C_{E+1}\) of degree at most \(E-1\); multiplying it by the next variable is legal. Thus every original clause \(f\), whose degree is at most \(w\le E\), satisfies \(f(T)e=0\).

The operators define a representation of the ordinary polynomial ring, because they commute. This uses the earlier full-representation control; the new point is that paid local coverage forces it by cutoff \(E+1\). All Boolean equations also annihilate \(e\). The input ideal contains one: for every Boolean assignment choose a violated clause, multiply it by the missing literals to obtain that assignment’s full indicator, and sum the indicators. This is used only as a full-ring identity, not as a low-degree derivation. Applying the commuting operators to that identity gives \(e=0\), since each generator annihilates \(e\). By (2), \(1\in C_{E+1}\), proving the asserted ordinary PC degree. \(\square\)

Apply the criterion to random tables

Theorem (random midpoint ceiling). In the ensemble with \(k=\lceil2\log_2v\rceil\), \(m=v+k\), independent uniform \(k\)-scopes and independent half-allowed tuples, with probability \(1-o(1)\) the original instance is unsatisfiable on all \(v\) actual variables and has binary PC degree at most

\[\left\lceil\frac v2+\frac{3v}{\sqrt{k}}\right\rceil+1.\tag{3}\]

Proof. Set \(d=\lfloor k/2-\sqrt{k}\rfloor\), \(t=k-d-1\) and \(r=t+1=k-d\). The complementary erasure argument supplies simultaneous degree-\(t\) local interpolation with probability \(1-o(1)\). Let \(E=\lceil v/2+3v/\sqrt{k}\rceil\), which lies between \(k\) and \(v\) for sufficiently large \(v\). For any fixed \(E\)-subset \(S\), the row intersection \(Z=|X_a\cap S|\) has mean \(kE/v\ge k/2+3\sqrt{k}\) and variance at most \(k/4\): for sampling without replacement its variance is \(kp(1-p)(v-k)/(v-1)\), where \(p=E/v\). Since \(r\le k/2+\sqrt{k}+1\), Chebyshev gives

\[\Pr[Zfor sufficiently large \(k\). Scopes are independent, so the probability that this \(S\) has no activating row is at most \(8^{-m}\). A union bound over at most \(2^v\) choices yields failure at most \(2^v8^{-m}\le4^{-v}\). Thus every \(E\)-subset is covered simultaneously. Intersect with interpolation, unsatisfiability and actual-variable coverage from the source theorem; the random scopes cover every variable twice with probability \(1-o(1)\), as shown in the retention record. The proposition gives (3). No independence between these final good events is needed. \(\square\)

What the bound teaches

The criterion is an actual upper bound, unlike the preceding whole-row saturation theorem. Its structural reason is complete coverage of all top-degree monomials by paid degree-lowering relations. The quotient becomes a representation of the full Boolean algebra one degree later, which cannot preserve a unit annihilated by an unsatisfiable input. Any candidate with this coverage below the proposed barrier is excluded, regardless of its origin; stronger local interpolation can make the obstruction worse.

For the current ensemble the bound is \(v/2+O(v/\sqrt{\log v})\), above the desired cutoff. It therefore rules out no near-half candidate here and supplies no reason to demand a new restriction in the accepted specification. Avoiding the coverage criterion is necessary in its applicable range, not sufficient for hardness. The much harder missing direction is to prove that remaining monomials survive all cross-row cancellations and multiplication. No arbitrary independent local orders are combined, and no leading-ideal heuristic is declared an invariant.

The canonical formula length stays \(O(v^3(\log v)^2)\), with no new variables. Refutations of a normalized core lift to the original, but this original-instance upper bound does not reverse that map or preserve the midpoint in the core’s own variable count. The full growing-width Frege ledger is still absent.

Follow-up. Continuing the Reed–Muller lead, with rotation at this four-entry spell boundary. The spell supplied source quality and normalization, and rejected a concrete global support method, but no necessary near-half implication advanced. It therefore earns no extension. The exact common-quotient lower-bound question remains queued; continue next with the existing code/binomial head, carrying these algebraic coverage tests as controls.

Sub-ideas

  • For the random-relation source below the midpoint, determine whether surviving monomials in the local leading-ideal complement support a proper multiplication-closed space after cross-row cancellations. The upper-bound criterion is one-way; an uncovered monomial does not supply a nonzero unit or a lower bound.

Verification gate. The sole fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed with no required mathematical fixes. It checked the local derivation cost, common quotient, commutation and idempotence, original-input and unit argument, uniform scope bound and original-variable scope. The condition that E is at most v is retained to make the coverage test nonvacuous, not as an essential algebraic hypothesis. The exact complementary-erasure and source records were read, as were Salizzoni’s definitions and proof through Proposition 3.10. The direct quotient proof tracks every multiplication in the ordinary ring. No new encoding or syntactic inference rule is introduced, and no numerical sweep is used to certify an asymptotic assertion.

Process assessment. Searching before naming the mechanism identified the known degree-of-regularity theorem. The paid specialization and random application are kept distinct from novelty or goal progress, and the queue rotates rather than extending a spell on unchanged hardness.

9 October 2026 — Two-state domains force binomial invariants to select a point

Working method obstruction; no actual PC upper bound. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, develop the queued code/binomial criterion outside graph-star locality. Can its term-equivalence invariant absorb the actual Boolean domain equations of a field-consistent but Boolean-empty code coset? The missing implication is a proper multiplication-compatible space near half the actual variables, not merely a phase relation on the full modular grid.

Route comparison. The earlier rank and covering-radius ceilings leave some code regimes open. Directly improving their distance or expansion would be premature if the binomial invariant cannot include the domain. We first audit the primary criterion, then allow the stronger repair in which domain and input polynomials need only belong to its linear span. We also test both ordinary Boolean coordinates and affine two-state character coordinates. The resulting obstruction concerns the method; it supplies no new reason to remove these formula families from the structural specification.

New mathematics. A proper degree-\(D\) pseudoideal spanned by polynomials with at most two monomials, containing Booleanity, has a Boolean point annihilating its entire degree-at-most-\(D-1\) part. For arbitrary two-state domains whose two values have nonzero sum, the same conclusion holds. When both values of every coordinate are nonzero, the whole space must be the degree-\(D\) kernel of evaluation at one point. These statements allow zero monomials and arbitrary field scalars, so they are not merely a syntactic objection to one trinomial input.

Goal progress. Unchanged. This excludes binomially spanned proper pseudoideals in the stated coordinate systems as near-half lower-bound witnesses. General PC pseudoideals need not be binomially spanned. No actual low-degree refutation of a candidate family follows, and neither lower-bound goal is solved. The code route remains open through richer linear relations or a separately costed representation.

General statement. lem:boolean-binomial-point holds over every field and every \(D\ge2\). thm:binomial-domain-rigidity extends it to all products of the stated two-point domains and includes the stronger full-space conclusion when both coordinate values are nonzero.

The source interface

Buss–Grigoriev–Impagliazzo–Pitassi’s Section 5, Theorem 6 and Lemmas 7–9 use a scalar-compatible equivalence of nonzero monomial terms, closed under legal variable multiplication. Its binomial span is a pseudoideal; avoiding unequal constant identifications preserves the unit. Lemma 7 removes cancelled monomials from a representation. The definitions, complete proof and Sections 6–7 applications were read, with the Boolean encoding and reduction direction checked. The graph theorem uses the full root-of-unity domain, not a two-value subdomain. Its linear lower bound in graph vertices is not a near-half theorem in total Boolean variables.

The following test permits more than direct binomial inputs: arbitrary input polynomials may lie in the span. It also allows monomial generators, covering an attempted zero-class repair.

Cancellation and the Boolean chart

Write \(P_{\le D}=F[x_1,\ldots,x_v]_{\le D}\) in the ordinary polynomial ring. A degree-\(D\) pseudoideal \(S\subseteq P_{\le D}\) is a linear space with \(x_i f\in S\) whenever \(f\in S\) and \(\deg f\le D-1\). Call it binomially spanned when it has a spanning set of polynomials with at most two nonzero monomials; one-monomial polynomials are expressly allowed. Proper means \(1\notin S\).

Cancellation fact. If a polynomial \(f\) lies in a binomially spanned space, it is a sum of elements of that space, each having at most two monomials, all drawn from the support of \(f\). To see this, use monomials as graph vertices and generators \(aM+bN\) with \(ab\ne0\) as weighted edges. In a connected component, edge relations express every vertex as a nonzero scalar times one chosen vertex. A singleton generator or an inconsistent cycle kills that entire component. Otherwise the quotient of that component is one-dimensional, with fixed nonzero vertex weights. The intersection of its kernel with any chosen support is spanned by pair differences on that support; a killed component instead contributes the individual supported vertices. Summing over components proves the assertion. This is the source’s cancellation mechanism with singleton generators included, not a new PC inference rule.

Lemma (Boolean binomial point). Let \(D\ge2\) and let \(S\) be a proper binomially spanned degree-\(D\) pseudoideal containing every \(x_i^2-x_i\). Define \(a_i=1\) if \(x_i-1\in S\), and \(a_i=0\) otherwise. Then

\[f(a)=0\qquad\text{for every }f\in S\cap P_{\le D-1}.\tag{1}\]

Monomials proportional to one. Suppose \(M-c\in S\), with \(M\) a monomial of degree at most \(D-1\) and \(c\ne0\). If \(x_i\) divides \(M\), then \(x_iM-M=(M/x_i)(x_i^2-x_i)\in S\) through degree at most \(D\). Multiplying \(M-c\) by \(x_i\), and subtracting this Boolean multiple and \(M-c\), puts \(c(1-x_i)\) in \(S\). Hence every variable in \(M\) has \(a_i=1\). Telescoping their relations \(x_i-1\), including repeated factors, gives \(M-1\in S\) through degree at most \(\deg M\). Properness now forces \(c=1\). Conversely, \(M(a)=1\) implies \(M-1\in S\) by the same telescoping argument. The constant monomial is included.

Apply cancellation. Consider an at-most-two-term polynomial \(g\in S\) of degree at most \(D-1\). A singleton \(cM\) cannot have \(M(a)=1\), since then \(M-1\in S\) would give a nonzero constant in \(S\). For \(g=cM+dN\) with \(cd\ne0\), if both monomials evaluate to zero then \(g(a)=0\). Otherwise suppose \(M(a)=1\). Subtract \(c(M-1)\) to get \(c+dN\in S\). The preceding paragraph gives \(N(a)=1\) and \(-c/d=1\), so again \(g(a)=0\). The cancellation fact expresses any \(f\in S\cap P_{\le D-1}\) using such low-degree elements. This proves (1). \(\square\)

Consequently no such \(S\) can contain an unsatisfiable Boolean input whose non-Boolean axioms all have degree at most \(D-1\). This is a restriction on binomial spanning, not on all pseudoideals.

Affine two-state charts

Theorem (two-state binomial rigidity). Let \(F\) be any field. For each coordinate choose distinct \(u_i,v_i\in F\) with \(u_i+v_i\ne0\). Let \(D\ge2\), and let \(S\) be a proper binomially spanned degree-\(D\) pseudoideal containing

\[(y_i-u_i)(y_i-v_i)=y_i^2-(u_i+v_i)y_i+u_iv_i.\tag{2}\]

There is a point \(a\in\prod_i\{u_i,v_i\}\) annihilating \(S\cap P_{\le D-1}\). If all \(u_iv_i\ne0\), then for one such point

\[S=\{f\in P_{\le D}:f(a)=0\}.\tag{3}\]

Nonzero coordinate values. Fix \(i\) with \(u_iv_i\ne0\). In the vector-space quotient by \(S\), the three monomials \(1,y_i,y_i^2\) are all nonzero. Indeed, \(y_i\in S\) would imply \(y_i^2\in S\), so (2) would put the nonzero constant \(u_iv_i\) in \(S\). If \(y_i^2\in S\), (2) would give \(y_i-c\in S\), where \(c=u_iv_i/(u_i+v_i)\ne0\). Legal multiplication through degree two then gives \(y_i^2-c^2\in S\), again contradicting properness.

The graph description in the cancellation fact makes distinct surviving components linearly independent, each one-dimensional. Equation (2) is a relation with three nonzero coefficients among three nonzero vertex images. No component can contain only one of them, so all three belong to one component. In particular \(y_i-a_i\in S\) for some \(a_i\ne0\). Multiplying and combining gives \(y_i^2-a_i^2\in S\). Subtracting from (2) yields the constant \((a_i-u_i)(a_i-v_i)\) in \(S\), so \(a_i\in\{u_i,v_i\}\).

Remaining zero-state coordinates. Substitute these forced values in all polynomials, writing the map as \(\pi\). For every polynomial \(f\) of degree at most \(D\), telescoping the linear relations gives \(f-\pi(f)\in S\), without exceeding \(D\). Thus \(\pi(S)=S\cap F[y_j:u_jv_j=0]_{\le D}\). This is a proper pseudoideal and is binomially spanned: substitution of nonzero constants sends each at-most-two-term generator to an at-most-two-term polynomial. Each remaining domain is \(\{0,c_j\}\) with \(c_j\ne0\). Rescale \(y_j=c_jx_j\), which preserves degree, binomial spanning and pseudoideal closure, and apply the Boolean lemma. Combining its point with the forced coordinates proves the first conclusion.

If there are no zero-state coordinates, every \(y_i-a_i\) lies in \(S\). Telescoping gives \(f-f(a)\in S\) for every \(f\) of degree at most \(D\). Properness then forces \(f(a)=0\) for every \(f\in S\), and the reverse inclusion follows from the same identity. This proves (3). \(\square\)

Tests and consequences for the code route

Characteristic two. Distinct two-state values automatically have nonzero sum. Thus every coordinatewise affine recoding of Boolean variables over a characteristic-two field falls under the theorem. The recoding \(y_i=u_i+(v_i-u_i)x_i\) is invertible and affine, uses exactly the same variables and preserves ordinary PC degree: multiplying by an affine polynomial is variable multiplication followed by a linear combination. Domain quadratics differ from original Booleanity by a nonzero scalar. At \(D\ge\max\{2,w+1\}\), a proper binomially spanned pseudoideal cannot contain all transformed width-\(w\) canonical axioms of an unsatisfiable formula.

In particular the character chart \(y_i=1+(\zeta-1)x_i\), with a nontrivial odd-order root \(\zeta\) in characteristic two, has two nonzero states. Even allowing the three-term Boolean domain in the span forces (3). Root-order equations are not needed for this conclusion. Enlarging the coefficient field or improving the code’s distance, rank or covering radius cannot repair this binomial-span method. Omitting the domain does not repair the proof: a field-consistent modular coset has actual root-valued solutions, so its full-grid phase algebra says nothing about Boolean emptiness.

Non-vacuity and boundary controls. For any allowed point \(a\), its degree-\(D\) evaluation kernel is a proper binomially spanned pseudoideal: monomials evaluating to zero and differences \(M-M(a)\) span it. Thus the theorem classifies nonempty examples rather than contradictory assumptions. The nonzero-sum hypothesis is essential for the full evaluation-kernel conclusion (3). Over a field of characteristic other than two, on one variable with states \(\{1,-1\}\), the truncated ideal generated by \(y^2-1\) is binomially spanned and proper but contains neither \(y-1\) nor \(y+1\). Reduction by parity leaves both \(1\) and \(y\) independent. This matches the source’s sign-coordinate setting instead of refuting it.

Repair outcome. The span-membership repair, zero-class allowance, arbitrary scalar coefficients and all affine two-state charts have now been tested. They do not produce the required invariant in characteristic two. Larger local alphabets or nonlinear multi-variable encodings are outside the theorem, but they must supply their own actual-variable and degree ledger. More general vector relations in a common quotient remain possible. The source’s full root-of-unity domain is outside the two-state hypothesis; its lower bound is not contradicted.

Follow-up. Continuing the code/binomial item. The primary criterion has been read beyond the earlier orientation screen, and its direct Boolean-domain interface is now ruled out even after the stated repairs. The highest-risk implication still needs a richer invariant or a paid representation outside these charts. The scheduled route review comes next and must compare those architectures; do not infer lack of all mechanisms from this scoped obstruction.

Sub-ideas

  • For the surviving code regimes, test a relation space with genuine multi-term dependencies, or a nonlinear multi-variable encoding whose binomial structure survives the actual Boolean domain. Supply the full variable/degree ledger and a nonzero truncated unit; the affine two-state and zero-class repairs are excluded by the present theorem.

Verification gate. The sole fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed with no required mathematical corrections or unused hypotheses. It checked singleton generators, arbitrary scalars, all cutoff steps, mixed charts and the characteristic-two transport. Its optional clarification is incorporated: the opposite-state control refutes the full evaluation-kernel conclusion without the nonzero-sum condition, not the weaker point-annihilation conclusion. The unchanged goal assessment was accepted. The primary Laurent definition, cancellation and pseudoideal proofs, graph/domain conventions and reduction direction were read. The earlier character transport, consistent-coset and covering-radius proofs were reread at their exact scope. All controls here are analytic; no encoding change or new syntactic inference rule is used.

Process assessment. The test develops two representation repairs before rejecting the interface. It changes the admissible proof machinery, not the structural family specification, and preserves the distinction between a method obstruction and an actual degree upper bound.

9 October 2026 — Convert domain rigidity into a non-vacuous structural screen

Working upper bounds and goal-level review; near-half hardness remains open. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. This scheduled goal-level review follows six research entries with the two necessary open statements unchanged. Which method requirements should be dropped, and which cheap consequences now justify an actual restriction of the formula specification? The user explicitly asks to derive inexpensive corollaries that strengthen the specification, rather than leave them implicit.

New mathematics. Boolean binomial inputs have degree at most their maximum degree plus one, and a paid binomial presentation gives the corresponding upper bound for an original formula. More concretely, a common polarity making all row relations intersection-closed gives PC degree at most the original width. The existing positive-linear-degree witness therefore survives exclusion of this structural class with no change of width or variables. A separate Kneser-core construction supplies unbounded four-colourability controls without short odd cycles; it supplies no PC lower bound.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The new necessary screen is non-vacuous but far from sufficient. The recent random-table results control local quality and normalization and give an upper bound above half, not the lower bound below half. The domain theorem rules out a proof class, not all representations or candidate families. Complete Frege transfer remains open.

General statement. cor:boolean-binomial-degree-ceiling and thm:renamed-horn-degree-ceiling are all-field upper bounds. cor:nonhorn-specification-witness supplies the unchanged unbounded witness for the refined specification. prop:kneser-critical-core-witness is a separate structural existence control. The finite translation checks are recorded as ex:domain-architecture-controls.

What the six entries established

Matching-centre elimination added exact degree-six boundary conflicts and exposed easy odd-cycle joins. The Reed–Muller spell proved simultaneous local immunity, then linear variable retention in every normalized minimal core. It also rejected whole-row support absorption even after replacing the coarse threshold by actual local leading ideals. The next algebraic test gave a genuine original-instance PC upper bound just above half. Finally, the Laurent audit proved binomial-domain rigidity, including zero classes and affine two-state recodings. No entry supplied the required near-half surviving unit.

Obstacle

The missing implication is an explicit non-binomial relation space whose kernel remains closed under multiplication after cancellations, while a unit survives near half the actual Boolean variables. Full solution algebras cannot do this on an unsatisfiable input. Local erasure ranks, surviving monomials, a scalar annihilator of input multiples and topological cycles do not individually establish the required closure. The field-consistent code route additionally has to absorb genuine Boolean-domain trinomials without reverting to a full Boolean-empty coset algebra.

Across the goal

  • Near-half Boolean PC family. Open. All six entries since the last review took this as their primary obligation and reported unchanged goal progress. Two open statements remain, as in the review twelve research entries back; the review is therefore goal-level.
  • Complete Frege transfer. Open. No entry in this six-entry block took it as the primary obligation. Growing-width sources and nonlinear encodings still require their complete source grammar and degree ledger.

Architecture review

Necessary target. For every fixed relevant \(\ell\ge2\), produce a polynomial-size UNSAT Boolean CNF on actual \(v\to\infty\), independent of the proposed proof-size exponent, with \(1\notin C_D\) over \(\mathbb F_2\), \(D=\lfloor v/2-s\rfloor\), \(s=o(v/(\log v)^{\ell-2})\). A constructed linear map \(\Phi\) with nonzero \(\Phi(1)\), zero on axioms and a multiplication-compatible kernel through \(D\) suffices. Its target need not be a global algebra or have a separately large dimension.

Extra requirements. Binomial spanning, satisfiable whole contexts, full commuting multiplication, uniform conclusions for arbitrary normalized cores, and a large image are not required. The latest theorems reject some of these conveniences. Efficient selection of a minimal core is also unnecessary for the stated existence goal. Conversely the actual-variable and original-degree ledgers cannot be dropped.

Independent alternative. Before seeing the preferred route, the sole medium reviewer proposed topology of partial four-colouring complexes. Its initial normalized-functional endpoint annihilated only bounded input multiples; root identified that as a static Nullstellensatz condition, insufficient for dynamic PC. The same reviewer corrected it to explicit maps \(T_i\) satisfying \(\Phi(x_ip)=T_i\Phi(p)\) on the allowed image and acknowledged that homology alone does not provide those maps. The independent-stage record preserves the mistake and correction. The Kneser witness below gives this alternative an honest source class; the direct chain tests below still fail multiplication transport.

Rank normalization. All kernel and defect comparisons must use one filtered image and one coefficient field. Orbit counts and homological dimensions are not ranks of the PC quotient. The binomial rigidity theorem produces a point because of the imposed spanning structure; it is not a dimension estimate for arbitrary PC spaces. The next code test must compare domain-relation images in the same phase quotient rather than subtracting dimensions from different spaces.

Decision. Retain the accepted envelope and add the concrete common-polarity Horn exclusion proved below. Keep the code head, but drop binomial spanning: test an explicit truncated phase quotient with the Boolean domain relations retained as genuine multi-term relations. The topological alternative is queued with a new structural witness and failed direct chain maps; it is not an independently completed proof architecture. Neither family is claimed likely hard merely because it survives a screen.

Candidate to test. Fix a field-consistent Boolean-empty \(Ax=b\) over an odd prime field \(\mathbb F_q\). Let \(E\) have characteristic two and contain a primitive \(q\)-th root \(\zeta\), and work in the ordinary ring \(P=E[y_1,\ldots,y_v]\). Let \(J_C\) be the full vanishing ideal of \(\{(\zeta^{z_i})_i:Az=b\}\). For each original row, let \(J_a\) be the full vanishing ideal of its allowed tuples on \(\{1,\zeta\}^{X_a}\), extended to \(P\). Define the specific candidate \[S_d=(J_C\cap P_{\le d})+\sum_a(J_a\cap P_{\le d}).\] This uses the ordinary degree filtration before taking any quotient; it is not the unknown PC closure. The affine change \(x_i=(y_i-1)/(\zeta-1)\) keeps the original variable count and degrees, and the local summands contain the transformed canonical axioms and domain quadratics. The candidate deliberately includes all low-degree global phase consequences, a stronger convenience whose cost in unit survival is part of the test.

Decisive test. On the code head, derive the multiplier defects of this specified \(S_D\), namely the maps from \((S_D\cap P_{\le D-1})/S_{D-1}\) to \(P_{\le D}/S_D\) induced by multiplication by each \(y_i\). Derive its actual multiplier-defect relations, including cross-domain cancellations, and test unit survival against the recorded paired maximal-radius constant-degree control. A generic radius-only survival claim must fail that control. An all-parameter properness and vanishing-defect criterion with an unbounded witness would advance the missing implication; an early unit or a nonzero defect must instead change the construction. Merely renaming the unknown PC closure or restating the defect equations counts as unchanged and earns no extension.

The cheap binomial corollary

Corollary (Boolean binomial ceiling). Let an unsatisfiable polynomial system over any field consist of Boolean equations and axioms with at most two monomials, all of degree at most \(w\), where \(w\ge2\). Its ordinary PC refutation degree is at most \(w+1\). If an original Boolean system derives an unsatisfiable set of such binomial axioms in the same variables and field through degree \(B\), its refutation degree is at most \(\max\{B,w+1\}\).

Proof. Set \(D=w+1\). Its degree-\(D\) PC closure is binomially spanned. To prove this, start with the span of the inputs. Whenever the current span is binomially spanned, the cancellation fact in the Boolean binomial-point proof expresses every element of degree at most \(D-1\) as a sum of binomials or singletons in that span with the same degree bound. Their legal variable multiples are still binomials or singletons. Repeating these additions stabilizes in the finite-dimensional space of ordinary polynomials of degree at most \(D\), and the resulting space is precisely PC closure. If it were proper, the Boolean point lemma would supply an assignment annihilating all original axioms, since they have degree at most \(D-1\), a contradiction. Thus one is derivable through \(D\). For the paid version, derive the target inputs first and reuse their final lines while replaying this refutation; the ceilings combine by a maximum. \(\square\)

Sharp cutoff control. Already at \(w=2\), the Boolean system \(xy-1,yz-1,xz\) has degree exactly three over every field. Through degree two, these three inputs and the three Boolean equations have independent degree-two leading monomials. Their span has no nonzero degree-at-most-one element, so no new legal multiplication can occur and one is absent. Through degree three,

\[x(xy-1)-y(x^2-x)-(xy-1)=1-x,\qquad z(yz-1)-y(z^2-z)-(yz-1)=1-z,\] \[xz+z(1-x)+(1-z)=1.\]

These identities test the extra degree rather than pretending that a low-degree final binomial pays for its whole derivation.

The paid corollary can screen a specified low-cost presentation. It must not be turned into the unrestricted requirement “no low-degree binomial presentation”: the one-term system \(1=0\) would make that requirement equivalent to the desired hardness. The following local semantic restriction avoids that circularity.

A concrete structural refinement

For one global polarity vector \(\sigma\in\{0,1\}^V\), replace \(x_i\) by \(x_i\) or \(1-x_i\) consistently in every row. Call a row intersection-closed if the coordinatewise AND of any two allowed tuples is again allowed.

Theorem (renamed Horn ceiling). If an unsatisfiable canonical Boolean relation system has scope size at most \(w\ge1\), and one global polarity makes every allowed relation intersection-closed, then its ordinary PC refutation degree is at most \(w\) over every field. No auxiliary variables are needed.

Horn representation. First use the selected polarity. For a forbidden tuple \(a\) of a row on \(X\), let \(T=\{i:a_i=1\}\). If no allowed tuple is one on all of \(T\), use the negative Horn clause forbidding that conjunction. Otherwise intersect all allowed tuples that are one on \(T\). Their intersection \(b\) is allowed and satisfies \(b\ge a\). Since \(a\) is forbidden, some \(j\notin T\) has \(b_j=1\); use the implication \(\bigwedge_{i\in T}x_i\Rightarrow x_j\). It is valid on the row and falsified by \(a\). Taking one such clause per forbidden tuple represents the row exactly, with width at most \(|X|\le w\) and at most \(2^{|X|}\) clauses. Empty allowed sets permit the empty clause. Every new clause polynomial is a multilinear function vanishing on the original allowed tuples, hence a scalar sum of the original forbidden indicators through degree at most \(|X|\). This is an ordinary polynomial identity.

Horn refutation and cost. Start with no forced true variables and repeatedly apply an implication whose premises are all forced. If a negative clause has all its premises forced, derive a contradiction. If this never happens, the assignment making exactly the forced variables true satisfies every Horn clause after the process stabilizes. Unsatisfiability therefore forces a contradiction. Algebraically, from \(M(1-x_j)\) and the already derived facts \(x_i-1\) for all factors of \(M\), eliminate one factor at a time by subtracting \((x_i-1)(M/x_i)(1-x_j)\). All products have degree at most the original Horn clause width, at most \(w\); the result is \(1-x_j\). A negative clause \(M\) similarly reduces to one. Reuse the initial Horn-clause derivations, also through \(w\). Finally undo the polarity by affine substitution; literal multiplication uses one variable multiplication and subtraction without increasing degree. This proves the theorem. \(\square\)

Corollary (non-Horn specification witness). Add to the accepted fixed-width structural envelope the condition that no global polarity makes all row relations intersection-closed. Its width-four branching witness remains an unbounded witness after deleting a finite prefix: its degree is at least \(c v\) for a fixed \(c>0\), so eventually exceeds four and contradicts the theorem if such a polarity existed. Width four, linear size, block minimality, essential scopes, at least two occurrences, branching density at least \(2/5\), and the positive linear degree bound all remain unchanged. No new variables or encoding cost are added.

This screen is necessary for any fixed-width near-half family after a finite prefix. It is not sufficient: the compact four-colour edge relation fails intersection closure under every polarity, yet the recorded odd-cycle joins have degree six. The checker supplies all sixteen polarity counterexamples. Structurally, the forbidden tuples of a flipped edge form a coset specified by the two bitwise differences; it has a tuple of weight at most two. Flip either of two zero coordinates separately. Both resulting tuples are allowed, but their AND is the forbidden tuple. Thus even one edge relation already passes the new screen.

Specification decision. Accept this refinement now, with the unchanged witness and exact upper bound. The user’s new guidance is persisted in the living Route decisions paragraph: derive cheap strengthening corollaries, then check non-vacuity and the complete budget. The broad binomial-presentation ceiling remains a tool for concrete paid encodings, not an unrestricted hardness condition.

A structural control for the independent alternative

Proposition (Kneser critical cores). There is an unbounded family of edge-minimal non-four-colourable graphs whose odd girth tends to infinity. Their compact two-bit encodings have width four, polynomial size, essential scopes, block minimality, every bit in at least four edge blocks, and satisfy the new non-Horn screen. No PC lower bound is asserted.

Proof. For \(k\to\infty\), use the graph on all \(k\)-subsets of \([2k+4]\), adjacent when disjoint. The instance with two disjoint sets and four colour classes of Alon–Frankl–Lovász’s Theorem 1.1 says it is not four-colourable: the strict inequality \(2k+4>2k+3\) is enough, so no endpoint convention is needed. Choose an edge-minimal non-four-colourable subgraph and discard isolated vertices. Each vertex has degree at least four, since deleting its incident edges gives a four-colouring and a vertex of smaller degree could then be coloured greedily.

If \(A_0,\ldots,A_{2t}\) is an odd cycle in the original graph, each ground element occurs in at most \(t\) of these sets, because adjacent sets are disjoint. Counting memberships gives \((2t+1)k\le(2k+4)t\), hence \(t\ge\lceil k/4\rceil\). The same lower bound holds in every subgraph. A non-four-colourable graph is not bipartite and therefore has an odd cycle, so its vertex count \(N\) is at least \(2\lceil k/4\rceil+1\) and tends to infinity. Edge minimality gives block minimality of the colouring formula. Each edge has four forbidden equal-colour tuples on four essential bits, so there are \(4|E|=O(N^2)\) width-four clauses, on exactly \(v=2N\) bits, with length \(O(v^2\log v)\). Minimum degree gives at least four block occurrences per bit; the preceding edge-relation argument gives non-Hornness under every polarity. Fixing a deterministic choice of core makes the family independent of the proof-size exponent. \(\square\)

For large \(k\) these graphs are triangle-free, hence avoid the adjacent-universal-anchor control. That removes one explicit easy geometry; it does not establish a near-half barrier. The topology theorem certifies non-colourability, not a filtered PC representation.

Outside leads

  • Laurent binomial geometry. Eisenbud–Sturmfels, Theorem 2.1 and Proposition 2.3, classify proper Laurent binomial ideals by partial characters and their torus point sets by subgroup translates. This is a relevant comparison for the new rigidity theorem, not evidence of novelty. The full-ideal theorem has no truncated PC degree ledger. A direct toric quotient retaining two-state domains is rejected by the reviewed rigidity result; localizing variables for free would also change the degree problem. Answers. A truncated multi-term replacement, with legal multiplication and a surviving unit, would answer near-half-pc-family; the full binomial quotient does not. Test. Falsified for the direct two-state toric-invariant translation, including its affine and zero-class repairs. The precise truncated statement still needs broader literature comparison before any novelty claim.
  • Kneser topology. Alon–Frankl–Lovász’s Theorem 1.1, specializing the Kneser chromatic theorem, supplies the non-four-colourable source just proved. Its statement, Sections 2–4 proof, graph conventions and dependence on the topological obstruction were read. The critical cores remain unbounded and avoid short odd cycles, with the full Boolean ledger above. Answers. A degree-filtered chain representation with explicit coordinate actions on these cores could address near-half-pc-family beyond the anchor obstruction; graph topology alone cannot. Test. Passed for a non-vacuous source and exact encoding ledger. The elementary chain maps below fail, so no PC transfer is claimed. Queue the construction of a valid filtered representation.
  • Boolean CSP dichotomy. The Schaefer classification, in Allender–Bauland–Immerman–Schnoor–Vollmer’s Theorem 3.1, suggests screening tractable local structure. The Horn case now has its direct degree ledger above. A stronger inference from a nontractable local language or failure of Horn closure to hardness of a selected family fails: compact four-colour edge predicates pass every polarity test while the anchor family has degree six. Answers. A structural criterion controlling actual PC multiplication, beyond language-level decision complexity, would address near-half-pc-family. Test. Falsified as a near-half certificate from local non-Hornness; the necessary Horn exclusion itself is proved and adopted.

Absurd bridges

  • Cellular automata and the Garden of Eden. Wacker’s Main Theorem 1 states surjectivity versus pre-injectivity for finite-state local rules on the specified amenable spaces. Translate a protected configuration into a marked algebraic state under local updates, then seek multiplication-compatible finite quotients. For the binary rule \(z_i=x_{i-1}+x_{i+1}\) on \(\mathbb Z\), choosing two adjacent input bits and recursing in both directions gives a predecessor of every output; a finite-support kernel vector must be zero. On the three-cycle, however, the map has rank two and kills the nonzero all-ones vector. Thus the proposed marked state is lost on the finite periodic quotient even though the infinite rule passes. Answers. A finite, degree-filtered preservation theorem for the actual marked unit could address near-half-pc-family; infinite pre-injectivity alone cannot. Test. Falsified for the direct periodic-quotient translation, by the exact three-site matrix.
  • Potts partition functions. Sokal’s Theorem 2.3 gives the Fortuin–Kasteleyn expansion; at the antiferromagnetic specialization it counts proper colourings. Try reducing normalized colour-state sums to binary coefficients as a source of a surviving unit and local multiplier actions. Pinning one vertex of a four-colour edge leaves three states, but pinning one vertex of a triangle leaves six: the latter mass is zero in characteristic two although the instance is satisfiable. Quotienting by colour permutations is not a free repair. On one vertex, identifying the four basis states makes \(e_{00}+e_{10}\) zero, but multiplication by the first Boolean bit sends it to \(e_{10}\), whose image is nonzero. Answers. A normalization and quotient respecting every bit multiplier could give the missing non-binomial state space for near-half-pc-family. Test. Falsified for pinned scalar masses and the proposed orbit-collapse repair; the exact counts and multiplier defect are retained.

Independent-chain controls and checks

The simplest augmented-chain unit fails because the boundary of any vertex is the empty face. Moving the mark to a higher cycle does not automatically repair multiplication: on the triangle boundary over \(\mathbb F_2\), \(e_{12}+e_{23}+e_{31}\) is a cycle, but retaining only edges incident to vertex one gives boundary \(v_2+v_3\). Thus the direct support projector does not act on homology. These are tests of proposed translations, not a rejection of every topological construction.

The complete GAP output checks all 256 three-bit relations, of which 122 are intersection-closed, and all eight polarities over each of the rationals, binary and ternary fields: 12,456 literal local-encoding identities. All 256 two-variable Horn clause sets are tested in each field, including the empty clause, with 306 forward-cancellation identities and 600 unsatisfiable formula/field pairs. The quadratic-binomial sharpness identities pass in all three fields. All sixteen four-colour polarities, the triangle chain projector, the three-site cellular map and the Potts/orbit controls pass their stated falsification tests. Three preliminary runs stopped on GAP naming or syntax errors before a successful complete run; they are tool failures, not failed mathematics. No larger sweep is warranted.

Sub-ideas

  • Analyze the specified ordinary-degree span of the full consistent-code ideal and the local two-state row ideals. Compute its cancellation-induced multiplier defects and unit behavior, with the paired maximal-radius constant-degree family as a compulsory negative control. The new space must retain genuine multi-term relations and must not be defined as the unknown PC closure.

Verification gate. The same fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed all new mathematical claims and controls. Its architecture correction is incorporated: the ambient ring, full phase ideal, local row ideals and ordinary truncation defining the next candidate are now specified, and the endpoint requires both properness and vanishing defects. The same reviewer checked that correction and its affine ledger. The malformed source-parameter notation was repaired. No unused substantive hypothesis or unresolved correctness gap remains; no successful future invariant is asserted. The binomial-domain and positive-linear non-vacuity proofs were reread in full, including the minimal-core dependency, and the new Horn encoding/refutation rules passed exhaustive small checks. No third-party full text is committed.

Process assessment. The user’s question exposed a cheap structural corollary that should not have been left at the proof-method level. The living rule now requires deriving such corollaries when inexpensive, while the actual screen is accepted only with a proof, a non-vacuity witness and a budget. The review also caught and repaired a static-versus-dynamic gap in its independent proposal.

9 October 2026 — Small Boolean-empty projections give a paid degree ceiling

Working projection bounds; a stronger code screen remains provisional. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, perform the scheduled architecture test for the specified sum of the full consistent-code ideal and local two-state row ideals. Does a small Boolean-empty projection destroy its unit, and can that semantic obstruction be converted into an actual PC upper bound with the global phase derivations paid?

Route comparison. Removing the global phase summand is a possible method repair, but the compulsory paired control already has a unit in the local summands. A different presentation of the same small-component code therefore deserves a structural test before another invariant attempt. The existing character transport can pay for the small projection’s equations, so we derive that general upper bound and its inexpensive corollaries. This is a consequence of the concrete unit failure, not a policy of searching for upper bounds by default.

New mathematics. A Boolean-empty coordinate projection gives an explicit unit in the proposed semantic span. More importantly, the original canonical system has a degree ceiling controlled by that projection’s size, without adding its cofactor degree to earlier phase proofs. Near-half hardness at fixed odd modulus and bounded row width therefore needs no Boolean-empty projection substantially below half the actual variables. Every row code decomposing into sublinear coordinate blocks is excluded, even if the original rows mix those components.

Goal progress. Unchanged. This rules out a structural code regime and identifies a new necessary screen; it supplies no near-half family. The known paired witness fails the stronger screen, so the strengthened code specification remains provisional until an unbounded witness with the full encoding budget is supplied. The accepted general non-Horn branching envelope remains non-vacuous and unchanged.

General statement. prop:coset-domain-projection-unit and thm:boolean-projection-degree-ceiling apply to every system in their stated scopes. The three corollaries give the paired unit control, the near-half projection restriction and the exclusion of sublinear code components.

The semantic candidate and its unit

Let \(q\ge3\) be prime, let \(L=\{z\in\mathbb F_q^v:Az=b\}\ne\varnothing\), and assume \(L\cap\{0,1\}^v=\varnothing\). All \(v\) coordinates are actual variables of the canonical Boolean encoding. Over a field \(E\) of characteristic different from \(q\) containing a primitive root \(\zeta\), work in ordinary \(P=E[y_1,\ldots,y_v]\). Let \(J_C\) vanish on the root-valued copy of \(L\), and let each \(J_a\) be the full ideal of its row’s allowed \(\{1,\zeta\}\)-tuples, extended to \(P\). The reviewed candidate is

\[S_d=(J_C\cap P_{\le d})+\sum_a(J_a\cap P_{\le d}).\tag{1}\]

Every coordinate belongs to a row, so its two-state domain polynomial belongs to a local ideal. This slightly broader coefficient-field scope contains the characteristic-two architecture test.

Proposition (projection unit). If \(T\subseteq[v]\), \(|T|=t\ge1\), and \(\pi_T(L)\cap\{0,1\}^T=\varnothing\), then

\[1\in S_{(q-1)t}.\tag{2}\]

Proof. On the \(q\)-th roots of unity the polynomial

\[b(Y)=q^{-1}\sum_{h=0}^{q-1}(1+\zeta^{-h})Y^h\]

is the indicator of \(\{1,\zeta\}\), by the finite geometric sums. Thus \(W_T=\prod_{i\in T}b(y_i)\) vanishes on the root-valued coset and belongs to \(J_C\), with degree at most \((q-1)t\). Also \(b(y_i)-1\) vanishes at both allowed coordinate values and belongs to every local row ideal containing coordinate \(i\). Order \(T\); the literal identity

\[W_T-1=\sum_{i\in T}(b(y_i)-1)\prod_{j\in T,\ jplaces the left side in the sum of local ideal pieces at the same degree. Subtract to obtain one. This proves a unit in the semantic span, not an original PC derivation of the global-phase term. \(\square\)

Corollary (paired domain unit). For every paired modulus-seven cycle from the maximal-radius witness, \(n\ge4\), \(3\nmid n\), the characteristic-two candidate already has \(1\in S_2\), using only local row ideals.

Proof. Use the affine Boolean coordinates of that record; the coordinate change preserves degree and all ideal-piece memberships. Put \(s_i=x_i+y_i\). Each exact-one row has the local affine consequence \(P_i=s_i+s_{i+1}+s_{i+2}-1\). Their scalar span gives \(s_i-s_{i+3}=0\); coprimality with three makes all \(s_i\) equal, and in characteristic two the row equation gives \(s_i=1\). Thus \(L_i=s_i-1\in\sum_a(J_a\cap P_{\le1})\). The first row also has \(R=s_1s_2\in J_1\cap P_{\le2}\). Ideal membership gives \(s_2L_1\) in the degree-two sum, and

\[R-s_2L_1-L_2=1.\tag{3}\]

This proves the claim. Independently, the full modular coset fixes each Boolean pair sum to five in \(\mathbb F_7\), so its projection onto any pair has no Boolean point. The general bound (2) would give twelve; (3) identifies the stronger local reason on this particular control. \(\square\)

Architecture test. The scheduled candidate test fails unit survival on the compulsory all-size control before the target cutoff; there is no reason to compute multiplier defects after the unit is gone. Dropping \(J_C\) alone does not repair this example. This is consistent with its known degree-six original refutation: full local ideals contain low-degree consequences whose original derivations first pay width six.

Pay for the projection’s phase equations

Let \(r=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb F_q}A\ge1\), let original row support be at most \(w\ge1\), and let \(F\) be any proof field of characteristic different from \(q\). Use all canonical forbidden-tuple clauses and Booleanity. Suppose the Boolean-empty projection \(T\) above has size \(t\). Define

\[M=\min\left\{v,\left\lceil(1-1/q)v+t/q+w\sqrt{2r\log(4r+2)}\right\rceil\right\},\qquad B_T=\max\{\lceil M/2\rceil+w+1,\ t+2\}.\tag{4}\]

Theorem (Boolean projection ceiling). The original canonical Boolean system has an ordinary PC refutation over \(F\) through degree \(B_T\). For fixed \(q,w\),

\[d_F\le\max\left\{(1-1/q)v/2+t/(2q),\ t\right\} +O_w(\sqrt{r\log(r+1)}+1).\tag{5}\]

Projected equations. Choose affine equations \(c_j\cdot z=\tau_j\) defining \(\pi_T(L)\) inside \(\mathbb F_q^T\). Extend each \(c_j\) by zero outside \(T\). Since the equation holds throughout the nonempty affine space \(L\), its coefficient vector annihilates \(\ker A\), so it is a row-space combination \(c_j=\lambda_j A\), with \(\tau_j=\lambda_j b\). Select \(r\) independent original rows; consistency preserves the phase of every such combination. Each selected row still has support at most \(w\).

Balanced derivations. Adjoin a primitive root \(\zeta\) in a finite algebraic extension \(E/F\), and write \(u_i(a)=1+(\zeta^a-1)x_i\), \(X(a)=\prod_i u_i(a_i)\). For one target \(c_j\), use the recorded two-stage random row-coefficient path: go from zero to independent uniform coefficients, then to \(\lambda_j\). At every state a coordinate is uniform, zero or its fixed final value. Expected support is at most \((1-1/q)v+t/q\). Changing one random coefficient changes at most \(w\) support coordinates. The bounded-difference estimate \(\exp(-h^2/(2rw^2))\), with \(h=w\sqrt{2r\log(4r+2)}\), and a union bound over \(2r+1\) states gives a path with all supports at most \(M\). The deterministic cap \(v\) is always valid.

The full character transport proof realizes that path as balanced binomials through \(\lceil M/2\rceil+w+1\). Initial row characters are scalar sums of their width-\(w\) forbidden indicators. Its literal Boolean identity pays each repeated factor, and final lines are reused rather than multiplying their old derivations. For every \(\beta\in\{0,1\}^T\), transport the endpoint to

\[Q_{j,\beta}=X(\beta+c_j)-\zeta^{\tau_j}X(\beta).\tag{6}\]

Both endpoint supports have size at most \(t\), and their difference is \(c_j\). The transport lemma’s additional ceiling is therefore at most \(t+2\), giving (4). A different bounded-support path may be used for each projected equation. No polynomial proof-size upper bound is claimed.

Boolean interpolation without an additive cofactor charge. On \(\{0,1\}^T\), the functions \(G_j=X(c_j)-\zeta^{\tau_j}\) have no common zero: a common zero would satisfy all projected affine equations. For each Boolean point choose one nonzero \(G_j\), give its reciprocal to \(H_j\) there and set the other \(H\)'s to zero. Then \(\sum_jH_jG_j=1\) pointwise. The \(2^t\) functions \(X(\beta)\), \(\beta\in\{0,1\}^T\), form a basis of all Boolean functions: in each coordinate \(1,u_i(1)\) is an invertible change from \(1,x_i\), with determinant \(\zeta-1\). Expand \(H_j=\sum_\beta h_{j,\beta}X(\beta)\).

On Boolean points, \(X(\beta)G_j\) equals (6), by multiplication of character values. Every polynomial (6) is multilinear, so uniqueness of multilinear interpolation makes

\[\sum_{j,\beta}h_{j,\beta}Q_{j,\beta}=1\tag{7}\]

a literal ordinary-polynomial identity. Every summand is already a derived line through \(B_T\). Taking their scalar combination proves the refutation. No product of a high-degree cofactor with an old proof is inserted. Finally expand coefficients in an \(F\)-basis of \(E\) containing one. The original axioms are defined over \(F\), so taking components preserves every inference and recovers a refutation over \(F\) without increasing degree. This proves (4)–(5). \(\square\)

Structural consequences, with their scope

Define the Boolean obstruction size

\[\beta(L)=\min\{|T|:\pi_T(L)\cap\{0,1\}^T=\varnothing\}.\tag{8}\]

It is finite because the full coset is Boolean-empty, and positive because the coset itself is nonempty. It depends on the coset, including its charge, and not on the chosen generating rows.

Corollary (near-half projection necessity). At fixed \(q,w\), a family with no refutation through \(D_v=\lfloor v/2-s_v\rfloor\), \(s_v=o(v)\), must have

\[\beta(L)\ge v/2-o(v).\]

More precisely, for all sufficiently large members, \(\beta(L)\ge D_v-1\). If \(\beta(L)\le2v/3\), the first term in (4), including its error and constants, is at most \((1/2-1/(6q))v+o(v)D_v\), yielding the integer conclusion. If \(\beta(L)>2v/3\), that conclusion is already automatic. This proves the corollary.

Corollary (decomposed code exclusion). Suppose the row code decomposes across a partition of the actual coordinates, \(C=\bigoplus_j C_j\), with each coordinate block of size at most \(b_v=o(v)\). At fixed \(q,w\), every consistent Boolean-empty coset presented by bounded-width original rows has

\[d_F\le(1/2-1/(2q))v+o(v),\tag{9}\]

and cannot satisfy the near-half target. Indeed \(L=x_0+C^\perp\) is the product of its affine block cosets. If each factor had a Boolean point, their product would be a Boolean point of \(L\). Some factor therefore is Boolean-empty, giving \(\beta(L)\le b_v\). Apply (5). This is intrinsic code decomposition, not disconnectedness of the displayed rows; mixing the generators leaves it unchanged. In particular the entire paired repetition row-code class is excluded at fixed modulus and bounded original width, for any bounded-width generating-row presentation of the same modular coset, using its canonical forbidden-tuple encoding. Its maximal covering radius alone was not enough to reveal this obstruction.

Specification decision. The general accepted non-Horn branching specification is unchanged. For the fixed-modulus code subregime, record the new necessary projection condition alongside rank and covering radius. Do not yet call the strengthened conjunction a non-vacuous accepted refinement: the paired structural witness has \(\beta(L)=2\), and no unbounded replacement meeting all strengthened conditions and polynomial encoding has been proved. The screen is an independent semantic projection property, not a definition using PC degree. Growing modulus or growing row width needs the exact bound (4); the fixed-fraction exclusion is not silently extended to those regimes.

Learning from the upper bound: changing a sparse presentation while keeping a row code with small independent coordinate factors cannot fix the obstruction. A strengthened fixed-modulus candidate must keep Boolean inconsistency global across roughly half its coordinates while also retaining the required large row-code covering radius. This is a new construction obligation, not a claim that those two requirements can coexist. General random relations and compact four-colouring are outside this modular-coset theorem.

Checks and next implication

The new exact GAP certificates use three Boolean-empty but field-consistent projections: a three-coordinate ternary affine line and two-coordinate quinary and septenary sum lines. Every proper coordinate projection remains Boolean-feasible. One field-matrix inverse per model expands the partition functions in the Boolean character basis, of ranks eight, four and four. All 24 transported terms recombine literally to one; omitting a nonzero term fails. The root-grid selectors and ordinary telescoping identity pass all 101 modular-grid evaluations. These check the recombination and semantic-unit mechanisms, not the asymptotic random-path estimate. The earlier exhaustive character-transport checks are reused, not rerun.

Follow-up. Continuing the code head. The architecture test produced an explicit early unit and an actual all-parameter exclusion, not a working lower-bound invariant. Next compare construction of a genuinely coupled row code with an incompatibility argument for the simultaneous radius and Boolean-projection requirements. A connected row presentation is no longer an adequate non-vacuity control.

Sub-ideas

  • Determine whether fixed-modulus bounded-row-width cosets can simultaneously have rank near half, covering radius near half and Boolean obstruction size near half, with an unbounded essential/block-minimal witness and polynomial encoding. Compare an intrinsic code-coupling construction with a structural incompatibility proof; do not reuse row mixing of a decomposed code as the witness.

Verification gate. The sole fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed with one scope correction, now incorporated: the decomposed-code application covers bounded-width generating modular rows with their canonical forbidden-tuple encoding, not arbitrary alternative predicates or encodings. It checked every semantic and original-degree step, the integer projection necessity, direct-sum argument and provisional non-vacuity status. Rank positivity is automatic; full coordinate coverage is retained as the actual-variable application convention, although the semantic unit only uses coverage on T. Its witness limitation is explicit rather than making a stronger specification vacuously look successful.
