9 October 2026 — Coupled near-half metrics can still hide a small separator

Working coupled witness and separator ceiling; no near-half PC lower bound. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, settle whether intrinsic code coupling can coexist with the large covering radius and large Boolean obstruction size now required, while retaining fixed width, actual-variable accounting and the accepted structural properties. Compare a construction with an incompatibility proof before assuming the strengthened code class is empty.

Route comparison. Sparse row mixing of independent small components is excluded. We instead construct a code whose coordinate support is genuinely indecomposable, using shared hub variables and local ternary gadgets. It meets all the previous asymptotic code screens and general structural conditions. A small hub set still yields short proofs. The projection argument can cheaply be strengthened to a paid separator theorem, so we derive that consequence rather than stop at the easy example.

New mathematics. The Boolean projection transport works for a local consequence, not only for one. Conditioning on a coordinate set that makes the punctured row code decompose therefore gives an actual degree ceiling. An explicit width-ten, linear-size, block-minimal, essential, non-Horn family has a full-support indecomposable row code with rank, covering radius and Boolean obstruction size all within \(O(\sqrt v)\) of half, but its PC degree is only \(O(\sqrt v)\) over every field. A sublinear coordinate puncture splits its code into triples.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The earlier joint metric screen now has a fully counted unbounded witness, but not hardness. The stronger necessary puncture-separator screen has no such witness yet and remains provisional. The accepted general envelope retains its separate positive-linear-degree witness. No degree lower bound or complete Frege transfer is obtained.

General statement. lem:localized-projection-transport and thm:puncture-separator-degree-ceiling hold for all systems in the stated modular regime. cor:near-half-puncture-necessity is their structural consequence. prop:coupled-metric-witness is an explicit all-size construction, not a fitted finite pattern.

Transport a local consequence

Let \(L=\{z\in\mathbb F_q^v:Az=b\}\ne\varnothing\), with odd prime \(q\), rank \(r\ge1\), and original row support at most \(w\ge1\). Let \(F\) have characteristic different from \(q\), and use the original canonical Boolean clauses and Booleanity. For \(1\le s\le v\), define

\[M_s=\min\{v,\lceil(1-1/q)v+s/q+w\sqrt{2r\log(4r+2)}\rceil\},\qquad B_s=\max\{\lceil M_s/2\rceil+w+1,s+2\}.\tag{1}\]

Lemma (local consequence transport). If \(T\subseteq[v]\), \(|T|=s\), and a multilinear \(f\in F[x_T]\) vanishes on \(\pi_T(L)\cap\{0,1\}^T\), then \(f\) has an ordinary PC derivation through degree \(B_s\).

Proof. Use the projection proof with target \(f\) instead of one. Choose affine equations \(c_j\cdot z=\tau_j\) defining \(\pi_T(L)\), and put \(G_j=X(c_j)-\zeta^{\tau_j}\) in a finite root extension \(E/F\). At every Boolean point where some \(G_j\ne0\), assign \(f/G_j\) to one \(H_j\) and zero to the others. At a common zero of the \(G_j\)'s, \(f=0\) by hypothesis, so set all \(H_j=0\). This gives \(\sum_jH_jG_j=f\). If there are no projected equations, the hypothesis says \(f=0\) identically and the conclusion is immediate.

Expand the \(H_j\)'s in the Boolean basis \(X(\beta)\), \(\beta\in\{0,1\}^T\). The already proved path and transport ledger derives every \(X(\beta+c_j)-\zeta^{\tau_j}X(\beta)\) through \(B_s\). Their scalar combination is literally \(f\), by equality on the Boolean cube and multilinearity. Taking coefficient components in an \(F\)-basis gives the derivation over \(F\). No old proof is multiplied by a cofactor. This proves the lemma, which specializes to the previous projection ceiling when \(f=1\). \(\square\)

Conditioning on a code separator

Let \(C\subseteq\mathbb F_q^v\) be the row code. For \(H\subseteq[v]\), put \(U=[v]\setminus H\), and let \(\pi_U C\) denote the punctured code, not the shortened code. Suppose it is a direct sum across a partition of \(U\), and let \(b\) be the largest block size. Set \(h=|H|\), \(s=h+b\); use \(b=0\) if \(U\) is empty. Thus \(1\le s\le v\).

Theorem (puncture separator ceiling). If the full coset is Boolean-empty, its canonical system has ordinary PC degree at most \(B_s\), with (1), over every proof field of characteristic different from \(q\). For fixed \(q,w\), if \(h+b=o(v)\), this is

\[d_F\le(1/2-1/(2q))v+o(v).\tag{2}\]

Proof. For each Boolean assignment \(\alpha\) to \(H\), let \(\delta_\alpha\) be its literal indicator. If no point of \(L\) extends \(\alpha\), this indicator vanishes on the Boolean points of \(\pi_H L\), so the lemma derives it with support at most \(s\). Otherwise the fibre is an affine space on \(U\) with direction

\[\ker A_U=(\pi_U C)^\perp.\]

It is therefore a product of affine block cosets. If all its block factors had Boolean points, together with \(\alpha\) they would give a Boolean point of \(L\). Some block \(V_\alpha\) has none. On \(T_\alpha=H\cup V_\alpha\), the polynomial \(\delta_\alpha\) vanishes on every Boolean point of \(\pi_{T_\alpha}L\). Apply the lemma using \(|T_\alpha|\le s\). Its bound is monotone in support size. Every \(\delta_\alpha\) is thus derived through the same \(B_s\); their literal sum is one. If \(H\) is empty the only indicator is one and the same argument uses a nonempty bad block. No additional conditioning-degree charge is introduced, since the indicator’s support was included in \(T_\alpha\). This proves (2). \(\square\)

Define the purely structural puncture separation size

\[\kappa(C)=\min\{|H|+b:\pi_{[v]\setminus H}C\text{ has a coordinate direct-sum partition with block sizes at most }b\}.\tag{3}\]

The empty puncture with one block makes it finite. Puncturing the complement of an information set gives \(\kappa(C)\le v-r+1\), recovering the old free-coordinate scale. The parameter depends on the code, not on the chosen row generators or their charges.

Corollary (near-half puncture necessity). At fixed \(q,w\), a family with a barrier through \(D=\lfloor v/2-s_v\rfloor\), \(s_v=o(v)\), must have \(\kappa(C)\ge v/2-o(v)\), and eventually \(\kappa(C)\ge D-1\). Apply the theorem at the minimum in (3). If \(\kappa\le2v/3\), the first term in (1) is below \(D\) by a fixed linear margin for large \(v\), so \(\kappa+2>D\). Otherwise the claimed integer bound is automatic. This repeats the exact comparison of the projection necessity. No witness for this stronger conjunction with the radius and structural requirements is asserted yet.

A fully counted coupled witness

Proposition (coupled metric witness). For each odd \(n\ge3\), there is a canonical modular-row family over \(\mathbb F_3\), with

\[m=4n^2+2n,\qquad h=4n+1,\qquad v=6m+h,\qquad r=3m,\tag{4}\]

having \(3m\) independent original rows of width at most ten, linear clause count, a nonempty field coset \(L\), and no Boolean point. Its row code \(C\) is indecomposable across coordinate partitions, and

\[3m\le\rho(C)\le3m+h,\qquad3m\le\beta(L)\le3m+h.\tag{5}\]

The relation blocks are minimally unsatisfiable and essential, every variable occurs in at least two blocks, exactly \(5m+h\) variables occur in at least three, and no global polarity makes the rows intersection-closed. Thus the earlier rank/radius/projection conjunction and the accepted general structural conditions coexist at unbounded sizes, with \(O(\sqrt v)\) metric deficits. Nevertheless, over every proof field,

\[d_F\le\max\{10,h\}=O(\sqrt v),\qquad \kappa(C)\le h+3.\tag{6}\]

No claim of a positive-linear or near-half degree lower bound is made.

Build a dense minimal hub formula

Let \(F(x)\) and \(G(y)\) be disjoint odd-cycle two-colouring CNFs on \(n\) variables each. For each cycle edge use \(x_i\vee x_{i+1}\) and \(\neg x_i\vee\neg x_{i+1}\), and similarly for \(y\). Each has \(2n\) clauses, written \(C_i\) and \(D_j\). They are minimally unsatisfiable: deleting either clause at an edge permits that edge’s endpoints to take the same suitable value, while the remaining even-length path is alternately coloured.

Introduce flags \(z_i\), one for each \(C_i\), and form the three-CNF

\[A_i=\neg z_i\vee C_i,\qquad B_{ij}=z_i\vee D_j.\tag{7}\]

It has \(4n\) variables and \(m=2n+4n^2\) clauses, each with three distinct variables. If it were satisfied, the unsatisfiability of \(F\) would give some false \(C_i\), forcing \(z_i=0\); then all \(D_j\) would be true, impossible. Deleting \(A_i\) permits an assignment satisfying all \(C\)'s except \(C_i\), with all flags one and arbitrary \(y\). Deleting \(B_{ij}\) permits assignments satisfying all \(C\)'s except \(C_i\) and all \(D\)'s except \(D_j\), with \(z_i=0\) and the other flags one. In either case the deleted clause is the only false one.

Add one common variable \(t\), and replace each three-clause by the signed NAE constraint on its three literal values and \(t\). With \(t=0\) these are the original clauses; with \(t=1\) a global complementation of the other variables converts satisfaction back to the original CNF. The NAE system is therefore unsatisfiable. The deletion witnesses above, with \(t=0\), prove minimality and make all four literal values of the deleted constraint zero. These \(h=4n+1\) variables are the hubs.

The ternary gadget

For one NAE constraint with literal values \(\ell_1,\ell_2,\ell_3,t\), introduce six private Boolean variables \(p,q,r,a,b,c\). Over \(\mathbb F_3\), define affine residuals

\[E_1=q-p-1-(\ell_1+\ell_2+\ell_3+t),\qquad E_2=r-p-2-(\ell_3+t),\qquad E_3=a+b+c-t.\tag{8}\]

The original constraint blocks are the three modular equations

\[G_1=E_1+E_2+E_3=0,\quad G_2=E_1+E_2-E_3=0,\quad G_3=E_1-E_2+E_3=0.\tag{9}\]

The mixing matrix has determinant \(-4=2\) in \(\mathbb F_3\), so the three equations are equivalent over the field to \(E_1=E_2=E_3=0\). Their actual scopes have sizes nine, ten and eight: the common hub \(t\) cancels from \(G_1\), while \(p\) and \(\ell_3\) cancel from \(G_3\). Every displayed nonzero scope coefficient is nonzero modulo three.

Boolean meaning. Put \(A=\ell_1+\ell_2\) and \(B=\ell_3+t\) as integers in \(\{0,1,2\}\). The first two equations require

\[(p,q,r)=(p,p+u,p+v),\qquad (u,v)=(1+A+B,2+B)\in\mathbb F_3^2.\]

A Boolean triple exists unless \(\{0,u,v\}=\mathbb F_3\), equivalently \((u,v)=(1,2)\) or \((2,1)\). These occur exactly at \((A,B)=(0,0)\) and \((2,2)\), the two forbidden NAE assignments. If not all three residues occur, translate the missing residue to two to obtain a Boolean triple. The auxiliary equation \(a+b+c=t\) always has a Boolean solution for Boolean \(t\), and has a field solution for every field-valued \(t\). Thus all gadgets together have a nonempty field coset and are Boolean-empty exactly as the hub NAE system is.

Block minimality. Delete one of the three equations in gadget \(j\). Choose the hub deletion witness making just its NAE clause false, with \(t=0\). All other gadgets have Boolean solutions. In the bad gadget the target difference vector \(d=(u,v)\) is one of \((1,2),(2,1)\). The Boolean map \((p,q,r)\mapsto(q-p,r-p)\) has image \(\mathbb F_3^2\setminus\{(1,2),(2,1)\}\). Hence its possible nonzero residuals after subtracting \(d\) omit only one nonzero vector; every one-dimensional direction has a realizable nonzero residual.

Let \(V\) be the inverse mixing matrix in (9). For the deleted row \(k\), the first two coordinates of \(Ve_k\) are not both zero: otherwise \(e_k\) would be a multiple of the third mixing column, all of whose entries are nonzero. Choose \(\lambda\in\mathbb F_3^\times\) so that the first two coordinates of \(\lambda Ve_k\) are realized by a Boolean \(p,q,r\). The auxiliary sum \(a+b+c\) realizes every residue of \(\mathbb F_3\), so realize the third coordinate too. Then the residual vector in (9) is \(\lambda e_k\): every remaining row is satisfied. This proves block minimality of all \(3m\) modular rows.

Essentiality and occurrences. Every row has an allowed Boolean tuple, for instance from a deletion witness for another row. Flipping any variable in its actual scope changes the field sum by a nonzero coefficient, so each scope is essential. The private variable \(p\) occurs twice; the other five private variables occur three times. Every hub occurs in at least three blocks, as each underlying hub variable appears in several clauses and every appearance survives at least two rows of its gadget. Thus the branching count is exactly \(5m+h\), with density greater than \(5/6\). There are at most \(3m2^{10}\) canonical clauses and no variables beyond the \(v\) counted in (4), giving length \(O(v\log(v+1))\).

Each row also fails intersection closure after every polarity. In any ternary linear equation on at least four Boolean variables with nonzero coefficients, a polarity still leaves all coefficients in \(\{1,-1\}\). Two coefficients agree. Set those two bits to zero and use two other bits to make the sum one such coefficient short of the target; two nonzero binary choices span all of \(\mathbb F_3\). Flipping either selected zero bit gives an allowed tuple, but their AND is the forbidden tuple. The rows in (9) all meet this arity condition.

Intrinsic code parameters

The linear parts of \(E_1,E_2,E_3\) in each gadget are independent because of its private \(q,r\) and auxiliary coordinates. Different gadgets use disjoint private coordinates, so \(r=3m\). For every codeword, the three private coefficients on \(p,q,r\) sum to zero, and the coefficients on \(a,b,c\) are equal. A received word with private blocks \((1,0,0)\) and \((0,1,2)\) in every gadget, and zero hub entries, is at distance at least one plus two per gadget from every codeword. The zero codeword attains \(3m\), so \(\rho(C)\ge3m\). The redundancy bound gives \(\rho(C)\le v-r=3m+h\).

The code is not a direct sum of small components. Each linear part of an \(E_i\) has minimal nonzero codeword support: a codeword supported inside it must have all other gadget coefficients zero by their private coordinates; within its own gadget, the absent private coordinates force the other two coefficients to vanish. All these minimal supports contain the common hub \(t\), with nonzero coefficient. A direct-sum coordinate decomposition would put each such support in a single part, hence all in the part containing \(t\). Their union contains every coordinate. Thus \(C\) is indecomposable.

For the projection lower bound, suppose a coordinate set omits one of the private \(p,q,r\) coordinates in some gadget \(j\). Use its hub deletion witness. Every other gadget has a Boolean solution. In the bad gadget, the three coordinates are a translated copy of all three field residues, and any specified two can be made Boolean by choosing \(p\). All auxiliaries can be zero because \(t=0\). This gives a field solution whose coordinates in the chosen set are Boolean. Consequently every Boolean-empty projection must contain all \(3m\) of those private coordinates. Conversely the projection to those coordinates and all hubs is Boolean-empty, since it would satisfy all hub NAE constraints. This proves the two bounds for \(\beta(L)\) in (5).

Puncture the hub coordinates. In every gadget the remaining row code is the direct sum of the zero-sum code on \(p,q,r\) and the repetition code on \(a,b,c\); different gadgets are disjoint. These are blocks of size three, so \(\kappa(C)\le h+3\). This is the structural feature missed by indecomposability, covering radius and unconditional Boolean projections.

The actual degree upper bound

Fix a forbidden four-hub NAE assignment in a gadget. Every assignment to its six private variables violates at least one row in (9). Extend that row’s original forbidden-tuple indicator by the missing distinct literals to obtain the full ten-variable indicator, through degree at most ten. Summing over all private assignments gives the forbidden four-hub indicator. Thus both canonical NAE clauses of every hub constraint are derivable through degree ten, over every field.

The hub CNF is unsatisfiable on \(h\) variables. For each hub assignment, extend one falsified hub clause to its full indicator and sum over all assignments; this is an ordinary degree-\(h\) refutation. Reusing the derived hub clauses gives degree at most \(\max\{10,h\}\), proving (6). Flattening the gadget indicator derivations instead puts one in the sum of original local ideal pieces through degree \(h+6\), so the earlier semantic candidate also loses its unit well below half. The actual PC bound is smaller because it reuses the four-hub final lines.

What is now non-vacuous, and what remains open

The rank, covering-radius and Boolean-projection screens do coexist with the accepted fixed-width structural conditions, including block minimality, essentiality, repeated occurrences and non-Hornness. The witness is explicit, linear-sized and independent of the proof-size exponent. Its near-half metric errors are \(O(h)=O(\sqrt v)\). This answers the previous compatibility question without pretending those metrics imply hardness.

The actual upper bound rules out this family. Its lesson is broader than “do not use these gadgets”: a sublinear coordinate puncture that fragments the row code is excluded by (2), even for another bounded-width modular-row presentation of the same coset. The new puncture-size condition must accompany the earlier necessary code screens. The full strengthened conjunction still has no non-vacuity witness, so it remains provisional; no hard family is defined merely by assuming a surviving PC space.

Follow-up. Continuing the code head. Compare a code construction retaining large puncture separation with a structural tradeoff between near-redundancy covering radius and puncture separation. The full deep-hole inequalities, not merely sparse systematic basis rows, are relevant. Do not spend another cycle changing only the sparse generator presentation of the present code.

  • Follow-up. Closed: prop:coupled-metric-witness proves an unbounded fully counted witness for the previous rank/radius/projection conjunction. The new puncture restriction is a separate construction question below.

Sub-ideas

  • Determine whether fixed-modulus bounded-row-width codes near the redundancy covering-radius bound can have puncture separation size near half the actual variables, while retaining a Boolean-empty coset and the full structural/encoding ledger. Compare intrinsic code construction with a radius–separator incompatibility theorem; indecomposability alone now has an explicit counterexample.

Checks

The single bounded GAP run exhausts the ten-bit gadget: 45 full Boolean solutions across allowed hub tuples and six local deleted-row witnesses for the two forbidden tuples. It checks all 1,792 polarity cases of the three actual row types by constructing a meet counterexample. All 384 literal gadget-proof identities pass over the rationals, binary and ternary fields, with omitted-indicator negative controls.

The smallest full instance, \(n=3\), has 42 base clauses, 13 hubs, 265 actual variables and modular rank 126. All 126 deleted-row Boolean witnesses pass; 223 variables branch. Its proved radius and projection intervals are both 126 through 139, not exact computed radii. The local private-code distance check supports the all-parameter lower-bound argument. No global Boolean cube, large covering-radius search or asymptotic probability experiment was run.

Verification gate. The sole fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed the proof after the separator block size was specified as the largest actual block, ensuring that its support parameter lies between one and the actual variable count. The reviewer found no remaining gaps and agreed that goal progress is unchanged. Rank positivity is automatic for a nonempty Boolean-empty affine coset and is retained only as the transport application convention. See the review record. The complete projection, transport and existing normalization records were read; the new gadget and inference identities passed exhaustive local checks. The initial bounded literature screen supplied no general fixed-field radius–separator classification, and no such theorem is imported.

Process assessment. Constructing a witness before strengthening the screen separated compatibility of the necessary metrics from hardness. The easy witness exposed an intrinsic separator, and the inexpensive general corollary was derived with its conditioning cost paid. The stronger screen remains provisional rather than silently losing non-vacuity.

9 October 2026 — Ternary deep holes force separators in graphical charts

Working graph-chart obstruction; the general near-redundancy code regime remains open. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, test the missing compatibility between large covering radius and large puncture separation. The coupled witness meets the earlier metrics but fails the separator screen. Does a deep-hole argument force that failure more generally?

Route comparison. Another row presentation cannot change the intrinsic separator. A direct construction with no small puncture is the competing route, but its nearly maximal covering radius must first survive the full simultaneous matching inequalities. We use those inequalities to test the graph case, where they have a concrete improvement mechanism. The resulting theorem excludes exact maximal-radius ternary codes and a stated near-maximal subclass; it does not justify imposing that subclass on all remaining candidates.

New mathematics. A full gain budget controls simultaneous changes in a systematic parity-check chart. Over the ternary field, if each nonbasis column meets at most two nonzero leader coordinates, at most four times the leader deficit coordinates need be punctured to leave direct-sum factors of codimension at most two. This gives a sublinear puncture separator when the deficit is sublinear. The actual PC corollary uses at most nine coordinates from each bad factor, improving the naive use of its entire size.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The graph-chart subclass is excluded in the near-half regime, and exact redundancy-radius equality over the ternary field is excluded without the extra chart hypothesis. The actual surviving regime allows positive sublinear deficit and wider columns in every adapted chart; no incompatibility theorem or unbounded hard witness is proved there. The full near-half family and Frege transfer remain open.

General statement. lem:deep-hole-gain-budget applies to every finite-field code and every chosen coset leader. thm:ternary-graph-separation and cor:ternary-graph-degree-ceiling are all-parameter statements with their graphical-chart hypothesis explicit.

The full matching budget

Let \(\mathcal C\subseteq\mathbb F_q^v\) have codimension \(k\), and let \(a\) be a coset leader of weight \(d\). Its support \(S\) extends to a column basis \(B=S\sqcup T\) of a parity-check matrix. Indeed, a nonzero codeword supported on \(S\) could be scaled to cancel one nonzero coordinate of \(a\) without creating any new support, contradicting minimality. Write \(t=k-d=|T|\), row-reduce this column basis to the identity, and partition the other columns as

\[H=[I_B\mid P],\qquad P=\begin{pmatrix}P_S\\P_T\end{pmatrix},\qquad a_B=(a_S,0),\quad a_i\ne0\ (i\in S).\tag{1}\]

Lemma (deep-hole gain budget). For every vector \(\lambda\) of nonbasis column coefficients,

\[\#\{i\in S:(P_S\lambda)_i=a_i\}\le\operatorname{wt}(\lambda)+\operatorname{wt}(P_T\lambda)\le\operatorname{wt}(\lambda)+t.\tag{2}\]

Proof. The same syndrome as \(a\) is represented by \(\lambda\) on the nonbasis coordinates and \(a_B-P\lambda\) on the basis. Its weight is \(d-\#\{i\in S:(P_S\lambda)_i=a_i\}+\operatorname{wt}(P_T\lambda)+\operatorname{wt}(\lambda)\). It cannot be below \(d\). This proves (2). The one-column specialization is the earlier deep-hole basis argument; the simultaneous budget, rather than that support bound alone, is needed below. \(\square\)

A ternary graph chart

Now let \(q=3\). Assume there is a leader and basis as in (1) for which each column of \(P_S\) has support at most two. Make a graph on \(S\) with one edge for each two-support column; parallel edges are permitted. Columns of support zero or one do not give edges. The nonzero coefficients at the two endpoints are arbitrary.

Theorem (ternary graph separation). There is a set \(Z\) of at most \(4t\) actual coordinates such that \(\pi_{[v]\setminus Z}\mathcal C\) is a coordinate direct sum of factors of codimension at most two. Consequently, for every integer \(b\ge1\),

\[\kappa(\mathcal C)\le 4t+b+\frac{2v}{b+1},\qquad \kappa(\mathcal C)\le4t+2\sqrt{2v}+2.\tag{3}\]

The bound may be larger than \(v\); the trivial bound remains available. If \(d=\rho(\mathcal C)\), then \(t\) is the covering-radius deficit. If \(t=0\), the graphical-chart hypothesis is automatic over \(\mathbb F_3\).

Three-vertex improvement. Take a path on three distinct vertices and its two selected edge columns. Restricted to these vertices, the two columns have rank two and a one-dimensional left kernel generated by \(u\), all of whose coordinates are nonzero (use the unique endpoint of each column). Put \(c_i=u_i a_i\in\mathbb F_3^\times\). If all three \(c_i\)'s agree, they sum to zero. The target vector on all three vertices is then in the span of the two columns, giving three matches at coefficient weight at most two. If they do not all agree, an edge of the path has opposite endpoint charges. The target on those two vertices is in that edge column's span, giving two matches at coefficient weight one. In either case some selection of the two columns gives at least one more match on \(S\) than its coefficient weight. Its support on \(S\) lies entirely inside the path. Its entries on \(T\) are unrestricted.

Pack improvements. Choose a maximal collection of vertex-disjoint three-vertex paths, of size \(p\). For each path use the preceding improvement and sum their nonbasis coefficient vectors. Their column sets and their supports on \(S\) are disjoint, so the gains add, giving at least \(p\) more matches than coefficient weight. Equation (2) gives \(p\le t\), regardless of cancellations on \(T\). Remove all \(3p\) path vertices and the \(t\) basis coordinates of \(T\); call this coordinate set \(Z\). The remaining graph has components of at most two vertices: a connected graph on at least three vertices contains a three-vertex path, contradicting maximality.

Puncture, not shorten. Every deleted coordinate was an identity column of \(H\). Therefore the punctured code is exactly the kernel of the matrix obtained by deleting those coordinates and their corresponding check rows. Any vector satisfying the remaining check rows extends uniquely on the deleted identity coordinates to satisfy all the old check rows. Each remaining nonzero column has support in one remaining graph component, each of size at most two. Zero columns give independent full one-coordinate codes. The remaining parity-check matrix is block diagonal across these coordinate sets; its factors have codimension at most two. This proves the first assertion.

For the size version, consider factors whose number of coordinates exceeds \(b\). There are at most \(v/(b+1)\) such factors. Within each, puncture a column basis of its parity-check matrix, of size at most two. The remaining projection of that factor is the full space, split into singleton coordinate factors. All unpunctured factors have size at most \(b\). Hence the total puncture size plus largest block is at most the first bound in (3). Take \(b=\lceil\sqrt{2v}\rceil\) for the second. Finally, when \(t=0\), a single nonbasis column in (2) matches at most one leader coordinate for each of its two nonzero scalars. Each nonzero entry on \(S\) matches for exactly one such scalar, so that column has support at most two. \(\square\)

The inexpensive specification corollary

Return to a field-consistent Boolean-empty modular system \(Ax=b_0\) over \(\mathbb F_3\), with row code \(\mathcal C\), rank \(r\ge1\), and original row support at most \(w\). Let \(t=(v-r)-\rho(\mathcal C)\), and suppose a maximum-distance leader admits the graphical chart above. Put

\[s=\min\{v,4t+9\},\quad M_s=\min\{v,\lceil 2v/3+s/3+w\sqrt{2r\log(4r+2)}\rceil\},\quad B_s=\max\{\lceil M_s/2\rceil+w+1,s+2\}.\tag{4}\]

Corollary (ternary graph ceiling). Over every proof field of characteristic different from three, the original canonical Boolean system has ordinary PC degree at most \(B_s\). For fixed \(w\) and \(t=o(v)\), this is \(v/3+o(v)\), excluding a near-half barrier. When \(t=0\), no graphical-chart hypothesis is needed, and there is already a Boolean-empty coordinate projection of size at most nine.

Proof. Use the puncture \(Z\) from the theorem, with \(h=|Z|\le4t\). Fix a Boolean assignment \(\alpha\) on \(Z\). If it has no field extension in the affine coset, its indicator \(\delta_\alpha\) vanishes on the Boolean projection onto \(Z\). Otherwise the remaining field fibre is a product of affine spaces of dimensions at most two, since its direction is the orthogonal complement of the punctured row code. Some factor is Boolean-empty. That factor has at most nine field points. For each point choose a coordinate at which it is not Boolean. The union \(V_\alpha\) of these coordinates has size at most nine, and the factor's projection to it is Boolean-empty.

It follows that \(\delta_\alpha\) vanishes on the Boolean part of the original coset projected onto \(Z\cup V_\alpha\). For another assignment on \(Z\) its value is zero, and for \(\alpha\) there is no such Boolean projection. The local consequence transport derives that indicator through \(B_s\), since its relevant support has size at most \(s\). Sum all the indicators to one. If \(Z\) is empty, apply the same argument to a bad affine factor directly; its small projection is a Boolean-empty projection of the whole coset. As in the separator theorem, there is no extra additive conditioning charge. The fixed-width error in (4) is \(o(v)\), proving the stated asymptotic bound. \(\square\)

This excludes a structural subclass, not merely a chosen proof method. Nevertheless it does not establish that every near-maximal ternary code admits a graphical chart. The accepted general specification and its positive-linear-degree witness are unchanged. The stronger code specification remains provisional: a near-half rank/radius candidate must evade the graph-chart hypothesis when its deficit is sublinear; asserting the desired barrier is not an admissibility condition.

Limits, alternatives and checks

The single-column bound at positive deficit is only \(3(t+1)\) for a systematic generator, not a two-support statement about \(P_S\). Wider columns permit interactions outside the selected three-vertex sets and destroy the disjoint-gain argument as stated. For an explicit control, take \(H=[I_4\mid (1,1,2,1)^T]\) and leader syndrome \((1,1,1,0)^T\). Its one-dimensional length-five code has covering radius three, so \(t=1\), but this adapted nonbasis column has three nonzero entries on \(S\). This refutes automatic graphicality of every adapted chart, not the existence of a different graphical chart for this particular code.

The ternary field is also used. Over \(\mathbb F_5\), the two incidence columns \((1,-1,0)^T,(0,1,-1)^T\), with three identity columns, have the all-one syndrome at distance three. The graph on those three leader coordinates is connected even though the deficit is zero. Thus the three-vertex gain argument does not extend unchanged to other odd fields. No claim about a different larger-block theorem is made.

The bounded exact GAP run checks all 128 nonzero ternary path-coefficient/target cases. It enumerates 361 zero-deficit and 3,249 one-deficit candidate charts with three leader positions and two nonbasis columns, retaining 145 and 2,481 leader cases respectively. Exactly zero and 528 retained cases have connected three-vertex graphs, as predicted. Every retained case satisfies the full gain inequality for all nine coefficient vectors. The quintic negative control and all 243 received words for the wide ternary control pass. These are local falsification controls, not enumeration of all codes or computation of the PC bound. The final success marker was inspected; GAP's global-name syntax warnings did not interrupt the run.

A bounded literature screen for redundancy-radius equality returned special code families and small-radius studies, not a theorem used here. No external classification is imported. The proof uses only the two exact notebook dependencies linked above and elementary linear algebra; its literature novelty is not claimed.

Follow-up. Continuing, with rotation at the code head's four-entry spell boundary. The general positive-deficit, nongraphical case remains an application, so the item is not closed. This spell supplied structural obstructions and witnesses but left the necessary near-half lower bound unchanged; that does not earn an extension. The next scheduled architecture review must assess this lack of progress, without adding an early review.

Sub-ideas

  • Determine whether the full simultaneous gain inequality forces a sublinear puncture separator for fixed-field codes with sublinear redundancy deficit without the two-support graphical-chart hypothesis, or construct a fully counted Boolean-empty large-separator witness that evades it. Use the wider-column control when testing any claimed reduction to graphs.

Verification gate. The sole fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed all claims without corrections, including the gain packing, puncture direction, codimension factors and nine-point PC ledger. It agreed with unchanged goal progress and found no unused substantive hypothesis; rank positivity is automatic for the Boolean-empty field-consistent application. See the review record. The full covering-radius and local-consequence/separator proofs were reread; source and historical searches found no existing statement with this graph-chart conclusion. No Lean or framework changes were made.

Process assessment. This cycle tested a specific consequence of the highest-risk compatibility gap and paid its PC consequence immediately. The theorem covers the exact ternary endpoint and a robust but restricted positive-deficit regime. Wider columns remain the material gap; the graph model must not silently replace the original code question.

9 October 2026 — Random partitions repair the growing-modulus core trap

Working uniform core-retention theorem; global near-half hardness remains open. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, develop the growing-modulus lead. The existing source has all its basic field and Boolean properties but permits logarithmic-size contradictory cores, even with connected incidence. Can a specified random incidence model exclude this failure uniformly over every core and normalization?

Route comparison. A uniform near-half PC invariant is the main missing implication, but the existing easy-core construction defeats any such claim for arbitrary three-occurrence scopes. Rather than strengthen local rank or assume an unknown invariant, randomize the three incidence partitions and check private-variable extension. The existing core-retention argument needs only coordinate extension for its size conclusion, not its stronger polynomial-immunity premise. This weakens the method requirement while preserving the source's growing-modulus and encoding ledger.

New mathematics. A second-moment bound makes each random modular row extend every assignment to a small fraction of its coordinates. Three independent random partitions give uniform small-row-set expansion. Together they force every unsatisfiable collection of nontrivial projected input relations, and hence every normalized minimal core, to retain a fixed positive fraction of the original variables.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The uncontrolled-small-core defect is repaired for this specified source model. No binary near-half PC barrier, terminal higher-occurrence density or complete growing-width Frege transfer is proved. This is an unbounded logarithmic-width alternative, not an adopted strengthening of the fixed-width specification and not a hardness assertion.

General statement. lem:modular-coordinate-extension gives a uniform finite-parameter probability bound. lem:projection-core-retention weakens the earlier peeling hypothesis and controls all nontrivial projected subsystems. thm:partition-modular-cores combines them with the proved source at unbounded sizes.

Uniform coordinate extension

For a Boolean relation \(R\subseteq\{0,1\}^k\), say that it extends through \(d\) if every assignment to any set of at most \(d\) coordinates extends to a member of \(R\). Equivalently, every such coordinate projection is the full Boolean cube. This is a property of the relation, independent of a proof field.

Lemma (modular coordinate extension). Let \(0\le d \[\frac{q}{2^k}\sum_{j=0}^d\binom kj4^j.\tag{1}\]

For \(m\) such rows on any fixed scopes, a union bound multiplies (1) by \(m\); overlapping scopes cause no additional cost.

Proof. Fix a pinned coordinate set of size \(j\) and one assignment on it. Conditional on its coefficients, the remaining coefficients and the adjusted target are independent uniform field elements. The number \(X\) of satisfying assignments to the remaining \(k-j\) coordinates is the sum of \(2^{k-j}\) pairwise independent indicators of mean \(1/q\). Indeed, for distinct Boolean vectors \(x,y\), the coefficient vectors \((x,-1),(y,-1)\) are linearly independent over \(\mathbb F_q\): proportionality would force the scalar to be one by the final coordinate, and then \(x=y\). Thus \(\mathbb E X=2^{k-j}/q\) and \(\operatorname{Var}X\le2^{k-j}/q\). Chebyshev gives \(\Pr[X=0]\le q/2^{k-j}\). There are \(\binom kj2^j\) pins of this size. Summing proves (1). The argument also covers \(k-j=0\) if needed, though the stated range keeps at least one free coordinate. \(\square\)

Full small coordinate projections do not imply low-degree polynomial immunity. For example, the even-parity relation on \(k\ge2\) bits has full projections onto every proper coordinate set but is annihilated by the nonzero degree-one polynomial \(\sum_i x_i\) over \(\mathbb F_2\). No local-immunity or PC conclusion is inferred from (1).

Reuse peeling under the weaker hypothesis

Let \(k,d,R\) be integers. Let input relations \(R_a\) have scopes \(X_a\) of size \(k\), all extending through \(d\), with \(0\le d \[|N(S)|>\beta k|S|\quad(1\le|S|\le R),\qquad d+1\ge2(1-\beta)k,\tag{2}\]

where \(N(S)=\bigcup_{a\in S}X_a\).

Lemma (projection core retention). Every input subsystem of at most \(R\) rows is satisfiable. Every unsatisfiable collection of non-universal existential projections of distinct input rows onto retained scopes \(Y_a\subseteq X_a\) uses more than \(R\) rows and has actual variable count

\[n>R\bigl(d+1-(1-\beta)k\bigr).\tag{3}\]

In particular, every minimal unsatisfiable input core, after any complete sequence of private-coordinate projections and irrelevant-coordinate deletions, retains this many variables, is block-minimal and essential, and has every variable in at least two blocks. All its row projections still extend through \(d\). If, over a specified field, the vanishing ideals in the Boolean function algebra contain no nonzero Boolean function of multilinear degree at most \(d\), that stronger property also survives projection.

Proof. In a row subset \(S\), let \(p\) count coordinates occurring once. Incidence counting gives \(k|S|\ge2|N(S)|-p\), so \(p>(2\beta-1)k|S|\). One row has more than \((2\beta-1)k\) private coordinates, hence fewer than \(2(1-\beta)k\le d+1\) shared coordinates. Their number is an integer at most \(d\). Solve the other rows recursively, then extend on the private coordinates using the extension hypothesis. Every smaller subset obeys (2), so this induction proves the first assertion.

A solution of any original row collection satisfies all its coordinate projections. Therefore an unsatisfiable projected collection has more than \(R\) original row labels. Each retained non-universal relation has scope size at least \(d+1\); a projection onto at most \(d\) coordinates would be universal. Pick any \(R\) retained rows. Their union has size

\[\left|\bigcup_{a\in S}Y_a\right|\ge|N(S)|-\sum_{a\in S}|X_a\setminus Y_a|>\beta kR-R(k-d-1),\]

which proves (3). Projection preserves extension through \(d\) immediately. Any low-degree function vanishing on a projected relation also vanishes on the original relation by pullback, proving the optional immunity assertion. The private-projection theorem preserves every block-subcollection's satisfiability, hence minimality, and supplies termination, essentiality and the two-occurrence property for the permitted normalizations. Arbitrary separate projections of shared variables need not preserve unsatisfiability; the more general assertion above is explicitly conditional on the projected collection being unsatisfiable. \(\square\)

The lower range bound \(\beta\ge1/2\) follows from the other integral parameter conditions and is retained as a range convention. This refines the scope of the earlier immunity-based retention argument: immunity implies coordinate extension by the forbidden-pin indicator, but only the latter was used for peeling and retained scope size. The old full-immunity conclusion still needs its old extra premise.

Three random partitions

For integers \(l\to\infty\), set

\[v=l2^l,\quad k=4l,\quad m=3\cdot2^{l-2}=3v/k,\quad v^2Independently choose three uniformly random labelled partitions of \([v]\) into \(v/k\) blocks of size \(k\). Treat their blocks as distinct row occurrences, even if scopes coincide across layers. Each coordinate has exactly three occurrences. Independently sample every supported coefficient and every target uniformly in \(\mathbb F_q\), as in the existing source theorem.

Theorem (partition modular cores). With probability \(1-o(1)\), this system has full row rank, a field solution, no Boolean solution, nonzero coefficients, essential nonempty input relations and extension through \(d\) in every row. Every minimal unsatisfiable core, after any permitted complete normalization, has

\[n\ge\gamma v,\qquad \gamma=\alpha/256>0.\tag{5}\]

Each terminal system has nontrivial essential relations, at least two and at most three occurrences of every variable, width \(O(\log n)\), polynomial canonical encoding length \(O(n^5)\), and the retained coordinate-extension property. The stronger assertion (3) holds for any unsatisfiable collection of non-universal projected input rows. All quantifiers hold on one common event, so the core and normalization can be chosen after seeing the instance.

Expansion probability. Fix a total of \(s\le R\) row labels, with \(s_i\) from layer \(i\). Their union within that layer is a uniform \(ks_i\)-subset of \([v]\). If the total union has size at most \(u=\lfloor\beta ks\rfloor\), some \(u\)-set contains it. For a fixed such set, independence between layers and sampling without replacement give

\[\prod_{i=1}^3\frac{\binom u{ks_i}}{\binom v{ks_i}}\le(u/v)^{ks}.\]

If any \(ks_i>u\), the event is impossible. Union bounding over row labels and sets gives at most

\[\binom ms\binom vu(u/v)^{ks}\le(em/s)^s(ev/u)^u(u/v)^{ks}.\tag{6}\]

As in the earlier scope proof, the logarithm of the last two factors increases with \(u\) for \(0 \[\left[3e\,e^{\beta k}\beta^{\delta k}x^{\delta k-1}\right]^s \le\left[\frac{3e}{\alpha}e^{-k}\right]^s,\tag{7}\]

for large \(k>256\): the power of \(x\) is positive, \(\delta\log\alpha=-2\), and \(\beta<1\). Put \(b_k=(3e/\alpha)e^{-k}\). Eventually \(b_k<1\), and summing (7) over \(s\) gives failure probability at most \(b_k/(1-b_k)=o(1)\). This proves (2) simultaneously for every relevant row subset. Repeated scopes across layers are included in the bad event rather than silently prohibited.

Uniform extension probability. Applying (1) to all rows costs at most

\[mq2^{-k}\sum_{j=0}^d\binom kj4^j.\tag{8}\]

For large \(k\), the summands increase up to \(d\), and \(\binom kd\le(ek/d)^d\). Since \(x\log(4ek/x)\) increases for \(0 \[\sum_{j=0}^d\binom kj4^j\le(d+1)(4ek/d)^d\le(d+1)(256e)^{k/64}.\]

Using \(e<4\), \(m=3\cdot2^{l-2}\), \(q<2l^2 2^{2l}\) and \(k=4l\), expression (8) is \(O(l^3 2^{-3l/8})=o(1)\). No independence from the expansion event is needed: the bound is uniform over all scopes.

The remaining source properties. The existing growing-modulus source theorem applies conditional on every sampled incidence graph, because every row has \(k\) distinct coordinates and every coordinate has three occurrences. Its exceptional bound \((m+3v)/q+mq/2^k+2^v/q^m\) tends to zero for (4). Intersect that event with expansion and extension. This gives full field rank and consistency, Boolean emptiness, nonzero coefficients and essentiality. The allowed-count refinement of that source is not needed here.

Retained variables and ledger. For large \(k\), \(d+1>k/64>k/128=2(1-\beta)k\). Also \(d+1-k/256>3k/256\). Eventually \(R\ge\alpha v/(2k)\), so (3) gives \(n>3\alpha v/512>\gamma v\). The original clause count is at most \(m2^k\), and the length at most \(O(m2^k k\log(v+1))=O(v^5)\). Projection does not increase the forbidden-tuple count or width. Since \(v\le n/\gamma\), the same length is \(O(n^5)\) and \(k=O(\log n)\). The constant \(\gamma\) is small but fixed and independent of any proof-size exponent; no optimization of it is needed.

Every projected clause is a scalar sum of original forbidden indicators. Thus over every proof field a degree-\(D\) refutation of a terminal canonical formula replays on the original instance through \( \max\{k,D\} \), exactly as in the already checked private-projection ledger. This is one-way replay, not a lower bound. Fixing any deterministic choice of good instances, minimal cores and normalization orders gives an unbounded source family independent of the proof-size exponent. \(\square\)

Disposition and next missing implication

The connected core trap is avoided by uniform expansion together with uniform extension; neither connectivity nor nonempty rows alone suffices. This supplies a constructive alternative to the recent fixed-modulus exclusions. However, terminal higher-occurrence density is not proved, and logarithmic width still needs a complete source/Frege degree ledger. The coordinate-extension property is weaker than polynomial immunity and cannot be substituted into a PC lower-bound theorem requiring immunity.

Follow-up. Continuing the growing-modulus lead. The source now has uniform core retention, so the next necessary task is an explicit characteristic-mismatch reduction or normalized dual construction whose unit survives legal multiplications near half the retained variables. Compare it with an actual upper-bound obstruction to that proposed construction. Do not spend another cycle sharpening the retention constant or sampling larger local subset-sum tables.

Sub-ideas

  • For the normalized growing-modulus cores, construct a proper binary degree-cutoff space or an explicit reduction map and prove multiplier compatibility and unit survival. Keep the one-way normalization transfer and the growing-width source ledger separate; coordinate extension alone supplies neither immunity nor PC hardness.

Verification gate. The sole fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed after explicit integer parameters and Boolean-function-algebra immunity conventions were added. It checked the pinned second moment, three-partition expansion, uniform core and normalization quantifiers, constants and one-way replay. The redundant lower range bound on the expansion parameter is identified. See the review record. The full growing-modulus source, immunity-based core retention and private-projection proofs were reread, including their scope warnings and dependency metadata. The proofs here are analytic; no finite computation is claimed to verify an asymptotic event, and the existing encoding rule was not changed. The even-parity relation is a direct all-size falsification control for the tempting immunity inference.

Process assessment. The existing core method was reused with its actually necessary hypothesis, rather than importing Reed–Muller immunity into a different row law. All-core and all-normalization quantifiers are paid by one uniform good event. The source-construction gap changes, but the main lower-bound implication remains unchanged and is carried to the scheduled review.

9 October 2026 — Field consistency can hide an immediate binary affine unit

Working structural screen and modular control; random-core near-half survival remains open. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, test the concrete binary sum \(K_D=\sum_a(J_a\cap B_{\le D})\) for the growing-modulus source. Can the proved field consistency, coordinate extension and uniform core retention alone ensure that this sum is proper, or must the random coefficient law supply another ingredient?

Route comparison. A direct proof of properness and legal multiplication closure remains the target. Before substituting coordinate extension for algebraic local quality, test the lowest-degree consequences in the actual proof field. This produces a full-instance obstruction, not a defect in a small subcollection whose image might be absorbed. Its scope is carefully limited: the constructed coefficients satisfy the deterministic good-source properties but are not a typicality theorem for the uniform coefficient ensemble. Arbitrary minimal-core selection may remove the affine certificate.

New mathematics. Jointly inconsistent local affine hulls give ordinary PC degree at most the input width; their unit persists under private/irrelevant-coordinate normalization without arbitrary row deletion. An explicit all-size choice of odd-modulus coefficients has full modulus-field row rank and all the recent deterministic source/core properties, yet its binary local ideal sum contains one already in degree one. The existing width-four witness proves non-vacuity of a cheap affine-consistency refinement of the general specification.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The deterministic source properties do not prove unit survival for the original instance. The control does not refute high-probability hardness of the uniform coefficient ensemble or survival on a suitably selected normalized minimal core. The new general structural screen is necessary and non-vacuous but far from sufficient. Neither the near-half PC family nor the full Frege transfer is proved.

General statement. thm:local-affine-degree-ceiling is the elementary interpolation/Gaussian-elimination mechanism in arbitrary arity and proof field, with its exact degree ledger. cor:affine-specification-witness preserves the existing unbounded witness. prop:modular-affine-trap gives an all-size field-consistent control with a literal global unit, not an extrapolated finite example. No novelty claim is made for local interpolation or Gaussian elimination.

Local affine consistency and its cost

Here \(B=F[x_1,\ldots,x_v]/(x_i^2-x_i)\), filtered by multilinear total degree, and \(J_a\) is the ideal of functions vanishing on the cylinder over \(R_a\). Define \(K_D=\sum_a(J_a\cap B_{\le D})\). This Boolean-algebra local sum is distinct from the earlier ordinary phase-ring candidate with a global coset summand.

Let a canonical Boolean relation system have scopes \(X_a\) of size at most \(w\ge1\), with allowed sets \(R_a\). Over a proof field \(F\), let \(L_a\) be the space of affine polynomials on \(X_a\) vanishing on \(R_a\). Local affine consistency means that the union of these affine equations has a solution in \(F^v\), equivalently that the cylinders over the affine hulls of the \(R_a\)'s intersect. This is not a requirement that the original Boolean relations have a common point.

Theorem (local affine ceiling). If these local affine equations are inconsistent over \(F\), the original canonical system has an ordinary PC refutation through degree \(w\). In the common Boolean function algebra, \(1\in K_1\) if and only if local affine consistency fails. The unit certificate in \(K_1\) survives any sequence of private-coordinate projections, irrelevant-coordinate removals and deletion of universal rows, provided non-universal rows are not arbitrarily discarded.

Proof. For \(f\in L_a\), literal multilinear interpolation gives

\[f(x_{X_a})=\sum_{u\in\{0,1\}^{X_a}\setminus R_a}f(u)\delta_u(x_{X_a}).\tag{1}\]

Each summand is a scalar multiple of an original clause polynomial, of degree at most \(w\). Equality on the Boolean cube is equality of these multilinear polynomials. Gaussian elimination on an inconsistent affine system expresses one as a scalar linear combination of its equations. Derive the required affine equations through (1) and take that combination, with no degree increase.

If every \(R_a\) is nonempty, an affine element of its extended ideal cannot depend on a coordinate outside \(X_a\): fix an allowed local tuple and vary that Boolean coordinate to force its coefficient to zero. Thus \(J_a\cap B_{\le1}=L_a\). If a row is empty, one belongs to both spaces and the assertions are immediate. A finite affine system is inconsistent exactly when its linear span contains one, proving the equivalence.

Write an affine unit certificate as \(1=\sum_a f_a\), \(f_a\in L_a\). A coordinate private to row \(a\) has coefficient zero in this identity, so its coefficient in \(f_a\) itself is zero. The same affine function then vanishes on that row's existential projection. For a semantically irrelevant coordinate in a nonempty row, varying it while holding an allowed tuple on the other coordinates fixed forces its affine coefficient to zero. An empty row stays empty under projection. A universal row has no nonzero affine consequence and can be removed. Repeat these observations along the normalization. Arbitrary removal of non-universal rows is different and need not preserve the certificate. \(\square\)

For the binary goal, accept local affine consistency over \(\mathbb F_2\) as a structural condition on the selected whole-block representation. It is invariant under one common Boolean polarity change, since that is an invertible affine coordinate map applied to every row. It is not invariant under arbitrary regrouping: splitting a relation into individual clauses can hide its affine consequences locally, while the already paid degree ceiling remains valid. A passing presentation never cancels an upper bound available through a paid encoding.

Corollary (affine specification witness). Adding binary local affine consistency preserves an unbounded witness for the accepted general specification with no new variables or encoding cost. Use the existing width-four branching witness, after the finite prefix already removed for the non-Horn screen. It has positive linear PC degree, so eventually exceeds width four and cannot violate the new condition. More directly, every block of that witness is a proper clause of arity three or four. Its allowed cube omits only one point; a nonzero binary affine function is nonzero either everywhere or on half the cube, at least two points. Thus every local affine vanishing space is zero. Block minimality, essentiality, linear clause count, at least two occurrences and branching density at least \(2/5\) remain unchanged. This proves non-vacuity, not near-half hardness. \(\square\)

An odd-modulus source with a binary affine trap

Proposition (modular affine trap). At all sufficiently large parameters of the growing-modulus source,

\[v=l2^l,\quad k=4l,\quad m=3v/k,\quad v^2there are three \(k\)-block incidence partitions and coefficients in \(\{1,3\}\), with at most one coefficient three in each row, such that the \(m\) modular equations have full row rank over \(\mathbb F_q\), a field solution and no Boolean solution. Every scope is essential, every variable occurs exactly three times, every row extends all pins through \(d=\lfloor k/64\rfloor\), every row is non-Horn after every polarity, and every normalized minimal core retains at least \(e^{-512}v/256\) variables. The canonical encoding has length \(O(v^5)\). Yet over \(\mathbb F_2\),

\[1\in K_1,\qquad d_2(\Gamma)\le k=O(\log v).\tag{2}\]

The unit persists under the permitted normalization of this full row collection. No claim is made that it persists under arbitrary selection of a minimal unsatisfiable core, or that this chosen coefficient pattern has nonvanishing probability in the uniform coefficient ensemble.

Incidence and field rank. Choose any realization of the three partitions satisfying the uniform expansion event proved in the preceding entry; such realizations exist for every sufficiently large parameter. Hall's condition follows from \(k|S|\le3|N(S)|\), so match every row to a distinct supported coordinate. Begin with coefficient one on every supported entry. At the matched coordinate of row \(a\), replace that coefficient by \(1+t_a\). The matching-column minor is multilinear in the \(t_a\)'s and has coefficient one on \(\prod_a t_a\). It is therefore nonzero as a polynomial. A nonzero multilinear polynomial cannot vanish on the entire grid \(\{0,2\}^m\) over an odd field: induction on variables, evaluating at the two distinct points, forces both coefficients of a vanishing linear polynomial to vanish. Choose a grid point with nonzero determinant. This gives full row rank with precisely the promised weights one or three; all are nonzero since \(q>3\).

Targets and Boolean contradiction. Put \(b_a=k/2\) in every row except one row of the first layer, where \(b_a=k/2+1\). Since \(q>k+2\), on Boolean inputs each modular equation is exactly the integer equation whose weights are one or three and whose total weight is at most \(k+2\). Reducing that integer equality modulo two gives the local affine consequence

\[\ell_a(x)=\sum_{i\in X_a}x_i+(b_a\bmod2)=0\quad\text{over }\mathbb F_2.\tag{3}\]

Sum (3) over the first and second partition layers. Every variable appears twice, while precisely one target is odd, so the sum is the literal constant one. This proves Boolean emptiness and (2) by the local affine theorem. Full row rank over \(\mathbb F_q\) still gives a field solution for these targets: consistency in that field is not binary affine consistency.

Coordinate extension and essentiality. Pin any \(j\le d\) variables in a row. Set its possible unpinned weight-three variable to zero. The contribution already pinned is between zero and \(j+2\). The remaining target is therefore between \(k/2-j-2\) and \(k/2+1\). For sufficiently large \(k\), this lies in the interval from zero to the number of unpinned weight-one variables, which is at least \(k-j-1\). Set that many of them to one. Thus every pin extends. Nonemptiness and nonzero coefficients imply essentiality: flipping any scope coordinate in an allowed tuple changes the modular sum by a nonzero amount.

No polarity makes a row Horn. Among its at least \(k-1\) weight-one coordinates, choose two with the same polarity. Set the possible weight-three coordinate to zero and exactly \(b_a-1\) of the other weight-one coordinates to one; there are enough since \(b_a-1\le k-3\) for large \(k\). Setting the chosen pair to \(10\) or \(01\) gives two allowed tuples. Their AND in the chosen polarity corresponds to \(00\) or \(11\) on the original pair, with every other coordinate unchanged. Its integer weight is \(b_a-1\) or \(b_a+1\), so it is forbidden. This works for every polarity.

All-core bound and encoding. The chosen incidence has the same uniform expansion as in the random-partition theorem, and the preceding paragraph establishes its coordinate-extension premise deterministically. The projection core-retention lemma therefore gives the identical lower bound on every normalized minimal core. It is a size assertion only. All original coefficients and variables retain their three occurrences, and the original canonical length remains \(O(m2^k k\log(v+1))=O(v^5)\). The affine unit may disappear when a non-universal row is removed during core selection, so an easy original system does not by itself bound the degree of every core. \(\square\)

What this decides about the proposed space

The full original local sum fails properness at degree one on this control; there is no multiplier-defect calculation that can restore its unit. The same holds after permitted normalization without arbitrary row deletion. Thus field consistency over the odd modulus, expansion, coordinate extension, absence of common-polarity Horn structure and large normalized cores are insufficient by themselves to prove properness of the original local sum.

The next-step target was a suitably selected normalized core of the genuine uniform-coefficient source. That target is not refuted here: special deterministic coefficients can have negligible probability, and selecting a core can discard affine certificate pieces. A proof must use additional information about the random row law or choose and justify a core with binary affine consistency. The new accepted general screen makes that requirement explicit without requiring the desired PC barrier as an admissibility axiom.

Follow-up. Continuing the growing-modulus lead. Test binary affine consequences for the genuine random modular row law, and then analyze the resulting selected core in the same common quotient. The strongest relevant next question is whether the coefficient law suppresses these low-degree consequences uniformly; coordinate projection coverage alone cannot answer it. Do not mistake the special coefficient trap for a high-probability upper bound.

Sub-ideas

  • Prove or disprove adequate binary affine consistency for an unbounded selection of normalized cores of the uniform growing-modulus source, using the actual coefficient distribution rather than coordinate extension alone. Preserve full variable accounting, and then test unit survival and legal multiplication in the concrete local sum.

Checks

The bounded GAP check exhausts all Boolean relations through arity three, all affine coefficient vectors over the binary and ternary fields, and coefficient vectors in \(\{-1,0,1\}\) over the rationals. All 2,504 valid local interpolation identities hold literally. A six-row, sixteen-variable, width-eight control over \(\mathbb F_{257}\) has rank six after the matching-grid choice; the unperturbed matrix fails full rank as a negative control. The chosen perturbation flags are \((0,0,0,1,0,1)\), with allowed-set sizes \(56,70,70,42,70,42\). All 774 pins through two coordinates and 1,536 polarity counterexamples pass; the first two layers sum to the binary affine unit.

The finite example does not satisfy a nonvacuous asymptotic core bound at these small parameters and is not used to claim one. One initial GAP attempt stopped on unsupported Boolean-to-integer conversion; the corrected run reached its final success marker. No global PC minimum or large random-instance experiment was computed. The degree-eight ceiling is the analytic interpolation ledger, not an exact-degree measurement.

Verification gate. The sole fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed after the Boolean ambient algebra, cylinder ideals and multilinear filtration were made explicit to distinguish this space from the earlier ordinary phase-ring candidate. It found no mathematical gap or unused substantive hypothesis and agreed with the limited unchanged-goal assessment. See the review record. The full fixed-cutoff, projection-core, private-projection, branching-witness and non-Horn records were read; the new local interpolation and source encoding checks passed. Searches covered affine hulls, local linear consequences, Gaussian elimination and coordinate-extension traps; the elementary mechanism is presented without a novelty claim.

Process assessment. The source control tests a named general inference from the proved deterministic source properties, not an arbitrary formula family. Its limitation under core deletion and its non-random coefficient law are retained. 9 October 2026 — A fourth moment gives affine-full modular cores

Working source repair with larger logarithmic width; no near-half lower bound. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, use the actual random coefficient law to evade the binary affine trap. Can one ensure the new affine-consistency screen survives every selected core and projection, rather than only verify field consistency over the odd modulus?

Route comparison. Direct high-cutoff properness and multiplication remain the main target, but the low-degree unit must first be excluded. A second-moment union bound over all binary affine hyperplanes is insufficient. We strengthen the probability argument to a fourth moment, then change the alternative source's width and occurrence parameters so that the resulting bound tends to zero. Full affine hull in each row is a sufficient convenience stronger than joint affine consistency; it is not added as a requirement on the general specification.

New mathematics. Odd-field dependencies among four Boolean points are ordinary parallelograms. Counting them bounds the fourth centered moment of a random modular fibre's intersection with any fixed subset of the cube. A union bound over binary affine half-cubes, with five incidence partitions and width \(8l\), gives full binary affine hull in every row with high probability. This property survives arbitrary coordinate projection, so all normalized cores pass the affine screen simultaneously.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The distribution-specific affine-collapse gap is repaired for the modified source, with uniform core retention and a polynomial encoding in the actual terminal variables. No unit-survival or multiplier-closure theorem at the near-half cutoff is proved, and the growing-width Frege transfer is still open. The previous three-partition source is not refuted by failure of this particular moment bound.

General statement. lem:modular-affine-moment is an all-parameter probability bound over every odd finite field. thm:affine-full-modular-cores provides an unbounded source with the complete revised parameter ledger and all-core quantifiers.

A fourth moment on the Boolean cube

Let \(q\) be an odd prime power, \(k\ge1\), and let \(a_1,\ldots,a_k,b\) be independent uniform elements of \(\mathbb F_q\). For a fixed nonempty \(T\subseteq\{0,1\}^k\), put \(N=|T|\), \(X=|\{x\in T:a\cdot x=b\}|\), and \(\mu=N/q\). These are ordinary real-valued random counts; no real moments are reduced into the binary proof field.

Lemma (modular affine moment). One has

\[\mathbb E(X-\mu)^4\le\frac Nq+\frac{3N^2}{q^2}+\frac{3\cdot6^k}{q^3},\qquad \Pr[X=0]\le\frac{q^3}{N^3}+\frac{3q^2}{N^2}+\frac{3q6^k}{N^4}.\tag{1}\]

Consequently the probability that the full allowed row relation fails to have affine hull \(\mathbb F_2^k\) is at most

\[16q^3/4^k+24q^2/2^k+96q(3/4)^k.\tag{2}\]

Bounds larger than one are simply uninformative; the application below uses a decaying parameter regime.

Boolean point dependencies. Any three distinct Boolean points are affinely independent over every field. Complement the coordinates where the first point is one to make it zero. Two distinct nonzero Boolean vectors cannot be nonzero scalar multiples: a nonzero coordinate forces the scalar to be one, and then the vectors would agree.

Over an odd field, four distinct Boolean points are affinely dependent exactly when some pairing has equal ordinary integer vector sums. To prove the nontrivial direction, again move one point to zero by coordinate complementations and write the other three as \(u,v,w\), with \(u,v\) independent and \(w=\alpha u+\beta v\). At least two of the coordinate types \(10,01,11\) occur for \((u_i,v_i)\). If \(10,01\) occur, then \(\alpha,\beta\in\{0,1\}\); distinctness from \(0,u,v\) forces both to be one, and type \(11\) cannot occur since \(2\notin\{0,1\}\). Hence \(w=u+v\) on disjoint supports. If the occurring independent types are \(10,11\) or \(01,11\), the same two binary-value conditions instead give \(w=u-v\) or \(w=v-u\). These are again parallelograms after relabelling. Coordinate complementations preserve equal pair sums. Conversely an equal pair sum gives a dependence, and the three-point fact makes its augmented-vector rank three (affine dimension two). For a fixed pairing, each coordinate has six ordered bit patterns satisfying the equality, so there are at most \(3\cdot6^k\) ordered dependent distinct quadruples.

Moment expansion. Write \(I_x=\mathbf1_{a\cdot x=b}\), \(p=1/q\), and \(Z_x=I_x-p\). For any collection of points, the probability that all its indicators are one is \(q^{-r}\), where \(r\) is the rank of their augmented vectors \((x,-1)\). Distinct pairs and triples therefore give independent indicators. Four distinct points give zero expectation for the product of their centered indicators unless they are a dependent quadruple, in which case that expectation is \(p^3-p^4\le p^3\).

In the expansion of \(\mathbb E(\sum_xZ_x)^4\), terms with three distinct points vanish by three-wise independence, and terms with multiplicities three and one vanish by pairwise independence. The all-equal terms sum to at most \(Np\). The two-pair terms are at most \(3N(N-1)p^2\le3N^2p^2\). The distinct dependent quadruples contribute at most \(3\cdot6^k p^3\). This proves the moment bound. The event \(X=0\) has \(|X-\mu|^4=\mu^4\), giving the second bound in (1).

Binary affine hull. If the allowed set is contained in a proper affine hyperplane over \(\mathbb F_2\), it misses the complementary binary affine half-cube. There are \(2(2^k-1)<2^{k+1}\) such half-cubes, each of size \(N=2^{k-1}\). Substitute this value into (1) and union bound. The three terms become exactly those in (2), with the displayed upper constants. This also excludes the empty allowed set. \(\square\)

Change the source parameters

For integers \(l\to\infty\), use

\[v=l2^l,\quad k=8l,\quad m=5\cdot2^{l-3}=5v/k,\quad v^2Choose five independent uniform labelled partitions of the \(v\) variables into \(k\)-sets, and sample every supported modular coefficient and every target independently and uniformly in \(\mathbb F_q\). Scopes may repeat across layers; row occurrences remain distinct. Keep

\[d=\lfloor k/64\rfloor,\quad \beta=255/256,\quad \alpha=e^{-512},\quad R=\lfloor\alpha v/k\rfloor,\quad \gamma=\alpha/256.\tag{4}\]

Theorem (affine-full modular cores). With probability \(1-o(1)\), the system has full modulus-field row rank, a field solution, no Boolean solution, nonempty essential input rows, exactly five occurrences per original variable, and extension of every pin on at most \(d\) coordinates. Every input row has full binary affine hull. Every minimal unsatisfiable core after any permitted complete normalization has \(n\ge\gamma v\) actual variables, essential non-universal rows of width \(O(\log n)\), between two and five occurrences per variable, full binary affine hull in every row, and no common polarity making all rows intersection-closed. Its canonical encoding length is \(O(n^9)\).

For every such terminal formula, work in \(B=\mathbb F_2[x]/(x_i^2-x_i)\) with the multilinear total-degree filtration and cylinder vanishing ideals \(J_a\), and put \(K_D=\sum_a(J_a\cap B_{\le D})\). This binary local ideal sum has \(K_1=0\). This does not assert \(1\notin K_D\) or multiplication closure for larger \(D\). Terminal PC degree \(D\) replays on the original system through \( \max\{k,D\} \) over every proof field.

Affine-hull probability. Conditional on any scopes, multiply (2) by \(m\). At (3) its terms are bounded respectively by

\[O(l^6 2^{-9l}),\qquad O(l^4 2^{-3l}),\qquad O\left(l^2\left[8(3/4)^8\right]^l\right).\tag{5}\]

The last base is \(6561/8192<1\), so the failure probability tends to zero. This is a simultaneous statement for every row, not one row chosen after observing the data. In contrast, substituting the earlier \(k=4l\), three-partition parameters into the last term produces the nondecaying base \(8(3/4)^4>1\). That is a limitation of this bound, not evidence that the earlier ensemble lacks full affine hull.

Expansion and pins. The random-partition proof works for five layers as follows. For a fixed row subset of size \(s\), with counts \(s_i\) in its five layers, the probability that all its coordinates lie in a fixed \(u\)-set is at most \(\prod_i\binom u{ks_i}/\binom v{ks_i}\le(u/v)^{ks}\). Union bound and the same monotonicity in \(u\le\beta ks\), with \(x=ks/v\le\alpha\), give

\[\Pr[\exists S,|S|=s:|N(S)|\le\beta ks] \le[5e\,e^{\beta k}\beta^{k/256}x^{k/256-1}]^s \le[(5e/\alpha)e^{-k}]^s.\tag{6}\]

For sufficiently large \(k\), summing the geometric series over \(1\le s\le R\) gives \(o(1)\). For pins, the proved modular coordinate-extension bound remains

\[mq2^{-k}\sum_{j=0}^d\binom kj4^j\le mq2^{-k}(d+1)(256e)^{k/64} =O(l^3 2^{-15l/4})=o(1),\tag{7}\]

where \(e<4\) gives the coarse exponent. The estimate is uniform over all scopes. Thus the same deterministic projection core-retention lemma applies, with \(d+1-k/256>3k/256\) and eventually \(R\ge\alpha v/(2k)\), yielding \(n>3\alpha v/512>\gamma v\).

Field rank, nonempty rows and Boolean emptiness. For any sampled incidence, \(k|S|\le5|N(S)|\), so for large \(k\ge5\) Hall's condition supplies a matching saturating the rows. The matching minor is a nonzero degree-\(m\) polynomial in the independent supported coefficients; the elementary polynomial zero bound gives rank-failure probability at most \(m/q\). At most \(5v/q\) is lost to zero supported entries. Full row rank gives field consistency for every target vector. Independently sampled targets give expected Boolean solution count \(2^v/q^m\le2^{-v/4}\), because \(m\log_2q>5v/4\). Nonempty rows already follow from full affine hull or pin extension. With nonzero coefficients, flipping any coordinate of an allowed tuple changes the modular sum, proving essentiality. These are the same elementary source arguments with the changed incidence degree; all exceptional events are combined by a union bound, not an independence claim.

Projection and the affine screen. For every coordinate projection \(\pi_Y\),

\[\operatorname{aff}_{\mathbb F_2}(\pi_YR_a)=\pi_Y(\operatorname{aff}_{\mathbb F_2}R_a)=\mathbb F_2^Y.\tag{8}\]

Consequently every projected row has no nonzero binary affine vanishing function, regardless of how the core or projection set is selected. The extended cylinder ideal also has no extra affine functions on spectator variables, as in the local affine theorem. Therefore \(K_1=0\) in the specified binary Boolean algebra for every normalized core. This is stronger than merely consistent affine consequences, and supplies that accepted screen without asserting anything about higher-degree cancellations.

Such an unsatisfiable collection cannot have a common Horn polarity. In that polarity a nonempty intersection-closed row contains the meet of all its allowed tuples. If that meet has a one coordinate, the coordinate is fixed to one on the row, contradicting full affine hull. Its meet is therefore zero, so zero satisfies every row, contradicting unsatisfiability. Full affine hull is preserved by the common affine polarity change. Essentiality, minimality and the two-occurrence lower bound follow from the existing complete normalization theorem; the upper occurrence bound five follows because projection and row deletion only remove incidences.

Encoding and tradeoff. The original canonical length is at most \(O(m2^k k\log(v+1))=O(v^9)\), with all variables counted and no auxiliaries. Projection cannot increase clause count or width. Since \(n\ge\gamma v\), the terminal length is \(O(n^9)\) and its width \(O(\log n)\). Literal scalar interpolation of projected clauses gives the stated original-degree replay. Deterministic choices of a good realization, core and normalization give an unbounded family independent of the proposed proof-size exponent.

Compared with the earlier source, the original occurrence count rises from three to five, the width from \(4l\) to \(8l\), and the polynomial encoding bound from degree five to degree nine. Actual variable count is unchanged. Field consistency, Boolean emptiness, uniform core retention and pin extension survive; binary affine fullness is gained. The terminal higher-occurrence density required by the fixed-width envelope is still not supplied, and this remains a logarithmic-width alternative with a separate Frege ledger. \(\square\)

What remains after the repair

The immediate affine trap is absent throughout every normalized core on the common good event. Full affine hull is not full local polynomial immunity: it rules out only affine vanishing functions. It also does not imply properness of the sum of higher-degree local ideal pieces or vanishing of multiplication defects after cross-row cancellation. Those remain the actual near-half obligation.

Follow-up. Continuing the growing-modulus lead. The next cycle is the scheduled goal-level architecture review after six research entries. It must compare this repaired logarithmic-width source and its unsolved higher-degree local sum with the other retained routes, distinguish the sufficient one-unit endpoint from full affine fullness or arbitrary-core conveniences, and choose a decisive global test. No extra early review or extension is claimed from these source repairs.

  • Follow-up. Closed: thm:affine-full-modular-cores supplies a revised unbounded uniform-coefficient source whose every normalized core has full binary affine hull in each row. Its higher-degree obligation is retained below.

Sub-ideas

  • For the affine-full five-partition modular cores, test higher-degree unit survival and multiplier compatibility of the explicit binary local ideal sum near half the actual retained variables. Use only the proved affine and pin properties unless the random coefficient law is analyzed further; do not upgrade affine fullness to polynomial immunity or a PC lower bound.

Checks

The bounded exact GAP run tests all 3,360 ordered distinct four-point configurations and 672 three-point configurations on the three-cube over the ternary and quintic fields. All dependence/parallelogram predictions hold. It verifies 30 exact fourth centered-moment identities over 10,590 coefficient/target/subset cases, including 26 nonzero dependent-quadruple contributions whose omission fails the identity. The characteristic-two tetrahedron is a negative control: it is affinely dependent without an ordinary integer parallelogram.

All 149 full-affine relations on the three-cube retain full affine hull in all 1,192 coordinate projections. The new decay base is checked exactly as \(6561/8192\), while the old-parameter base exceeds one. These verify identities, scope and arithmetic; no finite sample is called a verification of the asymptotic good event. The single run reached its final success marker, with one harmless GAP global-name syntax warning and no failed attempt.

It found no gap in the moment constants, revised probability and encoding ledger, projection quantifiers or non-Horn consequence, and agreed that goal progress is unchanged. See the review record.
