Process assessment. The previous method failure changed both the proof and the alternative source parameters, while preserving a proved unbounded source and recounting the larger polynomial encoding. The repair settles the affine-selection subproblem but leaves global near-half survival unchanged. 9 October 2026 — Preserve the unit through dynamic relations, not only local quality

Working criteria and goal-level review; no global near-half invariant. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. Six research entries have left the two necessary open statements unchanged. This is a scheduled goal-level review. Which current requirements can be removed, and which global test addresses the actual missing implication rather than adding another local source property?

New mathematics. The normalization ledger only needs original width below the target cutoff, so the lower-bound attempt can use the original independent random source and normalize after a barrier is proved. A bounded confluent reduction system gives a proper PC kernel, as a standard no-fall construction, but no such system is supplied for the source. Exact controls separate static designs, linear compression, leading-monomial homology and dynamic multiplication. The direct free-algebra Golod count becomes vacuous already in degree three after the Boolean commutation relations are paid.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The recent source repairs establish honest unbounded families and rule out affine collapse, but none establishes unit survival and multiplication closure near half. The review removes adaptive core selection from the immediate proof obligation and selects a whole-instance cancellation test. Complete Frege transfer remains open, especially for the logarithmic-width alternative.

General statement. cor:width-cutoff-core-transfer gives the exact normalization extension. prop:bounded-normal-form-transport is an all-field sufficient criterion, not a constructed hard-family invariant. prop:compression-action-defect and obs:commutative-golod-count give general method limitations. prop:horn-static-design-control is an all-field six-variable calibration, and ex:global-architecture-controls records the new finite checks.

What changed in the six entries

The Boolean-projection ceiling was strengthened to local consequence transport and an intrinsic puncture-separator bound. An explicit coupled witness showed that rank, covering radius and unconditional Boolean obstruction size can all be near half, yet a small separator gives short proofs. The ternary graph-chart theorem excluded exact maximal radius and a specified positive-deficit subclass, leaving wider columns open.

The next three entries repaired the growing-modulus source. Uniform coordinate extension and random-partition expansion retained a linear number of variables in every normalized core. A special coefficient construction then exposed a binary affine unit despite modulus-field consistency and those deterministic properties. The revised five-partition, width-\(8l\) source used a fourth moment to give full binary affine hull in every projected row, paying a larger \(O(n^9)\) encoding. This establishes \(K_1=0\), not high-degree properness or closure.

Obstacle

The unresolved implication is global and dynamic: on one actual unbounded Boolean-empty family, construct a linear relation space containing the active axioms whose kernel survives multiplication after cancellations and does not contain one at \(D=\lfloor v/2-s\rfloor\). Local affine fullness, coordinate extension, large cores, leading-monomial survivors and a normalized original-input annihilator do not prove that implication. Nor does an arbitrary linear elimination preserve the variable actions. The code-specific structural conjunction still lacks a full witness after its separator refinement; the logarithmic-width source still lacks a high-degree invariant and its full transfer ledger.

Across the goal

  • Near-half Boolean PC family. Open. All six entries since the last review advanced source construction, restrictions or tools but reported the necessary degree implication unchanged. The two open items have not dropped since the review twelve research entries back. The source route is now better specified and non-vacuous, but there is no evidence yet of a near-half surviving unit. Another local rank theorem alone would not answer this review. Six research cycles were spent on this obligation since the last goal-level review.
  • Complete Frege transfer. Open. None of these six entries took it as the primary obligation. The fixed-input-degree source theorem is available only with its complete binary blocks and product-only grammar; logarithmic width cannot be inserted without a new ledger. The missing old family remains the highest-risk prerequisite. Zero research cycles were spent on this obligation since the last goal-level review.

Architecture review

Necessary target. For every fixed relevant \( \ell\ge2 \), exhibit a polynomial-size Boolean CNF family on all actual \(v\to\infty\), independent of the proposed proof-size exponent, with no ordinary binary PC refutation through \(D=\lfloor v/2-s\rfloor\), \(s=o(v/(\log v)^{\ell-2})\). A proper linear kernel containing the active inputs and closed under each legal variable multiplication suffices. Equivalently one may construct a marked image with nonzero image of one and coherent filtered variable actions. A static Nullstellensatz design is not this endpoint.

Extra requirements. A separately large image, full affine hull in every row, rowwise immunity, all-degree no-fall behavior and equality with a full truncated global ideal are optional conveniences. Full affine hull was useful to repair the source but is stronger than joint affine consistency. Most importantly, the lower-bound proof need not analyze the law of an adaptively selected core: by the width-cutoff corollary below, prove the barrier on the original source first, then normalize. Full confluence of a chosen reduction system is another sufficient convenience; harmless falls allowed by the fixed-cutoff criterion must not be rejected automatically.

Independent alternative. Before seeing the preferred route, the sole medium reviewer received only the goal and bounded result statements. It found no justified alternative construction. It proposed a global multi-term filtered quotient with coherent variable actions, while explicitly saying that this was still a mechanism to construct, not a reduction or lower bound. Its warning about static annihilators is tested below. The independent-stage record is preserved. The preferred route revealed afterwards is the concrete sum of individual-row ideals for the five-partition modular source; a larger independently specified kernel remains allowed.

Rank normalization. All cancellation and defect images must be measured in one common quotient over the binary proof field, with the actual unit marked. A Schur complement can preserve the kernel of one matrix while losing the variable actions; the counterexample below does exactly that. Absolute local ranks or the number of monomials are not substitutes for the rank of the induced defect maps in the final quotient. Relative contraction of those images would be useful only together with a surviving unit in that same space.

Decision. Retain the repaired growing-modulus source for the next head entry, but work on its original independent coefficient law rather than on adaptive cores. Do not strengthen local quality again as the main task. Test global cross-row cancellation and one legal multiplier in the specified local sum, with lifted leading relations as an organizing tool. The confluence criterion is optional; it is not a new admissibility condition or a license to define the unknown PC closure as the answer. The code, random-half-table and Kneser alternatives remain queued.

Decisive test. On the original five-partition source, fix a relevant depth \(\ell\) and the sufficient cutoff \(D_v=\lfloor v/2-v/(\log(v+2))^{\ell-1}\rfloor\). Order rows by layer/block and variables by index. Scan pairs meeting in exactly one variable. On a pair's union \(U\), set \(K^{ab}_e=(J_a\cap B^U_{\le e})+(J_b\cap B^U_{\le e})\). Select the first pair and least \(e<|U|\) with a nonzero fall \((K^{ab}_{e+1}\cap B^U_{\le e})/K^{ab}_e\); use its first reduced-echelon complement vector \(q\) in a fixed degree-lex monomial order. This selection uses explicit local row tables, not global PC closure.

Take \(W\) to be the product of the first \(D_v-e-1\) variables outside \(U\), and \(x_j\) the next one. For large \(v\) these exist, since \(|U|=O(\log v)\). Then \(Wq\in K_{D_v}\) has degree at most \(D_v-1\). The first source-specific deliverable is either an all-row decomposition of \(x_jWq\) into pieces in \(J_c\cap B_{\le D_v}\), or a functional on \(B_{\le D_v}\) annihilating every such row piece and nonzero on \(x_jWq\). Target a high-probability assertion for this deterministic selection under the original coefficient law, intersecting with the established good-source event only at the end. If no pair/fall is selected, prove or quantify that outcome instead of conditioning it away.

A nonzero image rejects this chosen \(K_{D_v}\), not PC hardness of the source. A decomposition handles this selected class only; it must produce a mechanism for the remaining classes, not be called full closure. This transports the recorded spectator-fall control into the actually constrained whole instance, where other rows may absorb it. The next research entry must report this test's actual outcome, including an unsuccessful proof attempt. A local defect before quotienting by all rows or another affine-rank check does not answer it.

Normalize only after proving the barrier

Corollary (width cutoff transfer). Let an unsatisfiable canonical Boolean family have original width \(w_v\), no ordinary PC refutation through \(D_v=\lfloor v/2-s_v\rfloor\), and eventually \(w_v\le D_v\). Fix \( \ell\ge2 \) and \(s_v\ge0\) with \(s_v=o(v/(\log v)^{\ell-2})\). Every unsatisfiable whole-row core, followed by the permitted private/irrelevant-coordinate normalization, has terminal count \(n_v\ge D_v+1\) and no refutation through

\[\left\lfloor n_v/2-s'_v\right\rfloor,\qquad s'_v=\max\{0,s_v-(v-n_v)/2\}=o(n_v/(\log n_v)^{\ell-2}).\tag{1}\]

Polynomial encoding and independence from the proof-size exponent survive. In particular any \(w_v=o(v)\), including the present logarithmic width, meets the width condition for large \(v\). This extends the cutoff component of the fixed-width normalization corollary; it does not turn growing width into fixed width or establish Frege transfer.

Proof. A terminal degree-\(D\) proof replays on its core through \( \max\{w_v,D\} \) by the literal projection identity, and then on the original system by axiom inclusion. Thus no terminal proof through \(D_v\) exists. A complete point-indicator refutation on \(n_v\) variables has degree at most \(n_v\), so \(n_v\ge D_v+1\). Hence \(n_v=\Theta(v)\). If the expression inside the maximum defining \(s'_v\) is nonnegative, \(n_v/2-s'_v=v/2-s_v\); otherwise \(n_v/2

A sufficient reduction construction, not a solution

Work in the ordinary polynomial ring \(P=F[x_1,\ldots,x_v]\) over any field, with a degree-compatible monomial order. Fix \(D\ge2\) and finitely many monic rules \(g=m_g-r_g\) of degree at most \(D\), with \(m_g\ne1\) and every monomial of \(r_g\) strictly below \(m_g\). At degree at most \(D\), a reduction replaces a term \(c u m_g\) by \(c u r_g\), where \(u\) is a monomial. Require every local peak of this polynomial reduction relation to have a common reduct using only the same bounded space.

Proposition (bounded normal-form transport). These hypotheses give a linear normal-form map \(N_D:P_{\le D}\to P_{\le D}\) whose kernel is proper and closed under legal variable multiplication. If every active original axiom, including Booleanity, reduces to zero, there is no ordinary PC refutation through degree \(D\).

Proof. Each reduction decreases the largest changed monomial and never raises total degree. Among the finitely many monomials through degree \(D\), the support bit string in descending monomial order strictly decreases lexicographically at each step. Hence reduction terminates. Local joinability implies unique terminal forms: by well-founded induction, any two first successors have a common descendant, whose subsequent reductions and those of the two successors have the same terminal form. This is the terminating local-confluence argument, not an appeal to an unbounded completion algorithm.

Reduce monomials and extend their unique normal forms linearly. Every polynomial can be reduced term by term to that sum, and uniqueness makes this its normal form. Thus \(N_D\) is linear and sends every allowed contextual rule difference \(ug\) to zero. Since one is irreducible, \(N_D(1)=1\). If \(f\in\ker N_D\) has degree at most \(D-1\), its reduction to zero never leaves degree \(D-1\); telescoping gives \(f=\sum_i c_i u_i g_i\) with every summand of degree at most \(D-1\). Multiplying by a variable gives a sum of allowed degree-at-most-\(D\) contextual differences, all killed by \(N_D\). Hence the kernel is closed. Induction over PC lines proves the last assertion. \(\square\)

This repackages the existing no-fall induction using explicit reductions. It is stronger than the necessary fixed-cutoff defect condition, and no source-specific rule set satisfying it is established. Choosing rules from the unknown PC closure would merely restate the goal. Neither large image dimension nor PC derivability of every extra rule is required for this sufficient kernel criterion; properness, input absorption and legal closure are what matter.

Static annihilation can miss a short dynamic proof

Proposition (Horn static-design control). Over every field, the six-variable Boolean system

\[a-1,\ b-1,\ c-1,\ ab(1-u),\ bc(1-v),\ uv(1-w),\ w\tag{2}\]

has PC degree exactly three, yet has a normalized degree-three Nullstellensatz design. This is a small Horn-pebbling calibration of the already recorded static/dynamic distinction, not a new separation claim for the target family.

Proof. Sources force \(a=b=c=1\); the next two Horn clauses force \(u=v=1\), and the last forces \(w=1\), contrary to the sink. Every forcing step is an ordinary degree-three identity, for example

\[ab(1-u)-b(1-u)(a-1)-(1-u)(b-1)=1-u.\]

Use the corresponding identity for \(v\), then for \(w\), and add the sink axiom. Through degree two the cubic Horn inputs are unavailable; the remaining source, sink and Boolean axioms are satisfiable, so degree three is exact.

Define a linear functional by evaluations

\[\Lambda(f)=-f(1,1,1,0,0,0)+f(1,1,1,1,0,0)+f(1,1,1,0,1,0),\tag{3}\]

in variable order \(a,b,c,u,v,w\). Its mass is one in every field. It kills every multiple through degree three of the source and sink equations and Booleanity. The only permitted multiples of the cubic Horn clauses are constants; their values cancel for the first two clauses and are zero for the third. Thus (3) is the asserted static design. However \(\Lambda(1-u)=0\) while \(\Lambda(v(1-u))=1\), so its scalar kernel is not closed under a legal degree-two multiplication. This is why a normalized functional must come with the required action or kernel argument. \(\square\)

The older occupancy-probe theorem already separates PC and NS in the full weak-PHP setting; its exact proof was reread. The present control is selected for its explicit six-variable moments and all-field Horn form, not presented as a novel phenomenon.

Compression and free-algebra counts

Proposition (compression action defect). For idempotent linear maps \(P,T\) on any vector space over any field,

\[PTP-(PTP)^2=PT(I-P)TP.\tag{4}\]

This follows by substituting \(T^2=T\) and expanding. Thus retaining a linear image and a marked unit does not automatically preserve Boolean multiplication. In the Boolean algebra on \(x,y\), let \(P\) send \(1,x,y,xy\) respectively to \(1,0,y,1\). Then \(P^2=P\), it kills the two relations \(x,xy-1\), and \(P(1)=1\). Nevertheless \(P(yx)=1\) even though \(P(x)=0\). The static quotient is proper but its kernel fails a legal degree-two multiplication; adjoining that missing action relation creates one. For multiplication \(T_x\), the compression on \(\operatorname{span}\{1,y\}\) sends \(1\mapsto0,y\mapsto1\), so it is nonzero with square zero. This is an exact linear-elimination control, not a failure of the isospectral Schur theorem.

Observation (commutative Golod count). Present the homogenized Boolean algebra using \(n=v+1\) noncommuting generators \(x_1,\ldots,x_v,z\), all commutators, and \(x_i^2-zx_i\). The quadratic relation count is

\[r_2=\binom n2+n-1=\frac{(n-1)(n+2)}2.\]

These relations are linearly independent: each Boolean relation has its own square term, and the commutators are independent on ordered mixed terms. The reciprocal Golod lower series starts with

\[1,\quad n,\quad n^2-r_2,\quad n(n^2-2r_2)=n(2-n).\tag{5}\]

For \(v\ge2\), its degree-three coefficient is negative, so the standard truncated lower series supplies no positive bound from that degree onward. Higher-degree input relations only worsen this direct count. This is a limitation of the free-algebra counting translation, not a PC upper bound: even the Boolean base alone has nonzero \(z^D\) in every degree, witnessed by setting all \(x_i=0\). Omitting the commutators would change the commutative proof system.

Outside leads

  • Decreasing diagrams. Klop–van Oostrom–de Vrijer, Corollary 19, gives confluence from well-founded decreasing local diagrams. The bounded terminating specialization is proved above without importing an unbounded normal form. For the known control \(xy-1,yz-1,xz\) with Booleanity, all degree-two monomials have unique reductions, but \(xyz\) has terminal outcomes \(z,x,0\). Thus the first unjoinable overlap detects the paid degree-three obstruction. With tails zero, the same leading monomials give the satisfiable system \(xy,yz,xz\), whose tested reductions are confluent. Answers. A source-specific bounded diagram construction with a surviving unit could prove legal multiplier compatibility for near-half-pc-family; no such diagrams are supplied yet. Test. Passed as a degree-aware dynamic-consistency mechanism and negative control, not as a lower-bound certificate.
  • LCM-lattice homology. Gasharov–Peeva–Welker, Theorem 2.1, computes monomial-ideal Betti numbers from homology of open lcm intervals. For leading monomials \(xy,yz,xz\), the interval below \(xyz\) is three isolated atoms, giving two independent first-syzygy classes. Lifting their relations with the actual tails gives \(z(xy-1)-x(yz-1)=x-z\) and \(z(xy-1)-y(xz)=-z\); with zero tails both vanish. The lattice locates cancellations but does not determine the inhomogeneous result. Answers. A costed lift of these relations, including subsequent new relations and their multiplier images, could organize the global defect calculation for near-half-pc-family. Test. Passed as a cancellation locator; the stronger inference from leading monomials alone to unit survival is falsified by the paired controls.
  • Golod–Shafarevich growth. The basic inequality and proof in Regev–Regev, Theorem 2.1, and the explicit series convention in Iyudu–Shkarin's Golod–Shafarevich bound, suggest preserving a quotient by counting generators and relations. In the actual commutative Boolean presentation, (5) cuts off that direct lower series in degree three. Avoiding Booleanity or commutation is not a paid repair. Answers. A relative, marked-element growth theorem for the real Boolean base could address near-half-pc-family, but the free-algebra estimate does not. Test. Falsified for the direct generator-count translation by the all-parameter calculation; the target theorem itself is not refuted.

Absurd bridges

  • Quantum spectral elimination. Bach–Fröhlich–Sigal, Feshbach isospectrality theorem, uses an invertible complementary block to preserve kernels under the Feshbach map. Translate elimination of high-degree relation coordinates into an exact finite-field Schur quotient, while transporting every variable action. The finite matrix identity is field-independent, but (4) and the explicit quotient by \(x,xy-1\) show that preserving a static kernel and the unit is insufficient. Including the omitted action relation repairs the algebraic accounting and kills the unit in this easy example. Answers. A Schur reduction controlling all induced actions could give a smaller common quotient for near-half-pc-family; isospectrality for one matrix does not supply it. Test. Falsified for naive kernel-preserving compression; the action-aware correction passes the control but supplies no hard-family invariant.
  • Chemical complex balance. Feinberg's Theorem 6.1.1 gives positive steady-state structure for the stated weakly reversible deficiency-zero mass-action systems. Try to translate balanced local state weights into a normalized binary functional. For \(A\rightleftarrows B\), the deficiency is \(2-1-1=0\). Equal rates give weights \((1/2,1/2)\), but the binary stationarity and mass-one equations are inconsistent. Rates one and two instead give \((2/3,1/3)\); the odd denominator permits reduction, but it becomes the single-state weight \((0,1)\). Thus neither the symmetric normalization nor its odd-denominator repair provides the intended distributed binary state. Answers. A finite-field balance law preserving normalization and legal multipliers could address near-half-pc-family; positive real equilibrium alone cannot. Test. Falsified for these two direct translations, without rejecting every possible reaction-network construction.

Checks, sources and scope

The consolidated GAP control tests all 20 monomials through degree three in the two rewrite systems, the two lifted lcm relations and compression identities over the rationals, binary and ternary fields, and both reaction normalizations. It verifies 471 allowed original-input multiples of the static Horn design and the degree-three PC identities in those three fields. The first bounded run passed; a second run added the static/dynamic control and also passed. These are new checks of the selected mechanisms, not a new asymptotic hardness experiment.

The source ledger records exact statements and proof scope. The lcm theorem and basic Golod inequality were read with their proofs; the confluence proof sections and the Feshbach assumptions/proof were inspected. The chemistry statement is a real positive theorem, and its two-state tests are proved directly. Failed publisher retrievals were replaced by accessible primary proof sources; no inaccessible result is used as a premise. No third-party full text is committed.

Verification gate. The same staged GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed all new claims and the comparison with its independent proposal. It required a source-specific first deliverable; the deterministic local-fall selection, canonical spectator lift, all-row certificate alternatives and high-probability scope were added and passed on follow-up. No mathematical gap or unused hypothesis remained. See the consolidated review record. The no-fall criterion, fixed-cutoff transport, private projection and earlier PC/NS separation were reread in full. The local rule and action changes were checked exhaustively at their stated bounded scopes. No Lean or framework changes are made.

Sub-ideas

  • Execute the deterministic one-bit-overlap fall selection and spectator lift in the Decisive test paragraph. Prove a high-probability all-row decomposition or a separating functional for its chosen multiplier image, or quantify failure of the selection. A defect rejects only the chosen kernel; normalize after a proved barrier instead of conditioning on adaptive cores.

Process assessment. The review distinguishes source repairs from the missing global theorem and removes an unnecessary adaptive-core burden. The blind reviewer did not manufacture a successful alternative. The passed leads supply ways to organize genuine dynamic checks; the failed translations identify exact missing hypotheses. Their control examples are not evidence that the source itself is hard or easy.

Decision. Core: test whole-instance higher-degree cancellation and legal multiplication on the original affine-full source; retain the one-unit endpoint and the full ordinary-degree ledger. No publication, stopping or Lean work is authorized by this review.

9 October 2026 — Row interpolation absorbs the test and forces central targets

Working absorption and degree bounds; complement-symmetric source repair remains unproved hard. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. Execute the scheduled whole-instance multiplier test on the original five-partition source. Does another row absorb the selected spectator lift? Compare a positive decomposition with a separating functional, rather than treating a pair-local defect as global.

Architecture test. The selected multiplier images are absorbed with probability \(1-o(1)\), uniformly over every possible pair and fall in the specified selection rule. A degree-lowering row relation gives an explicit three-row decomposition. No conditional assumption that a fall exists is used: on the good incidence event every candidate is absorbed; an empty selection returns no obstruction. This does not prove all multiplier maps vanish or that one survives. The same local calculation gives an actual degree ceiling that rules out the original uniform-target source for the depth-\( \ell\ge3 \) barrier, so the next candidate must change.

New mathematics. A noncentral even-width modular row interpolates one degree lower than the balanced half-width bound. A fractional exact cover of the variables converts these local degrees into an actual PC ceiling. Uniform targets are noncentral in every row with high probability here, giving degree at most \(v/2-v/k+2\). Centralizing each target restores complement symmetry; its zero-coordinate slice has exactly the earlier uniform affine-row law, preserving full binary affine hull and uniform core retention in a revised unbounded source.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The original five-partition family is excluded at depths at least three, and the specified candidate-kernel test is settled by absorption. The central replacement is non-vacuous with its full ledger, but has no near-half lower bound or complete growing-width transfer. An original-instance upper bound does not automatically upper-bound every selected core.

General statement. lem:modular-row-interpolation and thm:fractional-row-degree-ceiling apply to all stated modular systems. prop:row-reduction-fall-absorption is an all-field ideal identity, and thm:canonical-padding-absorption applies it uniformly under the source's incidence law. cor:noncentral-modular-exclusion gives the actual source exclusion; thm:central-modular-cores proves the revised unbounded construction.

Interpolation inside one modular row

Let \(q\) be an odd prime, \(F\) a proof field of characteristic different from \(q\), and let one row on \(k\ge1\) Boolean coordinates have all coefficients \(a_i\in\mathbb F_q^\times\) and target \(b\). Call it central when \(2b=\sum_i a_i\) in \(\mathbb F_q\), which for a nonempty allowed relation is equivalent to invariance under simultaneous complementation. Noncentral rows have disjoint allowed and complemented sets.

Lemma (modular row interpolation). Evaluation onto the row's allowed Boolean tuples is surjective from multilinear polynomials of degree at most

\[t=\begin{cases} (k-1)/2,&k\text{ odd},\\ k/2-1,&k\text{ even and the row is noncentral},\\ k/2,&k\text{ even and the row is central}. \end{cases}\tag{1}\]

Every monomial of degree greater than \(k/2\) has, modulo this local vanishing ideal, a representative of strictly smaller degree. At even width the same is true in degree \(k/2\) for noncentral rows. Each resulting local relation has an ordinary PC derivation from the canonical row clauses through the original width \(k\), not automatically through its smaller final degree.

Proof. Reuse the recorded balanced-character identity. In a finite extension containing a primitive \(q\)-th root \(\zeta\), write \(u_i(c)=1+(\zeta^c-1)x_i\). For any subset \(S\) of the row scope,

\[G_S=\prod_{i\in S}u_i(a_i)-\zeta^b\prod_{i\notin S}u_i(-a_i)\tag{2}\]

vanishes on the allowed tuples. If \(|S|>k/2\), its unique top-degree term is a nonzero multiple of \(x_S\); all other terms have smaller degree. Nonzero coefficients are essential because \(\zeta^{a_i}-1\ne0\).

If \(k=2r\) and \(|S|=r\), the two relations \(G_S,G_{S^c}\) have leading terms on \(x_S,x_{S^c}\). Their coefficient determinant is

\[\left(\prod_i(\zeta^{a_i}-1)\right)\left(1-\zeta^{2b-\sum_i a_i}\right),\tag{3}\]

because \(\zeta^{-a_i}-1=-\zeta^{-a_i}(\zeta^{a_i}-1)\) and \(k\) is even. It is nonzero exactly for a noncentral row. Solve for each top monomial to obtain a representative of degree at most \(r-1\). Repeatedly reducing degree proves (1), since every function on a Boolean subset is represented by a multilinear polynomial.

Apply an \(F\)-linear coefficient projection from the finite extension to \(F\), fixing one, to each normalized relation \(x_S-p_S\). It remains a valid relation with the same leading coefficient and degree bound over \(F\). Finally, every multilinear local vanishing relation is literally the scalar sum of its values times the original forbidden-tuple indicators. That derives it through degree \(k\). This is an explicit consequence of the existing character mechanism, not a new uncharged division rule. \(\square\)

How one row absorbs a padded fall

In a common Boolean function algebra over any field, let \(J_a\) be ideals and \(K_d=\sum_a(J_a\cap B_{\le d})\). Suppose \(f\in K_{e+1}\cap B_{\le e}\), and let \(W\) be a squarefree monomial of degree \(L\). Assume some row ideal \(J_c\) contains \(p=x_S-r\), where \(S\subseteq\operatorname{supp}W\), \(|S|=s\), and \(\deg r\le s-1\).

Proposition (row fall absorption). For every variable \(x_j\),

\[x_jWf\in K_{L+e+1}.\tag{4}\]

Proof. Write \(W=W_0x_S\), and take \(f=\sum_a f_a\), \(f_a\in J_a\cap B_{\le e+1}\). Then, in the same Boolean algebra,

\[x_jWf=x_jW_0f\,p+\sum_a x_jW_0r\,f_a.\tag{5}\]

The first piece is in \(J_c\), of degree at most \(1+L-s+e+s=L+e+1\). Each other piece is in its \(J_a\), of degree at most \(1+L-s+(s-1)+(e+1)=L+e+1\). Repeated Boolean variables only reduce these degree bounds. This proves (4). In particular a modular row containing more than half of its scope in \(W\) supplies such a relation by (2). No disjointness from the support of \(f\) is needed for this ideal identity. \(\square\)

For a nonzero calibration, use \(g=(1-z)(1-x)\), \(h=zy\), and \(f=(1-x)y=yg+(1-x)h\), the earlier degree-two fall. Set \(W=ab\) and take another row \(a+b+d=0\pmod3\), whose Boolean tuples are \(000,111\). It has \(p=ab-a\). Then (5) is the literal degree-five identity

\[wf(ab-a)+wa(yg)+wa((1-x)h)=wabf.\]

Without the third row the spectator-lift obstruction applies; with it the selected image is absorbed using a nonzero lower-degree remainder. This is a satisfiable method control, not a hard-family witness.

The scheduled selector is absorbed in the full source

Theorem (canonical padding absorption). Use the original five random partitions with \(v=l2^l,k=8l\), and any nonzero modular coefficients and targets. Fix \( \ell\ge2 \) and

\[D=\left\lfloor v/2-v/(\log(v+2))^{\ell-1}\right\rfloor.\]

With probability \(1-o(1)\) over the partitions, every pair union \(U\) of size at most \(2k-1\), every \(0\le e<|U|\), and every pair-local fall \(f\in K^{ab}_{e+1}\cap B^U_{\le e}\) in the review's selection rule satisfy \(x_jWf\in K_D\), where \(W\) is the first \(D-e-1\) outside variables and \(x_j\) the next one. This event is uniform over all coefficients, all pairs and all possible fall representatives, so selecting them after seeing the data introduces no conditioning loss.

Proof: many majority rows in a fixed prefix. Put \(P=[D-2k]\) and \(N=v/k\). In the first random partition, let \(Y\) count blocks containing more than \(k/2\) coordinates of \(P\). The count in one block has hypergeometric law with population fraction \(p=|P|/v=1/2-\delta_v\), where \(k\delta_v^2=o(1)\) and \(k^2/v=o(1)\) for the displayed cutoff. Couple sampling with replacement to sampling without replacement; their variation distance is at most \(k(k-1)/(2v)\). For independent Bernoulli bits, Cauchy–Schwarz gives variation distance from fair bits at most

\[\frac12\sqrt{(1+4\delta_v^2)^k-1}=o(1),\]

by the product chi-square identity. The fair binomial strict-majority probability tends to one half; its central mass is \(\binom{k}{k/2}2^{-k}\to0\), for example by the product bound \(\prod_{i=1}^{k/2}(1-1/(2i))\le(k/2+1)^{-1/2}\). Hence each block is a majority block with probability at least \(1/3\) for large \(v\), and \(\mu=\mathbb EY\ge N/3\).

The indicators for two distinct blocks have nonpositive covariance. Conditional on the first block's prefix count being \(r\), the second count is hypergeometric with \( |P|-r \) successes in the remaining population, so its strict-majority probability decreases in \(r\). The first indicator increases in \(r\). The identity expressing twice their covariance using an independent copy of \(r\) as the product of these two opposite differences proves the sign. Therefore \(\operatorname{Var}Y\le\mu\), and \(\Pr[Y<\mu/2]\le4/\mu\le12/N=o(1)\). In particular \(Y\ge N/6>2k\) with high probability.

Proof: uniform absorption. Any set \(U\) of size at most \(2k-1\) meets at most \(2k-1\) blocks of that partition. Thus a majority block \(X_c\) is disjoint from \(U\), simultaneously for every such \(U\). Since \(D-e-1\ge D-2k+1\), the canonical \(W\) contains \(P\setminus U\). Consequently \(|X_c\cap\operatorname{supp}W|>k/2\). Apply modular row interpolation and (5), with \(L=D-e-1\). This proves the theorem. \(\square\)

The assertion is an unconditional good-incidence event intersected with the already proved nonzero-coefficient event. If the selector finds no fall, there is no vector to test; if it finds any, its image is zero on this same event. Equivalently the probability of a nonempty selection with a surviving image is \(o(1)\), without assuming that nonempty selection has high probability. The chance of an empty selection is not separately estimated or used. This resolves the proposed search for an obstruction from this selector; it proves neither full closure nor unit survival.

Fractional covers turn interpolation into an actual ceiling

Let an unsatisfiable canonical modular system on \(v\ge2\) variables have nonzero row coefficients, row sizes \(k_a\ge1\), maximum actual width \(w=\max_a k_a\le v\), odd prime modulus \(q\), and proof characteristic different from \(q\). Suppose nonnegative real weights \(\lambda_a\) form a fractional exact cover:

\[\sum_{a:i\in X_a}\lambda_a=1\quad(i\in[v]).\]

These are combinatorial weights, not proof-field coefficients. Let \(t_a\) be (1), and define \(\delta_a=k_a/2-t_a\): it is \(1/2\) for odd width, one for even noncentral rows, and zero for even central rows. Put \(\Delta=\sum_a\lambda_a\delta_a\) and \(T=\sum_a\lambda_a t_a=v/2-\Delta\).

Theorem (fractional row ceiling). The ordinary PC degree is at most

\[\max\{w,2,\lfloor T\rfloor+1\}+1.\tag{6}\]

When \(E=\lfloor T\rfloor+1\ge\max\{w,2\}\), this is \(\lfloor v/2-\Delta\rfloor+2\). Consequently a barrier through \(D=\lfloor v/2-s\rfloor\), with \(D\ge\max\{w+1,3\}\), requires \(\Delta

Proof. Take \(E=\max\{w,2,\lfloor T\rfloor+1\}\), which is at most \(v\). If an \(E\)-subset \(S\) met every row in at most \(t_a\) variables, exact cover would give

\[E=\sum_a\lambda_a|S\cap X_a|\le\sum_a\lambda_a t_a=T,\]

a contradiction. Thus every \(E\)-subset has a degree-lowering local interpolation relation. The paid interpolation ceiling, whose ordinary-degree proof is reread, gives degree at most \(E+1\). For the final claim, the width and constant terms of (6) are at most \(D\), so \(\lfloor T\rfloor+2>D\) must hold. This gives \(T\ge D-1\), whence \(\Delta\le v/2-D+1

This is a structural learning rule for the stated modular encoding. A bounded-width exact cover with linear weighted mass of odd rows or even noncentral rows is excluded. At logarithmic width, a loss of order \(v/\log v\) already excludes the depth-at-least-three target. Arbitrary predicates, zero coefficients counted as scope variables, and proof characteristic equal to the modulus are outside the interpolation proof.

Corollary (noncentral source exclusion). In the original five-partition, width-\(k=8l\), uniform-coefficient/uniform-target source, with probability \(1-o(1)\),

\[d_2(\Gamma)\le v/2-v/k+2.\tag{7}\]

Indeed all supported coefficients are nonzero with high probability, and each independent uniform target is central with probability \(1/q\), conditional on any coefficients. A union bound \(m/q=o(1)\) makes every row noncentral. Give every row weight \(1/5\); each variable is covered exactly once, and \(\Delta=m/5=v/k\). The source is unsatisfiable on its common good event, so (6) applies. Since \(k=\Theta(\log v)\), (7) excludes every proposed original-instance barrier with \(s=o(v/\log v)\), in particular every \( \ell\ge3 \). The same argument excludes the earlier three-partition, width-\(4l\) uniform-target original source at those depths. No such conclusion is claimed for arbitrary normalized cores, which can lose the fractional exact cover, or for the depth-two requirement alone. \(\square\)

Repair by complement symmetry, with a witness

Keep \(v=l2^l,k=8l,m=5v/k\), prime \(v^2 \[b_a=\frac12\sum_{i\in X_a}a_{ai}\quad\text{in }\mathbb F_q.\tag{8}\]

Theorem (central modular cores). With probability \(1-o(1)\), this central-target ensemble is field-consistent, has full row rank and no Boolean solution, has nonzero coefficients and essential original rows with five occurrences per variable, and every row has full binary affine hull and extends all pins through \(d=\lfloor k/64\rfloor\). Every normalized minimal core retains \(n\ge e^{-512}v/256\), essential non-universal full-affine rows, two to five occurrences, no common Horn polarity, width \(O(\log n)\), and canonical length \(O(n^9)\). All original rows are complement-invariant. No near-half lower bound is asserted.

Exact slice law. In one row distinguish its first coordinate. In its zero fibre the remaining \(k-1\) variables obey

\[\sum_{i=2}^k a_i x_i=b_a.\]

The map \((a_1,\ldots,a_k)\mapsto(a_2,\ldots,a_k,b_a)\) is a bijection of \(\mathbb F_q^k\), with inverse \(a_1=2b_a-\sum_{i=2}^k a_i\). Therefore this fibre has exactly the uniform affine modular-row law used in the fourth-moment theorem, now in \(k-1\) variables. Its affine-fullness failure bound differs by constant factors only, so union over \(m\) rows still tends to zero. The one fibre is the coordinatewise complement of the zero fibre, by (8). A full-affine zero fibre spans the entire hyperplane with first coordinate zero, and a complemented point lies outside it; hence the full row has affine hull \(\mathbb F_2^k\).

The zero fibre also extends every pin through \(d\) with failure bounded by

\[q2^{-(k-1)}\sum_{j=0}^d\binom{k-1}{j}4^j\le2q2^{-k}\sum_{j=0}^d\binom kj4^j.\]

The earlier bound remains \(o(1)\) after union over rows. If the distinguished coordinate is pinned to zero, use that fibre; if pinned to one, complement a zero-fibre extension; if unpinned, set it to zero. Thus the full row extends all the required pins. No conditioning on nonzero coefficients is used in these distribution identities; its exceptional event is combined afterwards.

Global source and budget. Field consistency is automatic at \(x_i=1/2\) for every coordinate. The coefficient matrix has the same random law as before, so its matching-minor full-rank bound and the \(5v/q\) zero-coefficient bound remain. For any fixed Boolean assignment, each row's condition is

\[\sum_{i\in X_a}a_{ai}(x_i-1/2)=0.\]

Its coefficients in the independent random entries are all nonzero, so it holds with probability \(1/q\). Different rows use independent coefficient entries, even on shared variables. Thus the expected number of Boolean solutions is \(2^v/q^m\le2^{-v/4}\). The incidence expansion is unchanged. Combine these events with the slice bounds and apply the previously proved projection core-retention lemma. Full affine hull survives projection, and its non-Horn consequence and the \(O(n^9)\) encoding ledger are unchanged. This proves all assertions. \(\square\)

The tradeoff loses independent targets but keeps independent coefficient rows, all actual variables, the same logarithmic width, occurrence bound, polynomial encoding and uniform core properties. It gains exact complement symmetry and evades the noncentrality loss in (6). On the original central source that theorem gives only \(d_2\le v/2+2\), compatible with a barrier below half; no assertion of sharpness is made. Stronger interpolation or a different paid upper bound could still exclude it. The arbitrary-predicate fixed-width envelope and its width-four witness are unchanged; this is a repaired logarithmic-width alternative, not a completed Frege source.

Disposition and checks

The actual upper bound teaches more than exclusion of one sample: odd row arity and failure of complement symmetry have a quantitatively paid cost under a fractional exact cover. Changing only row labels, graph connectivity or affine fullness does not remove that cost. Central even rows avoid this particular obstruction, and the preceding construction proves their non-vacuity with the required variable/encoding accounting. The specified multiplier test is positive for both old and central sources, but it covers only its canonical padded classes, not the complete defect space.

The single bounded GAP run checks 1,520 nonzero modular rows at \(q,k=(3,4),(3,6),(5,4)\), including 336 central and 1,184 noncentral cases. All 3,040 interpolation ranks over two mismatched proof fields per modulus attain the allowed-set size at the stated cutoff. Central-row, equal-characteristic and falsely counted zero-coefficient controls reject the stronger invalid statements.

The central slice bijection is exhausted on 1,354 coefficient vectors at \(q,k=(3,6),(5,4)\), with 28,328 slice-point counts and 56,656 complement checks; all 258 full-affine slices lift as claimed. The nonzero-remainder absorption identity passes over the binary and quintic fields and the rationals. These verify the rules and distribution identity, not the asymptotic majority-row or core probabilities. The run reached its success marker without a failed attempt.

Follow-up. Continuing, with rotation at the growing-modulus head's four-entry boundary. The source repair and exact absorption leave the necessary near-half lower bound unchanged, so no extension is claimed. Keep the central source and its unresolved global kernel under this item; the next local-section head can use the new absorption identity when testing more general relations in the common quotient.

  • Follow-up. Closed: thm:canonical-padding-absorption gives the requested all-row decomposition for every possible selected class on the uniform good-incidence event. Global closure and unit survival remain queued for the central replacement.

Sub-ideas

  • For the central even-row source, construct a proper binary near-half invariant or identify an additional actual ceiling. The canonical padded pair-fall test is absorbed and cannot establish a defect; more general relations or padding selected to avoid degree-lowering rows need a new all-row argument. Preserve complement symmetry, all actual variables and the growing-width transfer gap.

Verification gate. The sole fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed after the fractional-cover theorem defined its width as the maximum actual row width, ensuring it is at most the actual variable count. It found no other correction or unused hypothesis and checked the determinant/descent, absorption degrees, adaptive-selection scope, original-instance exclusion and central slice accounting. See the review record. The full balanced-character proof, local interpolation ceiling, fixed-cutoff spectator mechanism, one-bit fibre module and random affine/core source records were reread. Newly derived interpolation and absorption rules passed exhaustive bounded checks, with exact negative controls. No numerical experiment is used to infer an asymptotic degree minimum or probability.

Process assessment. Executing the prescribed global test produced an explicit absorption mechanism, and its cheap degree consequence ruled out the current original source for the higher-depth budget. The structural reason was applied to the older source and repaired by central targets with a proved unbounded witness. Neither the repaired source nor the positive test is called a near-half lower bound.

9 October 2026 — Global marginal updates repair selectors but not their kernels

Working operator construction and obstruction controls; near-half closure remains open. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On near-half-family, repair the local-section construction by updating every context, rather than conditioning only the components containing the selected bit. The missing implication is a proper kernel compatible with every legal multiplication through the near-half cutoff. Compatibility of local coefficient families is only the starting space.

Route comparison. A further restriction of the central modular source is not yet justified by an actual upper bound. Direct control of its full local ideal sum remains available, but the scheduled selector repair has a concrete construction using right inverses of overlap moment maps. We develop that method and test both vector axiom annihilation and order independence. Full global commutation would be stronger than the degree-bounded endpoint and, as proved below, impossible on the intended unsatisfiable source.

New mathematics. Surjective overlap moment maps give explicit idempotent all-context selectors preserving compatible coefficient families. The central modular source satisfies these hypotheses with high probability, including its repeated two-variable overlaps. Full commuting selectors on this space exist exactly when actual global solutions span it. The Hardy control repairs compatibility but fails vector axiom annihilation; a satisfiable path permits a nonlocal moment correction that repairs its commuting action.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The construction supplies an explicit polynomial-size linear state space and operators, but neither a proper graded PC kernel nor near-half unit survival. The controls identify failures of this recipe, not a degree upper bound on the central source. No formula-specification refinement or Frege transfer is inferred.

General statement. thm:compatible-selector-extension and prop:commuting-selector-gluing hold for arbitrary finite Boolean context covers over every field. thm:central-overlap-selectors proves the required overlap-map hypothesis on an unbounded binary source. prop:selector-completion-controls gives literal all-field controls, beyond the finite checks.

Compatible coefficients and all-context updates

Let \(F\) be any field, let \(v\ge1\), and let a nonempty finite family of contexts \(X_a\subseteq[v]\) cover all variables. Each has a nonempty allowed relation \(R_a\subseteq\{0,1\}^{X_a}\). Let \(V\subseteq\bigoplus_a F^{R_a}\) consist of coefficient families whose full marginals agree on each pairwise overlap, including agreement of total mass on empty overlaps. Write \(\epsilon:V\to F\) for this common mass. These are signed coefficient families, with no positivity assumption.

Set \[\mathcal M=\{\varnothing\}\cup\{\{i\}:i\in[v]\}\cup\bigcup_{a\ne b}2^{X_a\cap X_b},\qquad \mathcal M_a=\{S\in\mathcal M:S\subseteq X_a\}.\tag{1}\] The family is downward closed. For \(w_a\in F^{R_a}\), define \[E_aw_a=\left(\sum_{r\in R_a}x_S(r)w_a(r)\right)_{S\in\mathcal M_a},\qquad x_S=\prod_{i\in S}x_i.\tag{2}\] Assume each \(E_a\) is onto, and fix a linear right inverse \(L_a\). Equality of full marginals is equivalent to equality of the corresponding monomial moments: tuple indicators on an overlap expand as products of \(x_i\) and \(1-x_i\), over every field.

Theorem (compatible selector extension). Under these assumptions \(V\) contains a mass-one vector, and there are explicit linear maps \(T_i:V\to V\) satisfying \(T_i^2=T_i\), acting as pointwise multiplication by \(x_i\) on every component whose context contains \(i\). They satisfy \[\epsilon T_i(w)=\langle x_i,w\rangle,\qquad \epsilon T_iT_j=\epsilon T_jT_i.\tag{3}\] If a polynomial \(p\) on one context vanishes on its allowed relation, then \(\epsilon p(T)=0\) as a functional on \(V\), with any ordering of the factors within each monomial. Vector annihilation \(p(T)=0\), higher-order commutation and annihilation of cofactor multiples do not follow.

Construction and proof. Any global moment assignment \(\theta\in F^{\mathcal M}\) has a compatible realization \(w_a=L_a(\theta|_{\mathcal M_a})\). Choose \(\theta_\varnothing=1\) for a mass-one family. For \(w\in V\) and a fixed bit \(i\), prescribe new moments as follows. If some context contains \(S\cup\{i\}\), set \[\theta'_S=\langle x_{S\cup\{i\}},w_a\rangle;\tag{4}\] otherwise set \(\theta'_S=0\). The first value is independent of its chosen context: any two owners have the entire union in their overlap. In components containing \(i\), set \((T_iw)_a(r)=r_iw_a(r)\); in all other components set \((T_iw)_a=L_a(\theta'|_{\mathcal M_a})\). Every component has the prescribed moments, proving compatibility and linearity.

Applying this update twice gives the same vector. In a component containing \(i\), diagonal selection is idempotent. Every moment (4) is then unchanged, because its owner contains \(i\) and \(x_i^2=x_i\) there; the missing-union moments are again zero. The same fixed right inverse therefore gives the same outside components. The empty-set moment is the old \(i\)-moment. For \(\epsilon T_iT_j\), a context containing both bits gives their common pair moment, while if no such context exists both orders give zero. Finally choose a context supporting \(p\). Throughout any word on those variables its component undergoes exactly the usual commuting diagonal actions, and so its final mass is \(\langle p,w_a\rangle=0\). All components of the resulting compatible vector have that mass. This proves the scalar assertion, not that the vector vanishes. \(\square\)

The central source supplies the overlap maps

Use the central source with \(v=l2^l\), \(k=8l\), \(m=5v/k\), prime \(v^2<q<2v^2\), five independent uniform labelled partitions into \(k\)-sets, and independent uniform supported coefficients over \(\mathbb F_q\). Put \(b_a=\frac12\sum_i a_{ai}\). Here the coefficient space and maps (1)–(4) are over \(\mathbb F_2\).

Theorem (central overlap selectors). With probability \(1-o(1)\), all the previously proved central-source properties hold and every overlap moment map (2) is surjective. Consequently this Boolean-empty source admits the explicit compatible coefficient space and mass-one idempotent selectors above, with at most \(m2^k=O(v^9)\) raw linear-state coordinates. No additional Boolean variables are introduced, and no PC degree lower bound is asserted.

Overlap geometry. With high probability no two scopes intersect in three or more coordinates, and the graph whose edges represent intersections of size two is a matching. For one fixed pair of blocks from different layers, choose three coordinates of the first block; the chance all three lie in the second is \((k)_3/(v)_3\). Thus the probability of a triple intersection is \(O(k^6/v^3)\), and the union over \(m^2\) pairs is \(O(k^4/v)=o(1)\). Blocks in the same layer are disjoint.

For fixed distinct rows \(a,b,c\), condition on \(X_a\). If \(b,c\) lie in different layers from each other and from \(a\), the events that they each contain a chosen pair from \(X_a\) have conditional probability \(O(k^4/v^4)\). There are \(O(k^4)\) choices of the two pairs. If \(b,c\) lie in the same other layer, the chosen pairs must be disjoint; sampling two disjoint labelled blocks gives the same upper bound by assigning four prescribed distinct coordinates to those blocks. If either partner is in \(a\)'s layer, the event is impossible. Hence the probability of two double-overlap neighbours is \(O(k^8/v^4)\), and its union over \(m^3\) ordered triples is \(O(k^5/v)=o(1)\).

On this geometry event, \(\mathcal M_a\) contains the constant and every single variable, and at most one quadratic monomial, belonging to the unique double-overlap partner. Indeed if a pair contained in \(X_a\) first appears as another pairwise overlap, either that overlap uses \(a\) or both of its owners are double-overlap neighbours of \(a\). The latter is impossible on the matching event. No triple belongs to any \(\mathcal M_a\).

One quadratic in every row. For every pair \(\{s,t\}\) of local coordinates, consider the binary functions \[f=c_0+\sum_{i=1}^k c_ix_i+c_{st}x_sx_t.\tag{5}\] We prove that no nonzero such function vanishes on the allowed row, simultaneously for every row and pair. Distinguish the row's first coordinate. Its zero fibre, in the remaining \(k-1\) bits \(z\), has exactly the uniform affine modular-row law: the map from its coefficient vector to \((a_2,\ldots,a_k,b_a)\) is a bijection. The one fibre is the complement of the zero fibre. For a fixed nonzero \(f\), at least one of \(f(0,z)\) and \(f(1,1-z)\) is a nonzero Boolean polynomial of degree at most two. Its support has size \(N\ge2^{k-3}\).

For completeness, a nonzero binary multilinear polynomial of degree at most \(d\) on \(n\ge d\) bits has support at least \(2^{n-d}\). Induct on the number of variables using \(f=g+x_nh\). If \(h=0\), support doubles from \(g\). Otherwise at each point where \(h=1\) the two outputs differ, so their combined support is at least the support of \(h\), whose degree is at most \(d-1\). This proves the bound, including the constant base case. For the chosen nonzero slice polynomial, its support is fixed independently of the modular coefficients.

The subset fourth-moment bound in dimension \(k-1\) gives omission probability at most \[q^3/N^3+3q^2/N^2+3q6^{k-1}/N^4.\] There are fewer than \(2^{k+2}\) nonzero choices in (5). For a fixed pair, a union bound therefore gives \[2048q^3/4^k+768q^2/2^k+8192q(3/4)^k.\tag{6}\] Multiply by \(m\binom{k}{2}\). At the source parameters each term tends to zero: the first two have exponential factors \(2^{-9l}\) and \(2^{-3l}\), while the third has factor \((6561/8192)^l\), with only polynomial factors in \(l\). This proves the stated independence of the constant, singletons and any one quadratic on every allowed row. It proves surjectivity of the dual moment map (2) on the geometry event. It does not prove independence of all quadratic monomials simultaneously.

Intersect this event with the already proved central-source good event; no independence among these events is needed. The row-state inventory is bounded by \(m2^k\), regardless of how right inverses are chosen. Its coordinates are proof-method states, not formula variables. The original variable count, logarithmic width, polynomial canonical encoding and all-core retention statements remain exactly those of the central-source theorem. The construction here applies to the original source; no automatic identical overlap geometry is asserted after arbitrary normalization. \(\square\)

Why full commuting completion is too strong

Proposition (commuting selector gluing). For the full compatible coefficient space \(V\) of any finite Boolean cover above, globally commuting idempotent operators with the exact local diagonal action exist if and only if \(V\) is spanned by the restriction families \(\delta_x=(\delta_{x|X_a})_a\) of actual global satisfying assignments. This statement does not require the moment maps to be onto. In particular, an unsatisfiable source with a mass-one compatible family cannot admit such a globally commuting completion.

Proof. Given commuting idempotents, form the joint projectors \[P_x=\prod_{i:x_i=1}T_i\prod_{i:x_i=0}(I-T_i),\qquad x\in\{0,1\}^v.\tag{7}\] They sum to the identity. For \(u=P_xw\), the identities \(T_iu=x_iu\) and the exact local action force component \(u_a\) to be supported only at \(x|X_a\). If this tuple is forbidden in any context, that component is zero and its mass is zero. All component masses then vanish, and a coefficient vector supported at at most one tuple with zero mass is zero. Thus \(u=0\). If all tuples are allowed, every coefficient equals the common mass and \(u=\epsilon(u)\delta_x\). Summing over \(x\) proves the spanning assertion. Conversely, choose a basis of \(V\) from these actual solution families and set \(T_i\delta_x=x_i\delta_x\) on the basis. The operators commute, are idempotent and have the required local action on every linear combination. \(\square\)

This is the elementary simultaneous-idempotent form of the existing global-solution obstruction. It restricts a full representation, not the formula specification: the desired proof only needs degree-bounded actions on a selected cyclic state and an appropriate kernel. A blanket requirement of global commutation on \(V\) would discard the unsatisfiable source by construction.

Two exact completion controls

Proposition (selector completion controls). Over every field, the all-context update repairs the earlier Hardy marginal failure but can fail both vector axiom annihilation and legal cofactor annihilation. On a separate satisfiable two-clause path, changing a missing cross-context moment repairs global commutation and annihilates both clauses.

Hardy calculation. Use contexts \((a,b),(a,b'),(a',b),(a',b')\). The first allows all four tuples; the second and third forbid \(00\); the fourth forbids \(11\). In tuple order \(00,01,10,11\), use the earlier source family \[r_1=(1,0,0,0),\quad r_2=(0,1,1,-1),\quad r_3=r_4=(0,0,1,0).\tag{8}\] It is compatible and has mass one. The moment maps use only mass and singletons. On the first context choose the right inverse whose \(ab\)-moment is zero; the other three are uniquely determined. Write a compatible family by its coordinates \((m,a,a',b,b',c)\), where \(c\) is its \(ab\)-moment. Directly from (4), \[ \begin{aligned} T_a(m,a,a',b,b',c)&=(a,a,0,c,a+b'-m,c),\\ T_{a'}(m,a,a',b,b',c)&=(a',0,a',a'+b-m,0,0),\\ T_b(m,a,a',b,b',c)&=(b,c,a'+b-m,b,0,c),\\ T_{b'}(m,a,a',b,b',c)&=(b',a+b'-m,0,0,b',0). \end{aligned}\tag{9}\] The vector (8) is \((1,0,1,0,0,0)\). For \(S_a=I-T_a\), the repaired family \(S_ar\) has components \[(1,0,0,0),\quad(0,1,0,0),\quad(0,0,1,0),\quad(-1,1,1,0).\tag{10}\] The fourth component changes, unlike the failed componentwise recipe, and restores the \(b'\)-marginal. All masses remain one.

Yet \(p=(1-a)(1-b')\) is an original forbidden-tuple clause, and the ordered map \(p(T)=S_aS_{b'}\) gives \[p(T)r=(0,0,1,0,0,0),\qquad \epsilon p(T)r=0,\qquad \epsilon T_{a'}p(T)r=1.\tag{11}\] Thus the axiom image is a nonzero compatible vector of mass zero, and its legal degree-three cofactor has nonzero mass. Formula (9) verifies all these identities over every field. The Hardy relation system itself is satisfiable; its anchored \((a,b)=00\) section is not globally extendible. This is a counterexample for the specified initial family and completion, not for every possible unit or method.

Path calculation and repair. Let contexts \((x,y),(y,z)\) both forbid \(00\). Their compatible coefficient space has coordinates \(\theta=(m,x,y,z)\); each local moment map is an isomorphism. Zero fill gives \[T_x\theta=(x,x,x+y-m,0),\quad T_y\theta=(y,x+y-m,y,y+z-m),\quad T_z\theta=(z,0,y+z-m,z).\tag{12}\] At \(e=(1,0,0,0)\), the vectors \(T_xT_ye=(-1,-1,-1,0)\) and \(T_yT_xe=(-1,-1,-1,-1)\) differ, although their masses agree. Replace the missing \(xz\)-moment by \(m-y\): \[T_x\theta=(x,x,x+y-m,m-y),\qquad T_z\theta=(z,m-y,y+z-m,z),\tag{13}\] leaving \(T_y\) unchanged. The four actual satisfying assignments \(010,011,110,101\) have linearly independent vectors \((1,x,y,z)\) over every field (the determinant is a unit \(\pm1\)). On their span \(xz=1-y\); (13) is diagonal multiplication in this solution basis. Thus these maps are commuting idempotents, and \((I-T_x)(I-T_y)=(I-T_y)(I-T_z)=0\) on the entire space. The extra relation selects four models and excludes the allowed model \(111\); it is not claimed to follow from the clauses. This constructive correction shows why the zero-fill failure alone should change the completion before excluding the method. \(\square\)

Disposition and verification

The local-section source concerns compatible linear combinations, not PC multiplication. The earlier Hardy record reproduces its exact family; the present proof keeps that distinction. The design-presheaf construction with inclusion maps already restates locality of closure, whereas (1)–(4) use genuine marginal maps. The new source theorem supplies actual right inverses but not a solution to the old closure problem. Searches and exact records also distinguish the matching-specific product-kernel lift from this arbitrary cover.

The bounded GAP checker constructs actual overlap matrices, their nullspaces and right inverses using native exact linear algebra. Its complete output records the rationals and the binary and ternary fields, 72 compatibility checks, 12 idempotence checks and 288 mass-commutation checks. The Hardy state space has dimension six in thirteen local coordinates; the path has dimension four in six coordinates. The repaired path action and its clause annihilation, the Hardy cofactor failure and the old componentwise negative control all pass. It also checks 960 one-quadratic overlap ranks for all nonzero coefficient vectors at \(q=3,k=6\), and 93 nonzero degree-at-most-two support bounds on three bits. The single run reached its final success marker with harmless global-name warnings. These tests verify the finite rules and controls; they do not certify the asymptotic source event or a PC lower bound.

Follow-up. Continuing the local-section head. Compatibility and idempotence are now constructed on the intended source, but the graded kernel is open. Before further corrections, test whether the polynomial dimension of this space is compatible with any proper near-half PC kernel. Use leading monomials and their divisors to compare quotient dimension with the cutoff, including the case in which a stabilized quotient would give a forbidden full representation. Global commutation on the entire compatible space is no longer a permissible surrogate.

Sub-ideas

  • Determine the minimum possible quotient dimension of a proper degree-D multiplication-compatible Boolean kernel containing all active axioms of an unsatisfiable formula. Apply the exact cutoff and width conditions to the polynomial-dimensional compatible state space before repairing its operators.
  • For the central source's compatible coefficient space, derive the exact linear obstruction to selecting degree-two product vectors with prescribed local restrictions, symmetric variable order and the required degree-two axiom relations. Allow corrections in the kernels of the overlap maps and missing cross-context moments. Test whether those choices extend to a multiplication-compatible cyclic kernel; distinguish a low-degree repair from survival through the near-half cutoff.

Verification gate. The sole fresh-context GPT-6.1-Sol medium reviewer passed all four claims without corrections, found no unused mathematical hypotheses, and agreed with unchanged goal progress. It also agreed that the dimension-budget test should precede further repairs; see the review record. The full central-source, subset fourth-moment and separator-module records were reread, along with the closed design-presheaf scope and product-kernel comparison. The exact selector rules and negative controls have passed their bounded checks. No formalization or independent novelty claim is made.

Process assessment. The previous short turn only acknowledged already recorded guidance and made no research progress. This continuation revalidated the unfinished cycle, retained its complete output and develops its proof and checkpoint. The construction repairs the specific componentwise failure, while the vector/cofactor controls prevent presenting that repair as a PC invariant. No framework change is needed for this mathematical gap.
